### 14. recast() and horizontal (minor, effort M)

**Decision (D40):** Resolve.  
**For planning:** Fix all.

Rarely used combinations, but they refuse valid commands or draw wrong axes and styles.

- **A range source recast to line/scatter/connected refuses horizontal/vertical (twoway turns it); recast to bar/area/spike/dropline/dot with horizontal is turned by dash where twoway draws it upright** [RH-1, RH-2; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: dash.ado:2937-2972, 6659; twoway_y2xview_parse.class:51-65, 119; P01-P03, P10, P11, V2-V4
  - Fix: Judge h/v by kind range2yx: accept and turn line types; take silently and draw upright for bar-like types
- **A range source or ciplot() drawn as a yx type leaves the second bound out of the axis, the default title and the key** [RH-3, RH-4; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: dash.ado:6660; _dash_draw_range_as (dash_render.py:26601-26625); y2xview_g.class:60-75; P17-P19, P27, P28
  - Fix: Carry yvar2/hicol into the hull and the titles/keys
- **histogram recast(): drawn in the target's style (twoway keeps the histogram style), bars one unit wide (twoway uses the bin width), and 0 left out of the height axis** [RH-5, RH-6, RH-7; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: twoway_histogram_parse.class:160-193; map_histogram (dash_render.py:22223-22270); _dash_tag_eval_extent 61880-61897
  - Fix: Apply the histogram role's style and bar width; include 0
- **histogram/rbar with recast() plus horizontal: twoway draws a broken swapped serset; dash draws a clean transpose** [RH-8; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: twoway_yxview_parse.class:319-324, 456-468; P06-P12, Q22, Q23, V6
  - Fix: Document it under Differences (cheaper than copying)
- **Orientation flags judged differently: dot with a non-bar recast takes h/v (twoway refuses); scatteri recast to bar-like refuses h/v (twoway turns it); recast(b)/(ba) takes h/v and draws p1bar (twoway refuses and draws p1)** [RH-9, RH-10, RH-12; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: dash.ado:3258-3261, 2942; dash_render.py:45710; Q01-Q05, R02-R04, V7
  - Fix: Judge on the typed recast target; add scatteri to the derived lists
- **Abbreviated yx names on y2xtype sources (sc, li, co, b) are read as full names; twoway notes them and draws rspike** [RH-11; confirmed; tracked: untracked]
  - Evidence: y2xtype.class _unabbrev; dash.ado:3288
  - Fix: Use full-name minimums for yx names in the y2xtype table

## Help and README wording that is false today

Each line is a documentation change for planning. Packages 3, 5 and 12 add the help notes their D40 rulings require.

- dash.sthlp:156-157 and :181-182 'accepts the same syntax as twoway': say the twoway grammar, with the exceptions listed under Differences.
- dash.sthlp:185-187 'type dash where you would type twoway' and README.md:15-16 'dash takes the place of twoway': add caveats that name() registers instead of drawing, that there is no graph memory or graph export/putdocx/dyndoc image, that Python and bokeh are required, and that only macOS arm64 StataNow 19.5 MP has been tested.
- README.md:6-8 and dash.pkg:7-9 'Most twoway options are honoured; the rest are accepted and named in a note': options twoway refuses are refused, some valid options and values are refused, and some are noted and not drawn.
- README.md:59 'every difference from twoway': drop it, or make it true (jitter, histogram ticks and others are missing).
- dash.sthlp:737-745 'Two twoway plot types are not drawn' and :995-997 'names the two twoway plot types it refuses': remove sunflower; per D40 it is not a twoway plot type (see the decisions above).
- dash.sthlp:6459-6465 'a command copy-pasted from a do-file always runs': false (p3()-p19(), repeated label options, play(), unknown scheme(), dash's value bounds, resource limits, names, __dash variables, a bare plot after a group).
- dash.sthlp:286-289 'where dash differs, this file says so': false until the untracked items are documented.
- dash.sthlp:164-166 'the empty graph twoway draws ... with axes': twoway draws no axes.
- dash.sthlp:172-174 'works offline in any modern browser': say tested in Chrome only.
- dash.sthlp:4528-4529 (Stata's own ticks at build; range() widens the ticks), BUGS.md:4139-4143 and ROADMAP.md:5153 'exact at build': state where the model is used at build.
- dash.sthlp:4679 'a by() graph's panels take one fewer on y': on x as well.
- dash.sthlp:5670-5679 (aspectratio with xsize/ysize drawn at that size) and :5653-5655 (xsize(9) 'exactly as twoway does'): false in the browser.
- dash.sthlp:4760-4763 (yvarlabel/xvarlabel reach the titles and keys): not on the immediate, function, kdensity or histogram types.
- dash.sthlp:3472-3475 and :332 (headlabel and mangle behaviour), :4782-4786 (lalign same picture), :6573-6577 (dlalign), :6597-6600 ('measured on every plot type').
- dash.sthlp:6041 (area outline width): the default area has no outline. :6069 (V and v an open chevron). :6202-6205 (economist note in its box).
- dash.sthlp:372-386 (horizontal 'as twoway draws it', 'judged after recast()'). :596-598 (levels(): 'twoway takes any integer'). :5359-5361 (placement() drawn as twoway). :4567-4568 (custom 'as in twoway'). :4867 (tick grid in the minor style). :4617 (fill).
- dash.sthlp Differences item 5 and ~:1036 (refusals 'as twoway refuses them', 'naming the option given'): dash adds lines and names the full option.
- dash.sthlp:3013-3016 (the cap counts what the page embeds): say it counts _N times the columns.
- dash.sthlp:5434 and :5494 (colordiscrete with colorlevels 'drawn as Stata draws them'). :1177-1178 (msize any number). :3990-3995 (discrete draws one bar per value). :4192-4194 (a zero weight drawn at size zero 'as in twoway'). :5687-5690 ('gaps are not scaled').
- dash_setup.sthlp:41-43 and the 'Python is required but is not available to Stata' message: false when Stata found a Python by itself.
- The Differences section should list the resource limits, the Python requirement, the continuous colorvar mapping, jitter's rule, the histogram tick rule (until fixed) and recast+horizontal quirks.
- dash.sthlp:737-742: remove sunflower from 'twoway plot types not drawn' (D40).

## Differences a browser renderer cannot close

- The Graph Editor and play() edits: a .grec acts on Stata's graph objects, and interpreting it on a Bokeh figure is out of proportion (XL).
- Being a Stata graph in memory or a .gph file: a browser page cannot be one. It could only be emulated by also running twoway with nodraw.
- Exact jitter points: twoway's noise comes from the graph engine's own seed (gdi newjitterseed). The shape and scale can be matched; the points cannot.
- twoway's order among tied sort keys: it is not deterministic (sortseed), so no fixed order can be matched point for point.
- Dash pattern lengths below a pixel: Bokeh line_dash takes integers only.
- Running without Python: a Bokeh renderer needs Python and bokeh, so 'no dependencies' like twoway is not reachable.
- Dates past the browser's Date range (beyond about year 275760) cannot be drawn on a datetime axis.
- Reproducing Stata's compiled round-off (lpoly pilot fit at high degree, lpolyci zero-df band values) exactly is practically out of reach.
- A fixed physical graph size together with a responsive page: a browser figure at a given width cannot both fill the page and keep twoway's 7.5x4.5 in shape without letterboxing.
- twoway's broken pictures for histogram/rbar recast plus horizontal (RH-8) can be copied but should not be; documenting them is the sensible limit.

## What this audit could not cover

- Platforms: only console StataNow 19.5 MP on macOS arm64. Windows, Linux, Intel macOS, GUI Stata, Stata/BE, Stata/SE, Stata 19.0 and older Stata were not run.
- Mostly the saved page was compared. The served app was probed only for a few by() items, and behaviour after filter moves was excluded.
- No automated image diff. Comparisons used Bokeh models, SVG geometry and some headless-Chrome screenshots (Chrome only; SwiftShader WebGL, not a GPU). Some tick-label text written by CustomJSTickFormatter was not run in the axes area.
- Schemes: mostly stcolor and s2color, with some s1mono, economist and meta. 16 shipped schemes and user schemes were not re-run.
- The drawing fidelity of each of the 44 drawn types was not re-measured. Not covered: tsline on panel data, contour with recast().
- Many recast cells (lpoly, fpfit, mband, mspline, area/bar/spike sources, pc sources), ciplot geometry, and recast with mlabel/by/colorvar/yaxis(2)/weights.
- by(): per-type parity, clegend with contour, colorvar under by(), multi-variable by() with statistical layers, layouts under other schemes, kdensity range(varname).
- Estimator fidelity was taken from existing fixtures. Not measured: lowess tie determinism, mspline/mband under weights.
- Ecosystem: real dyndoc/putdocx/putpdf/markdown runs, iframe embedding, marginsplot/sts graph, GUI browser opening, dash serve r()/s().
- Installation: real pip runs (online, offline, proxy, conda); Python 3.15 and bokeh 3.10.
- Fonts: graph set fontface for plain text and non-ASCII font names (persistent preferences were off-limits).
- Browser load time and memory at 1M rows and at the cell ceiling.
- Repeated-option merges for titles and legend sub-options only partly drawn; other repeats in _dash_contour_split unprobed.
- Abbreviation binding for about 89 options compared by rc only, not by which option a shared prefix binds to.
- Data edges: %tC with tline/tlabel, %tc under set dp comma, value labels on a second axis; degenerate by() panels relied on existing cscripts.
- colorvar: key pixel geometry, served mode, hover, mlabel with colorvar, date-valued colorvar, schemes beyond s2color/s1mono.
- The full pytest and certification suites were not run (read-only audit). Only a few targeted tests ran.

## Appendix: every verified finding, by area

Withdrawn under D40 and its audit correction: plot-types PT-1, PT-2, PT-9 and PT-12; type-options-stat PT-4 and PT-17; docs-claims DT-01; tracking-tests TR-1; recast-horizontal RH-16. Finding ids repeat across areas, so each id is unique only within its area.

### plot-types (9)

- **PT-3** Plot-type names are read in any case; twoway refuses capitalised abbreviations and some capitalised full names — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: _unab_cmd (twowaygraph_g.class:953-967) expands abbreviations through -syntax-, so only lower case expands. Full names are then looked up as files (cutil which twoway_`cmd'_parse, which yxtype-`cmd'.style), so on macOS's case-insensitive file system some capitalised full names happen to resolve. Measured on macOS: refused r(198) '<word> is not a twoway plot type' for Sc, Li, Con, Hist, Rsc, Rl, Rcon, Fun, Fn, Mband; Histogram is refused r(198) (twoway_Histogram_parse.new class error); Contour price mpg weight is refused r(198) ('>15906 invalid name'); Scatter, SCATTER, Line, Bar, BAR, LFIT, Area, Rcap, Function, Scatteri, Mbands, Connected, Kdensity, Pcarrowi and Lowess are accepted with rc 0.
  - dash: Accepts every name and abbreviation in any case, rc 0, because it lowercases the word before matching. Measured: dash Sc/Li/Con/Hist/Rsc/Rl/Rcon/Fun/Fn/Mband/Histogram/Contour all 'graph registered'.
  - evidence: dash.ado:5868-5869 (strlower before _dash_expand_plottype); dash_parse.mata:2526-2528 (strlower again); probe p1.log TC 3/8/11 and p2.log T 11-21. There is no case test in test_plot_type_abbreviations.py or P1A-76. A2 (BUGS.md, closed) handled only lower-case prefixes.
  - fix: Match abbreviations case-sensitively (lower case only) and refuse a capitalised abbreviation with twoway's line. Decide what to do with capitalised full names: twoway's acceptance there depends on the file system, so on Linux it probably refuses them all (unverified). Pin the decision with a truth fixture and say it in the help.
  - verifier: _unab_cmd (twowaygraph_g.class:953-967) expands abbreviations through -syntax- options, which match lower case only; full names then resolve through file lookups. dash lowercases the word twice (dash.ado:5869; dash_parse.mata:2526-2528). p1.log TC 3/8/11 and p2.log T11-21 match the finding exactly (Sc, Hist, Contour, Li, Con, Rsc, Rl, Rcon, Fun, Fn, Mband and Histogram are refused by twoway and accepted by dash). The help says nothing about case for plot types, and no test pins it. dash accepts a superset, so no twoway do-file breaks; this is graded like the closed A2 abbreviation superset. Linux behaviour is unverified.
- **PT-4** The empty graph (dash, dash ||, dash (), dash in 1/5) draws two black axis lines where twoway draws no axes — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: 'twoway, name(e1)' exported to SVG draws only the graph-region and plot-region rectangles: no axis lines, ticks or labels. .Graph.xaxis1 does not exist.
  - dash: 'dash, saving(empty_dash.html)' draws a figure with two LinearAxis (left, below) with axis_line_color #000000, major_tick_out 5, Range1d 0..1 and an empty FixedTicker, i.e. two visible black axis lines. The help says 'With no plot, dash draws the empty graph twoway draws ... register a graph with axes and no plot', and test/test_bare_dash.py:74-80 asserts that the axes exist in both modes.
  - evidence: /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/plottypes/empty_tw.svg (rects only) and empty_dash.html (Bokeh JSON: LinearAxis axis_line_color #000000, twice); dash.sthlp:164-167; test/test_bare_dash.py:74-80
  - fix: In both renderers, draw no axes (axis visible False) on a figure that has no layer, as twoway does. Correct the help sentence, flip test_bare_dash.py's axis assertion, and add a truth check against twoway's exported SVG (also for 'twoway, title(T)').
  - verifier: I re-measured this myself (plottypes-verify/v1.do). Empty twoway under stcolor, under s2color, with title(T) and with 'if foreign': each exported SVG holds only the graph-region and plot-region rects (strokes the fill colour), plus the title text where given. There are no axis lines, ticks or labels. dash's empty page has two LinearAxis models with axis_line_color #000000 and no visible:false (parsed the Bokeh JSON). dash_render.py:63570-63576 adds only an invisible BoxAnnotation for a plot-less graph, so the axes stay. dash.sthlp:164-166 claims 'a graph with axes and no plot', and test_bare_dash.py:74-80 asserts the axes in both modes. No truth fixture covers the empty graph's drawing.
- **PT-5** The help's 'graph is read only before twoway' item is stale and contradicts the code and the help's own syntax section — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: 'twoway graph ...' and 'twoway graph twoway ...' are refused: 'graph is not a twoway plot type', r(198) (test/fixtures/twoway_command_form_truth.json prefix[23],[24]).
  - dash: The code refuses 'graph' (and gr, gra, grap, twoway, tw, ...) before any word, 'graph is not a twoway plot type', r(198) (dash.ado:109-118, _dash_prefix_word in dash_parse.mata:1149-1157), as dash.sthlp:156-163 says. But dash.sthlp:854-858, under Differences, says graph is still a prefix before twoway and that before any other word it is refused as "unrecognised plot type 'graph'". That message no longer exists anywhere in the code.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:854-858 against dash.sthlp:156-163 and dash.ado:109-118; no test pins the paragraph (grep of test/ is empty)
  - fix: Delete the paragraph, or rewrite it to match lines 156-163, and add a help-claim test.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:854-858 still says graph is a prefix before twoway and is otherwise refused as "unrecognised plot type 'graph'". dash.ado:107-118 refuses graph/gr/gra/grap/twoway/tw.. before anything, 'X is not a twoway plot type' r(198), as dash.sthlp:156-163 says. The quoted message appears nowhere in dash.ado, dash_parse.mata or dash_render.py except in history comments. No test under test/ pins the paragraph.
- **PT-6** tsline/tsrline on data that is not tsset: same rc as twoway, different message — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: 'time variable not set, use tsset varname ...', r(111) (from _ts, called by twoway_tsline_parse.class).
  - dash: 'dash: tsline needs a time variable: tsset your data first, as twoway tsline does.', r(111) (the same text for tsrline).
  - evidence: dash.ado:6033-6035 and :6278-6280; test/fixtures/parser_twoway_truth.json 'P1A-81 not tsset' logs; test_parser.cscript:2091-2093 compares rc only
  - fix: Print twoway's line, and have P1A-81 compare the message as well.
  - verifier: I re-measured this. 'twoway tsline mpg' on data that is not tsset gives 'time variable not set, use tsset varname ...' r(111). dash gives 'dash: tsline needs a time variable: tsset your data first, as twoway tsline does.' r(111) (dash.ado:6033-6035 for tsrline, :6278-6280 for tsline). test_parser.cscript:2091-2093 calls _p1a_vs_tw without a message, so only rc is compared, although the fixture holds twoway's line. Graded cosmetic rather than minor: the rc matches and only the text differs (the same class as PT-8).
- **PT-7** Refusals print lines twoway does not: the 6-line Supported: list after an unknown plot type, and a hint after a plot option given with no plot — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:743-745 (Supported list); hint line undocumented
  - twoway: One line: '<word> is not a twoway plot type' r(198), or 'option mcolor() not allowed' r(198) for 'twoway , mcolor(red)'.
  - dash: Prints the same first line and rc, then the Supported: list (6 lines) or 'After the plot groups' comma twoway takes graph options only; a plot's own options go inside its parentheses.'
  - evidence: dash.ado:5898-5904, :11106, :11238; probe p2.log PROBE DO 1/6/7; documented for the list at dash.sthlp:743-745
  - fix: Keep (and document the hint line) or drop both, to print exactly twoway's output.
  - verifier: The scope is wider than the finding says. In _dash_refuse_unknown (dash.ado:11186-11242) and the kernel path (:11104-11108), every unknown or misplaced option refusal prints a second dash-only line after twoway's 'option X not allowed', at plot level ('twoway does not take this option here.' / '...option names are lower case.') as well as after the plot groups' comma. It is not only for an option given with no plot. p2.log DO 1/6/7 show it. The Supported list is documented (dash.sthlp:743-745). The hint lines are not: dash.sthlp:910-920 and :965-973 describe these refusals as exactly twoway's.
- **PT-8** A weight glued to the plot type ('scatter[aw=price] ...') is refused with a different message — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway splits the type at '[' (gettoken parse(" ,["), twowaygraph_g.class:216). 'twoway scatter[aw=price] mpg weight' gives "invalid 'mpg'" r(198), and the same for hist[fw=rep78] mpg.
  - dash: Splits at a blank or comma only, so 'scatter[aw=price] is not a twoway plot type', r(198).
  - evidence: dash.ado:5868 (parse(" ,")); probe p2.log T/D 25-26
  - fix: Also split the type word at '[' so the rest reaches the varlist parser and gets twoway's refusal.
  - verifier: twowaygraph_g.class:216 uses gettoken parse(" ,["), and dash.ado:5868 uses parse(" ,"). p2.log T/D 25-26: twoway gives "invalid 'mpg'" r(198), and dash gives 'scatter[aw=price] is not a twoway plot type' plus the Supported list, r(198). Both refuse with the same rc and only the message differs, so cosmetic is right.
- **PT-10** matamatrix() plots (twoway mata) are refused, by name — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Plot types 'Not drawn'; BUGS.md B3 (closed as a by-name refusal)
  - twoway: 'twoway scatter matamatrix(M) [names]' plots a Mata matrix's columns (twoway_mata.sthlp, an undocumented [G-2] entry; twoway_yxview_parse.class:151-180), on every plot type.
  - dash: 'dash: matamatrix(M) is a twoway mata matrix plot, which dash does not draw...' r(198), whether or not M exists.
  - evidence: dash.ado:5935-5940; dash_parse.mata:2570-2588; dash.sthlp:737-743
  - fix: To close: read the matrix's columns by value into export columns, k-1 y columns against the last for scatter/line/connected and exact counts for the others, as twoway_mata.sthlp says.
  - verifier: The core finding holds: dash.ado:5935-5940 refuses matamatrix() by name and dash.sthlp:737-743 documents it (BUGS.md:3467 closed). The twoway_behaviour wording needs correcting, though. twoway_mata.sthlp lists only scatter, line, connected, area, bar, spike, dropline, dot and the r* range types; only twoway_yxview_parse.class reads matamatrix(). I measured 'twoway lfit matamatrix(M)' as r(111) 'invalid syntax', so it is not 'every plot type'. With M undefined, twoway says 'Q is not a mata matrix' r(198), while dash prints its own sentence r(198) on every type, so on the non-yxview types dash's rc (198) also differs from twoway's.
- **PT-11** After discard, the first dash command prints '_dash_dp_format() already exists' and '(11721 lines skipped)' — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway prints nothing extra after discard.
  - dash: discard drops the autoloaded dash.ado but not Mata. On reload, 'capture run dash_parse.mata' fails at the first function definition and the Mata error text still reaches the screen. The same happened after a twoway class-load failure (twoway normal, twoway Histogram).
  - evidence: probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/plottypes/p4.log (STEP 4 after discard; 'graph drop _all' alone does not trigger it); dash.ado:62-82 (the comment records rc 3000 on a second load but not the printed text)
  - fix: At load, skip the run when the library is already present (test that a dash Mata function exists first), or run it under quietly with output fully suppressed, and pin it with a cscript that runs discard.
  - verifier: p4.log:72-77: after discard, the first dash prints '_dash_dp_format() already exists' and '(11721 lines skipped)'. The same appears in p1.log after the twoway normal class-load failure. dash.ado:79-81 does 'capture run' of dash_parse.mata on every load of dash.ado, and the compile error text still reaches the screen. rc is 0 and the library still works. Nothing in BUGS.md, GAPS3.md or the help tracks it, and no cscript checks output after discard. It is output noise, not a syntax or drawing difference, so cosmetic.
- **PT-13** Open natscale entry: three of 264 tick sets differ after a filter move (or with no embedded ticks) — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'
  - twoway: Stata's compiled natscale (for example price #4 gives 5000(5000)15000).
  - dash: The fitted model gives 0(5000)15000 (and trunk #2/#3 differ), but only after a move or with no embedded ticks; at build the axis is Stata's own.
  - evidence: BUGS.md:4139 '- [ ] The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'; test/test_natscale.py _KNOWN
  - fix: As BUGS.md says: refit the model, or call gdi natscale where Stata is available.
  - verifier: BUGS.md:4139 is the only open entry ('- [ ] Title: The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'). test/test_natscale.py:65 _KNOWN holds auto price #4 and trunk #2/#3. At build the axis is Stata's own (WP-31 D15), so the initial drawn graph matches. It differs only after a filter move or for a spec built without the embedded ticks.

  Not covered: How twoway treats plot-type case on a case-sensitive file system (Linux) or on Windows: only macOS was measured, and twoway's acceptance of capitalised full names comes from the file system there.; The right arguments for iarrow, ilabels, ipoints and rtable were not found, so whether twoway accepts them is undetermined. 'normal' fails to load in Stata itself.; Drawing fidelity of each of the 44 drawn types was not re-measured. I confirmed only that truth fixtures exist for each; options and geometry belong to other areas.; tsline/tsrline on panel data (xtset/tsset with more than one panel, and onepanel): there is no truth test and I did not measure it.; The empty graph was measured only for bare 'twoway'. 'twoway, title(T)', 'twoway ||' and the empty forms under other schemes were not exported.; The served app's empty graph was not inspected directly. test_bare_dash.py asserts that axes exist there too.; User or community packages that add twoway plot types, beyond the one personal yxview_sarrows_draw.ado found, were not probed.

### type-options-basic (17)

- **PT-1** headlabel is ignored, with no note, on pcscatter and pccapsym that have mlabel(): labels stay at the first point — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'mlabprefix(), mlabsuffix() and plot-level lalign()...headlabel' closed; TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:81,84 still say unavailable)
  - twoway: pcscatter/pccapsym ..., mlabel(make) headlabel moves every label to the second point (y2,x2), as twoway_pccapsym/pcscatter.sthlp (gr_headlabopt) document. Measured: 'AMC Concord' moves from (3952,718) to (678,2559).
  - dash: rc 0 and no note. Labels are drawn at (x1,y1): in d26.html the label source __dash_mlabel_x/y decodes to weight/mpg. map_pcscatter and map_pccapsym call _apply_mlabel_opts with the first point only.
  - evidence: probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/plaintypes P26,P27 (o26.svg vs b26.svg; d26.html); dash_render.py map_pcscatter ~24940-25075, map_pccapsym ~25573-25660; dash_parse.mata _dash_tw_silent_tab headlabel row (generated from a probe without mlabel, noted_option_truth.json). dash.sthlp:3473-3475 says dash 'names it in the note' (false); dash.sthlp:6596 says twoway draws nothing different (false).
  - fix: Read headlabel in map_pcscatter and map_pccapsym in both renderers and label (x2,y2). Re-measure headlabel together with mlabel() in extract_noted_option_truth.do so tw_silent does not hide it. Correct the help.
  - verifier: I re-checked this. In dash_render.py, map_pcscatter (25019-25079) fills __dash_mlabel_x/y from x1/y1 only, and map_pccapsym (25692) passes x1var/y1var. Neither reads options['headlabel']. Only map_pcarrow (25461) and map_pcbarrow (25565) do, and the served copy test/serve_dashboard_app.py:47306/47410 is the same. Decoding d26.html gives label x=(2930,3350,2640,3250,4080) and y=(22,17,22,20,15), which are weight/mpg, the first point. In twoway's SVG, 'AMC Concord' moves from (3952,718) in b26 to (678,2559) in o26, and P27 (pccapsym) moves too. tw_silent's headlabel row (dash_parse.mata:8250) silences it on pcscatter/pccapsym. It was generated from extract_noted_option_truth.do:128, whose base command for pc types has no mlabel(). Help 3474 ('names it in the note') and 6593-6596 ('twoway draws nothing different') are both false for these two types. Headlabel on pcspike/pci is correctly silent: twoway draws no labels there (P28/P29 normalised diff 0). Untracked.
- **PT-2** headlabel does not move pcarrowi's immediate labels to the head — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md C1 entry 'pcarrow, pcbarrow and pcarrowi heads...' closed)
  - twoway: pcarrowi 20 2000 30 4000 "A", headlabel draws 'A' at the head: SVG text moves from (641,2727) to (5263,216).
  - dash: rc 0 and silent. The label source holds x1=2000, y1=20 (the tail). map_pcarrowi always passes x1/y1 to _immediate_labels ('A label sits at its arrow's tail').
  - evidence: probe plaintypes P30 (o30.svg vs b30.svg; d30.html __dash_pcarrowi_label source); dash_render.py map_pcarrowi ~25834-25888. dash.sthlp:3472-3473 and TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:71 ('headlabel drawn') claim it is honoured.
  - fix: In map_pcarrowi use x2/y2 for the labels under headlabel (both renderers). Add a truth probe.
  - verifier: map_pcarrowi (dash_render.py:25877-25886) always passes x1/y1 to _immediate_labels and never reads headlabel. dash.ado does not swap the values either; the only headlabel lines are 1502, 2856-2865 and 5433. In d30.html the label source is x1=2000, y1=20. twoway moves 'A' from (641,2727) to (5263,216). test_arrowheads.py:468-473 checks the head only for pcarrow/pcbarrow. test_mode_parity.py:2547 checks only that the saved and served modes match, not where the label is. So help 3472-3473 and TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:71 ('headlabel drawn') are false. Help 659 still says the label sits at the arrow's tail. Untracked.
- **PT-3** An mlabel() element that is a pattern naming several variables draws labels in dash; twoway draws none — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (closed BUGS.md 'degree() above 3, several variables in one mlabel() element...' is wrong)
  - twoway: scatter mpg price weight in 1/5, mlabel(t*) draws no marker labels: 12 <text> elements, the same as without mlabel. mlabel(trunk turn) draws 22. The same holds for mlabel(t*) when a variable t exists.
  - dash: rc 0. It draws two LabelSets (__dash_mlabel_trunk and __dash_mlabel_turn) through _dash_mlabel_list's unab splice.
  - evidence: probes /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/plaintypes5 (b1,o1,o2 svg) and plaintypes4 C1 (d1.html). BUGS.md:3311-3315 (C10b) closed this from parsed_values_truth.json serset rows, not the drawn picture. The help contradicts itself: dash.sthlp:3389-3393 says 'as in twoway', while 1244-1246 says dash refuses it and that twoway draws no labels.
  - fix: Draw no labels for a y whose mlabel element expands to more than one variable (measure first whether twoway draws none for the whole layer). Correct both help passages and the C10b record.
  - verifier: Measured in plaintypes5. 'scatter mpg price weight in 1/5, mlabel(t*)' gives an SVG with text identical to the no-mlabel baseline (diff 0 lines). mlabel(trunk turn) adds 10 labels. On one y, mlabel(t*) draws none either (o3 has 11 texts, o4 with mlabel(trunk) has 16). In plaintypes4 C1, where a variable t exists, twoway again draws none, but dash's d1.html has LabelSets on __dash_mlabel_trunk and __dash_mlabel_turn, rc 0. The C10b resolution (BUGS.md) rests on parsed_values_truth.json serset rows (the vars list includes trunk/turn), not on the drawn picture. The help contradicts itself: 1238-1240 (not 1244) says dash refuses the form and twoway draws none, while 3389-3393 says the pattern labels the following y's 'as in twoway'. mlabel(make-foreign) was not probed for the drawn picture either.
- **PT-4** lalign() on bar, area, rbar and rarea outlines is ignored in silence; dash also strokes the default outline centred where twoway strokes it inside — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: barlook_options and area_options document lalign (inside, outside, center). Default (alignstroke inside): bar outline rect x=836.73 w=394.74 inside the fill rect. With lalign(outside): x=791.37 w=485.46. With lalign(center) the outline changes again. Area, rbar and rarea also change.
  - dash: No note and no geometry change. Bokeh strokes every outline on its edge, so every outlined bar or area extends half an outline width beyond twoway's. The renderer only warns on an invalid word.
  - evidence: probes plaintypes P9,P33,P36,P39 and plaintypes3 P5 (tw_differs=True, dash note none); dash_render.py:15668-15677. dash.sthlp:4782-4786 claims twoway draws the same with any value: measured on line only (varlabel_mlabel_truth.json align.*, BUGS.md:3585). On lines (line, rcap, pcspike) twoway is indeed the same.
  - fix: Inset the bar, area and rarea outline by half the stroke (default inside), offset it outward for outside, and keep it centred for center (VBar/Patch geometry, both renderers). Otherwise name lalign() where it cannot be drawn. Add an SVG truth for the fill types.
  - verifier: twoway's parse folds LAlign onto area(linestyle(align)) (twoway_yxview_parse.class:567-569). Measured on bar P9: by default the outline rect (x 836.73, w 394.74, stroke 45.36) lies inside the fill rect (x 814.05, w 440.10). Under lalign(outside) it is x 791.37, w 485.46. On area and rarea (P33, P39), the default clips the stroke to the shape and outside switches to an evenodd outside clip. rbar (P36) and lalign(center) on bar (plaintypes3 R5) also change. dash_render.py:15668-15677 only notes an invalid word. map_bar has no inset and Bokeh strokes centred, so dash's default already differs: the bar extends half a stroke beyond twoway's, and on area the full stroke is visible where twoway shows only the inner half. The help's measurement was 'line mpg weight' only (extract_varlabel_mlabel_truth.do:116). Untracked.
- **PT-5** dlalign(center|outside) on dot changes the dots' size in twoway; dash takes it in silence — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md WP-32 'dash names options twoway takes and draws nothing of' made it silent)
  - twoway: Default inside: dot outer radius 4.93 (fill r 4.93 plus a ring r 1.69 w 6.48). dlalign(center): r 4.93 with a centred 6.48 stroke, outer radius 8.17. outside differs too (dot_truth.json D30 vs D01).
  - dash: No note. dlalign is never read (_dot_style docstring), and tw_silent drops it on every type because it was probed only as dlalign(inside), the default.
  - evidence: probes plaintypes P43, plaintypes2 P24; dash_parse.mata _dash_tw_silent_tab dlalign row; scripts/extract_noted_option_truth.do:125 probes dlalign(inside). dash.sthlp:6573-6577 says twoway draws the same picture (false).
  - fix: Size the dots' outline by alignment, or name dlalign center/outside. Probe non-default values when generating tw_silent: the table is keyed by option name and generalises a single value.
  - verifier: The dot pictures differ. P43 dlalign(center) gives r 4.93 with a 6.48 centred stroke, against the default's separate fill and r 1.69 ring (1127 normalised diff lines). plaintypes2 Q24, dlalign(outside), differs too (1500 lines), as does dot_truth.json D30 against D01 (dot_r 4.36 against 1.49). dash.ado:1452-1454 puts dlalign on _pending. _dash_silent_drop (dash_parse.mata:8267-8285) drops it by name alone on every type, because the tw_silent row came from a single dlalign(inside) probe (extract_noted_option_truth.do:125). This generalisation flaw is general: the table is keyed by option name and built from one value. Help 6573-6577 and the _dot_style docstring (dash_render.py:23704, 'named by dash.ado') are stale.
- **PT-6** mlalign(center) and mlalign(inside) give one picture in dash; twoway draws them differently, so the default marker outline may also differ — minor, unverified, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Three distinct SVGs: inside (default) c035a286, center 7de52be8, outside dd63ab73. The default marker outline is inside (fill r 21.74, ring r 16.88 w 9.72).
  - dash: inside and center are both silent and drawn alike. outside is noted. Bokeh strokes centred (dash_render.py:15937-15951), so the default picture is twoway's center unless the marker size compensates; that is not verified.
  - evidence: test/fixtures/varlabel_mlabel_truth.json malign.*; dash_render.py:15937-15951; dash.sthlp:4769-4772.
  - fix: Measure dash's marker outer diameter against twoway's default. Compensate the size for inside versus center, or name center.
  - verifier: varlabel_mlabel_truth.json malign gives three hashes: inside = bogus = c035a286, center = 7de52be8, outside = dd63ab73. dash_render.py:15937-15951 treats inside and center alike and in silence, and its own comment says Bokeh's stroke is twoway's center. _markersize_px (12583-12608) returns the nominal diameter, and I found no stroke compensation. So dash's default marker is most likely twoway's center picture, outer radius larger by half the outline width, not twoway's default inside. That last point is inferred from the code, not measured. The core claim, two twoway pictures drawn as one in silence, is confirmed.
- **PT-7** jitter() noise is not twoway's: different shape, scale and random stream — minor, wrong-output, partly, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: 'Spherical random noise ... percentage of the graphical area' (scatter.sthlp:354-370). Measured with jitter(10) jitterseed(1) on auto: displacement isotropic in graph units, radius at most 162 of a 5400x3240 graph, no points in the corners. It follows Stata's RNG under set seed.
  - dash: Uniform noise in a square of ±jitter/2 % of each axis's data range (about ±210 x ±128 graph units here), from numpy default_rng. jitterseed() never reproduces twoway's points, and an unseeded jitter changes on every render.
  - evidence: probe plaintypes2 P19 (b19/o19.svg); dash_render.py:13927-13957 _apply_jitter; dash.sthlp:5781-5792 documents dash's own rule without calling it a difference.
  - fix: Port twoway's jitter: isotropic, scaled to the graph, and drawn with Stata's runiform at registration (store the offsets) so jitterseed and set seed reproduce it.
  - verifier: Confirmed: _apply_jitter (dash_render.py:13927-13957) adds uniform square noise of ±pct/2 of each axis's data range from numpy default_rng. scatter.sthlp:354-358 says 'spherical random noise ... percentage of the graphical area'. In plaintypes2 Q19, twoway's maximum displacement is 162 graph units (|dx| ≤ 162, |dy| ≤ 150), which fits a circular, graph-scaled rule. Help 5781-5792 describes dash's rule without calling it a difference. Corrections: twoway's noise comes from the graph engine (view.jitter/.jitterseed). Without jitterseed it uses gdi(newjitterseed) (twoway_yxview_parse.class:1087-1091), not Stata's runiform. So 'follows Stata's RNG under set seed' is unproven, an unseeded twoway jitter likely also changes per graph, and the fix 'draw with Stata's runiform' would not reproduce twoway's points. Matching shape and scale is feasible; matching the exact points may not be.

In plaintypes, every probe that should change the picture does (normalised diffs: P1-P6 36-364 lines, P10/P11 4 (fill colour), P12 42, P14/P15 298, P16 25), and dash notes each. After normalising the random SVG ids, o23 (bmsize(2)) equals o24 (barbsize(2)), so the help's 'dash draws no back marker' is wrong for bmsize on the arrow types, which is barbsize, which dash draws. The rest is documented in dash.sthlp Unsupported ('Older spellings', 'Area, bar and line edge styles', 'Back markers'): category (b).

- **PT-6** function subchar() is not honoured: a valid command is refused, an invalid name is accepted, and the help claim is false — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: help Unsupported 'Type-specific options' (claim inaccurate)
  - twoway: function y = t^2, subchar(t) draws t^2 over range() (serset 0, .25, 1, 2.25, 4); subchar(1t) gives r(198) '1t invalid name'; subc() is the minimum (twoway_function_parse.class:56-71)
  - dash: subchar(t)/subc(t): r(111) 'error in expression: t^2', because the generator is run with x() = x. subchar(1t): rc 0 with an 'ignoring unsupported' note. If a variable t exists, Stata evaluates against it instead and the curve is wrong
  - evidence: probe1.log #230-#232; sersets2.log fsub; dash.sthlp:6551-6557 says subchar is 'named' and drawn as without it; dash_parse.mata:8128
  - fix: Validate subchar() as a name and pass it as x() to twoway__function_gen at registration
  - verifier: twoway_function_parse.class:55-71: SUBChar(name), and .func_x=subchar is passed as x() to twoway__function_gen. dash.ado:10448 hard-codes x(x), so 'function y = t^2, subchar(t)' gives r(111) 'error in expression: t^2' (probe1 #230/#231; sersets2 fsub: twoway draws 0, .25, 1, 2.25, 4). subchar(1t) gets rc 0 and a note where twoway gives r(198) '1t invalid name'. dash.sthlp:6550-6557 says dash draws the plot as without the option and names it, which is false for this case: dash errors and names nothing. Mitigation: subchar() is undocumented (absent from twoway_function.sthlp and g.pdf). dash's spelling table also gives 'sub' as the shortest form, where twoway's is 'subc' (not probed).
- **PT-7** lowess m (twoway's minimum for mean) is refused with a false 'twoway does not take this option here' — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: code comment only (dash.ado:1667-1671); not in help or BUGS.md
  - twoway: lowess mpg weight, m draws the running mean (serset identical to mean: mean 21.29768, sd 3.895097)
  - dash: r(198) 'option m not allowed / twoway does not take this option here'; me, mea, mean are accepted
  - evidence: probe1.log #71; run3.log #21-#22; lowm.log; dash.ado:1667-1671 (MEan declared one letter longer to avoid a clash with Msymbol)
  - fix: On lowess, read a bare m flag (no parentheses) as mean
  - verifier: twoway_lowess.sthlp:38 documents {cmdab:m:ean}, so 'm' is twoway's documented minimum. lowm.log: twoway lowess mpg weight, m gives the same serset as mean (mean 21.29768, sd 3.895097). dash.ado:1274 declares MEan and the comment at 1667-1671 explains why (clash with Msymbol). probe1 #71: dash r(198) 'twoway does not take this option here', which is false. Not in the help or BUGS.md.

'weight' is undocumented in twoway_lowess.sthlp.

### axes (21)

- **AX-1** xscale()/yscale(range()) beyond the data extends dash's default tick labels; twoway labels the data range only — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (residual note in closed BUGS.md entry P1.17b; test exclusion _DB_TICKS_ELSEWHERE)
  - twoway: On an axis with no label rule, range() widens only the scale. The ticks stay natscale over the data: yscale(range(0)) on mpg labels 10 20 30 40, range(100) labels 10 20 30 40, xscale(range(0 6000)) and xscale(range(1000 6100)) on weight label 2000(1000)5000. Only an explicit #N (ylabel(#5) yscale(range(100)) gives 20(20)100) or a by() graph widens the ticks over range().
  - dash: At build, on a default numeric or log axis, dash ticks over data plus range(): range(0) gives 0 10 20 30 40, range(100) gives 20(20)100, range(0 6000) gives 0 2000 4000 6000, range(1000 6100) gives 1000(1000)6000. Default date axes are already special-cased and match.
  - evidence: Probes /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/axis/p2 R1,R2,R3 and p1 C63 (twoway .Graph.yaxis1.major.dlg_tickpos against the dash saved-page FixedTicker). test/fixtures/axis_truth.json b2_range_0, b2_range_100 and b2_range_three all draw 10..40. Cause: dash_render.py:47748-47768 _dash_finish_count_ticks drops range() values only when _dash_ts_kind(kind). Known only as a residual in a closed entry (BUGS.md:3112 P1.17b, 'with yscale(range(0)) dash ticks 0(5)15 where twoway ticks 10(1)14'). Test excluded: test/test_bar_range_geometry.py:653-656 _DB_TICKS_ELSEWHERE.
  - fix: On a default (non-#N) numeric or log axis, place ticks over the data alone and keep range() in the hull only. Do this in the build, the served re-finish and the saved page's dash_axis_compute. Keep the widened ticks for explicit #N and for by() panels (b2_by_range0). Then remove the _DB_TICKS_ELSEWHERE exclusion and pin R1-R3.
  - verifier: I re-read the probes and the fixture. twoway R1/R2/R3 (SVG and dlg_tickpos) label 10 20 30 40 and 2,000-5,000. dash draws 0..40, 20(20)100, 0(2000)6000, and C63 gives 1000(1000)6000. axis_truth.json b2_range_0/_100/_three are twoway SVGs and all draw 10..40. test_axis_extents only checks that the hull holds the ticks, never that the ticks equal twoway's. Cause confirmed at dash_render.py:47748-47768: range() values join the tick range unless _dash_ts_kind. Only one residual note mentions it, inside the closed P1.17b entry (BUGS.md:3112), and that note blames it wrongly on '#N-over-range()'. dash.sthlp:4529 even states that dash's range() 'widens the ticks'. The fix exceptions are right: by() (R5 and b2_by_range0 match at 0..40) and #N (R4 matches). One nuance: on a log axis, R8 yscale(log range(5)) already matches (10 20 30 40), so the log case is not shown to differ. Major because ysc(r(0)) is a very common idiom.
- **AX-2** by() panels: twoway ticks the x axis with one tick fewer too; dash keeps 5 on x — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: In a by() graph twoway ticks the first x axis with the same reduced count as y (4 under stcolor), in every layout tried: by(foreign), by(foreign, cols(1)), by(rep78), xsize(10), xrescale. Examples: scatter mpg length, by(foreign) x 150 200 250; scatter mpg price, by(foreign) x 5,000 10,000 15,000; scatter mpg displacement, by(foreign) x 100(100)400.
  - dash: dash ticks a by() graph's x axes with the scheme's 5: length 140(20)240, price 0(5000)15000, displacement 100(100)500. The y axes (count 4) match. The fixtures used x variables (weight, mpg) where #4 and #5 coincide, so this was never seen.
  - evidence: Probes p8 Y1,Y3,Y4 and p9 W1,W3,W4,W5 (twoway SVG tick labels against dash FixedTicker). dash_render.py:40785-40799 _dash_by_numticks sets only out['y'] = ny-1. The help claim at dash.sthlp:4679 ('a by() graph's panels take one fewer on y') is incomplete.
  - fix: Give by() panels' first x axes numticks-1, as y has, keeping x2/y2 at the scheme's count (by_scales_truth f02/c17). Request that count from Stata at export (see AX-3). Add by() probes whose x variable has #4 != #5 (length, displacement, price) to by_scales_truth.
  - verifier: The twoway SVGs show length x as 150 200 250 in Y1, W1 cols(1), W3 by(rep78) and W5 xsize(10), so the count does not depend on width. Price x is 5,000-15,000 (Y3) and displacement x is 100-400 (Y4). dash draws 140(20)240, 0(5000)15000 and 100(100)500. _dash_by_numticks (dash_render.py:40785-40799) lowers only out['y']. dash's own model at #4 gives exactly twoway's length and displacement ticks (150 200 250; 100-400), so the fix is right. Price at #4 also needs Stata's answer: the model misses it (AX-3/AX-20). Every x variable in by_scales_truth (weight, mpg, dates) gives the same ticks at #4 and #5, which is why this was never seen. The help sentence at dash.sthlp:4679 is incomplete.
- **AX-3** Several axis kinds are ticked at build by the fitted natscale model, not Stata, so its misses show in the first drawing — major, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew' (open), whose build-time claim is false
  - twoway: twoway ticks with natscale/tsnatscale. Measured: by() y on price 5,000 10,000 15,000; ymtick(#4) and ymlabel(#4) on price at 5000 10000 15000; kdensity price, by(foreign) x 5000(5000)15000; line close date, xlabel(#4) xscale(range(14700 15600)) half-yearly 01jan2000..01jul2002.
  - dash: Each of these is drawn by the model at build: by() y on price 0(5000)15000 (scatter price mpg, by(foreign); by(rep78); by(foreign, yrescale) panel 1); ymtick/ymlabel(#4) at 0 5000 10000 15000; kdensity by() x 0(5000)15000; the date case yearly 2000..2003. Stata's ticks are asked for only at counts {5} plus #N found in [xy]N?(label|tick). So the by() count 4 and mtick/mlabel #N are never requested, and histogram, kdensity, function, scatteri, pci and pcarrowi layers are skipped.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C42; p2 B2; p3 N3,N4; p4 B4,B6,E2. dash_render.py:41811-41812 _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP; 41832-41838 counts and regex. False claims: BUGS.md:4139-4143 ('At build the drawn axis is Stata's own (WP-31 D15)') and ROADMAP.md:5153 ('exact at build through S45's embedded ticks'). The help (dash.sthlp:4528-4533) says computed layers 'take the model at once'.
  - fix: At export, also request the by() panels' reduced count (x and y) and every mtick/mlabel #N. For axes whose hull is not a variable range (fits, kdensity grid, function, immediates, range() under #N), ask Stata's _natscale/_tsnatscale over the computed lo/hi (dash already knows them at export) rather than the model. Correct the BUGS.md natscale entry and ROADMAP to say where the model is drawn at build.
  - verifier: The code confirms the mechanism. counts={5} plus #N matched by re.match(r'[xy]\d?(label|tick)\Z') (dash_render.py:41832-41838), so the by() count 4 and ymtick/ymlabel #N are never requested. _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP is at 41811. _dash_place_count_ticks falls back to the model when the table has no (lo,hi,n) entry. Build-time misses I confirmed in the probes: B2 and C42 (by() y on price: twoway 5,000-15,000, dash 0-15,000), B6 yrescale panel 1, B4 kdensity by() x, and E2 (date with #4 plus range: twoway half-yearly, dash yearly). The BUGS.md:4139 claim ('S45's stata_ticks table already does [it] for every axis and by() panel', 'At build the drawn axis is Stata's own') and the ROADMAP.md:5153 claim ('exact at build') are false. Two corrections. (1) The cited mtick/mlabel examples, N3 and N4, are visually identical: dash's extra tick or label sits at 0, on top of the major 0 tick or label. They show the mechanism, not a visible difference. (2) dash.sthlp:4529 already says histogram, kdensity, function, fit-grid and range-widened axes 'take the model at once'. That part is documented; the by() count and mtick/mlabel #N parts are not. This overlaps AX-2 and AX-20.
- **AX-4** histogram x axis is ticked over the bar edges; twoway ticks over the bin midpoints — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (test exclusion only)
  - twoway: twoway's histogram scale min/max are the bin midpoints (default_axes_truth P08: min 13.8125, max 39.1875 for mpg); the plot region still covers the edges. Measured: histogram mpg x 15(5)40; histogram price 5,000 10,000 15,000; price bin(8) and width(2000) 5000(5000)15000; rep78, discrete 1(1)5; weight, frequency 2000(500)4500; length, percent 140(20)220.
  - dash: dash reads every coordinate of the bars (left and right edges): mpg 10(10)40; price 0(5000)15000; width(2000) 0(5000)20000; rep78 discrete 0 2 4 6; weight 2000(1000)5000; length 140(20)240. The model over the midpoints gives twoway's answer in every case checked.
  - evidence: Probes p3 H1,H2,H9 and p2 H3,H4,H5,H6. H7 (discrete mpg), H8 (bin(5) start(10)) and B5 (by()) happen to agree. dash_render.py:46078 _dash_layer_extent reads all _DASH_X_COORDS. Known but excluded in test/test_default_axes.py:230-233 _NOT_DEFAULT = {('P08','x')}.
  - fix: Give the histogram's tick input as the bin midpoints (first to last bin centre, also horizontal) while the hull keeps the edges. Do this at build, in the served refit and in the saved page's refit. Drop the P08 exclusion and add probes for bin(), width(), discrete, frequency and percent.
  - verifier: default_axes_truth P08 records twoway's histogram mpg x scale as min 13.8125 and max 39.1875 (the first and last bin midpoints), with major 15(5)40. The SVGs confirm: H1 15-40, H2/H3/H4 5,000-15,000, H5 1-5, H6 2,000(500)4,500, H9 140-220. dash draws 10(10)40, 0-15000, 0-20000 (width 2000), 0 2 4 6, 2000(1000)5000 and 140(20)240. dash's own model over the midpoints (13.8125-39.1875, #5) returns 15(5)40. Histogram is in _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP, so the model runs over the bar edges. The only tracking is the test exclusion _NOT_DEFAULT={('P08','x')} (test_default_axes.py:230-233); no BUGS, GAPS or help entry mentions it.
- **AX-5** custom sub-option of xlabel()/ylabel()/xtick() is ignored: its styles reach every label and the grid — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort L, tracked: untracked (help claims it matches twoway)
  - twoway: custom applies the label, tick and angle edits to the ticks of that option only (tickset_g set_custom) and drops gridstyle edits. ylabel(25 "x", add custom labcolor(red) labsize(large)) draws only 'x' red and large. ylabel(25, add custom labcolor(red) grid glcolor(red)) keeps the grid #F0F0F0. ylabel(, custom labcolor(red)) with no list is refused, 'tick_styles[-1] invalid name', r(198).
  - dash: custom is in _DASH_AXLABEL_HONOURED and changes nothing. All y labels are drawn red at 14pt; the whole y grid is drawn red; ylabel(, custom labcolor(red)) is accepted, rc 0, all labels red.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C01,C02 and p4 G4 (SVG fills against Bokeh major_label_text_color and grid_line_color). G5 (custom with a full list) matches. _gs_parse_and_log_tickset.ado Options :427-438; dash_render.py:53662-53673. The claim at dash.sthlp:4567-4568 ('custom [is] accepted and change[s] nothing, as in twoway') was measured only without add (axis_look_truth custom_with/custom_without).
  - fix: Draw custom ticks as their own styled overlay: a second axis, or a LabelSet plus segment glyph at those positions with the custom label, tick and angle look. Ignore glcolor and other grid edits under custom. Refuse custom with style edits and no list as twoway does. Correct the help sentence.
  - verifier: _gs_parse_and_log_tickset.ado:427-438: with custom, grid_eds are dropped and the set/tick edits go to .set_custom for those ticks only. C01 SVG: only 'x' at 25 is #FF0000 and 157.44px; 10-40 stay black at 112.56px. dash draws every major label #ff0000 at 14pt. G4: twoway's y grid stays #F0F0F0; dash's grid_line_color is #ff0000. C02: twoway refuses with 'tick_styles[-1] invalid name', r(198); dash returns rc 0. 'custom' is in _DASH_AXLABEL_HONOURED (dash_render.py:53662-53673). The axis_look_truth custom_with/custom_without probes carry no style edits, so the help sentence at dash.sthlp:4567-4568 was never tested with them.
- **AX-6** Gridlines of xtick()/ytick()/xmtick()/ymtick() are drawn in the minor-grid style (dotted, gs5); twoway uses its major grid style — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: xtick(2500 3500, grid) and xmtick(2500 3500, grid) draw gridlines identical to the major ones: stcolor #F0F0F0, 9.72 units, dashed; s2color solid #EAF2F3, 8.64 units. Shipped s2color has 'gridstyle minor major' (scheme-s2color.scheme:1846).
  - dash: Drawn as the Bokeh minor grid. Under stcolor, xtick grid is #f0f0f0 dotted [1,3] at minor width, and xmtick grid is #505050 (gs5) dotted. Under s2color, xtick grid is dotted #eaf2f3.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C50 and p4 G1,G2 (SVG <line> styles against Grid minor_grid_line_*). dash_render.py:44966-44972 uses look minor_grid_color and minor_grid_dash; dash_render.py:13758. The help claim at dash.sthlp:4867 ('grid draws the minor grid in the scheme's minor_grid style') is false.
  - fix: Draw tick and minor-tick gridlines with the scheme's gridstyle for that tickset (major on every shipped scheme): major colour, width and pattern. Add an SVG truth case for xtick/xmtick grid under stcolor and s2color.
  - verifier: scheme-s2color.scheme:1846 has 'gridstyle minor major', and stcolor includes s2color. C50 (xtick grid) and G1 (xmtick grid) under stcolor: twoway draws the 2500/3500 gridlines exactly as the major ones (#F0F0F0, 9.72 units, dashed). G2 under s2color: solid #EAF2F3, 8.64 units. dash sets minor_grid_line_color to #f0f0f0 (C50), #505050 (G1) and #eaf2f3 (G2), always dotted [1,3]; the major grid is dashed [8,4] at 1.152. The cause is dash_render.py:44966-44972. The help sentence at dash.sthlp:4867-4868 describes dash's behaviour and is not marked as a difference.
- **AX-7** yscale(off fill) / xscale(off fill) does not keep the axis's space — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: fill (fillspace) keeps the space of an axis turned off: with yscale(off fill) the x labels stay at the default positions (2,000 at x=936), against x=557 for yscale(off).
  - dash: fill is accepted and read as the plain axis, so off fill is drawn exactly as off (axis visible=False, same borders); the plot widens.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C10 against C11 (SVG label x positions; dash pages identical). dash_render.py:45863-45869 lists fillspace as accepted, and _dash_scale_look (56719-56743) never reads it. The claim at dash.sthlp:4617-4618 ('extend, fextend and fill draw the plain axis, as in twoway') holds only without off.
  - fix: With off and fillspace, hide the line, ticks, labels and title but keep the axis's border space (min_border or an invisible axis of the same extent). Add an SVG case.
  - verifier: twoway C10 (off fill) puts 2,000 at x=936.39; C11 (off) puts it at x=557.38, so the space is kept. dash's C10 and C11 pages are identical (axparse diff shows no difference). The parser (_gs_parse_and_log_axoptions.ado:125-130) sets fill_if_undrawn. The help claim that fill draws the plain axis (dash.sthlp:4617) holds only without off. off fill is rarely used.
- **AX-8** ylabel()/xlabel() sub-options twoway draws but dash names and ignores: labelminlen(), noshowlabels/showlabels, nopgmgrid/pgmgrid, style() — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: labelminlen(10) pads the labels: y labels move from x=455 to 965 and the plot narrows. ylabel(, noshowlabels) hides the labels. ylabel(, showlabels nolabels) shows them. ylabel(, nopgmgrid) removes the y grid. ylabel(, style(major_vert_nolabel)) hides the labels. labelminlen is in twoway's help (axis_label_options.sthlp:123, 367); the others are in its parser (_gs_parse_and_log_tickset.ado Options).
  - dash: Each is named in a note ('ylabel() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: ...') and drawn as without it: labels shown, grid kept, no padding. ylabel(, showlabels nolabels) hides the labels. labelkeepleadingzero is also named, though twoway draws the same .1 .2 (measured, cosmetic note only).
  - evidence: Probes p1 C03,C05,C06,C07,C08 and p5 F9,F11. dash_render.py:53662-53673 _DASH_AXLABEL_HONOURED lacks these. None is in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Draw noshowlabels/showlabels as nolabels/labels and nopgmgrid/pgmgrid as nogrid/grid. Draw labelminlen(#) as a minimum label width (pad or min_border). Read ticksetstyle files for style(). Take labelkeepleadingzero in silence where twoway draws the same.
  - verifier: The Options syntax in _gs_parse_and_log_tickset.ado declares NOSHOWLABels, SHOWLABels, NOPGMGRID, PGMGRID, STYle(), labelminlen() and labelkeepleadingzero. Measured in twoway: C05 has no y labels; C06 has no y grid (122 lines against 322); C08 moves the labels to x=965.59 (from 455.29); F9 style(major_vert_nolabel) shows no y labels; F11 showlabels nolabels keeps the labels. dash names each one and draws it as absent; for F11 dash hides the labels (0px). The labelkeepleadingzero drawing matches (C03 = C04, '.1'), so that part is note-only, as AX-21 says. None appears in dash.sthlp.
- **AX-9** xscale()/yscale() atanh, logit, log1m and style() are named and not drawn; nolog/noreverse/ring() print notes twoway does not — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort L, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: _gs_parse_and_log_axoptions.ado accepts ATANH, LOGIT and LOG1m and scale.class draws them as transformed scales (yscale(atanh) tick gaps 734/674/674/734 units; logit 765/632/765). yscale(style(vertical_nogrid)) removes the y grid. nolog, noreverse and ring() are silent.
  - dash: atanh, logit and log1m are named in a note and drawn linear. style(vertical_nogrid) is named and the grid kept. nolog, noreverse and ring(3) are named, though the drawing matches where measured.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C12,C13,C14,C15,C16,C17,C19 (SVG positions; dash notes). dash_render.py:56685 _DASH_SCALE_SUBOPTS and 45863-45869 _DASH_SCALE_LOOK_SUBOPTS. scale.class:149-204. Not in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Take nolog, noreverse and nofillspace in silence. Read axisstyle files for style(). Draw atanh/logit/log1m through a data transform or a CustomJS scale, or name them in the help's unsupported list.
  - verifier: _gs_parse_and_log_axoptions.ado declares ATANH, LOGIT and LOG1m and sets the scale transform. The SVGs show non-linear spacing: C12 atanh gaps 734/674/674/734; C13 logit 765/632/765; C14 log1m runs reversed and non-linear. C17 style(vertical_nogrid) removes the grid (122 lines against 322). dash names all of these and draws a linear axis with the grid kept. Tracking correction: GAPS3.md D7 already listed 'atanh logit log1m' as named. The BUGS.md:3563 entry for D7 was closed ('Closes GAPS3 D7') without them; its title drops them. So this is a tracked gap closed without a fix, not one that was never seen. The nolog/noreverse/ring() notes duplicate AX-21.
- **AX-10** Contradictory scale transforms are accepted where twoway refuses them — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: yscale(log nolog) is refused, 'only one of options nolog or log is allowed', r(198). yscale(atanh log) is refused, 'only one of options log or atanh is allowed', r(198) (opts_exclusive on the transform words).
  - dash: Both accepted, rc 0, with a note naming nolog or atanh.
  - evidence: Probes p5 F1,F8 (twoway rc and message against dash rc).
  - fix: Apply twoway's opts_exclusive over nolog/log/noatanh/atanh/nologit/logit/nolog1m/log1m in one xscale()/yscale() copy (after the repeat merge, as twoway merges), with its message and r(198).
  - verifier: The twoway parser runs opts_exclusive over nolog/log/noatanh/atanh/nologit/logit/nolog1m/log1m. F1 gives 'only one of options nolog or log is allowed' and F8 'only one of options log or atanh is allowed', both r(198); dash returns rc 0 for both. Correction to fix_needed: the check applies within one copy only. C16 yscale(log) yscale(nolog) is rc 0 in twoway and draws a linear axis, and dash matches. Running the check after dash's repeat merge would wrongly refuse C16.
- **AX-11** Log axis over data with zero or negative values is ticked over the positive values only — minor, wrong-output, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter z weight, yscale(log) with z = mpg-25 (-13..16) ticks 10 20: natscale over all the data, ticks at or below 0 dropped.
  - dash: Ticks 5 10 15: natscale over the positive values only.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C20 and p4 G6. dash_render.py:46078 _dash_layer_extent counts 'positive ones on log'. The help (dash.sthlp:4663-4665) claims twoway's rule.
  - fix: On a log axis, run natscale over every finite value and then drop ticks <= 0. Keep only positive values for the hull.
  - verifier: The tick part is confirmed: twoway C20/G6 dlg_tickpos is 10 20, dash is 5 10 15. That contradicts dash.sthlp:4662 ('over the data's own values'). But twoway's whole drawing here is degenerate. In the C20 SVG, 60 non-positive points are piled at the bottom edge (cy=2693.76), the 14 positive ones are squashed into y=185-195, and the labels '10' and '20' sit 2 units apart at the top. dash draws only the positive points, spread over 1-17. Changing only the ticks, as proposed, would not reproduce twoway. This is pathological input (log of non-positive data), so it rarely matters, and the difference is the scale and markers, not just the ticks.
- **AX-12** ztick(), zmtick(), zmlabel() are ignored, and zlabel(, angle()) is named, on the colour key — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: axis_label_options include {z}tick(), {z}mlabel() and {z}mtick(), drawn on the colour key. zmlabel(7500) adds a '7,500' label to scatter ..., colorvar(price); ztick and zmtick add tick lines.
  - dash: 'ignoring unsupported option(s): ztick()' (also zmtick, zmlabel); zlabel(, angle(45)) is named. ztick/zmtick/zmlabel are not in dash.sthlp's Unsupported list, which the help says test_help_conformance keeps in step with the note.
  - evidence: Probes p6 Z1-Z4 (twoway SVG text and line counts against dash notes). dash.ado:995 declares only ZLABel/ZTItle/ZSCale. test/fixtures/catchall_options_truth.json has 0 occurrences.
  - fix: Declare and draw ztick/zmtick/zmlabel on the ColorBar (extra ticks and labels), or at least list them in the Unsupported section and the catch-all truth.
  - verifier: axis_label_options.sthlp:187-222 documents ztick(), zmlabel() and zmtick(). In twoway, Z3 SVG shows a '7,500' label on the key and Z4 rotates the key labels -45. dash prints 'ignoring unsupported option(s): ztick()' (and the same for zmtick and zmlabel), and names zlabel(, angle(45)). dash.ado:995 declares only ZLABel, ZTItle and ZSCale. ztick, zmtick and zmlabel appear in neither dash.sthlp nor catchall_options_truth.json (0 hits), so the help's conformance claim cannot catch them. Untracked.
- **AX-13** Axis title textbox sub-options other than size/color/prefix/suffix/axis are named, not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences from twoway (textbox sub-options paragraph)
  - twoway: xtitle()/ytitle() take every textbox option (axis_title_options.sthlp): orientation(), justification(), alignment(), margin(), linegap(), box/nobox, bcolor/fcolor/lcolor/lwidth/lpattern, bmargin(), placement(), tstyle(), width(), height(). An example is ytitle("Y", orientation(horizontal)).
  - dash: Named in a note ('ytitle() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: orientation(horizontal)'); the title is drawn in its default orientation and box.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C30-C33 (dash notes). dash_render.py:53826-53827 _DASH_TEXTBOX_HONOURED; 53841-53893 _dash_textbox (box sub-options only for title/subtitle/note/caption). Documented at dash.sthlp:1102-1105.
  - fix: Draw at least orientation() (axis_label_orientation), margin()/bmargin() (axis_label_standoff, borders) and justification()/placement() (axis_label_text_align); name the rest.
  - verifier: _DASH_TEXTBOX_HONOURED={size,color,prefix,suffix,axis} (dash_render.py:53826). The C30-C33 notes name orientation(), box, justification(), margin(), placement(), linegap(), width() and height(). This is documented under Differences from twoway (dash.sthlp:1093-1105), so it is a deliberate but real gap.
- **AX-14** tstyle(), labstyle(), gstyle(), tlstyle(), glstyle() and nogextend are named, not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Axis and Display Options ('Difference from twoway: nogextend ... labstyle() ...')
  - twoway: Each takes a style file: ylabel(, tstyle(major_notick)) removes the ticks (6 tick lines against 11). nogextend stops the gridlines at the scale ends (axis_look_truth gx_nogextend: 454-3795 instead of 390-3859).
  - dash: Each is named in a note and drawn as without it: ticks kept, gridlines across the whole plot.
  - evidence: Probe p5 F12. dash.sthlp:4568-4572. dash_render.py:53662-53673.
  - fix: Read the shipped tickstyle, textstyle, gridstyle and linestyle files (dash already reads schemes). Draw nogextend with Grid.bounds at the scale ends.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:4568-4572 ('Difference from twoway: nogextend ... labstyle(), tstyle(), gstyle(), tlstyle() and glstyle()'). F12 tstyle(major_notick): twoway removes the y ticks (318 lines against 322); dash names it and keeps them. axis_look_truth has gx_nogextend.
- **AX-15** xtick() and xmtick() share Bokeh's one minor tick set, so both are drawn at one length — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Minor ticks and labels paragraph
  - twoway: xtick(10 20) xmtick(15): major-length ticks at 10 and 20, a half-length minor tick at 15.
  - dash: All three drawn at one length (CustomJSTicker extra + mextra as minor ticks).
  - evidence: Probe p1 C51 (dash ticker args extra and mextra share minor_tick_out 5). dash.sthlp:4877-4879.
  - fix: Draw one of the two sets as a separate glyph (Segment) at its own length, or a twin axis carrying only those ticks.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:4868-4870 ('Bokeh draws one set of minor ticks per axis ... at the major ticks' length'). The C51 page puts extra and mextra on one minor tick set.
- **AX-16** More than 2,000 ticks in one option keep the default ticks — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Axis labels paragraph
  - twoway: xtick(1500(1)5000) draws 3,501 ticks; xlabel(1500(1)3600, nolabels) draws 2,101.
  - dash: Note 'an axis label option asks for more than 2,000 ticks ...; the axis keeps its default ticks.'
  - evidence: Probes p5 F6,F7. dash.sthlp:4524-4525.
  - fix: None required (resource limit). If kept, list it under Differences from twoway.
  - verifier: F6 and F7: twoway draws the 3,501 and 2,101 ticks; dash keeps its default ticks and prints a note. This is documented in the Axis labels paragraph (dash.sthlp:4524-4525) but is not marked 'Difference from twoway'. Such counts are very rare and hit a resource limit, so cosmetic.
- **AX-17** A reference line outside the axis's range is not drawn in the graph-region margin — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Reference lines (documented)
  - twoway: An xline()/yline() outside the scale is drawn where it falls on the canvas, e.g. in the margin strip beside the plot region (xline(5100) on scatter mpg weight).
  - dash: Clipped to the plot frame (Bokeh Span), so such a line is not seen.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp Reference lines paragraph (5056-5067). GAPS3 D12 corrected (refline_frame_truth.json).
  - fix: Draw the out-of-range line as a canvas-unit annotation outside the frame, or leave as documented.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:5098-5107. refline_frame_truth.json: x_5100 is outside the scale but on the canvas in twoway. x_10000 lands at a wrapped coordinate (px 842.4) in twoway, a twoway artefact dash need not copy. This only affects a thin margin strip, so cosmetic rather than minor.
- **AX-18** A date axis is kept where twoway labels Stata numbers (first layer lfit/qfit/kdensity/lpoly/function/scatteri/pci/pcarrowi); xscale(log) on a date axis is drawn linear — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Computed layers of a date / Log scales on date axes (documented)
  - twoway: kdensity date (sp500) labels 15000(100)15400; lfit close date, range(14900 15400) labels 14900(100)15400; xscale(log) on a %t axis draws a log scale in date labels.
  - dash: A date axis with calendar ticks (kdensity date: 01jan2001 ... 01jan2002; lfit: quarterly 01oct2000 ... 01apr2002). xscale(log) on a date axis is linear with a note.

  - fix: Label those axes with the variable's numbers when the first layer is one of those types, as twoway does; draw log date axes on a log scale with date labels.
  - verifier: Both parts are documented as differences: computed layers of a date, which keep the date axis where twoway labels numbers (dash.sthlp:4815-4817 and 4820 for scatteri/pci/pcarrowi/function), and log scales on date axes, drawn linear with a note (4723-4731).
- **AX-19** Smaller documented axis differences: string/hex formats, values past browser dates, plot-region margin after a resize — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Axis and Display Options (documented)
  - twoway: format(%12s/%21x/%16H) writes twoway's own text; values past years -271821/275760 are drawn; the plot-region margin is an absolute size.
  - dash: Such formats are written %9.0g with a note; values past the browser's dates are not drawn (note) and ticks past Stata's dates are natscale over numbers (0 2.00e+07 ... where twoway draws 2.50e+07 ...); the margin is a frame fraction fixed at build (up to ~1.3% of the width off after a resize).

  - fix: None needed for the claim, since each is documented. Optionally fix the past-Stata-dates tick rule.
  - verifier: Each part is documented: %12s/%21x/%16H written as %9.0g (dash.sthlp:4741-4744); values past browser dates, and the natscale ticks past Stata's dates (4820, 'Difference from twoway'); the plot-region margin fixed at build (4529-4530, 4639-4643).
- **AX-20** The natscale model misses three of 264 axes after a filter move (and wherever it is drawn at build, AX-3) — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort L, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'
  - twoway: price #4: 5000(5000)15000; trunk #2/#3: 0 and 20.
  - dash: Model gives 0(5000)15000 and 0(10)20 after a move. At build it is exact only where Stata's ticks are embedded (N1 trunk #3 and N2 price #4 matched at build). The miss reaches the first drawing through AX-3's routes.

- **AX-21** dash prints notes where twoway draws the same picture silently (nolog, noreverse, labelkeepleadingzero, ring()) — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Silent; same drawing.
  - dash: '(note: dash: yscale() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: nolog noreverse)' and similar.
  - evidence: Probes p1 C03,C15,C16,C19.
  - fix: Take them in silence where the drawing is the same, as the help's 'Not drawn' rule says.
  - verifier: The logs show notes for C03 labelkeepleadingzero, C15 'nolog noreverse', C16 nolog and C19 ring(3). The twoway drawings are the same as the defaults (C03 = C04 labels; C19 label positions and 322 lines same as the default; C16 linear in both). This also contradicts the ROADMAP.md:5152 claim 'every option twoway takes silently taken silently'.

  Not covered: Tick-label text: dash writes labels in the browser through CustomJSTickFormatter, and no JS was run. So format(), value-label text, %tb/%tC text, set dp comma, and twoway's '.5' against '0.5' were not compared; only positions were.; Pixel geometry beyond a few SVG positions: tlength/tposition/labgap/alternate/altgap/titlegap/outergap/plot-region margin were not re-measured; I relied on axis_look_truth.json and its tests.; xline()/yline()/tline() sub-options (style(), extend/noextend, lstyle, axis(#), date literals) and the t-axis family (tlabel/ttick/tmlabel/tscale(range(datelist))) were not probed; I relied on refline_*_truth.json and taxis_truth.json.; Axes 4-9 placement and alt, axisn date axes, horizontal layers on yaxis(2), axis(#) refusal ordering: not probed.; Only the saved page was parsed; the served app and after-move behaviour (dashboard-only) were not exercised beyond what the code shows.; Only stcolor (the installed default) and one s2color case were measured; other shipped schemes' tick counts, angles and grid styles were not.; Repeated-option merging of axis options (merged-implicit/explicit across copies), apart from yscale(log) yscale(nolog) and custom.; The ring() effect on xscale/yscale and labelkeepleadingzero in a value-label path: no visible effect was found in the one probe each.; zlabel/zscale details on contour and colorvar keys beyond ztick/zmtick/zmlabel/zlabel(angle) (colour-key area).; Whether the by() 'one fewer' rule holds for every layout and scheme: measured only for by(foreign), by(foreign, cols(1)), by(rep78), xsize(10) and xrescale under stcolor.

### legend-titles-text (27)

- **LT-1** Default style of a legend's title() and subtitle() is wrong: drawn at the key-text size and colour, not with textboxstyle leg_title (heading) and leg_subtitle (subheading) — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (legend_geometry_truth.json 'title' records only title_text)
  - twoway: legend(title("T")): under stcolor the title is 157.44 SVG px against 112.56 px keys. Under s2color it is 139.96 px in dknavy #1E2D53 against 99.99 px black keys. legend(subtitle("S")) is 123.69 px under stcolor (subheading).
  - dash: Legend.title_text_font_size and title_text_color are copied from the key labels (10pt, key colour) for both title() and subtitle(). The scheme's leg_title and leg_subtitle styles are never read: grep finds no leg_title, leg_subtitle or legend_title_position in dash_render.py.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:39260-39267 and 39300-39304. Probes p1 L_ttl and p2 M_ttl/M_subsz (SVG vs d_*.html Legend model) in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/legend-titles. Scheme lines: scheme-s2color.scheme:1322-1323 and :139/:316.
  - fix: Style the legend title and subtitle from SchemeParser.textbox_style('leg_title'/'leg_subtitle'), with color()/size() still overriding, in both modes. Add a truth probe for the size and colour.
  - verifier: dash_render.py:39260-39267 copies label_text_font_size/colour into title_text_*; the subtitle path (39300-39304) does the same. Grep finds leg_title/leg_subtitle nowhere in dash_render.py, and the 'legend_title' rows at 11580/11683 serve tstyle() lookup only. The probe SVGs agree: stcolor title 157.44px vs 112.56px keys (tw_L_ttl), s2color title 139.96px #1E2D53 vs 99.99px keys (tw_M_ttl), stcolor subtitle 123.69px (tw_M_subsz). dash draws 10pt in the key colour in all three. Scheme lines check out (s2color:1322-1323, stcolor:21 'textboxstyle leg_title legend_title'). The legend_geometry_truth 'title' record has only title_text, so this is untracked.
- **LT-2** legend(nobox) removes the legend's region box; twoway's nobox is the key-text textbox option and changes nothing — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (help documents the wrong behaviour as drawn)
  - twoway: Under s2color the SVG of legend(nobox) is identical to the default: white fill and black outline, 5.76 wide. dash's own fixture legend_geometry_truth.json 'nobox' records the box drawn.
  - dash: border_line_color=None and background_fill_color=None, so the outline and fill disappear. The help says 'nobox removes it', and test_legend_geometry.py:386-389 pins this behaviour without comparing it to the truth.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:39305-39307. dash.sthlp:4472. test/test_legend_geometry.py:386. Fixture: test/fixtures/legend_geometry_truth.json nobox == default_geometry. Probe p2 M_nobox vs M_ctl: SVG SAME, dash Legend border null.
  - fix: Treat nobox and box as the key-text textbox box (see LT-3). Leave the region box to region(). Fix the help line and flip the test to the truth.
  - verifier: In legend_options.sthlp:77, nobox and box belong to the textbox_options for the key text; the region box is region(). The fixture legend_geometry_truth.json 'nobox' has the same outline (#000000, 5.76) and box as default_geometry, and tw_M_nobox matches tw_M_ctl exactly (0 diff lines). dash_render.py:39305-39307 sets both border and fill to None. dash.sthlp:4472 says 'nobox removes it', and test_legend_geometry.py:386-389 pins dash's behaviour without the truth. Severity lowered from major: under stcolor, StataNow's default, the legend region is white fill with a white outline (tw_L_ctl rects at 4090/4093), so removing it shows only where the legend overlaps something (ring(0)) or under schemes with a visible box such as s2color.
- **LT-3** Key-text textbox options in legend() are not drawn: box, bcolor(), fcolor(), justification(), alignment(), margin(), linegap(), tstyle(); box is taken in silence — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: generic only (dash.sthlp:1043-1049 'a sub-option ... dash does not implement is named'); box untracked
  - twoway: legend(box) frames each key's text: rects at 4465,1304 733x112. bcolor()/fcolor() fill those frames. cols(1) justification(right) right-aligns the texts. alignment(top) raises them 17 units. margin(large) widens the legend. linegap(5) spreads multi-line labels. tstyle(heading) enlarges the key text.
  - dash: box: nothing drawn, no note. The others are named in the note ('legend() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored') and not drawn.
  - evidence: _DASH_LEGEND_SUBOPTS dash_render.py:53797-53803 ('box' honoured but unused, except in the nobox test at :39305). Probes p1 L_box, L_bcol, L_fc, L_just, L_al, L_mar, L_lg, L_ts: twoway SVG differs from its control each time.

The probe log shows no note for L_box. bcolor/fcolor/justification/alignment/margin/linegap/tstyle are named ('not implemented and ignored'). Twoway SVG diffs confirm each one: L_box adds two framed rects per key, L_bcol/L_fc recolour them, L_just moves the texts to text-anchor end, L_al moves y from 1400.74 to 1383.65 (17 units), L_mar shrinks the plot from 3641 to 3122, L_lg grows the box from 479 to 641, L_ts shrinks the plot.
- **LT-4** legend(ring(0) bplacement()) ignores bplacement — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (legend_geometry_truth 'bplacement' measured only pos(6) bplacement(w), outside)
