Twoway puts the legend rect at 449,152 for ring(0) bplacement(nw), at 449,2360 for ring(0) pos(1) bplacement(sw), and at 4090,1256 for ring(0) alone. dash draws location center_right for the first and the default top_right for the second, with no note. legend_options.sthlp:723-724 documents bplacement at ring(0). BUGS D4 is closed, and its fixture measured only bplacement outside the plot, so this is untracked. Major kept: the legend lands in a different part of the plot, over the data.
- **LT-5** legend(all), legend(draw) and legend(nodraw) are named and not drawn, so whether a legend appears differs from twoway — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked

Probes: tw_L_draw1 matches tw_L_on1 exactly, while dash's Legend has visible:false. tw_L_nodraw has no key text, while dash draws the legend. tw_L_mrgall differs from tw_L_mrg (legend_g.class:161-206 'all'), while dash merges to one LegendItem with visible:false. legend_g.class:239-240 reads force_draw/force_nodraw. The help's general statement (dash.sthlp:1043-1049) that unimplemented sub-options are named covers these, so 'generic only' is a better tracking label than 'untracked'.
- **LT-6** Legend layout sub-options named and not drawn: style(), width(), height(), xoffset(), yoffset(), showindex, region(style()), region(lstyle()), region(lalign()) — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort M, tracked: generic (dash.sthlp:1043-1049); TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:143 'Many legend region/layout suboptions are not implemented'

Twoway SVG diffs against L_ctl are non-zero for L_sty, L_w, L_h, L_xoff, L_yoff, L_idx, L_rsty and L_rls. L_rla against L_rlw moves the outline rect out (4113 to 4067). The tracking is generic: dash.sthlp:1043-1049 and TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md. Under stcolor the region box is white on white, so region(style/lstyle(none)) has no visible effect there.

Twoway widens the title box from 2833..2976 to 522..5286, and legend(bexpand) shrinks the plot from 3641 to 2438. - **LT-8** Title box default fill and line under stcolor: dash uses the added-text box colour, not textboxstyle's 'color textbox' — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (textbox_truth 'box' probe sets bcolor() explicitly)

Twoway under stcolor fills title("T", box) #FFFFFF with a 6.48 stroke and text(...,box) #D9E6EB with 12.96. dash fills the title box #d9e6eb at 0.768px, which matches s2color only (tw_S_tbox #D9E6EB). Severity lowered from major: it appears only when box is given.
- **LT-9** A fractional ring() is dropped in silence: titles and the legend fall back to the default ring — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Rings are ordinal, and non-integers are allowed. subtitle("S", ring(7.5)) is drawn outside the title. legend(pos(6) ring(4.5)) with note() is drawn outside the note.
  - dash: _dash_textbox_position accepts only [0-9]{1,2}, so the subtitle keeps ring 6 (Title tag ring=6) inside the title. The legend reads r'(\d+)' as a full match, so it stays at ring 3 inside the note. No note is printed.

  - verifier: ringposstyle.sthlp: '# 0<=#<=100, # real'. _dash_textbox_position (dash_render.py:54064) accepts only [0-9]{1,2}, so ring(100) also fails. The legend uses re.fullmatch(r'(\d+)') via _dash_legend_arg (:39598). Twoway: subtitle ring(7.5) is drawn above the title (S y=238 vs T y=405), and legend ring(4.5) is drawn outside the note, the same as ring(5). dash: the subtitle is tagged ring=6, and the below-panel order for ring(4.5) is Legend,Title (as with no ring) where ring(5) gives Title,Legend. No note is printed.
- **LT-10** Numeric marginstyle forms are not read for textboxes: margin(# # # #), l=#, t=#, l+# on title/subtitle/note/caption/text(); legend region(margin())/bmargin() reject l=# and collapse four sides to the largest — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md D11 fixed region margins only)
  - twoway: title("T", box margin(l=20)) widens the box on the left (2518..3290 against 2833..2976). text(..., box margin(l=10)) does the same. caption(..., bmargin(t=20)) moves the caption. legend(region(margin(l=20))) widens the legend's left side only.
  - dash: 'title(margin(l=20)) is not a recognised margin; using the scheme's default.' The same happens for margin(1 2 3 4), text margin(l=10), caption bmargin(t=20), legend region(margin(l=20)) and bmargin(t=20). region(margin(1 20 1 1)) pads all four sides by 77 px.
  - evidence: SchemeParser.margin_sides dash_render.py:10274-10292 knows named styles only. _dash_legend_margin_px dash_render.py:39150-39177 takes max(sides). Callers at :54233-54247 and :54709-54720. Python check: margin_sides('1 2 3 4') returns None. Probes p1 L_rm, L_bm4, p2 T_mfr, T_capfr, X_mfr, p3 R_tbm4, R_lrm4.
  - fix: Route textbox and legend margins through _dash_margin_sides, the full marginstyle grammar already used for regions (BUGS D11), and give Bokeh padding per side where the model allows it.
  - verifier: marginstyle.sthlp:41,54-57 documents '# # # #', l=#, l+#. SchemeParser.margin_sides (dash_render.py:10274-10292) looks up _MARGINSTYLES names only, and its callers at :54233-54247 (title/subtitle/note/caption) and :54709-54720 (text()) note anything else. _dash_legend_margin_px (:39150-39174) returns None for 'l=20' and takes max() of four numbers (20 units -> ~77px on every side). The probe log shows the 'is not a recognised margin' notes for L_rm, L_bm4, T_mfr, T_capfr, X_mfr and R_tbm4.
- **LT-11** text(..., alignment()) is noted and not drawn through the dash command, though the help and WP-32 say it is drawn — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (ROADMAP WP-32 claims 'text() alignment()' drawn)

The fixture holes cases use cols(2)/default only, so this is untracked.

  Not covered: Served mode (dash serve): every probe used the saved page. Saved/served parity for these sub-options was not exercised.; by() legends and titles: by(..., legend(at() pos() ring(0))), ilegends and the panel subtitle sub-options. These belong to the by() area and were only read in passing.; plegend() with colorvar() colordiscrete coloruseplegend: not probed.; Axis titles (xtitle/ytitle/ztitle textbox sub-options beyond size/color) and tick-label textboxes: axis area.; Schemes other than stcolor and s2color for the legend-title, box-fill and region defaults (LT-1 and LT-8 may differ under s1/sj/meta/user schemes).; Pixel-exact geometry (gaps, key sizes, box padding in px against twoway's units): only the presence and direction of each difference was compared, except where truth fixtures already measure it.; Filter-move behaviour of legends and keys: dashboard-only, out of scope here.; r1title() and l2title() inside legend() were not probed. The other six edge titles were refused by twoway, so these two very likely are too.; Probe files: /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/legend-titles/p1.do, p2.do, p3.do with logs p1.log, p2.log, p3.log, tw_*.svg and d_*.html.

### by-option (25)

- **BY-1** by()'s own note(), caption() and subtitle() with ring(0) are dropped silently — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: by(foreign, note("NNN", ring(0))) draws NNN at the array's bottom left (x 245, y 2968 of 3240), in place of the Graphs-by note. caption("CCC", ring(0) position(1)) draws at the top right (x 5267, y 212). subtitle("SSS", ring(0)) draws at the top centre.
  - dash: Saved and served alike: the text appears nowhere in the document and no note names it. For the note, 'Graphs by' is gone too. title(..., ring(0)) is drawn.
  - evidence: Probe s7/probe7.do (V1, V2, V4 SVGs). Python ring0_probe.py: no model holds NNN/CCC/SSS in either mode. Cause: _dash_by_chrome calls _dash_text_annotations with sides=("above",) and ("below",) (dash_render.py:55079, :55082). A ring(0) box's side is neither, so it is skipped at dash_render.py:54495 without being named.
  - fix: Draw a ring(0) by() note/caption/subtitle inside the panel array at its clock position, as twoway does (or at least name it). Add truth probes for this.
  - verifier: Code: _dash_textbox_position returns side 'center' at ring(0) or clock 0 (dash_render.py:54037-54080). _dash_by_chrome calls _dash_text_annotations only with sides=('above',) and ('below',) (55079, 55082), so the box is skipped at :54495 with no note. I re-ran ring0_probe in served mode and a saved-mode copy (by-verify/ring0_saved.py, r0n.py). For note("NNN", ring(0)) neither mode holds NNN or 'Graphs by', and the notes list is empty. caption(ring(0) pos(1)) and subtitle(ring(0)) are also missing. title(ring(0)) is drawn as a Label, and it is the only one of the four with a truth probe (bp36 in extract_by_text_truth.do). twoway SVGs s7/V1, V2, V4 draw NNN at (245, 2968) in place of 'Graphs by', CCC at (5267, 212) and SSS at the top centre. Untracked. The combination is rare, but I kept major because the user's text disappears without a note.
- **BY-2** scale() written outside by() is applied to the panels; twoway ignores it on a by() graph — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, scale(1.5) by(foreign) exports an SVG identical to by(foreign) (diff of the bodies is empty, marker r=14.34 in both). scale(0.5) by(foreign, iscale(1)) is also identical to by(foreign, iscale(1)). Only by(, scale()) scales the graph.
  - dash: Panel tick labels go from 8.5pt to 12.75pt and markers from 3.4 to 5.1, in saved and served modes. No note is given.
  - evidence: Probes s2/P5 vs P0 and s3/Q10 vs Q12. Python misc_probe.py and served_probe.py. _dash_scale_factor (dash_render.py:27487) reads graph_options scale with no by() check. No test pairs scale() with by().
  - fix: On a by() graph, ignore the outer scale() as twoway does, and apply by(, scale()) instead (see BY-11).
  - verifier: twoway: svg_P5 (scale(1.5) by(foreign)) is byte-identical to svg_P0 after the header, and Q10 (scale(0.5) by(foreign, iscale(1))) is identical to Q12. dash: _dash_scale_factor (27487) reads graph_options['scale'] with no by() check. _dash_apply_scale runs on every figure (64004; served 70701), and dash.ado's scale=mult row (3753) has no by() rule either. Re-running misc_probe gives panel tick labels 8.5pt -> 12.75pt and marker size 3.40 -> 5.10, with no note. The help (5686-5691) does not mention by(). No BUGS or GAPS3 entry. It is silent and changes every size on the graph.
- **BY-3** An at() hole for a legend or colour key that is not drawn is not reserved in the grid — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked

S3 (no colorvar, clegend(at(1))) makes no hole, and dash matches that. dash sets out['legend_at'] only inside `if drawn:` (55786-55874), and clegend_at only under words & {on,draw} (55990-56024). Re-running at_probe gives one_at1, two_off_at1 and cv_at1_noton at (0,0),(0,1), and cv_at1_on correct at (0,1),(1,0). Untracked.
- **BY-4** compact beside an explicit style() is drawn compact; twoway lets style() win — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: parse_settings uses compact only when style() is empty (bygraph_g.class:291-297). by(foreign, total style(default) compact) and by(foreign, total compact style(default)) both export the same SVG as by(foreign, total), not as by(foreign, total compact).
  - dash: _dash_by_axes_options sets compact, clearing edgelabel (dash_render.py:55242-55250). _dash_by_grid_options sets compact (:36610), so _dash_by_spacing draws zero spacing. The same applies to style(econ|stata7|rescale) compact.
  - evidence: Probe s6/U0-U3 (U2==U0, U3==U0, U2!=U1). Python compact_probe.py.
  - fix: Treat compact as style(compact) only when no style() is given.
  - verifier: bygraph_g.class:291-293 uses style(compact) only when style() is empty. twoway: U2 and U3 (style(default) beside compact) are byte-identical to U0 (no compact) and differ from U1. dash: _dash_by_axes_options sets compact on the bare word whatever style() is (55242-55250). The grid options do the same (36610). _dash_bystyle_route (36466-36520) removes style(econ|stata7|rescale) and leaves compact in place, so those combinations are drawn compact too. Untracked.
- **BY-5** aspectratio() with by(, shrink) is accepted; twoway refuses it — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, aspectratio(1) by(foreign, shrink): 'options aspect() and shrink may not be combined', r(198) (bygraph_g.class _check_aspect_shrink :559-566).
  - dash: Registers with rc 0 and draws.
  - evidence: Probe s1/probe1.log T14 (twoway rc=198; dash rc=0). There is no 'aspect() and shrink' string in dash.ado, dash_parse.mata or dash_render.py.
  - fix: Refuse shrink/overallshrink together with aspectratio() in twoway's words.
  - verifier: twoway _check_aspect_shrink (bygraph_g.class:559-566) refuses when shrink_plotregion is set, which both shrink and overallshrink set (:320-331), and graphs[1] has an aspect ratio. probe1.log T14 shows twoway's 'options aspect() and shrink may not be combined' r(198), and dash registering with rc 0. No 'aspect() and shrink' text exists in dash.ado, dash_parse.mata or dash_render.py, so the build would draw too. Untracked.
- **BY-6** by() with no variables before its comma is refused with dash's own code and words — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, by(, total): 'varlist required in by()', r(100).
  - dash: 'dash: by() must name at least one variable (got: , total)', r(198).
  - evidence: Probe s1 T17. dash.ado:4263-4267. An empty by() matches: both accept it and draw an unfaceted graph (probe s2 P11).
  - fix: Print 'varlist required in by()' and exit 100.
  - verifier: probe1.log T17: twoway prints 'varlist required in by()' r(100). dash prints 'dash: by() must name at least one variable (got: , total)' r(198) (dash.ado:4263-4267). by() with nothing in it (P11) and merged copies (P8-P10) match. No tracking entry and no test of the message.
- **BY-7** An unreadable by(, legend(position())) is placed at the scheme default with no note — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: (scatter)(lfit), by(foreign, legend(pos(abc))) prints '(note: named style abc not found in class clockdir, default attributes used)' and draws the legend above the panels (keys at y 297-454).
  - dash: No note. The legend is drawn at the right (stcolor's 3 o'clock).
  - evidence: Probe s1 T20 SVG. Python leg_probe.py: pos_abc gives side right. dash registration log for T20 and b2 has no note.
  - fix: Print twoway's clockdir note and use the clockdir default twoway falls back to (12 here).
  - verifier: twoway T20 prints '(note: named style abc not found in class clockdir, default attributes used)' and draws the legend keys at y 297 and 454, above the panels. dash: the registration log shows no note (the legend() clockdir note at dash_parse.mata:10426-10429 is not reached for the by() legend). _dash_by_clock_side falls back to the scheme default. Re-running leg_probe gives side 'right' for pos_abc and also for pos13. I lowered this to cosmetic: it only happens on a mistyped position().
- **BY-8** Notes for named-style errors in by() differ from twoway's; some are duplicated or spurious — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: style(foo): '(note: named style foo not found in class bygraphstyle, default attributes used)', drawn as default. scale(abc) and iscale(abc)/iscale(*abc): the named-style note, then '(note: named style 1 not found in class numstyle...)'. spacers(ring(2)) and subtitle(, width(20)) draw byte-identical to the default.
  - dash: style(foo) and scale(abc) give 'by() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: ...'. iscale(abc) prints its note twice and not the second one. spacers() and subtitle(, width()) are named as not implemented although twoway draws nothing different, which breaks the help's rule that only options twoway draws differently are named.
  - evidence: probe1.log T15, T18, T27; probe2.log P15/b3/b4 build notes; s1 T9==T0 and s2 P4==P0 SVGs.
  - fix: Echo twoway's named-style notes once each. Take spacers() and silent-no-op sub-options in silence.
  - verifier: probe1/probe2 logs: for iscale(abc) and iscale(*abc), dash prints the same named-style note twice, where twoway prints the abc note and then 'named style 1 not found in class numstyle'. style(foo) gets dash's 'by() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: style(foo)' in place of twoway's bygraphstyle note. scale(abc) is in the same 'not implemented' note (see BY-11). twoway T9 (spacers(ring(2))) and P4 (subtitle(, width(20))) are byte-identical to the default. dash names both (notes_probe, label_probe). The help's practice is to take in silence what twoway draws alike (dash.sthlp:6498, 6577, 6587), so these notes are noise only.
- **BY-9** subtitle() help text contradicts itself and the code about restyling the panel label box — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: n/a
  - dash: dash.sthlp:3290-3292 says subtitle()'s fcolor(), lcolor() and nobox 'do not restyle it in dash and are named in a note'. dash.sthlp:3339-3340 and the code (_DASH_BY_LABEL_BOX_WORDS dash_render.py:55020; _dash_by_panel_label :54939-54942) restyle the box. A probe of subtitle(, fcolor(red) lcolor(blue)) by(foreign) gives no note.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:3288-3292 vs 3339-3340. Python label_probe.py.
  - fix: Delete the stale sentence at 3290-3292 and pin it in a help-claims test.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:3290-3292 says subtitle()'s fcolor(), lcolor() and nobox 'do not restyle it in dash and are named in a note'. dash.sthlp:3339-3340 says they restyle every panel label's box. The code agrees with 3339: _DASH_BY_LABEL_BOX_WORDS (55020) and _dash_by_panel_label store title._dash_box_opts (54939-54942), and BUGS.md:3611's D10 resolution says the same. label_probe gives no note for subtitle(, fcolor(red) lcolor(blue)). The sentence at 3290-3292 is out of date.
- **BY-10** Panel-label sub-options twoway draws are named and ignored — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp:5382-5385 (named)
  - twoway: Measured on stcolor. subtitle(, justification(left)) by(foreign) left-aligns the labels (x 570 vs 1652). subtitle(, nobexpand) shrinks the label box to the text (width 484 vs 2197). subtitle(, lwidth(vthick) lcolor(black)) outlines the box (stroke 38.6). subtitle(, span) widens the box over the y-axis area (x 339..2863). subtitle(, alignment(top) height(15)) moves the text (y 242 vs 258).
  - dash: Each is named 'subtitle() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored', and the label is drawn centred with a full-width box. The same holds for linegap(), bmargin(), placement(), orientation(), width() and height(), and for lpattern() on the box outline.
  - evidence: Probes s1 T29, s2 P1-P4, P13 SVGs. Python label_probe.py. dash.sthlp:5382-5385 ('names the other placement and box sub-options').
  - fix: Draw justification(), nobexpand, the outline width/pattern, span and alignment on the panel label row.
  - verifier: _dash_by_panel_label passes only the box words plus position, ring, bexpand and nobexpand as extra (54886). label_probe names justification, alignment, linegap, bmargin, width, height, lwidth, lpattern, placement, orientation, nobexpand (outside ring(0)) and span. twoway draws them differently: T29 left-aligns at x 570 vs 1652; P1 nobexpand box 483.6 wide vs 2196.6; P13 alignment(top) puts the text at y 242 vs 258; P3 span widens the box. (Width and height alone draw no difference, P4==P0.) Documented as named at dash.sthlp:5382-5385, so this is a known difference.
- **BY-11** by(, scale(#)) is named and ignored; twoway scales the whole by() graph — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp:3363-3364 (generic 'other sub-options named')
  - twoway: by(foreign, scale(1.5)) draws every text 1.5x larger (tick labels 95.68->143.44, panel labels 105.13->157.78, Graphs-by note 89.94->135.0) and re-lays out the panels. by(foreign, scale(1.5) iscale(1)) behaves the same.
  - dash: 'dash: by() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: scale(1.5)'. Drawn at 1.
  - evidence: Probes s1 T1 vs T0 and s3 Q11 vs Q12. Python notes_probe.py. Falls under dash.sthlp:3363-3364 ('Any other sub-option ... is accepted, named in a warning, and ignored').
  - fix: Apply by(, scale()) as a multiplier on the whole by() graph, overall texts included.
  - verifier: twoway: T1 is not equal to T0. Tick labels go from 95.68 to 143.44, the panel label to 157.78 and the note to 135.0. Q11 (with iscale(1)) is not equal to Q12. dash: scale is a stage-1 row of _DASH_BY_GRAMMAR (36149) and is not in the handled branches of _dash_parse_by (36345-36404), so notes_probe gives 'not implemented and ignored: scale(1.5)'. Covered only by the generic sentence at dash.sthlp:3362-3364. Together with BY-2, dash takes scale() from the place twoway ignores and ignores it where twoway applies it.
- **BY-12** by(, l1title()/b1title()) and the other position titles inside by() are named and ignored — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp:3363-3364 (generic)
  - twoway: parse_make writes the outer axis titles as l1title()/b1title() before the user's options (bygraph_g.class:209-216), so by(foreign, l1title("LEFTX")) replaces the shared y title 'Mileage (mpg)' and b1title("BOTTOMX") replaces 'Weight (lbs.)'. t1title/t2title add rows above the panels, and r1title, l2title and b2title add rows on their sides; the panels re-lay out.
  - dash: 'by() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: l1title("LEFTX")' and likewise for the others. The variable-label titles are drawn.
  - evidence: Probes s1 T4-T7 SVGs. Python notes_probe.py. _DASH_BY_GRAMMAR rows r1title..b2title (dash_render.py:36177-36180) are parsed only. dash.sthlp:3363-3364.
  - fix: Draw the eight position titles on the by() graph, with l1title/b1title replacing the shared axis titles.
  - verifier: bygraph_g.class:203-216 puts l1title(<y title>) and b1title(<x title>) before the user's options, and parse_and_log_titles reads all eight edge titles (:224). twoway T4/T5: LEFTX replaces 'Mileage (mpg)' and BOTTOMX replaces 'Weight (lbs.)'. T6/T7 add rows. by_option.sthlp lists title_options as by()'s 'overall titles'. dash: _DASH_BY_TEXT_SPELLINGS covers only title, subtitle, note and caption (36216-36221), so notes_probe names l1title, b1title, t1title and r1title. BUGS.md:3611 (D10) suggested 'apply the stage-3 grammar rows', but its resolution did not cover these. Only the generic dash.sthlp:3362-3364 sentence remains. I lowered this to minor: the option is named in a note, not dropped silently, and the idiom is uncommon.
- **BY-13** by(, legloc(), clegloc(), plegend(), plegloc()) are named and ignored — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:3363-3364 (generic)
  - twoway: SetLegend/SetClegend/SetPlegend take `legend' `legloc', `clegend' `clegloc' and `plegend' `plegloc' (bygraph_g.class:142-144). by(foreign, legloc(pos(6))) draws exactly as by(foreign, legend(pos(6))) (SVGs identical), and clegend(on) clegloc(pos(6)) moves the colour key below the panels.
  - dash: Each is named 'not implemented and ignored'. The legend and colour key stay at the scheme position, or are not drawn for plegend().
  - evidence: Probes s2 P6==P6b and s1 T13 vs T13b SVGs. Python keys_probe.py, keys_probe2.py. dash.sthlp:3363-3364.
  - fix: Merge legloc()/clegloc()/plegloc() into the legend(), clegend() and plegend() placement, and implement plegend().
  - verifier: bygraph_g.class:142-144 passes `legend' `legloc', `plegend' `plegloc' and `clegend' `clegloc' to SetLegend, SetPlegend and SetClegend. twoway P6 (legloc(pos(6))) is byte-identical to P6b (legend(pos(6))). T13 (clegloc(pos(6))) moves the key: the y tick 20 is at 978 vs 1917 in T13b. dash: notes_probe names legloc, plegend, plegloc and clegloc as not implemented. My own probe (headroom colour key, by-verify/kp.py) confirms the key stays at the scheme side with clegloc(pos(6)). The auditor's keys_probe case used colorvar(price), which the test data lack, so its 'no key' output there is an artefact. Only the generic help sentence covers these.
- **BY-14** colorvar() with coloruseplegend under by(): plegend(on) draws nothing and clegend(on) draws a different key — minor, wrong-output, partly, effort M, tracked: untracked (plegend() falls under the dash.sthlp:3363 generic note)
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, colorvar(rep78) colordiscrete coloruseplegend by(foreign, plegend(on)) draws an overall plot legend at the right. With by(foreign, clegend(on)) instead it draws a colour-bar key labelled 1-5 with the title 'Repair record 1978'. Plain by(foreign) draws neither, and dash matches that case.
  - dash: plegend(on) is named and nothing is drawn. clegend(on) draws a discrete Legend '1 2 3 4 5 (missing)' with no key title.
  - evidence: Probes s1 T10-T12 SVGs. Python keys_probe.py (cvp, cvp_pleg_on, cvp_cleg_on).
  - fix: Implement by(, plegend()) for coloruseplegend keys and draw clegend(on)'s key as twoway does under coloruseplegend.
  - verifier: Confirmed: twoway T11 (coloruseplegend with by(, plegend(on))) draws a plot legend at the right with keys 3, 2, 1, Other. dash names plegend(on) and draws nothing (kp.py cvp_pleg). twoway T12 (clegend(on)) draws a colour-bar key labelled 1-5 with the upright title 'Repair record 1978'. dash draws a discrete Legend '1 2 3 4 5 (missing)'. Corrected: the dash key does carry the title 'Repair record 1978' (Legend.title set, kp.py), so 'with no key title' is wrong. What differs is the key's form and the extra (missing) entry. Plain by(foreign) draws no key in either.
- **BY-15** by(, bgcolor()) is named and ignored — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:3363-3364

The white graph-region rect drawn next covers the whole 5400x3240 canvas, so nothing visible changes under stcolor. dash names bgcolor(red) (notes_probe), and dash.sthlp:3363 uses it as its example. The real difference is a note twoway does not print, the same class as BY-8.

  - twoway: subtitle(, position(3|9)) on the panel label draws a narrow box beside the plot region. by(, title(..., position(3))) draws beside the array.

The general rule at 5372-5373 says beside-the-plot clocks (2-4, 8-10) are drawn above or below. _dash_by_panel_label uses _dash_textbox_place for non-inside clocks (54905-54921). Documented difference.
- **BY-20** aspectratio() under by() shapes each panel's figure, not its plot region — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp:5679-5684

- **BY-22** A by() graph draws at most 100 groups; larger cols()/rows() are clamped — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:3300-3304 (Resource limits)
  - twoway: Draws every group and any cols()/rows().
  - dash: Draws the first 100 groups with a note naming $dash_max_by_groups. cols()/rows() above the limit are drawn at it.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:3300-3304, 3262-3263; cscript BYG-19.
  - fix: None required: a resource limit with a documented raise.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:3300-3304 and 3262-3263 document the 100-group limit, its note naming $dash_max_by_groups, and the clamping of cols()/rows(). It is a deliberate resource limit that the user can raise, so no fix is needed, but it is still a difference by default.
- **BY-23** Panel-by-panel parity with twoway is certified for 5 plot types only — minor, unverified, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'Stata end-to-end gaps ... per-type by() parity' (closed, partial scope)
  - twoway: by() repeats each layer per group: fits, ci bands, smoothers, range, area, pc, dot, contour, tsline, immediate types and so on.
  - dash: test_wp31_truth.cscript U60-5 certifies by(foreign) glyphs and rows for histogram, kdensity, scatter, line and bar only. function is covered by C12. I checked scatteri myself (drawn in every panel in both).
  - evidence: BUGS.md:3917 resolution (U60-5).
  - fix: Extend the U60-5 sweep to every drawn plot type (by(foreign) and by(foreign, total)).
  - verifier: U60-5 (test_wp31_truth.cscript:14395-14411) checks histogram, kdensity, scatter, line and bar. It checks only that the drawn panel count equals levelsof foreign and that the saved and served glyphs match. It does not compare against twoway's drawing, so even those five are not certified against twoway's per-panel output. Coverage is narrower than the finding says. Other by() cscript cases (test_parser.cscript P1A-83/84 for lpoly, fpfit, lpolyci, mband and lowess) compare rc and refusals only. HC6-16 counts lpoly grid points per panel. The fixtures that use by() are mostly scatter with chrome and layout probes. The claim that by() draws each plot type's layer per panel as twoway does is unverified for nearly every type.
- **BY-24** Placement of by()'s title/note/caption under position() has no truth test — cosmetic, unverified, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: by(foreign, note("N", position(5))) puts N at the bottom right (x 5267). title("T", position(11)) puts T at the top left (x 245).
  - dash: Same sides (foot/right, head/left) by model inspection. Exact offsets are unmeasured: by_text_truth covers default, size and span placements only.
  - evidence: Probe s3 Q8, Q9. Python bytext_probe.py. No position() probe for by() texts in test/fixtures.
  - fix: Add position() probes to scripts/extract_by_text_truth.do and test_by_text.py.
  - verifier: Confirmed that by_text_truth (extract_by_text_truth.do:155-193, bp01-bp39) has no position() probe for by()'s note or title. Corrected: one position() placement is measured and pinned. test/test_legend_span_browser.py TestByTitlesSpan and TestByTitlesWithoutSpan check caption(..., pos(5)) ending at 4257.4, or at 5267.2 with span, from twoway measurements recorded 19oct2026, in both modes. note(position(5)), title(position(11)) and the other clocks remain unmeasured.
- **BY-25** by() panels' natscale ticks after a filter move (open natscale entry) — cosmetic, tracked-open, partly, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'
  - twoway: Stata's _natscale ticks.
  - dash: After a filter move dash's fitted model ticks the shared axes; it disagrees on 3 of 264 measured axes.
  - evidence: BUGS.md:4139.
  - fix: As the entry suggests.
  - verifier: BUGS.md:4139 is the only open entry ('The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'). The three misses are plain-axis ranges on auto (price #4, trunk #2 and #3), not by()-specific. The model draws ticks only after a filter move, or when Stata's ticks are not embedded. At build, dash draws Stata's own ticks for every axis and by() panel (S45 stata_ticks). It reaches by() panels only through the shared axes after a move, and that is dashboard-only behaviour. It is tracked open, and the entry itself grades it S3, so cosmetic for this claim.

  Not covered: Per-type by() parity for the plot types outside U60-5 (fits, ci types, lowess/lpoly, mband/mspline, area/range/pc/dot/dropline/spike, contour, tsline): no per-type probes run.; My new probes ran only under the default scheme (stcolor). Other schemes were not re-measured for BY-1..BY-14.; Findings on dash's side come from Bokeh model inspection in saved mode, and served mode for BY-1, BY-2 and BY-3. I did no browser or screenshot check of the new findings.; I did not run the Stata cscripts (test_by_suboptions.cscript, test_wp31_truth.cscript) myself; I relied on their source and the fixtures.; fxsize()/fysize() outside by(): twoway shrinks the whole by() graph (probes Q1, Q2) but I did not compare dash's per-cell semantics. clegend(span), clegend(ring()) and by() legend sizing under iscale() were not checked.; Value labels with double quotes, and the exact geometry of by()'s own texts under position() (BY-24), were not measured.; After-a-filter-move behaviour is excluded as dashboard-only, apart from the natscale entry.

### graph-wide (24)

- **GW-1** xsize()/ysize() with units (in, pt, cm) are ignored — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Takes a graphsize with a unit (region_options.sthlp:157-164). Under stcolor, xsize(10cm) draws a 3.937in x 4.5in graph, xsize(3in) 3x4.5, ysize(200pt) 7.5x2.778 (measured from graph export SVG). xsize(0.5in) and xsize(300cm) also run, rc 0.
  - dash: rc 0, but it prints '(note: dash: graph option xsize(10cm) is not a number; using None instead.)' and draws the scheme's 7.5x4.5 ratio (Bokeh aspect_ratio 1.667) for xsize(10cm), xsize(3in), ysize(200pt), xsize(0.5in), xsize(300cm) and xsize(400pt). The note also leaks Python's 'None'.
  - evidence: Probes p1/p2 (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/graphwide/p1.log, p2.log; t_xs_cm.svg width=3.937in height=4.500in). dash.ado:3753 xsize=style,1,100 passes a non-number to the renderer. dash_render.py:27453 (_dash_figure_aspect), :27384 (_dash_ref_in) and :27690 (_dash_apply_frame_aspect) all use float(), which fails on '10cm'. dash.sthlp:5651 says 'in inches' only. No BUGS/GAPS3 entry.
  - fix: Parse #in/#pt/#cm (1in=72pt=2.54cm) for xsize()/ysize() in dash.ado and in every renderer reader, both modes. First measure what twoway draws for out-of-range unit values (0.5in and 300cm both returned rc 0). Add a truth fixture row and a test.
  - verifier: I re-read region_options.sthlp:155-170, where graphsize is # followed by in, pt or cm. The exported SVGs measure 3.937in x 4.500in for xsize(10cm), 3x4.5 for xsize(3in) and 7.5x2.778 for ysize(200pt). In dash.ado:3753 xsize=style,1,100 lets '10cm' through as a name. The renderer's readers (_stat_option_float :27742, _dash_ref_in :27374, _dash_figure_aspect :27437) use bare float(). I re-ran the renderer: xsize(10cm) and ysize(200pt) give aspect_ratio 1.667 with 'is not a number; using None instead.', while xsize(3.937) gives 0.875. The help (dash.sthlp:5652) says only 'in inches'. BUGS, GAPS3 and the tests have no entry or test for a unit. Untracked.
- **GW-2** aspectratio() combined with xoverhangs or yoverhangs is accepted — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Refuses with 'may not combine option aspectratio() with xoverhangs or yoverhangs', r(198). This holds after the comma, in paren form, across || segments and under by().
  - dash: Accepts every form, rc 0, and writes the page.
  - evidence: /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/t/twowaygraph_g.class:155-161. Probes p2 (tw_ar_xo, tw_ar_yo, tw_byar) and p3 (tw_parxo, tw_segxo, d_reg): twoway rc 198, dash rc 0. grep finds the message nowhere in dash.ado or dash_parse.mata.
  - fix: In _dash_validate_opts, after all copies are merged, refuse aspectratio() together with xoverhangs or yoverhangs in twoway's words, r(198). Do the same on the registered and dash build routes. Add a cscript test against twoway's answer.
  - verifier: twowaygraph_g.class:155-161 refuses aspectratio() with xoverhangs/yoverhangs, r(198). 'may not combine option aspectratio() with xoverhangs' appears nowhere in dash.ado, dash_parse.mata or dash_render.py. The p2 and p3 logs show twoway at rc 198 and dash at rc 0 on every route: after the comma, paren form, || segment, by(), and registered then dash build. Untracked.
- **GW-3** maxaxes() is never checked — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (Unsupported 'Graph-wide settings' lists the name only)
  - twoway: MAXAXES(integer 9). maxaxes(x) and maxaxes(2.5) are refused with 'option maxaxes() not allowed', r(198). maxaxes(1) on a graph with a yaxis(2) layer gives 'yaxis() invalid -- invalid numlist has elements outside of allowed range', r(125).
  - dash: All three run, rc 0, with '(note: dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): maxaxes())'. The second axis is drawn.
  - evidence: twowaygraph_g.class:136. Probes p2 (mx_x, mx_f) and p4 (mx1y2). dash_parse.mata:7714 and :8090 list maxaxes as a catch-all name only. dash.sthlp:6596-6600 lists it under graph-wide settings with nothing to draw.
  - fix: Validate maxaxes() as a non-negative integer with twoway's refusal. Refuse yaxis()/xaxis() numbers above maxaxes() with twoway's r(125) line. Keep the note for a valid value only where nothing differs.
  - verifier: twowaygraph_g.class:136 declares MAXAXES(integer 9). The logs show maxaxes(x) and maxaxes(2.5) refused by twoway with r(198), and maxaxes(1) with a yaxis(2) layer refused with r(125). dash takes all three with the catch-all note. In dash, maxaxes appears only in the catch-all tables at dash_parse.mata:7714 and :8090. The help (dash.sthlp:6560-6564) lists it as a setting with nothing to draw, so the name is listed but the missing validation is untracked.
- **GW-4** fxsiz() and fysiz() abbreviations are noted, not drawn — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: graph.ado:161 declares FXSIZe(passthru) FYSIZe(passthru), so fxsiz(50) and fysiz(50) are fxsize()/fysize() (rc 0). fxsi() and fxs() are refused.
  - dash: fxsiz(50) and fysiz(50) reach the catch-all: '(note: dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): fxsiz())'. The size is not applied. fxsi() and fxs() are refused, which matches.
  - evidence: dash.ado:980-981 and :1308-1309 declare FXSIZE(string) FYSIZE(string), which allows no abbreviation. Probes p2 (fx_ab) and p3 (fysiz, fxsi, fxs). unknown_option_truth.json probes only the full spelling.
  - fix: Declare FXSIZe()/FYSIZe() on both parsers and in every list that names them (the unique-option list at dash.ado:8542, _dash_rep tables). Add the spellings to abbrev truth.
  - verifier: graph.ado:161 declares FXSIZe(passthru) FYSIZe(passthru), so twoway takes fxsiz(50) and fysiz(50) (rc 0 in the p2 and p3 logs). dash.ado:980 and :1309 declare FXSIZE(string) FYSIZE(string), which allows no abbreviation, so dash notes the option and does not apply it. fxsi() and fxs() are refused by both, which matches. On a lone graph twoway draws the plot in # percent of 4in, so the drawing differs too. Untracked.
- **GW-5** xoverhangs/yoverhangs move borders where twoway does not — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (BUGS D5 closed)
  - twoway: 'xoverhangs and yoverhangs are ignored if by() is specified' (advanced_options.sthlp). Only the labels of the smallest and largest ticks count. On the measured yoverhangs probe nothing moved (sizing_truth overhang_y0 == overhang_y1). xoverhangs moved the left edge by 138 units and the right by 80, asymmetric.
  - dash: Under by(foreign) xoverhangs, each panel's min_border_left/right goes from None to 24 px. On the yoverhangs probe, top and bottom borders go from None to 14 px. x uses the widest of all label overrides, not just the end labels, and raises both sides equally.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:27704-27738 (_dash_overhangs), called for every panel from _make_figure_body:64005 with no by() check. Renderer probe rp.py output. Truth: test/fixtures/sizing_truth.json overhang_y0/y1. test_sizing.py:316-325 checks only that x widens.
  - fix: Skip both options when by() is present. Measure only the end labels, per side. For yoverhangs, compare against overhang_y0/y1 and extend the probe to a case where twoway does move.
  - verifier: advanced_options.sthlp:151-156 says only the end labels count and the options are 'ignored if by() is specified'. My probe v1: under by(foreign) with a long first x label, twoway's SVG is byte-identical with and without xoverhangs. _dash_overhangs (dash_render.py:27704) has no by() check and is called on every panel (:64005). It takes the widest of all label overrides and raises both sides equally. Re-run of the renderer: by()+xoverhangs moves each panel's min_border_left/right from None to 24. yoverhangs moves top/bottom to 14, where truth overhang_y0 == overhang_y1. In truth overhang_x0/x1 the x shift is asymmetric (left +138.6, right -80.4 units). test_sizing.py:316-326 checks only that both borders widen. Untracked. A few pixels per side, but it narrows every by() panel.
- **GW-6** Graph-region outline not stroked unless an outline sub-option is given — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (documented as behaviour at dash.sthlp:4265)
  - twoway: Always strokes the graph region's outline in the scheme's colour: s2color #eaf2f3 at 5.76 units, stcolor and s1color #ffffff. With graphregion(fc(red)) that outline shows against the fill.
  - dash: Draws no graph-region outline unless color(), lcolor(), lwidth(), lpattern(), style() or lstyle() is given. The help states this as dash's rule, not as a difference.
  - evidence: region_truth.json s2color|D22, stcolor|D22, s1color|D22 graph_line. test/test_region_truth.py:194 skips the check: 'dash draws no graph outline unasked'. dash.sthlp:4265-4266.
  - fix: Stroke the border from the scheme's graphregion line style whenever the fill differs from it, and drop the test skip. Or label it a difference in the help.
  - verifier: region_truth.json probes s2color|D22, stcolor|D22 and s1color|D22 (graphregion(fc(red))) stroke the graph outline in #eaf2f3 or #ffffff, at 5.76 units (6.48 under stcolor). test_region_truth.py:194 skips this case with 'dash draws no graph outline unasked'. dash.sthlp:4265-4266 states dash's rule but does not mark it as a difference from twoway. The stroke is a hairline (about 0.4-0.8 px at 96 dpi, partly at the canvas edge) and shows only when the fill differs from the outline colour, so cosmetic is right.
- **GW-7** xsize()/ysize() refusal and note are not in twoway's words — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: xsize(0.5) and xsize(150): 'graph xsize invalid; / minimum xsize is 1 inch / maximum xsize is 100 inches', r(198). xsize(abc): '(note:  named style abc not found in class graphsize, default attributes used)', then a second note for 5, rc 0.
  - dash: xsize(0.5): 'dash: xsize() must be a name or a number between 1 and 100 (got: 0.5)', r(198). xsize(abc): '(note:  dash: graph option xsize(abc) is not a number; using None instead.)'.
  - evidence: Probe p1 (tw_xs_05, tw_xs_150, tw_xs_bad and the d_ versions). dash.ado:4049-4092 (kind style). dash_render.py:27453 via _stat_option_float:27768-27773.
  - fix: Print twoway's three lines for an out-of-range number. Print twoway's graphsize note for a word.
  - verifier: The p1 log shows twoway's three-line 'graph xsize invalid; / minimum xsize is 1 inch / maximum xsize is 100 inches' r(198). dash prints one 'dash: xsize() must be a name or a number between 1 and 100 (got: 0.5)', same rc (dash.ado:4073-4078). For xsize(abc), twoway prints two graphsize notes and dash prints one Python-worded note that ends 'using None instead.'. The help (dash.sthlp:1153-1193, the bin() example) shows dash's own bounds-message pattern, so the style is partly documented, but not as a difference for xsize/ysize.
- **GW-8** Unknown or repeated option refusals add a second error line — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Prints one line, e.g. 'option nocopies not allowed' or 'option scale() not allowed', r(198).
  - dash: Prints the same first line, then a second error line: '      twoway does not take this option here.', '      twoway takes this option once; this command gives it more than once.' or '      After the plot groups' comma twoway takes graph options only; ...'. Seen for nocopies, iscale(), imargin(), altshrink, xoptions(), yoptions(), pcy(), yvar(), scal(), fxsi(), ttex(), nodra, titel(), scale() and aspectratio() given twice, and yvarlabel()/yvarformat() after the comma.
  - evidence: dash.ado:11022 (_dash_refuse_repeat) and :11233-11240 (_dash_refuse_unknown). Probes p1-p4. The help (dash.sthlp:978-981, 1023-1028) promises the refusal 'as twoway refuses it'. The cscript tests compare only the first line.
  - fix: Drop the extra lines, or document them as a deliberate difference.
  - verifier: dash.ado:11022 (_dash_refuse_repeat), :11106, :11238 and :11240 (_dash_refuse_unknown) print a second error line after twoway's line. The p1-p4 logs show it for nocopies, iscale(), imargin(), altshrink, pcy(), yvar(), xoverhan, scal(), ttex(), nodra, xoptions(), yoptions(), fxsi(), fxs(), titel(), repeated scale()/aspectratio(), and yvarlabel()/yvarformat() after the comma. A grep of dash.sthlp, BUGS/GAPS3/ROADMAP/CHANGELOG and the tests finds no mention of these lines. The help promises the refusal 'as twoway refuses it'. Return codes match. Untracked.
- **GW-9** name() with a bad sub-option gives a garbled invalid-name message — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: name(t2, rep): 'option rep not allowed'. name(t2, REPLACE): 'option REPLACE not allowed'. name(t3, bogus): 'option bogus not allowed'. All r(198).
  - dash: r(198) with "dash: invalid graph name 't2??rep'. Names must start with a letter ...". The comma and blank are printed as '??'.
  - evidence: Probe p4 (nmrep2, nmrep3, nmbog). dash.ado:11273-11303: _dash_name_split recognises only 'replace', and the rest falls through to the invalid-name message with non-name characters masked to '?'.
  - fix: After the comma, read name()'s sub-options as twoway does and refuse an unknown word in its words.
  - verifier: dash.ado:11273-11303: _dash_name_split recognises only 'replace'. Any other word after the comma falls to the invalid-name message, with ', ' masked to '??'. The p4 log shows name(t2, rep), name(t2, REPLACE) and name(t3, bogus): twoway 'option rep not allowed' and the like, dash 'invalid graph name 't2??rep'...', both r(198). Related: name(1abc) is '1abc invalid name' in twoway and dash's long message, same rc. Untracked.
- **GW-10** The default font face from graph set is not read — cosmetic, unverified, confirmed, effort M, tracked: partly: BUGS 'set dp comma is never read ... graph set font faces are not read' (closed, tags only)
  - twoway: graph set window fontface sets the face of all unmarked text in the Graph window. graph set svg fontface does the same for SVG export (graph_set.sthlp).
  - dash: Reads only fontfacesans/fontfaceserif/fontfacemono from 'translator query gph2svg', and applies them only to {stSans}/{stSerif}/{stMono} text. Plain text keeps Bokeh's default face. The window settings are never read.
  - evidence: dash.ado:7365-7387 (_dash_graph_set_fonts). dash_render.py:48452-48469 (_dash_gt_fonts_of) and :17581-17583. dash.sthlp:5187-5188. data_locale_truth.json fonts.svg measured only tagged text ('{stSans:a}'), not plain text.
  - fix: Measure plain-text faces under graph set svg/window fontface. That needs a session allowed to change and restore graph set, which this audit was not. Then read the default face (window face for interactive, svg for batch) and apply it to every unmarked text.
  - verifier: _dash_graph_set_fonts (dash.ado:7364-7387) reads only fontfacesans/serif/mono from translator query gph2svg. _dash_gt_fonts_of (dash_render.py:48452) applies them only to {stSans}/{stSerif}/{stMono}. No default face is set for unmarked text, and fontfacesymbol and the window settings are never read. svg_options.sthlp:217-221 says fontface is the font 'for which no other font has been specified', default Helvetica. Bokeh's default is helvetica, so dash matches twoway unless a user has changed graph set fontface, which is a persistent preference this audit may not set. Downgraded to cosmetic: only text faces differ, and only for users with a non-default persistent setting. Plain-text faces have no measurement.
- **GW-11** scale() layout geometry is unverified; help says gaps are not scaled, twoway scales them — minor, unverified, partly, effort M, tracked: BUGS D5 / E11 (closed)
  - twoway: The space for a title and for axis labels/titles scales exactly with #. The plot region's top is 100.86 + 174.49*k and its left 100.8 + 289.94*k (base, scale_15, scale_05). Only the region margins stay fixed, so the gaps around text boxes scale too.
  - dash: Help says 'the gaps between boxes are not scaled'. The code multiplies fonts, standoffs, paddings, ticks and legend spacing. No test compares the plot region's position under scale() with the truth.
  - evidence: test/fixtures/sizing_truth.json base/scale_15/scale_05 'plot'. dash.sthlp:5689. dash_render.py:27529-27610 (_dash_apply_scale). test_sizing.py TestScale checks sizes only.
  - fix: Compare the frame insets under scale(0.5) and scale(1.5) with the truth. Scale whatever gap is left, and correct the help sentence.
  - verifier: Truth is linear in k, confirming twoway: plot top = 100.86 + 174.49k and left = 100.8 + 289.94k (base, scale_05, scale_15), so twoway scales the title gap and label gaps and keeps only the region margins. But _dash_apply_scale (dash_render.py:27529-27610) already scales axis_label_standoff, major_label_standoff, tick lengths, Title/Label standoff, padding and offset, and the legend's spacing, padding and margin. Those are the gaps twoway scales, so 'dash leaves gaps unscaled' is mostly refuted by its own code. What remains: the help (dash.sthlp:5687-5690) and the docstring wrongly say 'the gaps between boxes are not scaled', and no test compares the frame position under scale() with truth (TestScale, test_sizing.py:169-200, checks sizes only). The geometry stays unverified.
- **GW-12** A dashboard is not a Stata graph: graph export/save/display/combine and graph memory do not see it — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: Differences from twoway item 2 and Exporting (deliberate)
  - twoway: twoway leaves a graph in memory under name() (or Graph). graph export, graph save, graph display, graph combine, graph dir and putdocx all work on it.
  - dash: Creates no Stata graph. graph export after dash writes an earlier graph, or fails with r(601) if there is none. dash prints a note when graphs are in memory. A do-file cannot write an image of a dashboard.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:172-176, 816-823, 1632-1645. dash.ado:244. Probe p1: every dash command printed '(note: dash: graph export writes the graph in Stata's Graph window, not this dashboard; graphs in memory: ...)'.
  - fix: To make the claim hold for do-files that export: optionally run the same twoway command with nodraw under the given name, so graph memory and graph export behave as they would after twoway. Otherwise state the limit next to the drop-in claim.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:178-182 and 1633-1645 ('A dashboard is not a Stata graph'; graph export writes an earlier graph or fails with r(601)). The note is at dash.ado:244, and the p1 log shows it printed. A do-file that runs twoway then graph export/graph save/graph combine does not work unchanged with dash. It is a deliberate, documented consequence of the output medium. It is major for the drop-in claim because graph export after twoway is so common in do-files.
- **GW-13** name() is stricter than twoway, with a different rc for an existing name — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: Differences from twoway 2 ('What a name may be', 'Graphs and KPI cards share one namespace')
  - twoway: Takes a 28-character name and name(a__b) (rc 0). A second name(gx) without replace is 'graph gx already exists', r(110).
  - dash: Refuses names over 22 characters and any name holding '__', r(198). An existing name is 'dash: graph name 'gx' already exists. Use name(gx, replace) to overwrite.', r(198).
  - evidence: Probe p1 (name28, name2u, nameA/B). dash.sthlp:861-893. dash.ado:11294-11303, :11360-11364.
  - fix: Return r(110) with twoway's line for an existing name. Lift the 22-character limit by storing specs under a hashed or keyed global.
  - verifier: The p1 log shows twoway taking a 28-character name and a__b (rc 0), and giving 'graph gx already exists' r(110) on reuse. dash refuses names over 22 characters and a__b with r(198), and gives r(198) for an existing name (dash.ado:11294-11303, :11360-11364). dash.sthlp:861-883 documents the name rules. The r(110) vs r(198) difference is mentioned only in passing (the One name() paragraph, dash.sthlp:854-859). Deliberate, mostly documented.
- **GW-14** saving() writes HTML and refuses an existing file with a different rc and message — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: Differences from twoway 2 ('saving() keeps a file that is already there')
  - twoway: saving(sv1) writes sv1.gph ('file sv1.gph saved'). If it exists: 'file sv1.gph already exists', r(602). saving(x, replace) on a new file prints '(file x1.gph not found)'.
  - dash: Writes dsv1.html. saving(x.gph) becomes x.html with a note. An existing file is "dash: file 'dsv1.html' already exists. Use replace option to overwrite.", r(198).
  - evidence: Probe p1 (sav1/sav2/savgph) and p3 (savin). dash.sthlp:825-846. dash.ado:878, :11458-11508.
  - fix: Use r(602) and twoway's line for an existing file. The HTML format is inherent.
  - verifier: The p1 log shows twoway 'file sv1.gph already exists' r(602) and dash's own wording with r(198). dash.sthlp:825-830 documents this as a difference ('twoway says r(602)'), along with the .gph-to-.html note. Deliberate and documented. The rc can be aligned cheaply. A smaller difference: twoway prints '(file x1.gph not found)' for saving(x1, replace) on a new file, and dash does not.
- **GW-15** nodraw with saving(, asis) writes a page where twoway refuses — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: Differences from twoway 2 ('nodraw still writes the page')
  - twoway: 'no current graph', r(601).
  - dash: Writes the page and opens no browser.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:848-852.
  - fix: Refuse it as twoway does, or keep it documented.
  - verifier: I measured it (probe v1): twoway scatter ..., saving(v_asis, asis) nodraw gives 'no current graph', r(601). dash writes the page, as documented at dash.sthlp:848-852 and pinned by test_nodraw_honoured.cscript ND-06. dash is more permissive here, so no twoway do-file breaks. Deliberate and documented.
- **GW-16** play() refuses a valid Graph Recorder file — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort XL, tracked: Unsupported section, play() paragraph
  - twoway: Applies the recording's Graph Editor edits to the graph.
  - dash: Refuses any readable recording, r(198). A missing recording gives the same r(601) line as twoway (probe p1 play0 matched).
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6669-6680.
  - fix: Interpreting .grec edits on a Bokeh figure would be needed. Likely out of scope; keep documented.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6669-6680 documents that a readable .grec is refused, r(198). The p1 log shows a missing recording matching twoway's r(601) line. Deliberate. play() is rare, and applying the recording's edits to a Bokeh figure is out of proportion (XL).
- **GW-17** An unknown scheme is refused instead of drawn as s2color with a note — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: Option values / Stata scheme support ('That is stricter than twoway')
  - twoway: scheme(nosuchscheme): '(note: scheme nosuchscheme not found, using s2color)', rc 0.
  - dash: r(198), listing the shipped schemes.
  - evidence: Probe p1 (sch_bad). dash.sthlp:1261-1264, 6311-6330.
  - fix: Print twoway's note and draw s2color, or keep it documented.
  - verifier: The p1 log shows twoway printing '(note: scheme nosuchscheme not found, using s2color)' with rc 0, and dash refusing with r(198). dash.sthlp:1260-1264 and 6305-6329 document this as stricter than twoway. Deliberate. A do-file that names a scheme missing on another machine stops under dash but draws under twoway.
- **GW-18** Scheme-file reading differs from twoway — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: Stata scheme support (documented)
  - twoway: Follows a relative path in scheme(). Reads scheme files with no size or link limit. Draws every directive, including clockdir p (marker-label position), the textboxstyle of title/subtitle/caption/axis_title/tick/key_label, yesno plabelboxed, and color-*.style/textboxstyle-*.style files on the ado-path.
  - dash: A scheme must be a name, not a path. Files over 1 MB or linked out of their directory are skipped and s2color is drawn. dash-NAME is read only from the working directory. The listed directives are parsed and kept without a note but not drawn. color and textboxstyle style files are not read, so mcolor(mine) draws black and tstyle(mine) the default.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6141-6152, 6293-6303, 6368-6372. test/test_unread_scheme_directives.py:33-46 (W33S/W33Q lists).
  - fix: Draw clockdir p#, the textboxstyle keys and plabelboxed from the scheme. Read color-*.style and textboxstyle-*.style the way the other style classes are read.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6293-6300 covers path, size, link and dash-NAME. dash.sthlp:6141-6152 says color-*.style and textboxstyle-*.style are not read. dash.sthlp:6368-6372 says clockdir p and title textboxstyle are kept but not drawn. test_unread_scheme_directives.py:33-41 (W33S/W33Q) holds the measured directives twoway draws and dash does not. Documented; still a drawing difference for user schemes.
- **GW-19** Region inner box, alignment and plot-margin basis differ — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: Region Styling Options ('difference from twoway')
  - twoway: graphregion() has an inner box: istyle, ilstyle, ilcolor, ilwidth, ilpattern, ilalign and ifcolor draw it, and lalign aligns the outline. On plotregion(), lalign, ilalign, iintensity and ishadestyle are drawn. plotregion(margin()) is a share of the plot region's own size.
  - dash: Those graphregion and plotregion sub-options are named in a note, with their values. graphregion(ifcolor(red)) fills the whole border. The plot margin is a share of the whole graph, fixed at build time.
  - evidence: Probe p1 (grreg, plreg notes). dash.sthlp:4299-4311, 4331-4335.
  - fix: Draw graphregion's inner box as a second rectangle inside the margin. Compute the plot margin on the frame.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:4298-4309 marks the graphregion inner-box and lalign options and the plotregion lalign/ilalign/iintensity/ishadestyle as a 'difference from twoway', noted and not drawn, and says graphregion(ifcolor(red)) fills the whole border. dash.sthlp:4331-4335 marks the plot margin as a share of the whole graph. The p1 log shows the notes. Documented.
- **GW-20** Figure proportions and sizes do not always follow twoway — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: Figure proportions (documented)
  - twoway: aspectratio(#) always shapes the plot region. fxsize()/fysize() on a graph drawn alone keep the canvas and draw the plot in # percent of 4 inches, centred. Relative text sizes are a share of the graph's smaller side, and any xsize/ysize ratio is drawn.
  - dash: Without xsize()/ysize(), aspectratio() shapes the whole figure. fxsize()/fysize() shrink the figure's cell. Relative text is a fixed 2.88 pt per unit at any figure size. Extreme ratios are bent to fit a height band.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5680-5684, 5694-5699, 5705-5708, 5721-5723. sizing_truth.json fxsize_50/fysize_50/ar_1.
  - fix: Inherent to responsive pages for the most part. Without xsize, aspectratio() could still shape the frame (_dash_frame_sizes) with the figure stretched.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:5680-5684: without xsize/ysize, aspectratio() shapes the whole figure. 5694-5699: fxsize/fysize shrink the cell where twoway centres the plot in # percent of 4in. 5704-5708: relative text is a fixed 2.88pt whatever the size. 5721-5723: a height band. All documented. aspectratio() without xsize() is common, so the initial drawing differs from twoway's square plot region in ordinary use. Partly inherent to responsive pages.
- **GW-21** Graph-level text(): several textbox sub-options and =exp globals are not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: Added text (documented)
  - twoway: Draws width(), height(), linegap(), bexpand, bmargin(), lpattern(), lstyle() and lalign() on an added text box. A global holding `=exp' is evaluated.
  - dash: '(note: dash: text() draws no width() height() bexpand() linegap() bmargin() lpattern() lstyle() lalign(); see help dash##added_text)'. A `=exp' global is drawn as written.
  - evidence: Probe p1 (d_text). dash.sthlp:773-783.
  - fix: Draw a multi-line text box with linegap/width/height/bmargin/bexpand and the border line style.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:773-782 says the other textbox sub-options (width, height, linegap, angle ...) are noted and not drawn, and that a global holding `=exp' is drawn as written. The p1 log shows the note listing width() height() bexpand() linegap() bmargin() lpattern() lstyle() lalign(), where twoway took the same command with rc 0. Documented.

  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6249-6251 vs 5708-5713. dash_render.py:27374.

The Units paragraph is stale (BUGS P1.12a's residual Q1 was later closed by E11).

  Not covered: Graph window rendering: batch Stata draws no window, so twoway's on-screen output (and graph set window fontface) could not be measured; the rule against changing graph set or persistent preferences also ruled out probing fontface; play() with a valid .grec recording was not probed (documented refusal); set scheme ... permanently and user schemes found on PLUS/PERSONAL were not probed (persistent state); scale() plot-region geometry in dash was not measured against the truth (GW-11 rests on fixture arithmetic and code reading); GW-5 overhangs: measured in the saved renderer only (rp.py); the served app runs the same function (dash_render.py:70702) but was not exercised; A directive-by-directive audit of all 26 schemes against Stata's own scheme files; relied on the help's limits section and test_unread_scheme_directives.py; Region options under by(), and by()'s own regions (left to the by() area); recast() and the other plot-type advanced options (plot-type area); dash.sthlp:855-858 says graph is a prefix when twoway follows it, while :157-162 says dash graph ... is refused; left to the command-syntax auditor; The open natscale BUGS entry (ticks after a filter move) is an axis matter, not this area; Probe files: /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/graphwide/p1.do-p4.do with their .log files, and rp.py (renderer probe)

### syntax-data (21)

- **SD-1** A bare plot written after a parenthesised group is refused, where twoway draws it — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway (scatter mpg weight) line mpg weight → rc 0, draws 2 views (scatter, line). Same for (a) line .. || scatter .., (a) scatter price weight, (a) (b) scatter .., and a || (b) scatter ... (a) junk (b) → 'junk is not a twoway plot type' r(198). (a) line mpg weight (line price weight) → 'variable line not found' r(111).
  - dash: Every one is refused r(198): 'dash: unexpected text between plot groups: 'line'' plus a second line 'Each plot must be its own parenthesised group.' The (a) line .. (line ..) case gives rc 198 where twoway gives 111.
  - evidence: dash_parse.mata:3622-3640. The comment at 3627-3630 says 'A bare plot after a group needs || before it, as in twoway', which the measurement contradicts. Probes S04, S04b, S04d, Y05-Y08 and G01-G04 (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/syntax-data/p2out.log, p11out.log, p3out.log). test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-37 pins only the message.
  - fix: Make _dash_split_segs follow twoway's _parse expand: text after a group, up to the next || or the end, is one more plot command. Words that are not a plot type should get twoway's '<word> is not a twoway plot type'.
  - verifier: I re-read dash_parse.mata:3622-3640. Any non-blank top-level text that is not if/in is refused r(198) 'unexpected text between plot groups', and the comment at 3627-3630 claims twoway needs || there. Measured logs (p2out.log S04/S04b/S04d, p11out.log Y05-Y08) show twoway drawing `(scatter mpg weight) line mpg weight` with 2 views (scatter, line), rc 0, while dash gives 198. `(a) line .. (line ..)` is 111 in twoway and 198 in dash. For junk and 123, twoway says '<word> is not a twoway plot type' and dash uses its own words. Only open BUGS entry is natscale; nothing in BUGS/GAPS3/help documents this. test_parser_integrity.cscript:660-673 (P1B-37) pins dash's own message, which differs from twoway's. Untracked.
- **SD-2** The empty graph accepts axis options twoway refuses, and is drawn with axes that twoway does not draw — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'dash twoway with nothing after it...' closed A1 covers only the rc)
  - twoway: twoway, title(T) draws only the title over a blank plot region: e1.svg has 0 <line> elements and one text, 'T'. Axis options on a graph with no plot are refused r(198) '<opt> not allowed, xaxis1 does not exist'. Measured for xlabel, ylabel, xtitle, ytitle, xscale, yscale, xline, yline, xtick, xmlabel, ylabel(,angle(0)), xlabel(#3), ytitle(""), yscale(off), xscale(alt) and tlabel. by(foreign) gives 'xlabels(#4) not allowed'; the same holds with if or a || piece.
  - dash: All 17 forms register with rc 0. The figure is drawn with left and bottom axes; test_bare_dash.py asserts len(f.left)>=1 and len(f.below)>=1. The help says this is the graph twoway draws.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:164-166 ('draws the empty graph twoway draws ... register a graph with axes and no plot'). test/test_bare_dash.py:73-80. bare_prefix_truth.json records only rc and plot1_exists. Probes E10, E20-E51 (p3out.log); e1.svg vs e2.svg.
  - fix: Draw no axes on a graph with no plot. Refuse axis, scale and title options there with twoway's words. twoway names the plural form in the message (xlabels(1 2), xlines(1), xticks(1 2)). Add a truth extractor that measures the empty graph's SVG.
  - verifier: twoway, title(T) SVG (e1.svg) has 0 <line> elements and only the text 'T'; the empty-sample graph (e2.svg) does draw axes. p3out.log E20-E28, E34, E36, E40-E43: twoway refuses xlabel/ylabel/xtitle/ytitle/xscale/yscale/xline/yline/xtick/xmlabel/ttitle and by(foreign) on a plot-less graph, r(198) '<plural>(..) not allowed, xaxis1 does not exist'. dash gives rc 0 for all of them. dash.sthlp:164-166 says 'draws the empty graph twoway draws ... with axes', and test_bare_dash.py:79 asserts axes. GAPS3 A1's fix line said 'axes only', which was never measured, and bare_prefix_truth.json records only rc/plot1_exists/title. Also seen: plotregion(color(red)) is r(4018) in twoway and rc 0 in dash, an edge case not worth copying. Untracked.
- **SD-3** Graph-level p1() to p20() are named in the note and not drawn — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twowaygraph_g.class declares P1(string asis) to P20() and gives each to the #th plot. p1(mcolor(red)) drew 150 red marks (p1.svg vs p0.svg, which has 0). p2(msymbol(none)) cut the circles from 300 to 150. p21() is r(103).

So only p1(), p2() and p20() reach the note. My probe v1 (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/syntax-data-verify/v1out.log): p3(), p10() and p19() are rc 0 in twoway, but dash REFUSES them, r(198) 'option p3() not allowed' plus the after-comma hint. The fix must cover p1-p20 and the generator.

With colorvar, ztick()/zmlabel()/zmtick() are noted (Z21-Z23), and z2.svg shows twoway drawing '6,000' for zmlabel(6000) and z3.svg extra tick lines (284 vs 279). z1 and z1b (zlabel vs zlabels) are identical. dash.sthlp does not mention any of them. Untracked.
- **SD-5** bwi() is refused on lowess, kdensity, lpoly and lpolyci — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (abbrev_truth unresolved)

The repo's own abbrev_truth.json unprobeable.bwidth already records twoway rc 0 for b, bw, bwi, bwid, bwidt, but dash_abbrev_unresolved lists only bw/bwid, so the refusal of bwi is not tracked. bwid() works in dash (probe v1).

  - fix: Alias each to mlabel(), msymbol() and msangle(). This is a declaration-and-fold change.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp 'Older spellings' (Unsupported section): kind (b). twoway_yxview_parse.class:186 declares MLabels, so mlabels() is twoway's own full name and ml.svg shows the labels drawn. sy.svg shows hollow markers for symbol(Oh). dash notes them and draws the default. Folding them onto mlabel/msymbol/msangle is a small change.
- **SD-12** Refusals carry extra lines or different words from twoway's — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway prints one line: 'option X not allowed', 'foo is not a twoway plot type', '[ is not a twoway plot type', 'parentheses do not balance', 'variable nosuch not found' (for by(), mlabel() and colorvar()), 'option ba() not allowed' (function, dropline), 'option | not allowed', and legloc(6) 'option 6 not allowed', useplotregion(1) '1 is not a plotregion', add ' is not a plotregion'.
  - dash: dash adds a second line: 'twoway does not take this option here.', 'twoway takes this option once; ...', 'After the plot groups' comma ...', or a six-line 'Supported: ...' list after 'X is not a twoway plot type'. It also uses different words: '[aw=price] is not a twoway plot type', 'dash: unbalanced parentheses in the plot specification.', 'dash: by(): variable nosuch not found', 'option bands() not allowed' (naming the full name where twoway names the spelling typed), 'option line not allowed', and 'option legloc() not allowed'.
  - evidence: dash.ado:5896-5905, :11022, :11232-11240, dash_parse.mata:3635. Probes S07, E07, E08, Q14, T14-T25, V22, V23, K046, K087, S13d and O15-O17. The help (Differences 5) says dash names the word twoway names, which is false for ba().
  - fix: Print twoway's line alone, keeping any hint behind a dash-only switch, and name the word as typed.
  - verifier: dash.ado:5897-5904 prints a six-line 'Supported:' list after 'X is not a twoway plot type'. :11235-11240 add 'twoway does not take this option here.' or the after-comma hint, and :11022 adds 'twoway takes this option once...'. Measured in probe1.log: E08 '[aw=price] is not a twoway plot type' vs twoway '[ is not...'; Q14 'dash: unbalanced parentheses...' vs 'parentheses do not balance'; S07 extra list; E07 extra hint. p6out.log K046/K087: twoway 'option ba() not allowed', dash 'option bands() not allowed'. This makes false the help's claim (Differences 5, and dash.sthlp ~1036 'naming the option given') that dash names the word twoway names. The rc always matches; only the text differs.
- **SD-13** A || inside one plot's parentheses is refused, where twoway draws it when no graph option follows — cosmetic, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: (scatter mpg weight || line mpg weight) with no options is rc 0 and draws scatter and line. With title(T), with another group, or with a plot option, it is r(198) '| invalid name' or 'option | not allowed'.
  - dash: Always r(198) '| invalid name'.
  - evidence: Probes S13, S13b-d (p2out.log). The oracle P1A-85 covers only the variant with title().
  - fix: Match twoway's acceptance of the option-less form, or document the difference.
  - verifier: p2out.log S13: twoway `(scatter mpg weight || line mpg weight),` draws 2 views, rc 0. My probe v1 confirms rc 0 without the trailing comma too, while dash gives r(198) '| invalid name'. With title(), another group, or a plot option, twoway also refuses (S13b-d), and dash matches the rc there (S13d words differ: 'option line not allowed' vs 'option | not allowed'). A parser quirk; rare.
- **SD-14** A stale help paragraph says a typo goes to the note — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: titel(x) → r(198) 'option titel() not allowed'.
  - dash: dash refuses titel(x) the same way, r(198), as dash.sthlp:1026-1029 says. But dash.sthlp:6684-6687 says 'a typo is reported as ignored rather than rejected ... titel(x) appears in the note'.
  - evidence: Probe Y01 (p11out.log). dash.sthlp:6684-6687 vs 1026-1029.
  - fix: Delete or rewrite the closing paragraph of the Unsupported section.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6683-6687 still says a typo 'is reported as ignored rather than rejected ... titel(x) appears in the note'. dash.sthlp:1026-1029 says titel(x) is refused, and p11out.log Y01 shows dash r(198) 'option titel() not allowed'. Stale paragraph.
- **SD-15** The help's Unsupported list is not complete — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway takes and draws p1() to p20(), ztick(), zmlabel() and zmtick().
  - dash: These reach the note, but the Unsupported section, which says it is checked against the note, does not list them. test_help_conformance.py reads only catchall_options_truth.json's 52 candidates.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6456-6475. test/test_help_conformance.py:402-410. catchall_options_truth.json options list.
  - fix: Generate the candidate list from twoway's declarations (graph_g_opts, popts, the parse classes), not from a hand list.
  - verifier: test_help_conformance.py checks the Unsupported list only against the pending blocks (_ignored_options), catchall_options_truth.json's 52 options, and _dash_rep_und_tab (_undeclared_twoway_options). The tw_takes table (dash_parse.mata:8018 onward), which lets p1/p2/p20 and ztick/zmlabel/zmtick reach the note, is never compared with the help, so these are absent from dash.sthlp. Worse: p3()-p19() and zlabels()/zticks()/zmlabels()/zmticks() are refused. That makes false both the Unsupported intro ('None of them given once is an error ... a command copy-pasted from a do-file always runs') and README.md:6-8 ('the rest are accepted and named in a note').
- **SD-16** The default resource limits refuse commands twoway draws — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Resource limits
  - twoway: 300 plots in one graph: rc 0. twoway has no plot ceiling or embedded-value ceiling.
  - dash: More than 100 plots → r(134) 'this graph has more than 100 plots ...'. More than 5,000,000 embedded values is r(134) too. Both are raised through globals.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:2933-2973 (Resource limits). probe4 '@@RC dash 120 plots 134' and '300 plots 134'.
  - fix: None required (documented). Mention them in Differences from twoway.
  - verifier: Documented in dash.sthlp:2933-2973 (Resource limits): $dash_max_plots defaults to 100 (ceiling 1,000) and $dash_max_cells to 5,000,000 (ceiling 20,000,000), both r(134). p4out.log: twoway draws 120 and 300 plots rc 0, and dash refuses both, r(134). Kind (b). The fixed 20,000,000-cell ceiling means a scatter of more than 10M observations cannot be drawn at all, which is a real difference for big-data users. Not listed under Differences from twoway.
- **SD-17** name() registers the graph instead of drawing it — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences from twoway item 2
  - twoway: twoway ..., name(g1) draws the graph and keeps it in graph memory.
  - dash: It registers the graph only ('dash: graph 'g1' registered.'), and nothing is written until dash build. saving() on a named graph writes nothing. A do-file that names its graphs, which is common, produces no output when twoway is replaced by dash.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:816-823 (Differences 2). Every probe in probe1, probe3 and probe6 registered without writing a file.
  - fix: None, if this is kept as a deliberate design. The drop-in claim should name it prominently in the README and help synopsis.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:816-823 (Differences item 2): name() registers only, it never touches graph memory, and saving() on a named graph is noted and writes nothing. Kind (b), a deliberate design. It is still the largest qualification of 'type dash where you would type twoway': a do-file using name() produces no output without dash build. README.md:15-16 states the drop-in claim without the caveat.
- **SD-18** dash accepts about 35 option words that twoway refuses — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: documented piecemeal in dash.sthlp
  - twoway: r(198) 'option X() not allowed' for malpha() lalpha() falpha() jseed() colpalette() colornlevels() colormissing() plotbgcolor() pbgc() xlabel2() ytitle2() yscale2() ymlabel2() ytick2() xline2() tlabel2() xla2() mlg() cv() colv() marker() hover() table, and for bw() on bar, histogram and spike.
  - dash: Each is rc 0 and honoured as dash's own synonym or extension. This violates the 'same refusals' rule only in the superset direction: dash do-files do not run back in twoway.
  - evidence: Probes X08, X12-X33 and X50-X52 (p10out.log). dash.ado:1026-1040. dash.sthlp:3653-3657, 4252-4254 and 5818-5821.
  - fix: None, if deliberate. Add one list of all dash-only words to Differences from twoway.
  - verifier: p10out.log X08, X12-X33 and X50-X52: twoway r(198), dash rc 0. Affected: marker(), malpha(), lalpha(), falpha(), jseed(), colpalette(), colornlevels(), colormissing(), plotbgcolor(), pbgc(), xlabel2(), ytitle2(), xla2(), mlg(), cv(), colv(), hover(), table, xline2(), tlabel2(), ytick2(), yscale2(), ymlabel2(), and bw() on bar, histogram and spike (dash.ado:2125-2139 folds bw into barwidth there, where twoway's BARWidth minimum is barw). dash.ado:1026-1032 says outright that this is a deliberate superset. Only the refusal direction differs; there is no single list of these words in Differences.
- **SD-19** A weight with nothing above zero is refused under nodraw — cosmetic, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, partly, effort S, tracked: WP-31 C9 (closed, by design)
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight [fw=0], nodraw is rc 0. Drawn, it is r(198) with no message.
  - dash: r(198) with no message at registration, with or without nodraw. This matches the drawn twoway.
  - evidence: dash.ado:6948-6967. Probes W05, W28 and W29, and probe4 'zero-fw drawn 198'.
  - fix: None needed. Optionally honour nodraw.
  - verifier: The fact is right: dash.ado:6948-6967 exits 198 silently whenever no weight is above zero, and twoway with nodraw is rc 0 while the drawn command is r(198) (p4out.log). But dash's nodraw still writes the page (dash.sthlp 'nodraw still writes the page'), so the drawn twoway is the right comparison, and there dash matches. BUGS.md:3453 (C9) documents the silent r(198). Not a real gap against the claim.

  - twoway: abbrev_truth.json measures 93 options with a .gph-identity oracle. bgcolor is a twoway option (twowaygraph_g.class:128 BGColor), and bgc(red) and bgcol(red) are rc 0 in twoway.

Where two options share a prefix, which one it resolves to is not oracle-checked. abbrev_truth.json's unprobeable entry says 'twoway has no bgcolor()', and dash_abbrev_stata_rejects records 'bgc: stata rejects', which my probe X02 contradicts.

Yet abbrev_truth.json says unprobeable.bgcolor 'dash-only option; twoway has no bgcolor()', and abbrev_resolution.bgc / dash_abbrev_stata_rejects say 'stata rejects'. Both are wrong. Correction to the auditor: the rc-only sweep (p13out.log, 89 options, not 93) was not clean apart from lowess m. It also mismatches on mlabangle/mlabformat ml, mla and mlab, where `mlabel(make) ml(45)` is twoway 198 and dash 111; my v3 probe shows this is mlabel(<non-name>) answered 111 'variable 45 not found' where twoway says '45 invalid name' r(198). Which option a shared prefix resolves to is still oracle-unchecked for those options.
