I relied on the help, the existing twoway_option_parity truth and rc probes; Which option a shared prefix binds to was not oracle-checked for the 89 swept options; I compared rc only (.gph identity was not reproduced); I did not check how value labels appear on axis ticks (xlabel(,valuelabel)), or the default axis label format from a variable's display format beyond existing truth; I did not render pages in a browser; Served mode (dash serve), Windows and Stata/BE/SE were not probed; by() with more than 100 panels and data above 5,000,000 cells were not probed (documented limits); Panel-data ts operators were not re-probed; ts_operator_truth.json covers 224 answers; dash's empty-graph axes were taken from test_bare_dash.py's assertion, not rendered; Exact message comparisons in S11 and E08 were affected by my appended name()/nodraw option; I treated them as cosmetic; Probe files are in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/syntax-data/ (probe1-13 .do, *out.log, gen*.py, parse.py, *.svg)

### styles-schemes (22)

- **ST-1** compassdirstyle: placement() synonyms twoway takes are noted and drawn east (text(), aspectratio(, placement())) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: compass2dir.class _unabbrev reads syntax [, North South East West NWest NEast SWest SEast Center Top Bottom Left Right *], plus capital N S E W NE NW SE SW T B R L, and clock numbers 0-12. Measured on 19.5: text(30 3000 "QQ", placement(X)) for X = no nor N NE SE S W E T B R L cen ce ea eas ri rig righ bot bott to le lef so sou nea nwe draws exactly what the canonical n/ne/e/se/s/sw/w/nw/c draws (identical SVG), with no note. aspectratio(1, placement(rig|ri|R|E|le|L|W|top|nor|N|bot)) also equals its canonical form.
  - dash: _dash_at_place matches a fixed table (full names, one/two-letter lower-case forms, top/bottom/left/right, 0-12) exactly, so every other synonym returns "": dash prints twoway-worded '(note: named style nor not found in class compass2dir, default attributes used)' (a note twoway does not print) and draws the text east of its point / the plot region centred.
  - evidence: dash_parse.mata:11555-11570 (_dash_at_place table); dash.ado:1172-1190 (_dash_aspect_split uses it); probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/styles/p1/probe1.do (G4, G10; analysis ana.py): tw SVG hashes pl_nor==pl_n, pl_NE==pl_ne, pl_rig==pl_e, ap_rig==ap_e; dash logs da_*.log for pl_nor, pl_NE, ap_rig show the note. test/fixtures/added_text_truth.json _meta.compass measured only the table's own spellings.
  - fix: Port compass2dir/_unabbrev: case-sensitive option-abbreviation matching of north..center (min 1 letter, nw/ne/sw/se min 2) and top/bottom/left/right, the capital one-/two-letter forms, 0-12, 'default'; add the measured spellings to the fixture.
  - verifier: Re-read dash_parse.mata:11555-11570. _dash_at_place is an exact-match table: full names, n/ne/.., t/top, b/bottom, l/left, r/right, c/center, 0-12. compass2dir.class _unabbrev (base/c/compass2dir.class) takes syntax [, North South East West NWest NEast SWest SEast Center Top Bottom Left Right *] plus the capital N/S/E/W/NE/NW/SE/SW/T/B/R/L forms. The auditor's p1 SVG hashes (re-run with ana.py) show pl_no/nor/N/T/to == pl_n, pl_NE/nea == pl_ne, pl_ea/eas/ri/rig/righ/E/R == pl_e, cen/ce == c, and ap_rig/ri/R/E == ap_e, none with a note. dash prints the note and draws east (text()) or centred (aspectratio, dash.ado:1183-1186). The fixture _meta.compass holds only the table's own spellings. One more place has the same gap: legend(bplacement()) reads _DASH_BPLACEMENT_LOCATION (dash_render.py:38491) with .lower(), so 'NE' works but nor/nea/t/top do not (twoway bp_nor/bp_t/bp_top == bp_n). A related untracked observation, not part of this finding: with ring(0), dash never uses bplacement() (dash_render.py:39623-39625 takes the ring==0 branch without _bploc), while twoway's legend(ring(0) bplacement(ne)) equals legend(ring(0) position(1)) in the probe. No truth fixture covers ring(0) plus bplacement.

  - fix: Read each long name from its capitalised minimum (c, d, l, s, u, step, a, n) as the class does; extend the S block of the fixture.
  - verifier: _dash_connect_canon (dash_parse.mata:6844-6869) needs 3+ characters for direct, 5+ for stairstep and 4+ for stepstair, and takes only n/i/none for none. connectstyle.class _unabbrev is syntax [, Connected Direct Line Stairstep Upstairstep STEPstair Ascending None I *], so the minimums are c/d/l/s/u/step/a/n. Probe hashes: cn_st/sta/stai == stairstep, cn_up/ups == stepstair, cn_no/non == none, cn_di/co/li/as == direct, no notes. dash.ado:3417-3421 notes '?' and sets v=direct, so connect(st), connect(up) and connect(no) draw a direct line. The S block of connect_truth.json lacks every one of these spellings.
- **ST-7** User/package leaf style files (linepattern, linewidth, symbolsize, gsize, intensity, anglestyle, margin) on the ado-path are not read — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway resolves any named style of these classes through findfile. Measured with files in the working directory: lpattern(zqpat) (set pattern "4 1 1 1") draws a patterned line, lwidth(zqw) (set val 2) == lwidth(2), msize(zqs) == msize(5), title size(zqg) == size(10), fintensity(zqi) == fintensity(30), mlabangle(zqa)/msangle(zqa) == 30, plotregion(margin(zqm)) == margin(9.7222 x4); no notes.
  - dash: Only seriesstyle/markerstyle/areastyle/linestyle/shadestyle files are read; each of these is noted ('is not a recognised line pattern/width/size/fill intensity/angle'; for margin the twoway-worded 'named style zqm not found in class margin') and the default drawn. The help documents only color-*.style and textboxstyle-*.style as unread.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:10381 (_STYLE_FILE_CLASSES), translate_lpattern :9019, resolve_size, _INTENSITY, margin_sides :10274; probe1 G13 (lf_* hashes equal their numeric refs), p3 tw_1.svg == tw_2.svg and da_3.log note; dash.sthlp scheme section (style-file paragraph) names only the two unread classes.
  - fix: Extend the style-file walk (and the registration-time row embedding) to the leaf classes and feed their values into the existing readers; or document each as a difference.
  - verifier: _STYLE_FILE_CLASSES (dash_render.py:10381-10382) is only seriesstyle, markerstyle, areastyle, linestyle and shadestyle. In twoway p1, lf_lw == lf_lwref, lf_ms == lf_msref, lf_gs == lf_gsref, lf_in == lf_inref, ma_zqa == ma_30 and sa_zqa == sa_30; lf_pat differs from solid (patterned). In p3, tw_1 (margin(zqm)) == tw_2 (9.7222 x4). dash logs da_230..235 and p3 da_3 note each one. Help section 6141-6153 names only the textboxstyle and color files as unread, and no BUGS, GAPS3 or ROADMAP entry tracks these leaf classes. One correction: dash's margin note carries the 'dash:' prefix ('(note: dash: named style zqm not found in class margin ...)'), so it is not in twoway's exact words.
- **ST-8** title()/subtitle()/note()/caption() placement() inside the plot region is drawn as an anchor, and long names/clocks/synonyms are ignored — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Measured title("TT", ring(0) placement(X)): n, north, 12, top, t, nor, N all draw the same picture as the default (text at the top centre inside the plot region, x 2124.9); ne, neast, 1 draw the box at the top RIGHT inside the plot region (x 3749.3, same baseline).
  - dash: _dash_textbox_draw applies placement() only when it is one of the short added-text keys, as the Label's anchor at the clock point: placement(n) gives anchor bottom_center (box pushed above the frame top, unlike the default top_center), placement(ne) bottom_left (box above the frame, right of centre); north/12/top/t/nor/N/neast/1 are silently ignored. The help says placement() is drawn where twoway draws it; no truth test covers it (test_textbox_placement.py only checks it is not named).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:54187-54188 (is_label and placement in _DASH_ADDED_TEXT_PLACE), :54384-54388; probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/styles/p2/probe2.do: tw_1/3/5/7/9/11/13 identical, tw_15/17/19 identical; dash Label anchors via ../bk.py on da_2/da_4/da_16 (bottom_center/top_center/bottom_left); dash.sthlp:5337-5338.
  - fix: Measure twoway's ring(0)/position(0) box placement grid (placement x clock) and place the box inside the plot region at that compass point; read placement() through the shared compass reader (ST-1); add a truth fixture.
  - verifier: dash_render.py:54187-54188 sets the Label anchor from _DASH_ADDED_TEXT_PLACE (:54384-54388, the text() anchor table) when placement is one of its short keys. The p2 pages confirm it: da_2 (ring(0) placement(n)) anchor bottom_center at frame top; da_4/6/8 (north/12/top) top_center, so ignored; da_16 (ne) bottom_left at frame centre-top; da_18/20 (neast/1) ignored. Twoway's tw_1..13 are all identical and equal p1 tr_0, the default ring(0), so placement(n) changes nothing in twoway but lifts the box out of the plot in dash. tw_15/17/19 are identical. dash.sthlp:5359-5361 claims placement() is drawn as twoway draws it, and no truth fixture covers it (textbox_truth.json has no placement probe). Severity lowered to minor: title placement() with ring(0) is uncommon, and ST-3, the comparable silent title-position error, is minor.
- **ST-9** msymbol(invisible) draws nothing where twoway notes it and draws the scheme symbol — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: invisible is no symbol name (no symbol-invisible.style, not in symbol._unabbrev or symbolstyle.sthlp). Measured: '(note: named style invisible not found in class symbol, default attributes used)' and the SVG equals the default scatter.
  - dash: _MARKER_MAP and _INVISIBLE_SYMBOLS hold 'invisible', so the markers are drawn with fill_alpha/line_alpha 0 and no note.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:8159, :8202; p4 tw_1.svg == tw_2.svg (default) != tw_3.svg (none), tw_1.log note; da_4.html Scatter alpha 0, da_4.log no note; symbol_pattern_truth.json has no 'invisible' probe.
  - fix: Drop 'invisible' from both tables so it is noted and drawn as the scheme symbol.
  - verifier: 'invisible' is in _INVISIBLE_SYMBOLS (dash_render.py:8159) and _MARKER_MAP (:8202). Stata has no symbol-invisible.style, symbol.class _unabbrev maps only i->none, and symbolstyle.sthlp lists none/i. p4: tw_1 (msymbol(invisible)) == tw_2 (default) != tw_3 (none), and tw_1.log has the 'named style invisible not found in class symbol' note. dash's da_4 Scatter has fill_alpha/line_alpha 0 and no note. The BUGS E1 resolution removed the other non-Stata names (star, asterisk, hexagon, dot) but missed this one, so it is untracked.
- **ST-10** A scheme file of a shipped name found before BASE on the ado-path (adopath ++) is not used — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: findfile walks c(adopath) in order. With adopath ++ <dir> holding scheme-economist.scheme (#include s2color; color p1 red), twoway scatter ..., scheme(economist) draws red markers on s2color's #EAF2F3 (measured), findfile returns <dir>/scheme-economist.scheme.
  - dash: _resolve_path always returns dash-economist.scheme for the 26 names, so dash draws The Economist's look (#c6d3df, no red). The help says the shipped names come first 'as Stata's own are in BASE', which holds only while BASE precedes the user's directory.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:10556-10559; dash.sthlp:6288-6290; probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/styles/p3/probe3.do (tw_4.svg fill:#FF0000 x12, da_5.html no #ff0000, #c6d3df present; records F line).
  - fix: Rank the shipped translations at BASE's position in the passed ado-path, so a directory placed before BASE wins as in findfile; or document it.
  - verifier: _resolve_path (dash_render.py:10542-10560) returns _shipped_path for any of the 26 names before walking the ado-path. All 26 exist in BASE, so the help (6288-6290) holds for the default adopath (BASE first) but not when a directory is put before BASE. p3 records 'F|.../own/scheme-economist.scheme' from findfile. tw_4.svg has FF0000 24 times, and dash's da_5 has no ff0000 and has c6d3df. Rare edge case.
- **ST-11** Twoway-worded 'named style ... not found' notes printed where twoway prints none — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: No note for legend(position(bottom)), legend(ring(i)), connect(di), text(..., placement(nor)), orientation(hor), plotregion(margin(<user file>)).
  - dash: Prints e.g. '(note: named style bottom not found in class clockdir, default attributes used)' and '(note: gridringstyle not found in scheme, default attributes used)' in twoway's exact words, which tells the user twoway would have done the same. Conversely title("T", justification(l)) gets no note where twoway notes 'named style l not found in class horizontal' (same picture).
  - evidence: dash_parse.mata:10426-10435; dash.ado:3419; dash_parse.mata note in _dash_aspect_split/_dash_at_* callers; probe1 da logs; tw_ logs for tj_l.

Conversely, twoway's tj_l/tj_r/tj_c/tj_le logs have 'named style l not found in class horizontal' while dash's da_236 (justification(l)) prints nothing. The drawing matches: twoway draws tj_l == tj_center, and dash ignores non-full words at dash_render.py:54184. Correction: the margin note is dash-prefixed, not twoway-worded. This finding largely duplicates ST-1..ST-7; the only new item is the justification note.
- **ST-12** aspectratio() with xsize()/ysize() prints a Bokeh validation ERROR line on every build — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: No message.
  - dash: 'ERROR:bokeh.core.validation.check:E-1023 (MIN_PREFERRED_MAX_HEIGHT): Expected min_height <= height <= max_height' is printed to the Stata console; the figure is sizing_mode fixed with frame_width/height but keeps min_height 160 / max_height 560 and Bokeh's default height 600.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:27672-27677 (_dash_frame_sizes sets fixed + frame sizes) with :63428-63429 (max/min_height); p2 probe2.log lines 176 and 180 for 'aspectratio(1, placement(e)) xsize(8) ysize(4)' and 'aspectratio(1) xsize(8) ysize(4)'; not for xsize/ysize alone. Precedent W-1005 fixed for the sidebar (dash_render.py:66233).
  - fix: Clear max_height/min_height (or set height) when the frame is fixed; add a test that the build raises no Bokeh validation message.
  - verifier: _dash_frame_sizes (dash_render.py:27672-27677) sets sizing_mode fixed and frame_width/height, but the figure keeps max_height=_MAX_FIGURE_HEIGHT 560 and min_height 160 (:518-519, :63428-63429) while Bokeh's default height is 600. probe2.log:176,180 and probe1.log:295 show 'ERROR:bokeh.core.validation.check:E-1023 (MIN_PREFERRED_MAX_HEIGHT)' for aspectratio()+xsize()+ysize(). No BUGS, GAPS3 or ROADMAP entry mentions E-1023. Precedents W-1005 and E-1024 were treated as bugs, so the open question is the console noise; whether the drawing changes was not checked.
- **ST-13** Axis title orientation() (and other textbox sub-options on xtitle/ytitle) named, not drawn — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp graph-level options paragraph (textbox sub-options), 'every other sub-option is named'
  - twoway: ytitle("Y", orientation(horizontal)) -- a common idiom -- draws the y title upright; justification(), placement(), box, margin() etc. are drawn.
  - dash: _dash_style_axis_titles applies size() and color() only; every other sub-option is named in the note (Bokeh's axis_label_orientation is never set).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:53961-53995; dash.sthlp:1099-1105 ('every other sub-option is named, never drawn').
  - fix: Draw orientation() via Axis.axis_label_orientation (and the box/justification sub-options where Bokeh allows), with a truth fixture.
  - verifier: _dash_style_axis_titles (dash_render.py:53961-53995) sets only axis_label_text_font_size/color/alpha. axis_label_orientation is never set; it appears only in the _DASH_ALT_TITLE_PROPS copy list at :45402. Bokeh 3.9.0 LinearAxis has axis_label_orientation, so drawing it is feasible. dash.sthlp:1099-1105 says every other sub-option is named, never drawn. Kept at major because ytitle(, orientation(horizontal)) is a common idiom and the difference is visible; it is borderline with minor. It is a documented difference only through the generic sentence, with no dedicated BUGS or GAPS entry.
- **ST-14** Inline SMCL markup on part of a text and raised/lowered runs without Unicode forms drawn in one style on one baseline — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: BUGS.md [x] 'Inline SMCL style runs and raised text with no Unicode form are drawn in the text's own style ...' (documented limit); dash.sthlp Text in graphs
  - twoway: title("a {bf:b} c") draws only b bold; a{sup:q} raises q; {it:p}-value italicises only p.
  - dash: A drawn text has one style/font/baseline: partial markup is dropped (with one note per graph and kind), {sup:}/{sub:} only via Unicode super/subscript characters (digits, + - = ( ), n, i); a symbol inside {stSymbol:} is drawn as itself.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5170-5183 and :5198-5199; BUGS.md 'Inline SMCL style runs ...' resolution (M17, D-42a); dash_render.py _dash_graph_text_runs / _DASH_GT_SUP/_DASH_GT_SUB :48410-48425.
  - fix: Draw a text as several positioned runs (multiple Label/Div models or an HTML text renderer) for titles, axis titles, legend and tick labels.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:5171-5183 (one style, one baseline; partial markup drawn in the text's own style, with a note) and closed as a documented limit in BUGS.md:3689 'Inline SMCL style runs and raised text with no Unicode form ...'. The stSymbol residual is at dash.sthlp:5194-5195. Major is reasonable: partial {it:}/{bf:} and {sup:} on letters are common in academic titles. It is a known, documented difference against the claim.
- **ST-15** color-*.style and textboxstyle-*.style files on the ado-path are not read — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'textboxstyle and color-*.style files on the ado-path are not read' (documented limit D36)
  - twoway: mcolor(mine) with color-mine.style draws its RGB; tstyle(mine)/mlabtextstyle(mine) draw the file's size/colour. Third-party schemes that name package colours rely on this.
  - dash: Black markers with the 'named style mine not found in class color' note; titles in the default style; marker labels in body style.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp scheme section lines 197-208 of the section (help ~6142-6153); BUGS.md [x] 'textboxstyle and color-*.style files on the ado-path are not read' (resolved as documented limit, DECISIONS D36); dash_render.py:10381.
  - fix: Extend the style-file reader to the color and textboxstyle classes (with ST-7).
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6141-6153 states that the textboxstyle and color style files are not read, with the exact notes. BUGS.md:4385 '[x] textboxstyle and color-*.style files on the ado-path are not read' and the E10 resolution (BUGS.md:3729, 'Residual, deferred') track it. _STYLE_FILE_CLASSES (dash_render.py:10381) confirms. It is a documented difference that still breaks the drop-in claim, for example for third-party schemes and packages that ship color-*.style.
- **ST-16** Unknown or path-like scheme() refused r(198) where twoway notes and draws s2color; scheme file guards — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Stata scheme support / Option values (deliberate)
  - twoway: scheme(nosuch): '(note: scheme nosuch not found, using s2color)', rc 0; a relative path is followed; any size/link is read.
  - dash: r(198) with a long list of names; a name holding blank/quote/slash/colon/.. refused; links out of the directory and files over 1,048,576 bytes not read (note, s2color drawn); dash-<name> read only from the working directory.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp scheme section ('A name that resolves to nothing is refused', 'Four things differ from twoway, for safety'); dash_render.py:10542-10575, :10672-10703.
  - fix: None if kept deliberate; otherwise note-and-s2color for the one-line form.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6309-6326 shows scheme(nosuchthing) refused with r(198) before Python runs, on every entry point, and calls this stricter than twoway. Help 6293-6300 lists the four safety differences: no paths, regular files of at most 1,048,576 bytes inside their directory, dash-<name> read only from the working directory, and files read as data. The code is _resolve_path/_scheme_name_ok (dash_render.py:10533-10575). This is a deliberate documented difference, and it is a refusal of a command twoway runs (with a note).
- **ST-17** Scheme directives read but not drawn: clockdir p/p# (default marker-label position), textboxstyle of title/subtitle/caption (text size/colour), title clocks 2-4 and 8-10 — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Stata scheme support and Textbox options (documented)
  - twoway: A user scheme's 'clockdir p 9' moves every marker label to 9 o'clock; 'textboxstyle title subheading' draws the title in subheading's size/colour; 'clockdir title_position 3' puts the title at the right of the plot.
  - dash: Marker labels default to 3 (hard-coded unless mlabstyle()); title text style always heading (textboxstyle title is read only for the box margin/fill); clocks 2-4/8-10 drawn above/below.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:17647 (pos default '3'), :54101/:54126 (textboxstyle read for margin/fill only); dash.sthlp scheme section ('A directive dash does not draw yet -- such as clockdir p ... or the textboxstyle of a title'; 'Clock positions 2 to 4 and 8 to 10 ... drawn on the nearer of the top and bottom'); dash.sthlp:5340-5345.
  - fix: Read clockdir p#/p for the default mlabposition; resolve the title boxes' text style through textboxstyle <box>; draw side-panel title boxes horizontally.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6368-6371 says 'A directive dash does not draw yet -- such as clockdir p ... or the textboxstyle of a title -- is read and kept'. In dash_render.py:17648-17649 the mlabposition default is '3' unless mlabstyle() gives one, so the scheme's clockdir p is not consulted. Clocks 2-4 and 8-10 are drawn above or below, as documented at dash.sthlp:5340-5345. All three items are documented differences.
- **ST-18** Title textbox orientation(), width(), height(), angle(), lstyle() and bl* sub-options named, not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Textbox options (documented)
  - twoway: Drawn (rotated or fixed-size boxes).
  - dash: Named in the note; edge titles likewise name orientation().
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5379-5382, :5324-5325; dash_render.py:53833 comment; test_textbox_placement.py docstring.
  - fix: Draw with Label angle / fixed-size box, measure first.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:5379-5382 says 'orientation(), width(), height(), angle(), lstyle() and the bl... sub-options are named in the note: a title box has no angle and no fixed size'. Edge titles likewise name orientation() (help :5324-5325; BUGS.md:4287 resolution). This is a documented difference.
- **ST-19** Symbol rendering residuals: arrow/arrowf/a/A drawn as a head without its shaft; point/p a fixed one-pixel dot — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, partly, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md [x] 'Marker symbols arrow, arrowf, A, a and pipe ...' (residual documented)
  - twoway: arrow/a an open head on a shaft, arrowf/A a filled head on a shaft; point drawn with r = 1 SVG unit.
  - dash: Triangle as tall as the whole arrow, no shaft; point one CSS pixel.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:8170-8186 comments, _MARKER_MAP 'arrow'/'arrowf' -> triangle; dash.sthlp scheme section Series paragraph ('twoway draws an arrowhead on a shaft'); BUGS.md E1 resolution.
  - fix: Custom marker path (Bokeh Scatter marker via MultiLine/Patches) for the shaft.
  - verifier: The arrow residual is confirmed and documented: _MARKER_MAP maps arrow/a/arrowf/A to a triangle (dash_render.py:8170-8186), dash.sthlp:6069-6071 says 'twoway draws an arrowhead on a shaft', and the BUGS.md:3641 resolution records the missing shaft. The point/p half is not a demonstrated difference. The BUGS E1 measurement says twoway draws point as a fixed-size dot with no outline at any msize(), and dash draws a fixed one-pixel dot (_SYMBOL_FIXED_PX, :8192; help 6067). No measurement shows the sizes differ on screen, and a 1-SVG-unit radius at 720 units per inch is sub-pixel.
- **ST-20** Older spellings symbol(), msymangle() and the back-marker/alignment options are named, not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Unsupported Stata twoway options
  - twoway: symbol() and msymangle() are honoured as msymbol()/msangle(); bmsymbol()/bsymbol(), bmcolor() etc. draw back markers; mlabalignment(), mlabjustification(), alalign()/blalign()/clalign(), mlalign(outside), dlpattern() change the picture.
  - dash: Each is named in the 'ignoring unsupported option(s)' note and the default drawn.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp Unsupported Stata twoway options: 'Older spellings', 'Back markers', 'Marker-label text layout', 'Area, bar and line edge styles', 'A dot plot's line pattern'; dash.sthlp:4769-4772 (mlalign(outside)).
  - fix: Alias symbol()->msymbol(), msymangle()->msangle() (S); the rest need drawing support.
  - verifier: These are in the dash.sthlp 'Unsupported Stata twoway options' list: Older spellings (symbol(), msymangle(), mlabels()), Back markers, Marker-label text layout, Area/bar/line edge styles, and A dot plot's line pattern. mlalign(outside) is named at dash.sthlp:4768-4771. Twoway honours each: twoway_yxview_parse.class:595-596 maps Symbol to marker(symbol), :617-618 maps MSYMANgle to marker(symangle), :623-630 map BMSymbol/BSymbol/BMColor/BMSIze to marker(back*), and :667-670 cover MLABJUSTification/MLABALIGNment. yxview.class:432-437 really draws a backing symbol when backsymbol != none, so the back-marker claim holds. Documented differences; aliasing symbol() and msymangle() would be small (S) work.
- **ST-21** graphregion inner box and region lalign() not drawn; plot-region margin a share of the whole graph — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Region Styling Options ('difference from twoway')
  - twoway: graphregion(ilcolor(red)) outlines the inner box, ifcolor() fills it; lalign() moves the outline; margin() is a share of min(W,H) applied to the plot region.
  - dash: istyle/ilstyle/ilcolor/ilwidth/ilpattern/ilalign/lalign named; graphregion(ifcolor()) fills the whole border; margin fixed at build as a share of the graph, sides capped at 45%.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:4296-4307 and :4331-4340 (Region Styling Options).
  - fix: Draw the inner box as an extra BoxAnnotation; compute margins against the plot region.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:4298-4310 names istyle/ilstyle/ilcolor/ilwidth/ilpattern/ilalign/lalign and states that graphregion(ifcolor()) fills the whole border, as a difference from twoway. Help :4329-4335 states that the plot-region margin is a share of the whole graph, fixed at build, with sides held within 45%. Both are documented differences.
- **ST-22** Colour and stylelist edge differences: navy*52, blank stylelist element message, bounds checked on every plot type — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Option values / Style lists (documented); BUGS.md E5 resolution (navy*52 UNCLEAR, D-41c)
  - twoway: mcolor(navy*52) is #010304; mcolor(red " ") on two y is 'option(s) marker(fillcolor(`')  linestyle(color(`'))) not allowed', r(198); scatter mpg weight, lwidth(-1) draws (lwidth only checked where a line is drawn); msize(1e400) passes.
  - dash: #010102 (the one mismatch of the 125 measured option colours; *50, *100, *1000 match); 'dash: mcolor() element 1 is blank; use . for the default', r(198); lwidth(-1) on scatter refused; msize(1e400) refused; xsize(()) passes to the renderer.
  - evidence: Re-checked OptionTranslator.stata_colour against test/fixtures/colour_grammar_truth.json opt_01..opt_125: only opt_17 differs; dash.sthlp:1262-1268, :1210-1222, :1350-1356; test/fixtures/stylelist_truth.json R14.
  - fix: Match twoway's clamp at *52; reword the blank-element refusal; check width/angle bounds only where twoway does.
  - verifier: In colour_grammar_truth.json, opt_17 navy*52 is #010304 in twoway; dash.sthlp:1317-1318 states that dash draws #010102 (BUGS E5 resolution, D-41c UNCLEAR). stylelist_truth.json R14: twoway refuses with "option(s) marker(fillcolor(`')  linestyle(color(`'))) not allowed", r(198), while dash uses its own words (help :1346-1349). dash.sthlp:1239-1249 documents that dash refuses msize(1e400) and scatter lwidth(-1), which twoway draws, and :1252-1256 that dash accepts xsize(()), which twoway refuses. All are documented. Cosmetic for real use, although the refusal of lwidth(-1)/msize(1e400) is strictly an accepted-syntax difference.

  Not covered: Per-plot-type scheme fidelity for all 26 schemes beyond the scheme visual truth's scatter/line/connected/histogram (e.g. rarea, rcap, pcarrow, lfitci under economist, s1rcolor, sj): not measured; s2color directives that dash never names (heuristic scan): gsize gap/title_gap/axis_space/notickgap/alternate_gap/key_gap/key_linespace, yesno extend_axes_*/extend_grid_*/grid_draw_min/max/grid_force_nomin/x2axis_ontop/y2axis_onright/use_labels_on_ticks/alternate_labels/cmissings, numstyle grid_outer_tol, margin axis_title/label/key_label, compass2dir graph_aspect/key_label, vertical_text/horizontal box justifications: not measured whether a user scheme changing them changes twoway's picture; User symbol-*.style files (custom marker shapes) and clockdir/compass/connectstyle style files: not probed; Fonts as rendered: actual face substitution, {fontface} with non-ASCII names (dash keeps only [A-Za-z0-9 _-]), graph set window vs svg faces, PostScript behaviour; Served-app (dash serve) parity of ST-1..ST-9: only saved pages were probed; Windows/Linux case-sensitive scheme and style-file lookup (only macOS available); Third-party packages that ship color-*.style for their schemes (e.g. blindschemes) are not installed here, so the practical impact of ST-15 was not measured; Probe files: /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/styles/p1..p4 (probe*.do, tw_*.svg/log, da_*.html/log, records.txt, ana.py, bk.py)

### visual-fidelity (12)

- **VF-1** Default ticks and range on a histogram's bin axis are worked out from the bar edges, not from twoway's bin midpoints — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (excluded from test_default_axes.py by _NOT_DEFAULT; no BUGS.md entry)
  - twoway: twoway's histogram scale runs from the first bin's midpoint to the last (default_axes_truth.json P08: x.min 13.8125, x.max 39.1875) and takes natscale over that span. The drawn range is the bar edges. Results: histogram mpg 15(5)40 over 12-41; histogram price 5,000 10,000 15,000; histogram rep78, discrete 1(1)5 over 0.5-5.5; bin(5) and frequency 15(5)40; width(5) start(10) 10(10)40; histogram pop 0..20,000,000; horizontal gives 15(5)40 on y.
  - dash: dash runs the natscale model over the bar edges, then stretches the axis to its outermost ticks. Results: mpg 10(10)40 with the range reaching 9.58 (empty band left of the first bar); price adds a 0 tick; rep78, discrete 0(2)6 over about 0-6; width(5) start(10) adds 50; pop adds 25,000,000; horizontal 10(10)40. Initial saved page.
  - evidence: Probes p05 q05 q06 q07 q08 r06 r08 r10 in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/visual/ (twoway SVG beside dash's drawn ticks read from BokehJS axis views; side-by-side images p05_pair.png and q06_pair.png). dash_render._dash_natscale_ticks(12,41,5) gives [10,20,30,40], but (13.8125,39.1875,5) gives [15..40]; (0.5,5.5) gives [0,2,4,6], but (1,5) gives [1..5]; price midpoints give [5000,10000,15000]. The test skips this case on purpose: test/test_default_axes.py:230-233 has _NOT_DEFAULT = {("P08","x")}, commented 'twoway's histogram draws its own x axis (15(5)40...)'. dash.sthlp:4529 says a histogram axis 'takes the model at once'. Matches twoway only when by() or another layer sets the axis (q18, p18, t05).
  - fix: Give the model (or Stata's embedded ticks) the histogram's first and last bin midpoints, in both modes and after a move, and keep the drawn range on the bar edges. Remove P08 x from _NOT_DEFAULT and add fixture rows for discrete, bin(), width()/start(), frequency and horizontal.
  - verifier: I checked this at HEAD 7dced3a. The truth row default_axes_truth.json P08 for twoway histogram mpg has x major 15(5)40, scale 13.8125-39.1875 (the first and last bin midpoints) and cur 12-41. The probe pages, built by the console binary with the repo first on adopath, draw these x ticks: p05 [10,20,30,40] over 9.58-41.42; q06 [0,2,4,6]; q05 [0,5000,10000,15000]; q07 and q08 [10..40]; r06 [10..50]; r10 [0..25e6]; r08 (horizontal) y [10..40]. The tick text in twoway's SVGs is: q06 1-5; q05 5,000 10,000 15,000; r06 10-40; r10 0-20,000,000; r08 15(5)40. I reproduced the model's outputs myself: _dash_natscale_ticks(12,41,5)=[10..40] but (13.8125,39.1875,5)=[15..40]; (0.5,5.5)=[0,2,4,6] but (1,5)=[1..5]. test/test_default_axes.py:230-233 leaves P08 x out on purpose. BUGS.md, GAPS3 and the help (dash.sthlp:4529 only says a histogram axis takes the model) do not record it. Category (c), untracked. Every default histogram's bin axis shows different ticks and an empty band, so major is right.
- **VF-2** The shared y-axis title of a by() graph is never drawn: its cell's canvas is 1 px tall — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway draws the outer y title (for example 'Mileage (mpg)', 'Price', 'Density') to the left of the panel array, centred on it.
  - dash: The dash_by_ytitle cell has a Title model with visible=true. Its element is 28x560, but its canvas is 28x1 px with 0 painted pixels, so no y title shows on any by() graph. The x title, the 'Graphs by' note and the legend do draw.
  - evidence: Probes p09 q01 q02 q03 q18 r09 s01 t04, measured in headless Chrome 155 at 760, 1200 and 1400 px (pix.js and canv.js in the probe directory): canvas [0,60,28,1], dark=0. Screenshots p09_dash_b.png and q01_pair.png have no title. Cause: _dash_by_text_cell (dash_render.py:55040) builds the Plot with frame_height=1; _dash_by_cell (dash_render.py:55386-55406) adds frame_width=1 and height_policy='max' for (1, None) but leaves frame_height=1. Deleting frame_height from a scratch copy of the page gives a 28x560 canvas with the title painted (295 dark pixels). Setting frame_align true does not help. The browser tests miss it: test/test_legend_span_browser.py:519-547 (TestByPanelsFillTheirRow, cited as the pin in BUGS.md 'A by() panel row was taller than its panels') computes the title box from layout bboxes, and test_by_axes_keys_browser.py checks visible flags only.
  - fix: In _dash_by_cell, clear frame_height (set it to None) when the frame's height is to fill the track, and check the same for dash_by_legend and dash_by_clegend, which have 147x1 canvases. Add a browser test that reads the title cell's real canvas size or painted pixels.
  - verifier: Reproduced three ways. (1) The Stata-built q01 page (BokehJS 3.8.0): dash_by_ytitle has bbox 28x648, but its canvas is 28x1 with 0 dark pixels, even though it holds Title 'Mileage (mpg)' with visible=true. (2) Saved and served pages I built through the test harness (bokeh 3.9, ytitle("Price") by(foreign)): canvas 26x1, bbox 26x560. (3) The screenshot (visual-verify/by_saved.png) has no y title, while the x title and the 'Graphs by' note are drawn. Cause as stated: _dash_by_text_cell (dash_render.py:55040) sets frame_height=1, and _dash_by_cell (55398-55405) with frame (1, None) sets height_policy='max' but never clears frame_height. TestByPanelsFillTheirRow (test_legend_span_browser.py:519-547) checks the layout bbox only. One correction: dash_by_legend also has a 147x1 canvas, yet its legend IS drawn in my screenshot, so only the y title is visibly lost. I did not test clegend. Category (c), untracked.
- **VF-3** aspectratio(#) with xsize()/ysize(): the browser ignores the fixed frame, so the help's promised size and plot-region ratio are not drawn — major, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md WP-31 D5 entry marked Fixed)
  - twoway: aspectratio(1) xsize(4) ysize(4) gives a 4x4 in graph with a square plot region (SVG plot region ratio 1.000).
  - dash: The model has sizing_mode 'fixed' and frame_width = frame_height = 334, but width and height stay at Bokeh's default 600. BokehJS draws the figure at 600x560 (max_height) with a 544x509 frame (ratio 1.07), at both 1200 and 700 px viewports.
  - evidence: Probe q04 (fw.js reads el [600,560], frame [544,509], fw 334, fh 334, w 600, h 600). Removing aspect_ratio or max_height in scratch copies does not change it; the 600 defaults win. dash.sthlp:5670-5679 says 'the figure is drawn at that size (96 pixels an inch...) and its plot region is the largest of ratio # that fits'. test/test_sizing.py:262-275 (TestAspect::test_survives_sizes) checks only model attributes.
  - fix: Set width and height to the computed frame plus borders (or to None) on figures dash sizes this way, so frame_width and frame_height take effect. Add a headless-Chrome test of the drawn frame size and ratio, placement() included.
  - verifier: Confirmed, and it is worse than reported. _dash_frame_sizes (dash_render.py:27654-27678) sets sizing_mode='fixed', frame_width/frame_height and min_border_* for the requested box, but never sets width or height. BokehJS therefore takes width_policy fixed at the default width 600 (height 600, capped at max_height 560). Measured: q04 (aspectratio(1) xsize(4) ysize(4)) el 600x560 with a 544x509 frame. aspectratio(0.5) xsize(4) ysize(4) gives a 544x344 frame (ratio 0.63). In my own Stata-built page (visual-verify/stata/ar2s.html, BokehJS 3.8), aspectratio(2) xsize(8) ysize(4) gives a frame 0 px wide: no plot is drawn at all, only a y axis (screenshot ar2s.png). The min_borders were sized for 768 px and add up to 600 or more. twoway draws an 8x4 graph with a 1242.5x2358.4 plot region (ar2_tw.svg). The promise at dash.sthlp:5670-5679 is false. test_sizing.py:262-275 checks the model only. BUGS.md marks WP-31 D5 Fixed. Category (c), untracked. It is major, close to blocker for the tall-ratio case.
- **VF-4** A single graph's proportions give way to the 160-560 px height band, so at desktop widths it is drawn much wider than twoway, and xsize()/ysize()/aspectratio() alone change nothing — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Figure proportions (geometry), documented
  - twoway: Under stcolor a graph is 7.5x4.5 in (1.667:1); xsize(4) ysize(4) is square; aspectratio(1) gives a square plot region.
  - dash: The figure takes the page width and stops at _MAX_FIGURE_HEIGHT = 560 (dash_render.py:518). The default stcolor scatter is 1200x560 (2.14), 1440x560 (2.57) and 1920x560 (3.43); the scheme ratio holds only below about 933 px (800 px gives 800x480). xsize(4) ysize(4) (p16) and aspectratio(1) (r04) draw exactly like the default, 1200x560 with frame ratio 2.25; twoway's plot regions are 0.98 and 1.00. xsize(9) is 2.14:1 at 1200 px where twoway draws 2:1. Only wide shapes are honoured (xsize(10) ysize(2) gives 1200x240).
  - evidence: dims.js measurements of p01, p12 and p16 at 600, 800, 1440 and 1920 px; probes p01 p16 q19 q22 r04. dash.sthlp:5641-5662 and 5721-5723 ('The ratio is honoured within a height band'). dash.sthlp:5653-5655 says xsize(9) 'gives a graph 9 inches wide and 4.5 tall, exactly as twoway does', which holds for the proportions only, below 1120 px.
  - fix: Decide whether the requested ratio should win on a lone graph (lift or raise max_height when a page holds one graph, or when xsize()/ysize()/aspectratio() is given), or keep the band and give its numbers and their effect in the help.
  - verifier: _MAX_FIGURE_HEIGHT=560 and _MIN_FIGURE_HEIGHT=160 (dash_render.py:518-519). Measured: xsize(4) ysize(4) and aspectratio(1) alone both draw at 1200x560 (frame 1144x509, sizing_mode stretch_width, aspect_ratio 1). The default stcolor scatter (p05 dump) draws at 1200x560 = 2.14:1, where twoway's 7.5x4.5 in is 1.667:1. The help gives the band only in words ('The ratio is honoured within a height band', dash.sthlp:5721-5723) with no numbers. The 'exactly as twoway does' sentence for xsize(9) (5653-5655) holds only below about 1120 px. Category (b): documented, but it changes the initial drawing of every lone graph at desktop widths, so it counts against the claim. Major is defensible.
- **VF-5** Text, markers and lines use a fixed 4-inch size reference: 0.889 of twoway's size under stcolor, and about 0.69 of its share of the figure at desktop widths — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp 'The size reference' (geometry) and scheme 'Units', documented
  - twoway: Sizes are relative to the graph's smaller side. Under stcolor that side is 4.5 in, so 1 unit = 3.24 pt: tick labels 3.47 units, line width 9.72/720 in (0.972 pt), marker outer diameter 1.34 units.
  - dash: 1 unit = 2.88 pt (3.84 px) whatever the scheme or figure size. Under stcolor the line is 1.152 px (0.864 pt) and every text, marker and line is 0.889 of twoway's. Against the drawn figure, a 10pt tick label is 2.38% of a 560 px figure where twoway's is 3.47% (0.69x); at 600 px it is 1.07x. Under s2color (4 in side) absolute sizes match exactly (lines 0.864 pt both).
  - evidence: Probes p01 p04 p12 p19 (SVG stroke widths, circle radii, font sizes against BokehJS glyph sizes); dash.sthlp:5706-5718 and 6236-6257.
  - fix: None needed for the claim if it stays a design choice. Optionally scale the unit with the drawn figure's smaller side, or with the scheme's graphsize (4.5 in under stcolor, the default scheme).
  - verifier: stcolor's graphsize is 7.5x4.5 and s2color's is 5.5x4 (scheme files, lines 9-11 and 31-33). twoway's relative unit is 1% of the smaller side: 3.24 pt under stcolor, 2.88 pt under s2color. dash.sthlp:5706-5718 ('draws it at 2.88 points whatever the graph's size') and 6236-6257 ('drawn as it would be on Stata's four-inch reference graph') document the fixed 2.88 pt unit. So every relative size is 0.889 of twoway's under the default scheme, and smaller still as a share of a 560 px figure. Category (b), documented. Minor is right.
- **VF-6** by() panels in the first column have narrower plot regions than the others — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'A spanned panel title's box started at the panel's canvas' records a related difference that stays, for the title box only)
  - twoway: Every by() panel's plot region has the same width; the y tick labels sit outside the left column's plot regions (SVG plot regions 1596/1596, 1393 x5, 2197/2196 SVG units).
  - dash: The left column's y tick labels take space inside that panel's canvas, so its frame is narrower: q03 407 vs 448 px (9% narrower), q01 326 vs 344, p09 516 vs 535. Panels in one row therefore have different pixels per unit on a common x scale.
  - evidence: Probes q01 q03 p09: BokehJS frame bboxes compared with twoway SVG plot-region rects (compare.py and the by() panel width check).
  - fix: Give every panel the same left border (the widest y label), or draw the shared y labels in the outer column, so all frames are equal; check in a browser.
  - verifier: twoway's q03 SVG has both panels' plot regions 1595.70 wide. BokehJS frames on the Stata-built q03 page are 407 vs 448 px. On my harness-built by(foreign) page they are 430 vs 449 (visual-verify/frames.js). The left column's y labels sit inside its canvas, and both panels share the x range 1722.8-5037.2, so pixels per unit differ across the row. No BUGS.md, help or GAPS3 entry covers the plot-region width. Category (c), untracked.
- **VF-7** The scheme's default graph-region margin (margin graph medium, 3.5 units) is not drawn — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (dash.sthlp:4290-4295 describes only the change applied by graphregion(margin()))
  - twoway: The plot region's top and right sit 3.5 units (113.4 of 3240 SVG units) inside the graph edge.
  - dash: Bokeh's min_border of 5 px is used (about 1.3 units); only a graphregion(margin()) change relative to the scheme is added.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:40612-40631 (_dash_graph_margin returns early when sides == start; docstring: 'Bokeh has no outer margin but min_border'). Probe p01: dash frame 5 px from the canvas top and right; twoway 0.035 of the height and 0.021 of the width.
  - fix: Add the scheme's margin graph sides to min_border_* by default, or document it.
  - verifier: s2color (which stcolor includes) has 'margin graph medium', and 'gsize medmargin relative3p5' is 3.5 units. In the p01 twoway SVG the plot region starts 113.4 of 3240 from the top (3.5%) and ends 113.4 from the right. The dash p01 frame is 5 px from the top and right of a 1200x560 canvas. _dash_graph_margin (dash_render.py:40612-40650) returns early unless graphregion(margin()) changes a side ('if sides == start: return'). Only the span placement JS uses the graph margin (BUGS.md:3985, 4005). The help (4290-4295) describes only the change applied by margin(), so category (c), untracked.
- **VF-8** Legend key geometry, and textfirst/stacked/symplacement and the legend's own note/caption — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp legend section (documented); ROADMAP WP-31 residual 'Legend text before its symbol (M17)'
  - twoway: A marker key is drawn at the marker's own size, only as wide as the marker. textfirst puts the text before the key, stacked puts the text under the symbol, and the legend's note() and caption() are written inside the box.
  - dash: The marker is drawn at 0.4 of a symxsize x symysize box (31x13 px under stcolor, 50x13 under s2color), key then text. textfirst, stacked, symplacement() and the legend's note()/caption() are named in the note and drawn as the default.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:4453-4462 and 4491-4501. Probes p02/p08/q15 legend glyph_w/glyph_h.
  - fix: Only if the claim must cover these: draw marker keys at the marker size, and add text-first and stacked key layouts.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:4453-4462 documents marker keys at 0.4 of the symxsize x symysize box. 4491-4501 documents that textfirst, stacked, symplacement() and the legend's note()/caption() are named and drawn as the default. ROADMAP.md:5141 lists 'Legend text before its symbol (M17)' as an expected residual. Category (b), documented and on the roadmap.
- **VF-9** Ticks after a filter move come from fitted models (the noted natscale exception) — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort L, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'
  - twoway: twoway re-ticks with its compiled _natscale and _tsnatscale.
  - dash: The initial page uses Stata's embedded ticks. After a move the natscale model misses 3 of 264 measured axes (98.3% of random ranges), and the date model agrees on 58-66% of %td-%th ranges (98% of %ty).
  - evidence: BUGS.md:4139 '- [ ] Title: The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'; dash.sthlp:4528 and 4658-4668.
  - fix: As in the BUGS entry: refit the model or obtain natscale's algorithm.
  - verifier: BUGS.md:4139 is open: '- [ ] Title: The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew'. It is pinned by test_natscale.py::...test_the_three_known_misses_are_still_missed. The help (4528) says the drawn axis is Stata's own at build and gives the model's 58-98% agreement after a move. This is the noted after-a-move exception, category (a), tracked.
- **VF-10** Axes of kdensity/function layers and fit grids are ticked by the model at build, not by Stata's embedded ticks — minor, unverified, partly, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp axis section (documented as model use); no truth rows beyond the probes
  - twoway: _natscale over the layer's computed range.
  - dash: 'An axis whose drawn range is not its variables' ... takes the model at once', so these build-time axes are only as exact as the model. They matched in every probe (p06, q14, t02, t04, p08, q15).
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:4529; the model's measured agreement is 261/264 drawn axes and 98.3% of random ranges (test/test_natscale.py).
  - fix: Ask Stata's _natscale over each computed layer's range at export (as is already done for the data columns), or extend default_axes_truth to kdensity, function and fit-grid axes on several datasets.
  - verifier: Model use at build is real: dash.sthlp:4529 says these axes 'take the model at once'. But the claim that they are unverified is wrong. default_axes_truth.json has twoway truth rows for function (P12), kdensity (P15) and fit grids (P03, P14, P20 qfitci, Q02 by(), Q06 if). test_default_axes.py compares every axis of these except P08 x, and I ran it today: 50 passed. What is left is narrower: one dataset (auto), and the model's 1.7% miss rate on random ranges (test_natscale.py), with no Stata-embedded ticks for computed ranges. Category (b)/(d): documented, partly verified.
- **VF-11** Plot-region margin is fixed when the page is built; fxsize()/fysize() shrink the cell — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences notes (documented)
  - twoway: The plot-region margin is an absolute size. fxsize()/fysize() alone keep the graph's size and draw the plot in # percent of 4 in, centred.
  - dash: The margin is a fraction of the frame fixed at build (up to about 1.3% of the plot width off after a large resize). fxsize()/fysize() shrink the figure's cell.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:4529 and 5693-5698.
  - fix: None for the initial graph at its built width.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:4529 documents the margin as a fraction fixed at build, up to about 1.3% of the plot width off after a large resize, which is resize-only. 5693-5698 documents fxsize()/fysize() shrinking the cell where twoway keeps a lone graph's size and centres the plot. Category (b), documented. The fxsize difference changes the initial drawing, but it is rare on a lone twoway graph.
- **VF-12** A computed layer of a date variable keeps a date axis where twoway labels day numbers — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp 'Difference from twoway' (Computed layers of a date)
  - twoway: twoway kdensity date (and lfit/qfit/lfitci/qfitci/lpoly/lpolyci first, and scatteri/pci/pcarrowi/function first) labels the axis 15000..15400.
  - dash: dash keeps a date axis and draws dates.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:4817-4818 and the 'Dates on every axis' paragraph (4820).
  - fix: Label with numbers as twoway does, if the claim must hold here.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:4817-4818 has the explicit 'Difference from twoway' text: twoway labels 15000.. when the first layer is lfit/qfit/lfitci/qfitci/kdensity/lpoly/lpolyci, and dash keeps a date axis. 4820 adds the same for scatteri/pci/pcarrowi/function first. Category (b), documented.

  Not covered: The served app (dash serve): only the saved page was measured. VF-1 and VF-2 are probably shared through the common code, but this was not confirmed.; Schemes other than stcolor and s2color (s1*, economist, mono, sj, ...): left to test_scheme_visual_truth.py, not re-measured.; Browsers other than headless Chrome 155 on macOS arm64 (Safari/Firefox text metrics, real DPI scaling).; Whether VF-2 is a recent regression: older renderers (e572f10, 9cc0a23, 6370ca4) cannot read the current dash.ado's data file and render blank pages, so it could not be bisected.; Whether a by() colour key (dash_by_clegend, a canvas-painted ColorBar in a frame_height=1 cell) paints. Neither twoway nor dash drew a key in the probed colorvar() by() graph. dash_by_legend has the same 147x1 canvas, but its keys and text were visible.; Marker symbols other than circle, line patterns, arrowheads, dot rows, contour and the other plot types' own geometry (other audit areas).; Multi-graph dash build layouts and tabs, bs grids, phone widths, very large data on the WebGL backend.; Pixel-level glyph comparison (anti-aliasing, exact text widths and wrapping); only geometry, sizes, colours and text strings were compared.

### ecosystem (17)

- **ECO-1** A registered graph draws the data in memory at dash build, not the data it was registered on — major, wrong-output, partly, effort L, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: name() keeps the drawn graph with its own data. `preserve; collapse (mean) mpg, by(rep78); twoway line mpg rep78, name(tc); restore`: graph tc still shows the collapsed 6 rows. A graph drawn from a tempvar inside a program, or under `frame f2:`, survives too: `graph display tf` gives rc 0.
  - dash: `dash line mpg rep78, name(gc)` under preserve/collapse, then restore, then `dash build`: rc 0, no note, and the page draws 69 points (the restored 74 rows) where the one-line form drew 5. A tempvar graph fails at build with "variable __000000 not found", r(111). `frame f2: dash scatter lexp gnppc, name(gf)` followed by a build from the default frame also fails, r(111). Graphs registered from different datasets or frames cannot share one dashboard.
  - evidence: Probe p3 sections C and D (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/ecosystem/p3/p3.log): ColumnDataSource shape [5] in c_direct.html against [69] in c_build.html. Probe p2 section D4. dash.sthlp:190-212 only implies build-time evaluation. dash.sthlp:3038-3039 advises reducing data 'inside preserve and restore', which is a trap on the name() route.
  - fix: Snapshot each registered graph's columns, or its qualifier columns, when it is registered. At minimum, record the datasignature, c(N) and c(frame) at registration and refuse or note at build when they differ. State plainly in Difference 2 that registered graphs read the build-time data.
  - verifier: Behaviour confirmed: p3.log C (collapsed N=6, restored N=74, build rc 0, no note), and I grepped the pages: every ColumnDataSource is shape [5] in c_direct.html and [69] in c_build.html. D2 gives r(111) 'variable __000000 not found'; p2 D4 gives r(111) 'variable lexp not found'. dash.ado never calls datasignature, c(frame), c(N) or c(changed), so nothing is fingerprinted when a graph is registered. Correction to 'untracked / only implies': dash.sthlp:194-205 says plainly that at build Stata evaluates each qualifier 'against the data then in memory', and that a variable dropped since registration stops the build. Difference 2 (815-821) says name() registers the 'specification'. So build-time data is documented (category b). What the help does not say: that name() keeps no data, frame or tempvars; that graphs from different datasets or frames cannot share a dashboard; and that a dataset changed after registration is drawn with no note. Major stays: the preserve/collapse + name() + restore pattern draws different data with rc 0 and no warning.
- **ECO-2** A do-file cannot write an image of a dashboard, so graph export, putdocx/putpdf image and dyndoc dd_graph have nothing to embed — major, tracked-open, confirmed, effort L, tracked: ROADMAP.md Deferred: the platform package, scripted image export (P1.28 B3)
  - twoway: `twoway ...; graph export f.png|svg|pdf|eps, width()` writes an image that putdocx, putpdf, putexcel, dyndoc and markdown reports embed.
  - dash: Only HTML (or a .py app) is written. graph export gives r(601) "no Graph window open" in a fresh session, or writes an older Stata graph: in batch it wrote a dropped graph with rc 0. Images come only from the browser toolbar (PNG at screen resolution, SVG).
  - evidence: Probe p2 A1/A2 (r(601) for both export and save). Probe p1 F1/F4 (stale export and save, rc 0). dash.sthlp:1631-1663 ('A do-file cannot write an image of a dashboard'). ROADMAP.md:5061 (Deferred: scripted image export, P1.28 B3).
  - fix: Implement the deferred `dash export using file.png|pdf|svg` with a headless browser, returning r(filename). Until then, say in the Description that reports (putdocx, putpdf, putexcel, dyndoc, markdown) cannot include a dashboard.
  - verifier: p2 A1/A2 give r(601) 'no Graph window open' and 'no current graph'. p1 F1/F4 wrote a stale png and gph with rc 0. dash.sthlp:1631-1663 says a do-file cannot write an image, and 1642-1644 documents the stale batch export. ROADMAP.md 'Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)' lists 'dash export using file.png|pdf|svg' (P1.28 B3). Tracked open. dash.sthlp:179-182 already says putdocx and dyndoc do not see a dashboard; putpdf, putexcel and markdown are not named.
- **ECO-3** name() registers without drawing; saving() beside name() writes nothing — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences item 2
  - twoway: `twoway ..., name(g1, replace) saving(g1, replace)` draws the graph, keeps it in memory and writes g1.gph.
  - dash: Only registers g1. Nothing is drawn until `dash build`. saving() prints "(note: dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): saving())" and no file is written (confirm file gives rc 601). A do-file that names every graph produces no output at all.
  - evidence: Probe p3 B1/B2. dash.sthlp:815-821 (Difference 2) and 1407-1412.
  - fix: None needed for the claim if this stays a deliberate difference. Otherwise draw and register on name(), and honour saving() beside name().
  - verifier: p3 B2: the note 'ignoring unsupported option(s): saving()', 'graph ns registered', and confirm file gives rc 601. twoway B1 wrote ns_tw.gph. Documented as a deliberate difference at dash.sthlp:815-821 ('saving() on a named graph is accepted, named in the note, and writes nothing'). Major stands for the claim, since name() is common in twoway do-files and such a do-file then draws nothing.
- **ECO-4** graph combine has no full counterpart: dash build refuses combine's options, and the help never maps one to the other — minor, ecosystem, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `graph combine g1 g2, cols() rows() holes() colfirst xcommon ycommon iscale() imargin() note() caption() xsize() ysize()` combines graphs from memory or disk, including graphs from different datasets.
  - dash: `graph combine g1 g2` after dash gives "g1 is not a memory graph", r(198). `dash build` refuses xcommon, ycommon, cols(), holes(), colfirst, iscale(), imargin(), note(), caption() and xsize() as "option X not allowed", r(198). layout(), title() and subtitle() are the only counterparts, and no shared axis scales are offered (link() shares a selection, not axis scales).
  - evidence: Probe p3 section E. Probe p1 F6. dash.sthlp:1404-1518 and 1778-1856 never mention graph combine.
  - fix: Document graph combine to dash build layout(). Consider xcommon/ycommon (shared ranges), note()/caption() and holes() on dash build.
  - verifier: p3 E: dash build refuses all ten combine options with r(198) 'option X not allowed'. p1 F6: 'g1 is not a memory graph', r(198). graph combine is a separate command, not twoway, so this is an ecosystem and documentation gap, not a syntax gap. The help names graph combine only for by(,style(combine)) (dash.sthlp:3356-3359) and for xcommon/ycommon at 6635. Nothing maps combine to dash build layout(), and the help offers no shared axis ranges.
- **ECO-5** No graph-memory commands for the registry; `dash clear <name>` silently empties the whole registry — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: graph dir, describe (which stores r(command)), drop, rename, copy, display and replay act on single named graphs.
  - dash: `dash dir|describe|drop|rename|display|use|save|combine g1` are refused "X is not a twoway plot type", r(198). Stata's graph commands do not see registry items (graph display g1 gives r(111)). `dash clear g2` returns rc 0 and clears every graph and card: _dash_clear takes no syntax.
  - evidence: Probe p2 section E. Probe p1 J1 (registry empty after `dash clear g2`). dash.ado:11948-11997.
  - fix: Refuse arguments to `dash clear` (`syntax` with no args), or make `dash clear namelist` drop only those items. Consider a dir/describe/drop for the registry.
  - verifier: _dash_clear (dash.ado:11948-11997) has no syntax statement and drops every dash_* global whatever it is given. p1 J1: 'dash clear g2' gives rc 0 and an empty registry. The help's syntax (dash.sthlp:131, 1505) is 'dash clear' with no arguments. p2 E: dir, describe, drop and the other words are refused with r(198) 'X is not a twoway plot type'. Note: dash clear is dash-only syntax, so its silent acceptance of arguments is a dash bug, not a twoway parity gap. The missing per-graph memory commands follow from Difference 2.
- **ECO-6** A duplicate graph name is refused with r(198) and dash's own words, where twoway gives r(110) — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: "graph t1 already exists", r(110)
  - dash: "dash: graph name 'g1' already exists. Use name(g1, replace) to overwrite.", r(198)
  - evidence: Probe p1 G1/G2. dash.ado:11360-11363. BUGS.md:1499 itself states twoway's r(110). The help documents r(198) only for a graph/KPI collision (dash.sthlp:860-866).
  - fix: Exit 110 with "graph g1 already exists" for a graph-to-graph clash, or document the difference.
  - verifier: dash.ado:11360-11363 exits 198 with dash's own words. p1 G1 (twoway): r(110) 'graph t1 already exists'. G2 (dash): r(198). test/test_registry_collision.cscript:987 pins dash's message, so this is a certified choice, but no Difference lists it. dash.sthlp:860-866 documents r(198) for a graph/KPI clash only, and 876-878 quotes twoway's r(110) without saying dash differs.
- **ECO-7** dash leaves its internal s() macros behind; twoway leaves s() empty — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: After twoway scatter, sreturn list is empty, and an earlier s(foo) is cleared.
  - dash: After the one-line form: s(_recast_type)="scatter" and s(g_saving)="p2b.html, replace". After name() and after dash build: s(_recast_type) and s(g_name)="g1". dash kpi leaves s() empty.
  - evidence: Probe p2 B/B2/B4. Probe p1 B/C. The sclass parsers at dash.ado:934 and 1203 run, and the dispatcher at dash.ado:154-185 never calls sreturn clear.
  - fix: Call sreturn clear at the end of each dash route.
  - verifier: p2 B: twoway clears an earlier s(foo). B2 and B4: dash leaves s(_recast_type), s(g_saving) and s(g_name). The dispatcher (dash.ado:154-185) has no sreturn clear on exit, and the help's Stored results (dash.sthlp:1750-1774) say nothing about s(). Downgraded: no program can rely on s() surviving twoway, and the names left are dash's internal ones. Nothing drawn or refused differs.
- **ECO-8** Commands that draw through twoway internally, and addplot() hosts, cannot use dash; dash histogram is not the histogram command — cosmetic, ecosystem, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: histogram, kdensity, lowess and lpoly (as commands), marginsplot, sts graph, stcurve, avplot/rvfplot, qnorm/pnorm, xtline, coefplot and binscatter all draw through graph twoway, and many take addplot() or twoway options.
  - dash: Nothing routes these to dash. `dash histogram mpg, normal|addlabels|addplot()` and `dash kdensity mpg, normal` are refused r(198), as twoway histogram and kdensity refuse them. `dash lowess` draws only the line, where the lowess command also draws the scatter. The help says only that addplot() belongs to commands such as histogram (dash.sthlp:6638).
  - evidence: Probe p1 section M (rc and first line match twoway). No intercept in dash.ado (grep finds no addplot or marginsplot).
  - fix: Document that only graphs typed as twoway can be replaced, with recipes: histogram plus kdensity/function overlays, margins results through rcap/connected, sts generate plus connect(J). Optionally add wrappers.
  - verifier: Facts are accurate, but this is not a gap against the claim. The claim is 'type dash where you would type twoway', and histogram, marginsplot, sts graph and the others are not typed as twoway. p1 M1-M8 show dash giving the same rc and first line as twoway histogram and twoway kdensity for normal, addplot() and addlabels. 'twoway lowess' also draws only the line, so dash lowess matches it. The only difference is the extra advice line (ECO-11). What remains is a documentation suggestion (recipes); dash.sthlp:6637-6638 already says addplot() belongs to commands outside graph twoway.
- **ECO-9** Help claim false: 'graph is read only before twoway' — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `twoway graph scatter` gives "graph is not a twoway plot type", r(198).
  - dash: `dash graph twoway scatter ...` and `dash graph scatter ...` are both refused "graph is not a twoway plot type", r(198). The help says graph is a prefix before twoway and that anything else is refused as "unrecognised plot type 'graph'". dash.sthlp:156-163 says the opposite.
  - evidence: Probe p1 L1-L3. dash.sthlp:854-858 against dash.sthlp:156-163 and dash.ado:112-120.
  - fix: Delete or rewrite dash.sthlp:854-858.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:854-858 says graph is a prefix before twoway and is otherwise refused as "unrecognised plot type 'graph'". dash.ado:106-119 (_dash_prefix_word) refuses graph/gr/gra/grap in every position with '<word> is not a twoway plot type', r(198), as dash.sthlp:156-163 says. p1 L2/L3 confirm this for both 'dash graph twoway' and 'dash graph'. The paragraph is stale and contradicts the help's own opening.
- **ECO-10** Help claim false: a typo 'is reported as ignored rather than rejected' — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `titel(x)` gives "option titel() not allowed", r(198).
  - dash: Same refusal, r(198), not a note. dash.sthlp:6683-6687 says titel(x) appears in the note, which contradicts Difference 5 (dash.sthlp:1013-1017).

  - fix: Rewrite the final paragraph of 'Unsupported Stata twoway options'.

Addition: the paragraph just before it, dash.sthlp:6629-6640 ('Some names that reach the note ... xreverse ... xcommon ... lockaspect, zoom(), ddensity'), is also stale. WP-32 (BUGS.md:2599) made dash refuse these as twoway does, and Difference 5 names xreverse as refused (pinned by test/test_option_validation.cscript:2833, U50). Both paragraphs need rewriting.
- **ECO-11** Refusals carry extra advice lines that twoway does not print — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: One line: "option normal not allowed" or "dir is not a twoway plot type".
  - dash: Adds "      twoway does not take this option here." (or the upper-case and after-comma variants), and a five-line "Supported: ..." list after "X is not a twoway plot type". The rc is the same.
  - evidence: Probe p1 M2/M4/M6/M8. Probe p2 section E. dash.ado:11233-11240. Not documented in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Drop the extra lines, or document them as dash's addition.
  - verifier: dash.ado:11104-11108 and 11233-11240 print the second line '      twoway does not take this option here.' (and its variants). dash.ado:5897-5904 prints the 'Supported:' list, which the comment at 5893-5896 calls 'dash's own'. The rc and first line match twoway (p1 M, p2 E). A grep finds neither text in dash.sthlp, so it is undocumented.
- **ECO-12** Graph names are limited to 22 characters with no '__'; twoway takes 32 — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences item 2, 'What a name may be'
  - twoway: name(abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz12) (28 characters) and name(a__b) give rc 0.
  - dash: Both refused, r(198).
  - evidence: Probe p1 G3-G6. dash.sthlp:877-897.
  - fix: Key the registry so that 32-character names and '__' fit (for example a hashed global suffix), or keep the documented limit.
  - verifier: p1 G3-G6: twoway takes a 28-character name and a__b, and dash refuses both with r(198). Documented as deliberate at dash.sthlp:877-897 ('What a name may be': 22/18 characters set by the 32-character global names, and '__' reserved for by() panels and KPI fields).
- **ECO-13** saving() of an existing file gives r(198) where twoway gives r(602) — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences item 2, 'saving() keeps a file that is already there'

  - dash: "dash: file 'd1s.html' already exists. Use replace option to overwrite.", r(198)

- **ECO-14** The Graph Editor is unavailable and a readable play() recording is refused — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort XL, tracked: dash.sthlp Unsupported section, play() paragraph
  - twoway: play(rec) applies Graph Editor edits, and the graph can be edited interactively.
  - dash: A missing recording gets twoway's r(601) message. A valid .grec is refused r(198). There is no editor; pan and zoom in the browser are the only interaction.
