  - fix: None for the claim (documented). Optionally say in Differences that the Graph Editor cannot open a dashboard.
  - verifier: p1 K1/K2: a missing recording gets twoway's r(601) message. _dash_play_check (dash.ado:8239-8272) refuses a readable .grec with r(198). Documented at dash.sthlp:6668-6679 and certified by test_option_validation.cscript U54 (from about 3108). A valid twoway option refused is a documented gap. The Graph Editor itself belongs to the output medium.
- **ECO-15** Window model: each one-line draw opens a new browser page, and saving() opens none — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, partly, effort S, tracked: partly dash.sthlp nobrowser_global; untracked for one page per command
  - twoway: Each draw replaces the single 'Graph' window. saving() both displays and saves.
  - dash: Each one-line command without saving() writes a uniquely named preview and runs `open '<path>'`, so a do-file of N graphs opens N browser pages. Only the newest five previews are kept, so older pages stop reloading. With saving(), no window opens.
  - evidence: dash.ado:10036-10055 (saving() sets open=0) and 10145-10200 (_dash_open_page). Preview name in probe p2 G1. dash.sthlp:1616-1621 documents saving() keeping the window shut; nothing documents one page per command. Not observable in batch.
  - fix: Document it, or reuse one preview path or tab per session.
  - verifier: Code confirms it. _dash_browser_wanted (dash.ado:10045-10058) sets open=0 whenever saving() is given, and each preview has a unique name (p2 G1 path), opened by _dash_open_page with `open`/xdg-open/start. Most of it is documented, though: saving() keeping the window shut (dash.sthlp:1608-1611), unique preview names with the newest five kept (1769, 2864-2867), and the printed 'temporary preview that later previews may remove'. Only 'N commands open N browser pages' is unstated. It cannot be observed in batch.
- **ECO-16** The help and README understate what a drop-in user loses from the graph ecosystem — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: n/a
  - dash: dash.sthlp:179-182 names only graph export, graph save, putdocx and dyndoc. The help never mentions graph combine, display, use, describe, rename, copy, drop and replay, bare `graph` replay, the Graph Editor beyond play(), putpdf, putexcel, markdown or webdoc, ECO-1's build-time data, or iframe embedding (dash.sthlp:2763, which is not linked from Exporting). README.md says 'dash takes the place of twoway' with no caveat.
  - evidence: grep of dash.sthlp and README.md for these terms. Probe p2 A3 (bare graph: "no existing graph command to replay").
  - fix: Add a short 'Stata graph commands and reports' paragraph to Differences and a one-line caveat to the README.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:179-182 names only graph export, graph save, putdocx and dyndoc. A grep of dash.sthlp finds no putpdf, putexcel, markdown, webdoc, graph display, use, describe or replay. README.md:15-16 says 'dash takes the place of twoway' and gives no ecosystem caveat; it only points to help for option differences (README:6-8, 59). The iframe note at dash.sthlp:2763 exists but is not linked from Exporting.
- **ECO-17** dash depends on Python and bokeh, and only macOS arm64 StataNow 19.5 MP has ever been measured — major, platform-environment, confirmed, effort XL, tracked: ROADMAP.md Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)
  - twoway: Runs on every Stata edition and platform with no dependencies.
  - dash: Without a linked Python, every dash command except clear is refused r(198). Windows, Linux, Intel macOS, GUI Stata, Stata 19.0, BE and SE are unmeasured, including the browser opener and c(mode)/c(graphics) under Windows /e.
  - evidence: dash.ado:300-318. README.md Requirements. ROADMAP.md 'Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)'.
  - fix: Carry out the deferred platform package.
  - verifier: _dash_require_python (dash.ado:306-318) exits 198 without a linked Python, and _dash_require_deps runs for every subcommand except clear (dash.ado:102-105). The main program is 'version 19' (dash.ado:89), and README:32-33 says 'Stata 19 or later; tested on StataNow 19.5 (MP), macOS arm64 ... Stata 19.0, Stata/BE and Stata/SE have not been run'. Tracked as ROADMAP.md 'Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)': Linux, Windows, Intel macOS, GUI Stata and c(mode)/c(graphics) under Windows /e (S12). Category (d), unverified off this platform. twoway also runs on Stata versions before 19, which dash does not.

  Not covered: Interactive GUI and console Stata (browser opening, interactive graph export r(601)): every probe ran in batch through stata_slot.sh.; Windows, Linux and Intel macOS: not available.; Real dyndoc, putdocx, putpdf and markdown runs, and embedding a saved page by iframe in dyndoc HTML output: not run; inferred from graph export behaviour.; dash serve's r() and s(): it blocks in the foreground, so it was not probed.; Playing a valid .grec: not re-run; the repository's test_option_validation.cscript U54 covers it.; marginsplot, sts graph, coefplot and the other commands that draw through twoway: not run, since no mechanism exists to route them; checked by code inspection only.; Per-command render time compared with twoway: not measured.

### platform (16)

- **PT-1** dash and dash_setup refuse a Python that Stata found by itself (python_exec never set by hand) — major, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (ROADMAP WP-07 TR-07.1/7.3 assumed that an empty c(python_exec) means no Python)
  - twoway: twoway needs no Python. Stata's own python: works without any python set exec: on first use Stata searches for an interpreter (here it found /usr/local/bin/python3, 3.14.6).
  - dash: c(python_exec) stays "" unless python set exec was typed, even after python: has run. So every dash command except clear stops with r(198) "Python is required but is not available to Stata", and dash_setup stops with r(198) "Unmet: Python (not linked to Stata)". The message tells the user to run dash_setup, which refuses the same way.
  - evidence: Probe run with a fresh HOME, so no saved python_exec (probe4.do/probe5.do in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/platform): before python ran, python_exec=[]; dash scatter rc=198; python: printed '3.14.6 /usr/local/bin/python3' (rc 0); afterwards python_exec=[] and dash still rc=198. python query shows 'set python_exec /usr/local/bin/python3' and 'initialized no', and two sessions saved no python_exec to the rc file. Code: dash.ado:296-301 (_dash_python_ok tests only for an empty string), dash.ado:308-319, dash_setup.ado:96-107, dash_setup_lib.py:829-834 (_linked reads c(python_exec)). Never tested, because test/run_all_cscripts.do:61 always sets python_exec explicitly.
  - fix: When c(python_exec) is empty, use quietly python query and its r(execpath). That is a setting, not a Python run: Python stayed uninitialised in the probe. Use it in _dash_require_python, dash_setup and _dash_child_python, and fix the remedy text. Add a cert test that runs Stata with a fresh HOME.
  - verifier: Re-read the auditor's probe4/probe5 logs and reproduced the result with my own fresh-HOME probe (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/platform-verify/v1.do). c(python_exec) is empty, and quietly python query returns r(execpath)=/usr/local/bin/python3, r(version)=3.14.6 and r(initialized)=0, so Python is not started and c(python_exec) stays empty. The fresh rc file saved no python lines across three console sessions. The [P] manual (p.pdf, Configuring Python) says Stata finds a Python by itself on first use, and c(python_exec) reports only the python set exec setting. Code confirmed: dash.ado:290-319 (_dash_python_ok tests for an empty string; _dash_require_python gives r(198) 'Python is required but is not available to Stata'), dash_setup.ado:96-107, dash_setup_lib.py:829-834. dash_setup.sthlp:41-43 documents the behaviour, but the message is false and its 'Run dash_setup first' remedy loops back to the same refusal. Corrections: (1) test/run_all_cscripts.do:54-63 sets python exec only when DASH_PYTHON_EXEC is set, not always. The suite never covers this case because the maintainer's ~/.stata19 rc holds pyexec_64 /opt/anaconda3/bin/python3. (2) Lowered from blocker to major. There is a one-line workaround (python set exec), and the message points to help python. Whether GUI Stata saves the Python it finds (which would limit the problem to the first session) is unmeasured. The proposed fix through r(execpath) is workable: measured, it gives a path without starting Python.
- **PT-2** Stata version floor is 19; twoway runs on every Stata — minor, tracked-open, partly, effort XL, tracked: ROADMAP 'Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)'; GAPS3 I1
  - twoway: twoway runs on Stata 8 and later.
  - dash: On Stata 18.5 or older, dash and dash_setup stop with r(9): 'dash requires Stata 19 or later; this is Stata X.Y'. This refusal has never run on a real older Stata; it is pinned only statically. Part of the floor is technical: rpcap/rpspike are Stata 19 types and sfi.BreakError needs 18.5. The rest reflects that nothing older was certified.
  - evidence: dash.ado:84-89 (_dash_require_stata before version 19); dash.ado:420-447 (floor 19, exit 9); dash.sthlp:6701-6707; README.md:32-33; BUGS.md:3268, P1.35 residual 'Stata 16-18.5 are refused, not certified'.
  - fix: Certify Stata 16 to 18.5 or confirm they cannot be supported: version-parametrised runners, version-keyed truth, a Break fallback below 18.5, version-dependent advice for rpcap/rpspike. Otherwise drop the unqualified drop-in claim for those releases.
  - verifier: Facts confirmed: dash.ado:84-89 and 438-447 (_dash_require_stata, floor 19, exit 9), dash.sthlp:6701-6707, README.md:32-33. ROADMAP.md:5057-5060 tracks 'Stata 16-18.5 certified' in the deferred platform package, and the BUGS.md P1.35 residual says the same. However, the floor is maintainer decision M2 and is documented in dash.pkg, the help and the README as a package requirement. It is a requirement, not a behaviour difference: on a Stata that dash admits there is no gap. A small correction: SF-01 (test_wp30_truth.cscript:424-431) measures Stata's own rc 9 at runtime, and ENV-24/28/29 drive the guard by value, so the refusal is not pinned only statically. It has still never run on a real older Stata. Lowered to minor: it scopes the drop-in claim rather than breaking it.
- **PT-3** Stata/BE, Stata/SE and Stata 19.0 are admitted but never run; all truth comes from StataNow 19.5 MP — major, unverified, confirmed, effort L, tracked: ROADMAP platform package ('Stata 19.0, BE and SE measured'); GAPS3 I1
  - twoway: twoway runs on every edition and every 19.x update.
  - dash: Admitted but never run. Every *_truth.json fixture is measured on StataNow 19.5 MP, so any twoway behaviour that differs in 19.0 is unknown. BE has fixed limits: maxvar 2,048 and c(macrolen) 264,392. Of these, only the macro length was checked, by emulation (see PT-4).
  - evidence: dash.pkg 'Measured on MP only; Stata 19.0, Stata/BE and Stata/SE have not been run'; dash.sthlp:6701-6703; limits.sthlp:25, 52 and note 2 (:236-245, BE macro length fixed); probe log: ed=MP, ver=19.5.
  - fix: Run make cert and the parity tests on BE, SE and 19.0. Re-measure the truth that depends on edition or update.
  - verifier: Confirmed: dash.pkg ('Measured on MP only; Stata 19.0, Stata/BE and Stata/SE have not been run'), dash.sthlp:6701-6703, README.md:33. The code comments cite 'Measured on StataNow 19.5' throughout (for example dash.ado:1829, 2274, 3115, 3370). limits.sthlp:25 and 52 and note 2 confirm that BE has maxvar 2,048 and a fixed macro length of 264,392. One BE-specific gap is already measured (PT-4). There is another likely one, not reported: _dash_maxvar_room (dash.ado:7856-7879) refuses r(900) when dash's working columns (__dashq/s/t/w) exceed c(maxvar)-c(k). On MP or SE the user can raise maxvar; on BE they cannot. Severity stays major because the editions and the 19.0 release cover a large share of users and the truth fixtures come from one build only. It is a verification gap, with no 19.0 twoway difference shown yet.
- **PT-4** Named graphs with function layers are capped by c(macrolen), which Stata/BE cannot raise — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: documented in dash.sthlp n() entry (refusal r(134)); the BE part is untracked
  - twoway: twoway draws (function y=sin(x), range(0 10) n(10000)) (function y=cos(x), range(0 10) n(10000)) with rc 0, on any edition and with no upper bound on n().
  - dash: Under BE's fixed macro length (emulated with set maxvar 2048, giving c(macrolen) 264392), registering that graph with name() is refused r(134): 'holds 408,235 characters of function points, more than the 264392 one Stata macro holds ... or raise c(macrolen) with set maxvar'. BE cannot set maxvar. One n(10000) layer is 203,324 characters, so at the MP default (645,200) four such layers are refused too. The one-line form uses a string scalar and draws fine. The limit in the message is printed unformatted.
  - evidence: probe3.do: F2 rc=134, twoway rc=0, one-line F3 rc=0, F4 at maxvar 5000 rc=0; probe3 first version: F1 fpts len=203324. Code: dash.ado:11366-11377. Help dash.sthlp:3713-3716 says '645,200 by default ... set maxvar raises it', which is untrue on BE.
  - fix: Keep a named graph's function points in a string scalar or a file, as the one-line route does, instead of a global. At minimum, correct the help and the message for BE.
  - verifier: Re-read probe3.log. Under set maxvar 2048, c(macrolen) is 264392, which matches BE's fixed value in limits.sthlp note 2. Two n(10000) function layers with name() were refused r(134) with '408,235 ... more than the 264392'; twoway gave rc 0, the one-line form rc 0, and maxvar 5000 rc 0. Code at dash.ado:11366-11377 prints c(macrolen) unformatted and advises 'raise c(macrolen) with set maxvar', which BE cannot do. Help dash.sthlp:3713-3714 says '645,200 by default ... set maxvar raises it', which is untrue on BE. Also note that dash caps n() at 10000 where twoway has no upper bound (dash.sthlp:3716-3717). That is documented, and it is a separate deliberate difference. Minor is right.
- **PT-5** Windows, Linux, Intel macOS and GUI Stata never run; OS-specific branches checked only by simulation — major, tracked-open, confirmed, effort XL, tracked: ROADMAP 'Deferred: the platform package (P1.34.6)' (M4, S02, S04, S12, S25, S34, S41-S57); GAPS3 I1; BUGS.md P1.34 residual
  - twoway: twoway is supported on Windows, macOS (Intel and Apple silicon) and Linux, in the GUI and on the console.
  - dash: Only console StataNow on macOS arm64 has run dash. Code that exists for other platforms but has never run there: the browser opener (start "" "path" through Stata's shell/cmd.exe; xdg-open), the os.name=='nt' private-file branches (O_NOFOLLOW, getuid and fchmod missing), venv Scripts\python.exe, cmd.exe quoting, c(mode) under Windows /e or /b, the serve lifecycle and parent-watch (POSIX-signal tests are skipped on win32), the processor hint on non-Apple-silicon machines, and the case rules of the scheme walk. The cert and pytest runners are POSIX sh, so the suites cannot run on Windows as they stand.
  - evidence: dash.ado:10146-10240 (opener, tested only with os()/dryrun: SB-13..30); dash_render.py:3561-3570, 3647-3655, 3771-3787 (nt branches; test_private_files.py:1040, 1056, 1334, 1853-1912 monkeypatch os.name); test_serve_lifecycle.py:364, 394, 1048 skip on win32; scripts/stata_batch.sh accepts only stata/stata-mp/se/be/ic console names; dash.sthlp:2521, 2722, 2890, 2922 '(measured on macOS; Linux and Windows have not been measured)'.
  - fix: Port the runners (Windows batch is /e), then run make cert, the parity suite and the browser tests on Windows, Linux and Intel macOS, and in GUI Stata.
  - verifier: Confirmed tracked: ROADMAP.md:5057-5070 (the deferred platform package lists Linux, Windows, Intel macOS and GUI; S04, S12, S25, S34, S41-S57); BUGS.md:3260 P1.34 residual. Code exists but has never run on those platforms: dash.ado:10115-10200 (start/xdg-open opener, tested only through os()), dash_render.py:3598, 3639, 3709, 3735 and 3764 (os.name=='nt' branches), test_serve_lifecycle.py:364, 394 and 1048 (skipif win32), scripts/stata_batch.sh:19 (console names only; POSIX sh runners). Windows is the largest Stata platform, so major stands.
- **PT-6** saving() with ~ on Windows: dash reads HOME and does not expand ~\ — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Stata for Windows understands ~ as the home directory, documented with the form ~\mydir\file ([U] 11.6.2), so twoway saving("~\dir\g.gph") writes into the user's home.
  - dash: dash expands only a leading '~/', and only through the HOME variable. Windows usually does not set HOME, so saving("~/x.html") is refused r(198) 'HOME is not set'. saving("~\dir\x.html") is not expanded at all: it is treated as a path relative to the working directory, and _dash_abs_path does not count it as absolute.
  - evidence: dash.ado:756-763; dash_parse.mata:1455-1475 (_dash_abs_path: '~' is absolute only off Windows); u.pdf section 11.6.2. Not run, because no Windows machine was available.
  - fix: Expand ~ as Stata does on each OS: USERPROFILE or Stata's own expansion on Windows, accepting ~\ and ~/. Add a cert case.
  - verifier: [U] 11.6.2 (u.pdf) says Stata understands ~ as the home directory, with ~\mydir\mydata.dta on Windows and ~/ on Mac and Unix. dash.ado:756-763 expands only a leading '~/' and only from HOME, refusing r(198) when HOME is unset. dash_parse.mata:1455-1475 (_dash_abs_path) treats '~' as absolute only off Windows, so ~\ is left relative to the working directory and r(filename) is wrong. Confirmed from code and the manual. Not run on Windows, so whether HOME is unset depends on the user's environment.
- **PT-7** The cell cap counts every observation even when if/in keep a few rows, refusing pages that would be small — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, partly, effort M, tracked: untracked (the limits are WP-19; the if/in over-count is in no BUGS entry)
  - twoway: twoway scatter y x if _n<=100 on 3,000,000 observations: rc 0. On 11,000,000 observations: rc 0 in 0.74 s.
  - dash: dash scatter y x if _n<=100 (and in 1/100) on 3,000,000 observations is refused r(134): 'would embed 9,000,000 values (3,000,000 observations x 3 variables)'. The qualifier column counts as a variable. Yet the saved page holds only the selected rows: on 1,000,000 observations the same command writes a 1,645,141-byte page, against 47,792,219 bytes for all rows. This contradicts the help's statement that the limit 'counts only what the saved page will actually embed'.
  - evidence: probe1.do: P2 twoway rc=0, P2 dash if rc=134, in rc=134. probe2.do: Q1 size 1645141, Q2 size 47792219. Code: dash.ado:5247-5254 (nobs defaults to _N), dash.ado:11720-11727 and 13170-13176 (callers pass no selected count). Help: dash.sthlp:3013-3016 versus :2947-2957. BUGS.md P0.11 (2870-2872): the page embeds only selected rows.
  - fix: On the saved route, count the rows the embed plan selects (the qualifier-selected rows) instead of _N, or evaluate the qualifier before the cap. Fix the help to say what the cap counts.
  - verifier: The measurement is confirmed (probe1.log: 3M obs, 'if _n<=100' and 'in 1/100' refused r(134) at 9,000,000 values, twoway rc 0; probe2.log: the selected-row page is 1,645,141 bytes against 47,792,219, and my check of q1.html shows only selected rows embedded). But this is deliberate and documented, not an untracked contradiction. dash.ado:5226-5229 says the export writes every observation and 'if/in reduce nothing here and are not offered as a remedy'. dash.sthlp:2950-2953 says the cap counts 'qualifier columns times the observations', and :3048-3050 says outright 'A saved page may hold fewer observations than the limit counts'. The export, and its Stata and Python memory, does scale with _N. So the 'contradicts the help' part is refuted, but the general sentence at :3013-3016 is loose wording. $dash_max_cells up to 20M admits this case (9M cells). It is a sub-case of PT-8's resource-limit difference: minor.
- **PT-8** The 20M-cell hard ceiling refuses data twoway draws (about 10M obs for one y and one x) — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: deliberate (ROADMAP WP-19 resource limits); absent from the help's Differences section
  - twoway: twoway draws any number of observations: 11,000,000 rows with nodraw takes 0.74 s.
  - dash: Above 20,000,000 cells, dash refuses r(134) at any setting: 11M observations x 3 columns is refused even with dash_max_cells 20000000, and dash_max_cells 30000000 is itself refused r(198). The default cap is 5,000,000 cells, which is about 2.5M observations for one y and one x, or 1.67M with a qualifier. These limits are listed under 'Resource limits' but not under 'Differences from twoway'.
  - evidence: probe1.do: P3 rc=134, P3b rc=198, P3 twoway rc=0. dash_render.py:2360-2362 (_DASH_LIMITS cells 5M/20M, bytes 50M/500M); dash.sthlp:2947-2966; Differences section dash.sthlp:809+ has no entry for this.
  - fix: Either draw large data (for example aggregate or subsample above the ceiling with a note, or decimate as Bokeh supports) or state the limit in 'Differences from twoway' and qualify the drop-in claim.
  - verifier: probe1.log: 11M obs refused r(134) at dash_max_cells 20000000; 30000000 refused r(198); twoway rc 0. dash_render.py:2360-2361 (_DASH_LIMITS cells 5M/20M). Resource limits are documented (dash.sthlp:2947-2960), but 'Differences from twoway' (dash.sthlp:808ff) mentions only the registry limit (line 889-890), not the cells, plots or by() limits. Major stands: data of a few million rows is common, and twoway draws it.
- **PT-9** Plot-count limit is per graph, not twoway's 100 variables per plot; wrong rc and message — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: A plot may hold at most 100 variables, x included (limits.sthlp:104-105). twoway scatter y1-y100 x and y1-y101 x are refused r(103) 'too many variables specified'; scatter y1-y99 x gives rc 0; (scatter y1-y60 x) (scatter y61-y120 x) gives rc 0.
  - dash: dash accepts scatter y1-y100 x. It refuses y1-y101 x with r(134) and its own message. It refuses (scatter y1-y60 x) (scatter y61-y120 x) with r(134) (120 plots, more than 100), although twoway draws it. With $dash_max_plots 200 it draws scatter y1-y120 x, which twoway refuses.
  - evidence: probe6.do: PL twoway rc=103, PL dash rc=134, PL100 twoway rc=103. probe7.do: P99 twoway rc=0, P120 twoway rc=0, P120 dash rc=134, P120b dash rc=0. Code: dash_render.py:2364; dash.ado:5204-5219 (_dash_plot_cap_refuse).
  - fix: Refuse more than 100 variables in one plot with r(103) 'too many variables specified', as twoway does. Make the per-graph limit a resource limit that does not refuse what twoway draws by default, or raise its default.
  - verifier: limits.sthlp:104 ('# of variables in a plot 100'). probe6/probe7 logs: twoway refuses y1-y100 x and y1-y101 x with r(103) 'too many variables specified', accepts y1-y99 x, and accepts (scatter y1-y60 x)(scatter y61-y120 x). dash refuses the latter r(134), and draws y1-y120 x under $dash_max_plots 200. My own probe (platform-verify/v1.log) confirms the previously unmeasured case: dash scatter y1-y100 x gives rc 0, while twoway gives r(103). Code: dash.ado:5204-5219 (_dash_plot_cap_refuse, r(134)); no per-plot 100-variable check exists. Untracked. Minor.
- **PT-10** by() draws at most 100 panels by default (1,000 at most); twoway draws all — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: deliberate (WP-19 Resource limits); not in Differences
  - twoway: twoway scatter y x, by(g) with 120 groups: rc 0, all 120 panels.
  - dash: dash prints a note and draws only the first 100 groups in sort order. $dash_max_by_groups raises the limit, but never past 1,000.
  - evidence: probe6.do: B1 twoway rc=0; dash note 'by(g) has 120 groups, more than the 100 one graph draws; the first 100 in sort order are drawn'. dash_render.py:2367; dash.sthlp:2978-2980.
  - fix: Raise or remove the default (keep the ceiling as protection), or list it under Differences.
  - verifier: probe6.log: twoway by(g) with 120 groups gives rc 0; dash notes 'the first 100 in sort order are drawn' with rc 0. dash_render.py:2366 (by_groups 100/1000); dash.sthlp:2976-2978. It is not in the Differences section. It is deliberate (WP-19) and minor.
- **PT-11** Python 3.11+ with bokeh, numpy>=2.3 and pandas>=2.3 is required; twoway needs nothing — minor, ecosystem, partly, effort S, tracked: documented in dash.sthlp Dependencies; not in Differences
  - twoway: twoway works on any installation with no outside software.
  - dash: Every dash command except clear refuses r(198) unless the linked Python is 3.11 or newer and has numpy, pandas and bokeh (3.8 to 3.9 verified). The numpy and pandas floors date from June 2025. An installed package below its floor (for example an older Anaconda base) is reported and deliberately not upgraded, so dash stays blocked until the user upgrades numpy or pandas in their analysis environment, which can affect other packages there. dash_setup does not install Python, and the help says nothing about conda.
  - evidence: dash.ado:338-406 (_dash_require_deps); dash_setup.ado:64-69 (minimums and ceilings), 439-507 (below minimum: reported, not upgraded); dash.sthlp:6709-6719; README.md:34. The maintainer's own Stata Python is Anaconda 3.13.9 with bokeh 3.8.0, numpy 2.3.5 and pandas 2.3.3, so a conda base on macOS is exercised.
  - fix: State the requirement in the drop-in claim and in 'Differences from twoway'. Consider documenting conda (conda install of bokeh/numpy/pandas) and a dash_setup, venv() default for environments that cannot be upgraded.
  - verifier: Partly wrong. dash is not blocked by an old numpy or pandas. _dash_require_deps (dash.ado:351-400) checks only that numpy, pandas and bokeh are present and that Python is 3.11 or newer. dash_render.py:186-210 (_dash_dep_versions) notes an old numpy or pandas, and dash 'goes on', as dash.sthlp:6752-6753 says. Only a bokeh older than 3.8 or a missing package blocks dash. dash_setup does count an old numpy or pandas as unmet and prints an upgrade line without upgrading (dash_setup.ado:439-507), so 'dash_setup does not complete' is true and 'dash stays blocked' is not. No conda guidance exists: grep finds none in dash.sthlp, dash_setup.sthlp or README. The Python dependency is inherent to the design and documented under Dependencies. The remaining work is documentation (qualify the drop-in claim and add it to Differences), so minor rather than major.
- **PT-12** The hash-pinned lock covers 3.11-3.14 on five platform tags only; nothing helps offline installs — minor, platform-environment, confirmed, effort S, tracked: documented in dash_setup.sthlp (The hash-pinned lock); Python and ARM64 coverage untracked
  - twoway: twoway needs no installation.
  - dash: dash_setup always installs with pip install --only-binary=:all: --require-hashes from dash_requirements.txt. Outside the lock's coverage pip stops. Affected: a Python newer than 3.14 (contourpy is pinned only with python_version 3.11 or 3.12-3.14 markers, yet dash has no Python ceiling and admits 3.15); Windows ARM64 (tzdata is pinned only for platform_machine AMD64, but pandas needs it on win32); glibc or architectures without matching wheels. Offline installs: PIP_FIND_LINKS and PIP_NO_INDEX pass through child_env, but the help does not mention a wheelhouse route.
  - evidence: dash_requirements.txt:6-7 (pythons, platforms), :11, :83, :600; scripts/make_requirements_lock.py:64-92; dash_setup_lib.py:54, 187-193, 432-437; dash_render.py:90 (_DASH_MIN_PYTHON with no maximum); dash_setup.sthlp:91-108 ('On another platform or Python there is no wheel to match and pip stops'). pip was not run (it needs the network).
  - fix: Regenerate the lock when a new Python is released, or have dash and dash_setup refuse or warn above the newest locked Python. Add the win_arm64 target or widen the tzdata marker. Document an offline wheelhouse install (pip download -r dash_requirements.txt, then PIP_NO_INDEX/PIP_FIND_LINKS).
  - verifier: dash_requirements.txt:6-7 (pythons 3.11-3.14; five platform tags), :11 and :83 (contourpy markers stop at 3.14), :600 (tzdata only for win32 AMD64); dash_setup_lib.py:54 (--only-binary=:all:), :430-437 (--require-hashes); no Python ceiling anywhere (grep for pymax or 3.15 finds nothing; dash_render.py:90). dash_setup.sthlp:100-101 already says 'On another platform or Python there is no wheel to match and pip stops; install by hand as below', so the general case is documented. Python 3.15 (due in October 2026) and Windows ARM64 specifically are untracked, as is an offline wheelhouse route. This is an installation matter, not a twoway behaviour: minor.
- **PT-13** Graph export from a do-file has no equivalent — major, tracked-open, confirmed, effort L, tracked: ROADMAP platform package ('A scripted image export', M8); GAPS3 I1
  - twoway: twoway ...; graph export f.png|pdf|svg writes the drawn graph.
  - dash: dash has no scripted image export. After dash, graph export writes whatever Stata graph is in memory (with a note) or fails. The toolbar's PNG/SVG buttons need a browser and a person to click them.
  - evidence: BUGS.md:3212-3220 (P1.28a/P1.28b residual: 'a do-file cannot write an image of a dashboard (the deferred platform package, M8)'); dash.sthlp Exporting section.
  - fix: dash export using file.png|pdf|svg, driven by a headless browser (needs a dependency decision).
  - verifier: BUGS.md:3212-3220: the P1.28a resolution (r(filename) and a note when graph export would write a Stata graph) and P1.28b (a client-side Save-as-SVG button). The scripted export is deferred: ROADMAP.md:5061 'dash export using file.png|pdf|svg' (M8). dash.sthlp:177-180 says graph export does not see a dashboard. 'twoway ...; graph export' is a very common do-file workflow with no dash equivalent, so major.
- **PT-14** Large data: about 6-8 s and 1.4 GB per million rows; the page stores numbers as JSON lists, twice the binary size — minor, platform-environment, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (GAPS3 G3/G4 covered the data file and the carried columns only)
  - twoway: twoway scatter y x with 1,000,000 observations and nodraw: 0.16 s.
  - dash: dash scatter y x with 1M rows took 7.99 s (first command, including Python start) and 5.58 s (warm); with an lfit layer, 5.95 s. The page is 47.8 MB and Stata's peak RSS reached 1.44 GB. In the page, each double column is a JSON list of numbers (20.6 MB per million) and the row id an int list (7.9 MB). Bokeh's base64 form would take about 10.7 MB per million float64. The data file has been binary since WP-32, but the page is not.
  - evidence: probe1.do timer 1/2/3 and sizes; probe2.do RSS0 63.7 MB, RSS1 1438.6 MB, Q2 5.58 s. Page JSON inspection: data_source entries [0] and [1] are 20,630,580- and 20,631,885-character float lists, entry [2] a 7,888,890-character int list. dash_render.py:2271-2279 (_HTML_BYTES_PER_CELL 24).
  - fix: Hand Bokeh numeric columns as numpy arrays so they serialise as base64 ndarrays. Measure browser open time and memory at 1M and at the ceiling.
  - verifier: probe1/probe2 logs: 7.99 s cold, 5.58 s warm, RSS 1,438 MB, page 47.8 MB at 1M rows. I inspected q1.html: the CDS data is a JSON float list ('"entries":[["y",[-0.738101715...'), not an ndarray or base64. dash_render.py:2279 (_HTML_BYTES_PER_CELL 24). Caveats: the twoway figure of 0.16 s is with nodraw, so it is not like for like; the 2x size applies to full-precision doubles, and short values such as integers serialise smaller as JSON. This is a performance and size matter, not drawing or syntax: minor.
- **PT-15** Served child process on Windows: no UTF-8 setting for its pipes — cosmetic, unverified, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked (falls under the platform package's serve lifecycle on Windows)
  - twoway: Not applicable: twoway starts no child process. Stata text is UTF-8.
  - dash: dash serve starts the app with subprocess.Popen(..., text=True) and no encoding, and sets neither PYTHONIOENCODING nor PYTHONUTF8 for the child. dash_setup's children get utf-8. On a Windows ANSI code page (Python 3.11-3.14 do not default to UTF-8 mode), output from the child holding non-cp1252 text, such as a Unicode variable name in a note, could raise UnicodeEncodeError or be garbled.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:73145-73164 (Popen without encoding or env); a grep finds no reconfigure, PYTHONIOENCODING or PYTHONUTF8 in dash_render.py; dash_setup_lib.py:43-44, 201-204 (setup children: PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8, encoding='utf-8'). Not run on Windows.
  - fix: Pass env with PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 (or -X utf8) to the served child and encoding='utf-8' to Popen. Test it on Windows.
  - verifier: The code fact is confirmed: dash_render.py:73161-73164 calls Popen(text=True, errors='replace') with no encoding and no env, and there is no PYTHONIOENCODING or PYTHONUTF8 anywhere in dash_render.py. But the risk is smaller than stated. The app hand-over is binary (_dash_child_stdin writes to proc.stdin.buffer, dash_render.py:72828-72844, and _DASH_BOOT decodes UTF-8 itself). Notes are relayed from the child's stderr, which Python on Windows writes with backslashreplace, and the parent decodes with errors='replace'. So the likely effect is garbled text, not a UnicodeEncodeError, except for non-cp1252 text printed to the child's stdout, which is unverified. It is also serve-only, which this audit's rules exclude unless it changes the drawn graph or the accepted syntax. Not run on Windows.
- **PT-16** 'Works offline in any modern browser' is verified on Chrome only; bokeh 3.10+ unverified — minor, unverified, confirmed, effort M, tracked: ROADMAP platform package (S04, S41: 'Firefox and Safari runs', 'browsers other than Chrome'); GAPS3 I1
  - twoway: Not applicable: twoway's graph window is Stata's own.
  - dash: The page is self-contained (no external script src; CSP default-src 'none', connect-src 'none'). But only headless Chrome has run the browser and CSP tests; Firefox was checked once by hand and Safari never. A user's bokeh at 3.10 or later draws with a note and is unverified.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:172-174; q1.html: no <script src>, CSP header as quoted. BUGS.md:3226 (P1.29 residual 'Firefox was checked once by hand and Safari not at all'); dash_render.py:236-237 (_DASH_BOKEH_CEIL (3, 10)).
  - fix: Run the CSP, browser and accessibility tests in Firefox and Safari (and Edge on Windows). Verify each new bokeh minor and move the ceiling.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:172-174 claims 'works offline in any modern browser'. The BUGS.md P1.29 residual and ROADMAP.md:5064 and 5068 (S04, S41: Firefox and Safari runs, browsers other than Chrome) track it. dash_render.py:237 (_DASH_BOKEH_CEIL (3, 10)): a newer bokeh draws with a note. Output as HTML is documented Difference 1 (dash.sthlp:811-815). Browser coverage affects whether the drawn page is verified, not twoway syntax. Minor.

  Not covered: Windows, Linux, Intel macOS, GUI Stata, Stata/BE, Stata/SE, Stata 19.0 and Stata 18.5 or older: only console StataNow 19.5 MP on macOS arm64 was available. BE was emulated for c(macrolen) only (set maxvar 2048), not for other BE behaviour.; Whether GUI Stata saves the Python it finds into its preferences, which would fill c(python_exec) in later GUI sessions and hide PT-1 there. Only console batch was tested: two sessions saved nothing.; dash_setup's real pip runs (online, offline, behind a proxy, inside conda) were not executed, because they need the network and would change Python environments. This was judged from code and help.; Browser load time and memory for large pages: a headless Chrome --dump-dom timing with a virtual time budget gave unreliable numbers, so none are reported.; Served-mode performance and memory at 1e6 rows. Memory at the 20M-cell ceiling (about 10M observations) was not measured; extrapolated from 1.44 GB at 1M rows it would be several GB.; Python 3.15 and bokeh 3.10 were not available, so the lock gap is inferred from the lock's markers and pip's documented behaviour.; Whether Stata 19 can run on Windows ARM64 natively (affects PT-12's tzdata marker) was not confirmed from Stata's documentation.

### docs-claims (29)

- **DT-02** The help's main claims, 'accepts the same syntax as twoway' and 'type dash where you would type twoway', are false in both directions — major, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Accepts play() with a recording, unknown scheme() (with a note), bin()>1000, n()>10000, jitter()>100, aspectratio()<0, lwidth(-1) on scatter, long or '__' graph names, and __dash* variables. Refuses SC, bw() on scatter/bar, ytitle2(), title("'90s"), kernel(GAUSSIAN), a string colorvar(), and so on.
  - dash: The reverse in every case listed. Probes: P01, P02, P12, P12b, P19, P20, Q02-Q09, Q11, Q12.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:156-158, :181-183, :185-187, :286-289 ('where dash differs, this file says so'); README.md:15-16; dash.pkg:4-9. The individual gaps are DT-13 to DT-25.
  - fix: Reword these lines to state the scope: the twoway grammar, with the exceptions listed under Differences. Or close the gaps they depend on (DT-13 to DT-19).
  - verifier: The unqualified claims are as quoted: dash.sthlp:156-157 and :181-182 ('accepts the same syntax as twoway'), :185-187, and README:15-16. The probe logs reproduce the two-way mismatches (SC, bw(), ytitle2(), title("'90s"), kernel(GAUSSIAN), colorvar(make), unknown scheme(), bin(2000), n(20000), jitter(200), aspectratio(-1), lwidth(-1), __dash variables). The help does hedge: :286-288 ('where dash differs, this file says so') and :994-999 (bounded values). Most items are documented differences; the hedge itself fails on jitter (DT-25) and the stale passages (DT-05 to DT-12). This is a roll-up of DT-01 and DT-13 to DT-25, not a separate code gap; the fix is rewording (S).
- **DT-03** README and dash.pkg say 'Most twoway options are honoured; the rest are accepted and named in a note'. This is stale — minor, doc-claim-false, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: n/a
  - dash: Options twoway refuses are refused (probe P3: xreverse, xcommon, lockaspect, addplot(), margin(), zoom(), xlog on scatter, bargap() on scatter all give r(198) in twoway's words). Values outside dash's own bounds are refused. Options that twoway draws no differently are taken in silence. Only the 52 catchall names, plus some sub-options, are 'accepted and named'. README.md:59 also says help dash lists 'every difference from twoway', which DT-01, DT-14 and DT-25 contradict.
  - evidence: README.md:6-8 and :59-60; dash.pkg:7-9; dash.sthlp:984-991; test/fixtures/catchall_options_truth.json 'options' (52 names); ROADMAP.md:5152
  - fix: Rewrite as: options twoway refuses are refused; the options dash cannot draw are named in a note (help: Unsupported); a few values are bounded. Drop 'every difference' or make it true.
  - verifier: README:6-8 and dash.pkg:7-9 are mostly accurate. Options twoway refuses are not 'twoway options' there, and those taken silently draw what twoway draws, so they count as honoured. The real exceptions are the twoway-valid inputs dash refuses: play() with a recording, unknown scheme(), the bounded values, and lwidth(-1) on scatter. README:59 ('every difference') is false because of DT-25 (jitter is not marked as a difference). DT-14 does not count against it: the help's 'Case' paragraph (dash.sthlp:528-532) documents it.

  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6459-6465 ('so a command copy-pasted from a do-file always runs'); dash.sthlp:994-999 ('runs whenever dash draws its plot type -- Plot types names the two twoway plot types it refuses'); probes Q04, Q05, Q06, Q07, Q11, Q12
  - fix: Narrow both sentences to what is true, or cross-reference the refusals list.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:6461-6463 ('a command copy-pasted from a do-file always runs') is contradicted in the same section by :6667-6683, where play() with a readable recording is r(198).  Correction: :997-999 already excepts the bounded values ('such as bin(2000) ... are refused'), so DT-17 is not a contradiction of that sentence. Unknown scheme(), names, __dash variables and resource limits are still unmentioned exceptions there.

Code: _dash_fold_bw, dash.ado:2125-2145. Correction to the tracking: this is a documented difference (category b), since dash.sthlp:3655-3658 states both the bar-family barwidth() reading and the note elsewhere. It still contradicts ROADMAP.md:5152 ('every option twoway refuses refused').
- **DT-14** Plot-type names are read without regard to case: SC and Hist are drawn where twoway refuses them — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: help 'Case' paragraph (GAPS3 section 7 item 3)

- **DT-15** Forms twoway refuses that dash accepts: title("'90s"), kernel(GAUSSIAN), xsize(()), a string colorvar(), and a colorvar() plot whose if keeps no observation — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: documented in help 'Some values twoway refuses, dash accepts' and the colorvar section
  - twoway: Measured: r(100) for title("'90s") (Q02); 'invalid kernel function', r(198), for kernel(GAUSSIAN) (Q03); r(7) for colorvar(make) (Q09); r(3001) for 'scatter ... if price<0, colorvar(price)' when drawn (Q01). 'option(s) ( ) not allowed' for xsize(()) (from the help).

  - fix: Refuse kernel() in upper case, xsize(()), a string colorvar() and the empty colorvar plot, with twoway's codes. The '90s quote case is deliberate (safety); either emulate twoway's compound-quote failure or keep it documented.
  - verifier: All measured in p2.log: Q02 r(100), Q03 r(198), Q09 r(7), Q01 r(3001) in twoway, and rc 0 in dash in each case. Documented at dash.sthlp:1079-1087, :1251-1260, :5537-5539 and :5575-5576 (category b). Nuance: twoway's empty-colorvar failure is an internal Mata error (_calc_levels() 3001), and only when the graph is drawn (P16 with nodraw gave rc 0), so emulating it is questionable.
- **DT-16** Option spellings of dash's own that twoway refuses — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: help (each spelling is marked 'dash's own')
  - twoway: r(198) for ytitle2() (probe P19), mlg() (probe Q08), and (per the help) the other second-axis spellings, tlabel2() and its siblings, marker(), plotbgcolor()/pbgc(), colv()/cv(), colpalette(), colornlevels(), colormissing(), malpha(), lalpha(), falpha(), jseed(), hover() and table.
  - dash: All are accepted. 'ytitle2() ... is not drawn: no layer' is noted when no layer is on axis 2.

  - fix: None needed for twoway-to-dash pasting, since these are supersets. Synonyms that only duplicate twoway spellings (mlg(), marker(), cv()) could be dropped to keep the accepted syntax identical.
  - verifier: P19 (ytitle2) and Q08 (mlg) show twoway gives r(198) and dash gives rc 0. dash.sthlp:4950-4952 ('twoway refuses all twenty') and :3436 (mlg()) mark these as dash's own spellings. They are a superset, so a pasted twoway command is unaffected.
- **DT-17** Values twoway accepts that dash refuses because of its own bounds — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort M, tracked: help 'Option values' ('Some of those bounds are dash's own')
  - twoway: rc 0 for histogram bin(2000) (P20), scatter jitter(200) (Q06), lfit n(20000) (Q07) and scatter aspectratio(-1) (Q12). Also takes any jitterseed(), contour levels() above 1,000, and the abbreviations of interp(), clwidths(), clpatterns() and colorlines.
  - dash: r(198) with dash's own sentences (P20, Q06, Q07, Q12). Contour levels() above 1,000 is refused. Short spellings of interp(), clwidths(), clpatterns() and colorlines are read as other options.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:1225-1236, :597-602, :3986-3990; dash.ado:3875-3889
  - fix: Remove the bounds on jitter(), aspectratio() and jitterseed(). Raise or cap internally (with a note) instead of refusing bin(), n() and levels(). Match the contour abbreviations to twoway_contour_parse.class.
  - verifier: Measured: P20 bin(2000), Q06 jitter(200), Q07 n(20000) and Q12 aspectratio(-1) are rc 0 in twoway and r(198) in dash. Contour levels() above 1,000 and the full spellings required for interp() (twoway_contour.sthlp:53 documents int:erp), clwidths(), clpatterns() and colorlines are documented at dash.sthlp:594-601. Bounds are documented at :1225-1236 (category b). twoway's jitterseed() takes values of 0 or less and draws a new seed for them (twoway_yxview_parse.class:1089-1091).
- **DT-18** lwidth(), mlwidth() and msangle() are checked on every plot type, and msize(1e400) is refused, where twoway draws — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: help 'dash also refuses some forms twoway reads'
  - twoway: Probe Q05: 'twoway scatter mpg weight, lwidth(-1)' gives rc 0, because twoway checks only where the line is drawn.
  - dash: Probe Q05: twoway's gm_linewidth message plus a line of dash's own, r(198). Separately, lwidth(*40000) is noted and drawn at the default where twoway refuses it.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:1238-1250, :5869-5872
  - fix: Apply the bound only on the types where twoway draws that element (an applicability table like the one for alwidth()), and refuse lwidth(*40000).
  - verifier: Q05: twoway scatter lwidth(-1) gives rc 0, while dash prints gm_linewidth plus its own line and gives r(198). Documented at dash.sthlp:1243-1250 (with msize(1e400)), and lwidth(*40000) at :5869-5872 (category b).
- **DT-19** Refusals and return codes chosen differently from twoway: unknown scheme(), saving() onto an existing file, nodraw with saving(, asis), play() with a recording, graph names, __dash variables, resource limits — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: help Differences 2, Resource limits and Scheme sections; play() is a maintainer ruling (ROADMAP.md:5152)
  - twoway: Unknown scheme: '(note: scheme ... not found, using s2color)', rc 0 (Q04). Existing file: r(602). nodraw with asis: r(601). play() of a readable recording plays it. name() takes any 32-character name. Variables named __dash* are fine (Q11). No size caps.
  - dash: Unknown scheme: r(198) (Q04). Existing file: r(198) with dash's words. nodraw with asis: writes the page. play(): r(198). name(): at most 22 characters, no '__', not _all. __dash* variables: r(110) (Q11). Working columns can give r(900) at c(maxvar). Over $dash_max_cells (5M, ceiling 20M), $dash_max_plots (100), $dash_max_reflines or $dash_max_added_text: r(134). function points must fit c(macrolen).
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6311-6325, :824-830 (dash.ado:876-879), :846-851, :6667-6683, :878-893 (dash.ado:11295-11302), :211-226, :239-243, :2933-2985
  - fix: S: give saving() r(602) in twoway's words; draw s2color with twoway's note for an unknown scheme(); refuse nodraw with asis as r(601). M: rename the working columns to tempnames or a frame so __dash* names are free. The play(), name() and resource-limit rulings would need maintainer reversal.
  - verifier: Each item is documented as a deliberate difference. Q04 shows an unknown scheme() is r(198) where twoway notes s2color (dash.sthlp:6311-6325). Q11 shows __dash* variables give r(110). saving() onto an existing file is r(198) where twoway gives r(602) (:824-830); nodraw with asis writes the page (:846-851); play() is at :6667-6683; names are limited to 22 characters with no '__' (:878-893). play() is a maintainer ruling (ROADMAP:5152). Category b.
- **DT-20** 52 twoway options are still named in a note and not drawn, including the old aliases symbol(), msymangle() and mlabels(), which twoway draws — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort M, tracked: help 'Unsupported Stata twoway options'
  - twoway: Probe R4: 'twoway scatter mpg weight, symbol(T)' draws triangles (marker stylename 'triangle'). The help (:6520-6524) says msymangle() and mlabels() are spellings of msangle() and mlabel(). The bar/area edge styles bcolor(), blwidth(), blpattern(), afcolor() and afintensity(), bartype(spanning), dropto(), dlpattern(), numkeys(), omit*miss, xlog/xtransform() on the fits, subchar(), back/noback, onepanel, heatlabel, zvarlabel and zvarformat are all twoway options.
  - dash: Probe P3: symbol(O), mlabels(make), afcolor(red), omitallmiss, numkeys(2), below and titling each print '(note: dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): ...)' and draw the default.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6501-6566; test/fixtures/catchall_options_truth.json 'options' (52 names; it records acceptance only, not whether twoway draws differently); probe logs p3.log and p4.log
  - fix: S: alias symbol()->msymbol(), msymangle()->msangle(), mlabels()->mlabel(). M: draw bcolor()/blwidth()/blpattern()/afcolor()/afintensity() on the bar, area and range types, bartype(spanning), dropto(), dlpattern() and numkeys(). Measure the rest with graph export SVG, then take in silence what draws the same (as WP-32 did for the pending list).
  - verifier: The fixture holds 52 names (catchall_options_truth.json 'options'). twoway's parser binds Symbol and MSYMANgle to the marker symbol and angle (twoway_yxview_parse.class:595-596, :617-618), and MLabels(string) is the full name of mlabel() itself (:141, :186). So mlabels(make) labels in twoway, and R4 shows symbol(T) draws triangles. P3 shows dash names each of these and draws the default. The list is documented in dash.sthlp:6501-6566 (category b), but contradicts ROADMAP:5152 ('every option twoway draws drawn'). Whether twoway draws bcolor() and the other bar/area styles differently is unmeasured, since the fixture records acceptance only.
- **DT-21** Sub-options named and not drawn — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort L, tracked: help (each named); textfirst is in ROADMAP 'Expected by the index' (M17)
  - twoway: Draws all of these.
  - dash: Named in a note: legend textfirst, stacked, symplacement(), note() and caption() (Q10); axis nogextend, labstyle(), tstyle(), gstyle(), tlstyle() and glstyle(); title and edge-title orientation(), width(), height(), angle(), lstyle() and bl*; text() width(), height(), linegap() and angle(); graphregion istyle/ilstyle/ilcolor/ilwidth/ilpattern/ilalign/lalign; plotregion lalign/ilalign/iintensity/ishadestyle (R1, R2); other by() sub-options and the style(combine) layout; other clegend() sub-options; zlabel ticks and zscale on a discrete key; contourline legend keys; mlalign(outside); %12s, %21x and %16H formats are drawn as %9.0g.

  - fix: Draw each in both renderers (Bokeh Title/Label have no fixed box, angle or stacked legend layout, so some need custom annotations). textfirst has a maintainer ruling (ROADMAP.md:5141, M17).
  - verifier: Q10 shows legend textfirst and stacked are noted. R1 and R2 show plotregion iintensity() and ishadestyle() are noted. R3 shows graphregion iintensity() is silent. The cited help passages document the rest (category b). textfirst is ROADMAP 'Expected by the index' M17.
- **DT-22** An unbinned numeric colorvar() is mapped continuously on markers where twoway bins at the z-axis ticks, and the colour key is drawn differently — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: GAPS3.md section 7 item 4 / help colorvar section
  - twoway: Bins the markers at the z axis's ticks (about 5 colours). The key title runs up the key's right side, and the key has an axis line. A colorvar() plot whose observations are all missing draws the key title alone.
  - dash: 256-step continuous gradient on markers (lines are already binned). The title is drawn above the bar, with no axis line. A '(missing)' entry is drawn for the all-missing case.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5523-5534, :5575-5576 (lines take twoway's levels at :5402-5410); GAPS3.md section 7 item 4 (DISCOVERY Q5)
  - fix: Bin markers at the key's cuts, as lines already are. Turn the key title and draw the axis line.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:5523-5534 says 'How the colours are drawn is dash's': a 256-step continuous map where twoway bins at the z-axis ticks, the title above the key, and no axis line; the all-missing case is at :5575-5576. Lines and the markers of connected/rconnected already take twoway's levels (:5402-5410), so binning scatter markers is in reach. Major is kept because every unbinned numeric colorvar() marker plot draws different colours and a different key. Documented (category b, GAPS3 section 7 item 4).
- **DT-23** Geometry Bokeh draws differently, each documented — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: help (each marked as a difference); BUGS.md:3525 (WP-31 D3) records the side-position choice
  - twoway: Draws as listed in the help for each item.
  - dash: Titles at clock 2-4 or 8-10 are drawn above or below the plot, not beside it. by() labels at 9 and 3 are drawn below. Reference lines outside the plot region are clipped (twoway draws them in the margin). Legend marker keys are drawn at 0.4 of the key box, not the marker's own size. xtick() and xmtick() share one tick length. The plot-region margin is a share of the frame, up to 1.3% off on resize. Relative sizes are fixed at 2.88 pt whatever the graph size. fxsize() and fysize() shrink the cell. The aspect ratio holds only within a height band. A by() graph margin smaller than the scheme's insets nothing. rarea's closing edges are not stroked. graphregion(ifcolor()) fills the whole border. msymbol(arrow) is drawn as a triangle with no shaft.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5339-5346 and :6195-6197; :3343-3345; :5096-5106; :4456-4460; :4867-4871; :4328-4335, :4529, :4636-4641; :5705-5712; :5693-5698; :5719-5722; :3331-3333; :452-455; :4305-4307; :6069-6071
  - fix: Case by case. Side panels for titles, and per-tick lengths, need custom renderers. Legend key marker size, rarea closing edges and the arrow symbol are small fixes.
  - verifier: Each cited passage was spot-checked and each states the difference: titles at the side clock positions :5339-5345, reflines clipped :5096-5106, legend marker key at 0.4 :4456-4460, one minor tick length :4867-4871, margin share :4328-4335, size reference :5705-5712, fxsize/fysize :5693-5698, rarea's closing edges :452-455, ifcolor :4305-4307, arrow symbol :6069-6071. Category b.
- **DT-24** Text and label output that differs from twoway — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: help (each documented)
  - twoway: Draws as listed in the help for each item.
  - dash: A by() panel of a date variable is labelled with the date (twoway writes 14610). A value label with double quotes stays on one line. A date mlabel() variable's value labels are ignored. A pc observation missing only its second point gets no marker or label. Out-of-range mlabangle() on pcarrow still draws the labels. Partial bold/italic/sup markup is drawn in one style. {stSymbol:} is drawn as itself and {col #:text} as typed. Globals holding `=exp' are not evaluated. mcolor(navy*52) uses a different intensity rule. A kdensity/lfit/function-first date axis keeps dates where twoway labels numbers. A log %tc axis is drawn linear. Dates past the browser's range are not drawn. Style files for textboxstyle and color are not read. Missing scheme keys use s2color's value. The scheme directives 'clockdir p', the titles' textboxstyle and legend_col_gap are not drawn.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:3279-3282, :3287, :3515-3517, :3475-3479, :3443-3447, :5170-5215, :783-784, :1290-1293, :4811-4818, :4724-4731, :4820-4822, :6141-6155, :6104-6108, :6366-6370, :6225
  - fix: Most are S-M fixes (date by() label, quoted value labels, date mlabel value labels, pc half-missing rows, scheme directives). Partial markup needs rich-text rendering (L).
  - verifier: Spot-checked and each is documented: date by() labels :3279-3282, quoted value labels :3287, date mlabel value labels :3515-3517, pcarrow mlabangle :3443-3447, partial markup :5170-5180, globals with =exp :782-783, fit-first date axis :4816-4818, log %tc axis :4724-4731, browser date range :4820, style files :6141-6152, scheme fallback :6104-6106, directives read without a note :6366-6370, legend_col_gap :6225. Note that the undrawn scheme directives are kept silently, with no note. Category b.
- **DT-25** jitter() is uniform box noise over each axis's data range from numpy's RNG, where twoway adds spherical noise sized to the graph area from Stata's RNG — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter.sthlp:354-370: 'adds spherical random noise ... # represents the size of the noise as a percentage of the graphical area'; jitterseed() seeds Stata's RNG.
  - dash: Each axis independently gets uniform(-#/2%, +#/2%) of that variable's data range, from numpy default_rng. The same jitterseed() therefore gives other points and another shape and spread, and with no seed the points differ on every render.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:13927-13957 (_apply_jitter); dash.sthlp:5781-5784 describes dash's rule without marking it as a difference
  - fix: Reproduce twoway's spherical noise scaled to the plot area. Exact points would need Stata's RNG output exported at registration, as function() does. At minimum, document it as a difference.
  - verifier: Measured (probe /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/docs-verify/j1.do, graph export SVG, 2,000 points at (5,5), jitter(20)). twoway draws a disc of the same radius on x and y on the page: 324 of the graph's 3,240-unit smaller side, so a diameter of #% of it. No points fall in the corners, and the median radius is 0.76 of the maximum. dash (dash_render.py:13927-13957) adds independent uniform noise of ±#/2% of each variable's data range, a rectangle on the page; a constant variable falls back to a range of 1.0. The help (dash.sthlp:5781-5784) states dash's rule without marking it as a difference, so it is untracked (category c). Correction: 'with no seed the points differ on every render' is not a difference, because twoway also draws a new seed on each draw (twoway_yxview_parse.class:1089-1091).
- **DT-26** Numerical fidelity limits the help documents for the estimators and contour — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: help (documented); contour cap: ROADMAP.md:5141 (D4)
  - twoway: Stata's exact results.
  - dash: lpoly rule-of-thumb bandwidth is about 1% off at degree 6-7 (386.3 vs 390.0); three exact-polynomial cases are drawn differently; above degree 7 a note is printed, and the fit stops at degree 30. fpfitci on a date-time x can choose another model (curves within 5e-4). lpolyci gives a zero-df grid point zero width where Stata leaves it missing. contour keeps the smallest z at a repeated node (twoway's choice follows sortseed), cuts polygon vertices differently, and subsamples above 1,000 points. ndots above 2,000,000 dots are thinned. More than 2,000 ticks keep the default ticks.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:3895-3916, :1180-1185, :3970-3975, :4031-4034, :565-577, :583-586, :3795-3796, :4524-4525
  - fix: Mostly accepted round-off or Bokeh limits. The lpoly high-degree bandwidth and lpolyci df=0 cases could be matched (M).
  - verifier: Documented in the help: lpoly bandwidth 386.3 against 390.0 at degree 6 (dash.sthlp:3901-3905), exact-polynomial cases :3913-3915, fpfit date-time model :3970-3975, contour duplicates and subsample :565-586. Mostly round-off or deliberate caps (category b).
- **DT-27** natscale: axes of computed layers are ticked by dash's fitted model even at build, which the open BUGS entry and the ROADMAP say is exact; the help contradicts itself — minor, tracked-open, partly, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew' (open)
  - twoway: Stata's compiled _natscale/_tsnatscale ticks every axis.
  - dash: At export, Stata's ticks are requested only over the layers' variables. The axes of histogram, kdensity, function, scatteri, pci and pcarrowi, a fit's grid and a widening range() take the model at build (help :4529). The model agrees on 98% of numeric ranges and 58-66% of non-yearly date ranges. After a filter move every axis uses the model (the noted natscale exception). Probes Q13 (kdensity mpg: y 0(.02).08, x 10(10)40) and Q14 (histogram date: x 01jan2001..01jan2002, y 0(.001).003) matched twoway, so the size of the gap is unmeasured.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:41809-41812 (_DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP), :41815-41880; BUGS.md:4139-4143 and ROADMAP.md:5153 ('exact at build'); dash.sthlp:4528 (Stata's own ticks) against :4653-4692 (the model presented as the default, 58-66% for dates)
  - fix: Ask Stata for these axes too: the computed range is known at export for histogram bins, kdensity/function grids and fit grids. Correct the BUGS entry's scope, and reword help :4653-4692 to say the model applies only after a move or on those axes.
  - verifier: The mechanism is right. _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP (dash_render.py:41811-41812) asks Stata for no ticks on histogram, kdensity, function, scatteri, pci or pcarrowi axes, and the table is keyed by range, so those axes and a fit's grid take the model at build. But this is documented at dash.sthlp:4529 ('takes the model at once'), so it is category b, not a contradiction. The BUGS.md open entry and ROADMAP:5153 ('exact at build') are scoped to the three variable-range axes and are true for them. :4653-4692 describes the model without saying where it applies; that is an omission, not a contradiction. The size is unmeasured: Q13 and Q14 matched.
- **DT-28** The output medium itself: HTML instead of a Graph window, and name() and saving() repurposed — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, partly, effort XL, tracked: help Differences 1-2
  - twoway: A Stata graph that graph export, graph save, putdocx and dyndoc can see. saving(fig.png) writes a .gph named fig.png. name() stores the graph in graph memory.
  - dash: An HTML page. saving(fig.png) writes fig.html, with a note. name() registers the graph for dash build, and saving() on a named graph writes nothing. A note about graph export is printed whenever Stata holds a graph.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:172-176, :811-823, :1628-1661
  - fix: None. This is the premise of the package.
  - verifier: The description is accurate (dash.sthlp:172-176, :811-823). But this is the package's premise, and it is excluded from the claim under audit, so it is not a gap to close.
- **DT-29** dash_setup help contradicts itself and dash.sthlp about versions and ceilings — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: n/a
  - dash: venv() installs from the hash-pinned lock (bokeh 3.9.0, numpy 2.4.3, pandas 3.0.1), not 'at their minimums'. dash_setup installs numpy<2.5 and pandas<3.1, but dash.sthlp says 'Only bokeh has a ceiling'.
  - evidence: dash_setup.sthlp:124-126 contradicts :90-94 and :208-213; dash_setup_lib.py:1085-1093 (lock_install); dash_requirements.txt:9, :308, :382; dash.sthlp:6716-6718
  - fix: Fix the venv() sentence. Reconcile the ceiling statements.
  - verifier: dash_setup.sthlp:124-126 says venv() installs 'at their minimums', but :90-94 says every install, the venv() one included, uses the hash-pinned lock at the newest tested versions. dash.sthlp:6716-6718 ('Only bokeh has a ceiling') is at odds with dash_setup.sthlp:208-213 (numpy<2.5, pandas<3.1 installed; only bokeh's ceiling is reported). Not related to the drop-in claim.
- **DT-30** The 0.1.0 CHANGELOG lists behaviour that later work in the same release reversed — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: n/a
  - dash: contour, contourline, rpcap and rpspike are listed as 'refused by name' (now drawn). yaxis(3) is listed as r(198) (axes 1-9 are now taken). '[aw=price+1] is r(198)' (expressions are now taken; help :4150-4160). tmlabel() and tmtick() are listed as 'named until minor ticks are drawn' (now drawn). mlalign() is 'named until drawn' (center and inside are now silent). WP-32 'filed as open BUGS.md entries' (the scheme keys and meta's outline are now closed). TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md's summary still says unknown options are 'accepted and reported as ignored'.

TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:18 ('Unknown option names are normally accepted and reported as ignored') is false since titel() is refused (P04). Release-notes hygiene only.

  Not covered: Help sections 1778-2933 (Layout, Tabs, Linking, Toolbar, Data tables, Filters, KPI, Server Mode, Temporary files) were read only for twoway-parity claims; they are dashboard-only; The drawing differences in DT-23, DT-24 and DT-26 were not checked visually or by probe; they rest on the help text and the closed BUGS resolutions; Whether twoway draws something different for each of the 52 catchall options on each plot type was not measured (the fixture records acceptance on one type); Undocumented internal twoway plot classes reachable as plot types (rbarm, rbspike, patch, normal, ipoint(s), ilabel(s), iarrow, meta_*, rtable) were not probed;  only StataNow 19.5 is installed; No test suites were run (read-only audit); saved-page vs served-app parity was not examined; CHANGELOG sections before 0.1.0 'New', 'Changed behaviour', WP-31 and WP-32 were skimmed by grep only; Probe files are in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/docs/ (p1.do-p4.do and their logs); no repository file was modified

### tracking-tests (23)

- **TR-2** No systematic picture comparison of twoway and dash over a broad corpus such as Stata's own help examples — major, unverified, confirmed, effort L, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Stata's twoway, scatter, line and graph-option help files hold 109 runnable graph-twoway gr_example commands (sysuse datasets).
  - dash: No harness runs them. `git grep gr_example` finds nothing. test/fixtures/twoway_corpus.do (299 probes) checks only return codes and runs only by hand through scripts/extract_rejected_options.do. conformance_corpus.txt (75 lines) checks only that each command registers. test_mode_parity.py compares the saved page with the served app, so dash against dash. About 140 truth fixtures each measure one feature on mostly auto.dta. My one-off harness (probe5/probe6) found: all 109 accepted by both; identical tick and text sets on 47 of 109, most of the rest artefacts of my comparison; and real differences no test catches (TR-3 to TR-6, TR-14, TR-15).
  - evidence: /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/tracking/grex_keep.json, probe5.log (109 of 109 rc 0 in both), ex/tw*.svg and ex/d*.html, compare.py and axes.py.
  - fix: Commit a corpus harness to the cert tier: extract every gr_example (and the manual's examples), run twoway with graph export svg and dash (saved page and served models), and compare tick values and labels, titles, legend keys, glyph counts and extents, and colours, with a known-differences ledger.
  - verifier: `git grep gr_example` finds no harness. The twoway_corpus.do and conformance_corpus.txt checks cover rc and registration only. test_mode_parity compares dash with dash. The 129 *_truth.json fixtures each measure one feature. Nothing compares pictures over a broad corpus. The auditor's 109-example run (grex_keep.json, ex/) caught TR-3 to TR-6, which no committed test catches. That is why major is right for verification of the drop-in claim.
- **TR-3** Default axis ticks on by() panels: twoway draws fewer ticks, dash keeps the un-faceted ticks — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `twoway scatter weight length, by(foreign)` labels x 150 200 250. Without by() it labels 140(20)240. `tw histogram le, discrete by(region, total)` labels x 50(10)80; without by() it labels 55(5)80.
  - dash: Uses the un-faceted ticks on every panel: x FixedTicker 140(20)240 and 55(5)80.
  - evidence: Probe probe7, iso cases 4-6: without by() twoway and dash agree; with by() they differ. Stata's own examples affected (probe6): twoway_histogram.sthlp (2), twoway_kdensity.sthlp (2), twoway_lpoly.sthlp and twoway_lpolyci.sthlp (by(foreign)). The by() tick fix (BUGS.md '#N ticks miss twoway's ... by() panels') was measured only with an explicit #N: every by() row of natscale_axes_truth.json carries ylabel(#N). by_scales_truth's by(foreign) rows use mpg weight, where both tick sets happen to agree.
  - fix: Measure the tick count twoway uses on default by() panel axes (as a function of panel count, size and iscale) and ask _natscale with that count (dash_render.py _dash_common_count_ticks:47831). Add truth rows without #N.
  - verifier: Re-checked iso case 6: twoway labels x 150 200 250 on by(foreign); dash labels 140(20)240. My own probe (v1.do V4) gives the same result for by(foreign, cols(1)): twoway 150 200 250, dash 140(20)240. So the reduced count does not depend on the panel layout. Cause: dash_render.py:40785-40801 _dash_by_numticks lowers only the y count on by() panels (out['y']=ny-1). It was fitted on rows where x was weight, and weight gives 2000(1000)5000 at #4 and #5 alike. The x axis stays at 5. V3 (length on y) agrees, 150 200 250, because y already gets n-1. Fix is S, not M: apply the n-1 rule to x1, keeping x2/y2 at the scheme count (f02), and add truth rows without #N. Untracked: BUGS 'by() panels' entry 2457 was measured with ylabel(#5) only.
- **TR-4** A time-series-operator y variable breaks the date axis's Stata ticks — major, wrong-output, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `twoway area d.gnp96 date` (twoway_area.sthlp's example) and `twoway line d.gnp96 date` label x 1970q1 1980q1 1990q1 2000q1.
  - dash: Ticks the DatetimeAxis at 1967q2, 1976q1, 1984q4, 1993q3 and 2002q2: evenly spaced from the data's minimum to its maximum, 35 quarters apart. `area gnp96 date`, with no operator, agrees with twoway.
  - evidence: probe7 iso cases 0-2: FixedTicker ticks [-86918400000, 189302400000, 465436800000, 741484800000, 1017619200000] ms against twoway's 1970q1(40)2000q1. The operator column is made by _dash_tsop_gen (dash.ado:8112); Stata's ticks are requested by _dash_stata_ticks_gen (dash.ado:7424).
  - fix: Pass the rows of the operator-generated columns to the _tsnatscale request, so the axis takes Stata's own date ticks. Pin it in ts_operator_truth.
  - verifier: The measurement is confirmed (iso 1-2: dash ticks 1967q2..2002q2 in 35-quarter steps where twoway labels 1970q1(40)2000q1). The diagnosis and fix are wrong, and the scope is wider. My probe V1, `line y date` with a plain y missing only in obs 1 and no operator, gives the same wrong dash ticks while twoway gives 1970q1(40)2000q1. tsline d.gnp96 (V2) does too. The time-series columns exist before the request (dash.ado:11789 runs before :11821). The cause: _dash_axis_ticks_stata (dash.ado:~7460-7520) summarises the x variable over every marked row, including rows whose y is missing. Stata's range starts at 1967q1, the drawn range at 1967q2, so _dash_stata_pick (dash_render.py:41708) finds no match and the date fallback draws linspace-like ticks. Any missing value at the start or end of a date series hits this, and an operator always creates one. Fix (S): summarise only rows where all of the layer's variables are nonmissing (twoway's omit rule), or match on the drawn rows. Pin it in ts_operator_truth and add a plain-missing case.
- **TR-5** qfitci with stdf overlaid with scatter: twoway labels y 10(10)40, dash 0(10)40 — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (possibly related to BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew')
  - twoway: `twoway qfitci mpg weight, stdf || scatter mpg weight` (in twoway_qfitci.sthlp, twoway_pcarrowi.sthlp and twoway_scatteri.sthlp) labels y 10 20 30 40. qfitci alone labels 0 to 40.
  - dash: Labels y 0 10 20 30 40 in both cases.
  - evidence: probe6 examples 85, 94, 95 and 105; probe7 iso case 3 (qfitci alone agrees). The cause is not established. The help says an axis whose drawn range comes from a fit's band or grid 'takes the model at once' (dash.sthlp:4529), so this may be the natscale model's known quirk (BUGS.md open entry) applied when the page is built.
  - fix: Measure twoway's y range and tick request for the overlay; if the model is the cause, ask Stata's _natscale for axes that span computed ranges when the page is built.
  - verifier: Re-read ex/tw94.svg against ex/d94.html: twoway labels y 10 20 30 40 (with the axis running below 10), dash labels 0(10)40. Alone, qfitci agrees (iso 3: 0..40 in both). Plausible mechanism: qfitci is not in _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP (dash_render.py ~41812). Its band widens the drawn range past the variables' range, so Stata's embedded ticks never match and the model ticks the widened range. twoway instead labels over a range that leaves the band out in this order of plots. Cause not proven; the medium confidence stands.
- **TR-6** yvarlabel() on function is not used for the legend key — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (GAPS3 D8 closed)
  - twoway: twoway_function.sthlp's example `scatter open close, msize(*.25) mcolor(*.6) || function y=x, range(close) yvarlab("y=x")` draws the legend key 'y=x'.
  - dash: Draws the key 'y', with no note.
  - evidence: probe6 example 58: LegendItem values ['Opening price','y']; headless-Chrome screenshot ex/shot_d58.png. The help says the legend keys take yvarlabel() (dash.sthlp:4761-4767). GAPS3 D8 is closed.
  - fix: Apply yvarlabel() to the function layer's legend key and y title in both modes. Add a test on this example.
  - verifier: In dash_render.py:58117-58118 the function branch returns _dash_function_yname(...) and never reads _yvl. The fits, ranges and default branches do read yvarlabel(). ex/d58.html LegendItem shows 'y'; tw58.svg shows 'y=x'. This contradicts dash.sthlp:4761-4767.
- **TR-7** histogram accepts mutually exclusive scale options that twoway refuses — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `histogram mpg, density fraction` (and the pairs fraction percent, density frequency, frequency percent) gives r(198) "options density fraction may not be combined".
  - dash: rc 0: the page is drawn, with no note.
  - evidence: probe8 h1-h4 (tw 198, dash 0). value_truth.json histogram_freqpct_1 records twoway's r(198). No check exists in dash.ado (only discrete with bin(), dash.ado:4793).
  - fix: Refuse any two of density, fraction, frequency and percent in twoway's words.
  - verifier: In probe8.log (h1-h4) twoway gives r(198) 'options density fraction may not be combined' and the other pairs; dash gives rc 0 and saves the page. dash.ado has no such check: only discrete with bin() (:4846) and width with bwidth (:2115). value_truth.json histogram_freqpct_1 records twoway's refusal. Untracked, and the help does not mention it.
- **TR-8** A non-numeric word in an axis label or tick option is noted and drawn, where twoway refuses it — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: xlabel(abc), ylabel(abc), xtick(abc), ytick(abc), xmlabel(abc), ymlabel(abc), xmtick(abc) and ymtick(abc) give r(198) "invalid label specifier, : abc:".
  - dash: rc 0 with "(note: dash: xlabel() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: abc)", and the graph is drawn.
  - evidence: probe2 and probe3 (8 of 8 accepted on the one-line route). _dash_axlabel_refusal (dash_parse.mata:2326) refuses only a label with no value, a second rule and an unfinished rule. The help claims that in the axis labels and ticks 'what twoway refuses is refused with its return code and words' (dash.sthlp Differences, item 5).
  - fix: Refuse a head word that is neither a number, a rule nor a quoted label, with twoway's line.
  - verifier: The difference is real: value_truth.json xlabel_1 and the other seven rows give twoway's r(198) 'invalid label specifier, : abc:'. probe3 shows dash notes 'abc' and draws. _dash_axlabel_refusal (dash_parse.mata:2335-2392) does not refuse a bare word. Classification correction: this is documented (b), not untracked. dash.sthlp:4522-4523 says 'a word that is no value, form, rule or label is named in the note'. The documented rule contradicts dash.sthlp:1134 'A value is refused where twoway refuses it'.
- **TR-9** Bad option values are refused with r(198) where twoway uses another return code — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort M, tracked: DROPIN-REMAINING.md P2.64, never carried into GAPS3 §4/§9; no BUGS.md entry
  - twoway: base(abc) gives r(4026). histogram bin(0|abc|2.5|-3) gives r(125), r(121) and r(126). width(-1|abc|0) gives r(125) and r(121). start(abc) gives r(121). mlabel(make..) gives r(101) "factor-variable and time-series operators not allowed". rbar barwidth(.) gives r(199). mlabangle(1e308) gives r(111).
  - dash: r(198) for every one, mostly in dash's own words ("dash: base() must be a number (got: abc)", ".. not allowed in option mlabel()").
  - evidence: probe2 ran all 81 twoway refusals in test/fixtures/value_truth.json through dash: 25 get a different rc. 10 are these refusals; the other 15 are accepted (TR-7, TR-8, and the dash-only spellings xlabel2() and ylabel2()). probe1 adds base(abc), rbar barwidth(.) and mlabel(make..). Tests pin dash's code: test_option_validation.cscript OV-42 (lines 71 and 757: rc 198) and test_varlist_expansion.cscript:1383 PS-06, which is labelled 'in twoway's order' but gives r(198) where twoway gives r(101).

Ten are wrong-rc refusals: base_1 4026, bin_1/2/3/6 125/121/126/125, width_1/2/3 125/121/125, start_1 121, mlabangle_5 111. probe1 adds rbar barwidth(.) 199 and mlabel(make..) 101, all r(198) in dash. DROPIN-REMAINING.md:320 P2.64 lists base(abc), barwidth(.) and mlabel(make..) and is not mapped in GAPS3 or BUGS. Partly documented: dash.sthlp:1144-1146 states dash's one-line r(198)/r(111) refusal form. function base(abc) already gives r(4026) (dash.ado:6758), so the fix exists for one type only. OV-42 and PS-06 pin dash's code.

- **TR-11** The twoway value oracle is never compared with dash probe by probe — minor, unverified, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: test/fixtures/value_truth.json holds twoway's rc and message for 311 value probes.
