  - verifier: The only reader of value_truth.json is test/test_option_validation.py:1395-1510 (TestTheOracleFixtureIsInStep). Its guards are structural: every rule has a probe, and a refusing kind has some twoway refusal. No test asserts dash's rc or first line probe by probe. test_option_validation.cscript pins dash's own codes (OV-42 rc 198).
- **TR-12** Open BUGS.md entry: the natscale model (three misses of 264 axes) still applies, and its scope is understated — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew' (open)
  - twoway: Stata's compiled _natscale ticks.
  - dash: A fitted model that agrees on 261 of 264 measured axes and 2,829 of 2,878 random ranges. The entry grades it S3, 'only after a move or without Stata's embedded ticks', but the help says axes whose drawn range is computed (histogram, kdensity, function, a fit's grid or band, a widening range()) take the model when the page is built (dash.sthlp:4529). So the model also decides some ticks on the initial graph.
  - evidence: BUGS.md:4139; test/test_natscale.py _KNOWN and test_the_three_known_misses_are_still_missed; dash.sthlp:4528-4529; TR-5 may be an instance.
  - fix: Ask Stata's _natscale when the page is built for axes spanning computed ranges (the extents are known in Python), so only post-move ticks rest on the model; restate the entry's scope.
  - verifier: BUGS.md:4139 grades the entry S3 'only after a move or without Stata's embedded ticks'. But dash.sthlp:4529 says axes whose drawn range is computed take the model at once. dash_render.py _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP leaves histogram, kdensity and function out of the Stata request, and fit bands widen ranges so no match is found (TR-5). The model therefore decides some initial-graph ticks too, and the entry's scope is understated. The three known misses are exact at build (test_stata_ticks.py).

ROADMAP.md:5141 lists 'contour above the cap (subsample, D4)' as expected residual. sandstone (6,400 points) is beyond the cap, so every Stata contour and contourline help example is approximate.

- **TR-15** jitter() spread differs from twoway's: dash adds per-axis uniform noise over the data's range — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway adds 'spherical random noise ... as a percentage of the graphical area' (scatter.sthlp:354-363; yxview.class gm_jitter). Measured, jitter(10) jitterseed(1) on two point clusters: spread about 160 x 146-156 SVG units in a 4,793 x 2,653 plot region, so close to equal on both axes; xscale(range(-4 5)) doubles the x spread to 321.
  - dash: _apply_jitter (dash_render.py:13927-13957) adds independent uniform noise with half-width pct x the data's range / 2 on each axis, from numpy's generator. The spread therefore follows the plot's aspect ratio (here about 3x twoway's in x), ignores the axis range, and jitterseed() cannot reproduce twoway's points. The help describes dash's rule (dash.sthlp:5781-5792) without marking it as a difference.
  - evidence: probe1: j0.svg, j10.svg, j10wide.svg, measured with a circle-centre parse. No truth fixture measures jitter's geometry.
  - fix: Measure twoway's jitter magnitude and shape and reproduce it in graph units; document that the random generator differs.
  - verifier: _apply_jitter (dash_render.py:13927-13957) adds independent uniform noise of pct*range/2 per axis in data units with numpy's RNG, and every jitter site calls it. twoway's jitter is a gmetric (yxview.class:48 'jitter amount, gmetric'; scatter.sthlp:354-358 'spherical random noise ... percentage of the graphical area'). Re-measured the auditor's SVGs: jitter(10) spreads about 160 x 146-156 units, and xscale(range(-4 5)) gives 321 x 146. dash's screen spread follows the plot's aspect ratio instead. dash.sthlp:5781 describes dash's rule without marking it as a difference. No jitter geometry truth fixture exists.
- **TR-16** Stata-graph integration: name(), saving(), nodraw, graph export, graph combine and play() — minor, deliberate-difference, partly, effort XL, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences items 1-2 (documented)
  - twoway: name() stores the graph in graph memory for graph export, save, combine and display. saving() writes a .gph file; an existing file is r(602). nodraw with saving(f, asis) gives r(601). `graph` before a word that is not a subcommand gives r(4023). play() replays Graph Editor recordings.
  - dash: name() registers a dashboard item. graph export, save, combine, putdocx and dyndoc do not see a dashboard. saving() writes HTML, and an existing file is r(198). nodraw with saving() writes the page. `graph` is r(198) 'unrecognised plot type'. A second name() is refused where twoway gives r(110). A readable recording in play() is refused. So a do-file that follows twoway with graph export or graph combine is not drop-in.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:174-180, 816-856, 870-880, and the play() paragraph near 6650.
  - fix: An image-export companion (GAPS3 I1 'scripted image export'), or state this limit beside the 'type dash where you would type twoway' claim.
  - verifier: Documented and inherent to the output medium. dash.sthlp:176-180, right after the claim, says graph export, save, putdocx and dyndoc do not see a dashboard. Differences items 1-2 and the saving/nodraw paragraphs (:812-852) cover name(), the saving() r(198) vs r(602) and nodraw. One bullet is wrong: dash.ado:107-118 refuses `dash graph ...` with 'graph is not a twoway plot type', r(198). That is exactly what `twoway graph ...` gives, so under the dash=twoway mapping it is no difference; the r(4023) case compares against `graph foo`. The cited 'unrecognised plot type' wording comes from stale help, dash.sthlp:854-858 (see TR-21). Remaining work is mainly a README qualifier beside 'dash takes the place of twoway' (README:15-16), unless an image-export companion is wanted. So minor, not major.

### silent-divergence (23)

- **SD-1** histogram width() or discrete needing more than 1000 bins is redrawn with other bins (width() ignored, or discrete squeezed into 1000 bars) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway__histogram_gen tabulates the bins that hold observations and draws only those (twoway__histogram_gen.ado:205-216). On auto: `histogram price, discrete` draws 74 bars at the prices, each as wide as the smallest gap; `width(1)` draws 74 bars 1 wide; `width(10)` draws 68 bars 10 wide. No limit applies.
  - dash: _MAX_BINS = 1000 counts every bin across the range, empty ones included. discrete: 'needs 12616 bins ... using 1000 bins', so the bars come out about 12.6 wide and nearby values merge. width(1) and width(10): 'would need more than 1000 bins; deriving the width from the data instead', so dash draws the default 8 bins and drops the requested width. rc 0 with a note.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:22508 (_MAX_BINS=1000), :22539-22546 (discrete cap), :22566-22576 (width() thrown away); dash.ado:3696 (width=pos, no cap in Stata). Probe p1/p2: twoway__histogram_gen r(n_x) gives 74, 74 and 68 bins; dash prints both notes. The help does not mention this cap: dash.sthlp:3990-3995 says discrete draws 'one bar per distinct value', and the Option values section bounds only bin() at 1000.
  - fix: Bin into the occupied bins only, which number at most N (floor((v-start)/width) with np.unique), as twoway does, in Python and in the saved-page JS twin. Then drop the 1000 cap for width() and discrete, or refuse up front with a documented message. Add a truth test for price discrete, width(1) and width(10).
  - verifier: I called GlyphMapper._histogram_grid directly on auto's prices. discrete gives 1000 bins of width 12.6155 with the note 'needs 12616 bins'. width(1) and width(10) each give 8 bins of width 1576.9 with the note 'deriving the width from the data'. twoway__histogram_gen.ado:205-216 tabulates only the occupied bins, and the p1/p2 logs show 74, 74 and 68 bins. The cap is not in dash.sthlp: :3989-3995 bounds only bin() and says discrete draws 'one bar per distinct value'. It is partly tracked: TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:98 still lists 'maximum 1,000 bins'. I lowered the severity to minor because it only happens when range/width is over 1000 and a note is printed. The fix proposed (bin only the occupied cells) is sound.
- **SD-2** by(, compact) and by(, style(compact)) leave out twoway's black plot-region and panel-label outlines — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: bygraphstyle-compact.style sets special_options "subtitle(, box astextbox blstyle(foreground)) plotregion(lstyle(foreground))". Every panel's plot region gets a black outline, and so does its label box. Measured: F_tw.svg has stroke #000000 width 5.51 rects around both panels and both labels; without compact these are #FFFFFF and the label has no outline.
  - dash: compact only zeroes the gap between panels (_dash_by_spacing) and turns edge labels off. Panel labels keep the scheme's by_label_outline and plot regions get no outline. The pages for by(foreign, compact) and by(foreign) have the same colour sets (outline_line_color #ffffff only).
  - evidence: /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/style/bygraphstyle-compact.style (special_options; diff against bygraphstyle-default.style); dash_render.py:36801 (compact = margins only), :56100-56105 (label outline is always by_label_outline), :36350-36353; no reader of the compact special_options anywhere (only :36431, for style(rescale)). Probe p1 case F: F_tw.svg vs F0_tw.svg rects; F.html vs F0.html colour counts are the same. bystyle_truth.json measures combine, econ, rescale and stata7, but not compact.
  - fix: Under compact or style(compact), expand the style's special options as _DASH_BYSTYLE_EXPAND does for rescale: plotregion(lstyle(foreground)) and subtitle(, box blstyle(foreground)). Add a bystyle truth row for compact.
  - verifier: bygraphstyle-compact.style:39 has special_options 'subtitle(, box astextbox blstyle(foreground)) plotregion(lstyle(foreground))'. _DASH_BYSTYLE_EXPAND (dash_render.py:36420) has no compact entry. Compact only zeroes the margins (:36801) and clears edgelabel (:55249). I rendered by(foreign, compact), by(foreign, style(compact)) and by(foreign) in s2color through the test helpers: every panel has outline #ffffff and label box #d9e6eb, the same in all three. twoway draws black outlines under compact. The repo's own fixture shows this (by_remainder_truth.json bystyle_compact: plot region and label box stroked #000000 at 4.61, against #ffffff and #d9e6eb under default), and so does p1's stcolor F_tw.svg. No test reads bystyle_compact. dash.sthlp:3307 and TWOWAY_COVERAGE_AUDIT.md:146 present compact as implemented.
- **SD-3** Outline alignment: twoway draws outlines inside the shape and honours lalign()/mlalign() on closed shapes; dash always centres them and says the picture is the same — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (help claims parity)
  - twoway: s2color sets 'alignstroke p inside' (and inside for plotregion, legend and histogram). A bar's or marker's outline is drawn inside its edge. lalign(outside) or lalign(center) moves it. Measured on bar mpg x, lwidth(vthick): inside rect x=474.32 w=627.93; outside x=434.00 w=708.57; center x=454.16 w=668.25. Marker outline radius: inside 70.04, outside 110.36, center 90.20. Default scatter: fill r=26.35, outline r=22.03 w=8.64, so the outline sits inside.
  - dash: Bokeh centres every outline on the path, so markers and bars reach half an outline width further than twoway's. lalign(inside|outside|center) on bar, area, rbar or histogram is taken silently as 'the same picture'. mlalign(inside) is silent too; only mlalign(outside) is named.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:15668-15677 (lalign silent: 'twoway draws a line the same'), :15936-15951 (mlalign); no inset or size compensation anywhere (no alignstroke reader). The 'same picture' claim was measured on `line` only: scripts/extract_varlabel_mlabel_truth.do:116, varlabel_mlabel_truth.json align.* share one SVG hash. dash.sthlp:4782-4786 and :4769-4772 claim twoway's picture for every type. Probe p3 SVGs; probe p5 N4: dash bar lalign(outside) is silent.
  - fix: For closed shapes (bars, areas, rbar, histogram, and marker size against outline), inset or outset the geometry by half the line width to match the alignment (inside by default), or name lalign()/mlalign() on those types and correct the help.
  - verifier: In p3's bar SVGs, twoway's inside, outside and center differ by half the line width (x 474.32 / 434.00 / 454.16 at stroke 40.32). varlabel_mlabel_truth.json malign.center, inside and outside have three different hashes; only the line align.* cases share one. dash_render.py:15668-15677 keeps lalign silent on every type, and there is no code that offsets a stroke. One correction: the marker part is already documented as a difference. dash.sthlp:4769-4772 says dash draws twoway's center while inside is twoway's default, so markers are kind (b). The bar, area, rbar and histogram part is untracked. The claim at :4782-4786 holds for lines only. The default difference is half the outline width, so it shows mainly with thick outlines.
- **SD-4** Colour bar thickness and label gap are fixed in pixels and ignore the scheme's clegend_width — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: The contour colour key is as thick as the scheme's 'gsize clegend_width' (huge in s2color). Measured: K2_tw.svg key 174.1 of 3960 units on a 5.5 in graph, about 23 px at 96 dpi.
  - dash: ColorBar(width=12, label_standoff=8) and the contour bar's cb.width = 12 are fixed, so the key is about half twoway's thickness whatever the scheme. Only clegend(width()) changes it.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16751-16752, :24622 (cb.width = 12); scheme-s2color.scheme:185 (gsize clegend_width huge); no reader of clegend_width in dash_render.py. Probe p2 K2_tw.svg. Not measured by any fixture: scheme_layer_truth s33_colorkey_* covers ticks and the axis line only.
  - fix: Read gsize clegend_width (and clegend_height) through resolve_size as the default thickness. Add a truth row.
  - verifier: The bar's width is fixed at 12 px: ColorBar(width=12) at :16751 and cb.width = 12 at :24622. Nothing in the code reads gsize clegend_width (scheme-s2color.scheme:185, huge). In K2_tw.svg the key bands run from x 3258.96 to 3433.07, so 174.1 units of 3960 on a 5.5 in graph, about 23 px. dash's own clegend(width(huge)) resolves to 26.7 px (resolve_size gives 20 pt), so its default is roughly half what twoway draws. Only user-set widths change it (_colorvar_key_place :16903-16916).
- **SD-5** xscale()/yscale() logit, atanh and log1m transforms are named as not implemented and the axis is drawn linear — minor, missing-feature, confirmed, effort L, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway takes undocumented scale transforms logit, atanh and log1m (and their no forms) and places the data on the transformed scale. Measured: scatter mpg p, xscale(logit) puts markers at cx 2644.82 against 2382.75 linear, with different ticks.
  - dash: The scale table knows the words, but only log, reverse and range are honoured. The rest are named: 'xscale() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: logit'. The axis is drawn linear.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:53615-53631 (_DASH_AXSCALE_SUBOPT_TABLE has atanh, logit, log1m), :56685-56712 (_dash_scale_spec honours log/reverse/range); Stata ado/base/t/transform.class:114-143, tickset_g.class:377-391. Probe p1 case C: C_tw.svg vs C0_tw.svg, and dash's note. Not in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Draw the three transforms (transform the data and ticks, label in original units), or document them as named. They are hidden in twoway's own help, so documenting is the cheaper option.
  - verifier: _DASH_SCALE_SUBOPTS = {log, reverse, range} plus _DASH_SCALE_LOOK_SUBOPTS (:45863), so logit, atanh and log1m are named in a note. The p1 log shows the note, and C_tw.svg against C0_tw.svg puts the markers at cx 2644.82 against 2382.75. Tracking: these three were listed in GAPS3 D7, but BUGS.md:3563 closed D7 on 15oct2026 without drawing them and without recording them as a residual. That makes them untracked in practice. They are not documented in Stata's own axis_scale_options.sthlp, which documents only log, so few users will type them.
- **SD-6** jitter() adds uniform noise per axis as a share of the data range; twoway adds spherical noise as a share of the graph area — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter.sthlp:354-358: 'adds spherical random noise ... # represents the size of the noise as a percentage of the graphical area'. It is applied in the graphics engine (yxview.class gdi gm_jitter, jitterseed), so the same jitterseed gives the same picture.
  - dash: _apply_jitter adds independent uniform noise of +/- pct*range/2 per axis in data units, with numpy's RNG. A constant axis uses range 1.0 data unit, which can be invisible next to the axis span. jitterseed reproduces dash's picture, not twoway's. The help describes dash's rule without calling it a difference.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:13926-13958; dash.sthlp:5780-5792. No jitter geometry truth fixture: jitter appears only in rc and value fixtures.
  - fix: Draw spherical noise scaled to the plot area (in screen units), and document that positions cannot match Stata's RNG. At minimum, list it as a difference.
  - verifier: _apply_jitter (:13926-13958) adds independent uniform noise of ±pct*range/2 on each axis in data units, with range 1.0 for a constant axis. scatter.sthlp:354-357 says 'spherical random noise ... percentage of the graphical area', and yxview.class:48 has 'jitter = 0 // jitter amount, gmetric', applied in gdi (:436-447). dash.sthlp:5781-5789 states dash's own rule without calling it a difference. No geometry truth test exists. The magnitudes are of the same order, but the shape differs, and the constant-axis case is in data units.
- **SD-7** A weighted marker layer under by() with a panel whose weights are all zero is drawn; twoway stops with r(198) — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway scatter mpg weight [aw=w], by(foreign) with w = 0 for every foreign car: rc 198, no message.
  - dash: rc 0, page written. The no-weight-above-zero check is skipped under by().

  - fix: Apply the check per by() panel (each panel's rows), or document the exception.
  - verifier: dash.ado:6952 skips the check when `_ss_by' is set. I re-ran it (q1): twoway scatter mpg weight [aw=w] with w=0 for foreign gives rc 0 without by() and rc 198 with by(foreign), so the refusal comes from the panel. The p2 log shows dash rc 0. dash.sthlp:4196-4197 states the r(198) rule with no by() exception.
- **SD-8** title() with notitling is accepted with a note; twoway refuses it with r(4018) — cosmetic, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway scatter mpg weight, title(T) notitling: 'title.edit , mtextq(`"T"') : class type not found', r(4018).

  - verifier: p4 case 23: twoway prints 'title.edit , mtextq(`"T"') : class type not found', r(4018). The p5 N1 log shows dash rc 0 with only notitling named. twowaygraph_g.class:577-582 sends the titles to parse_and_log_titles_edits under notitling. dash.sthlp:6560-6564 documents notitling as named, but not this combination. The twoway behaviour looks like an internal error, so this is low priority.

No test pins these lines. One correction: dash.sthlp:1184-1186 does document an extra dash line after the gm_linewidth refusal, but not these.
- **SD-10** An axis label list of more than 2,000 ticks is replaced by the default ticks — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway scatter mpg weight, xlabel(0(2)5000): rc 0; draws 2,501 labels and widens the axis to 0-5000.
  - dash: '(note: dash: an axis label option asks for more than 2,000 ticks (0(2)5000); the axis keeps its default ticks.)' The range and ticks differ. A #N above 2000 is clamped silently.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:40233 (_MAX_AXIS_TICKS = 2000), :42228-42233, :42205 (count clamp), :7155-7160. Probe p1 case E. Not in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Document the bound (it is a resource guard), or at least widen the axis to the listed range.
  - verifier: _MAX_AXIS_TICKS = 2000 (:40233). An over-long rule keeps the default ticks with a note (:42228-42233), and #N is clamped silently (:42205). I measured it (q1): twoway xlabel(0(2)5000) is rc 0 and its SVG has 2507 text elements, with labels running to 4,996 / 4,998 / 5,000. The plain graph has 10. The bound is not in dash.sthlp, neither under Resource limits nor elsewhere.
- **SD-11** A marker size above 1,000 units is drawn at the default size and noted as 'not a recognised size', against the help's 'any number' — cosmetic, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway scatter mpg weight, msize(1500): rc 0, draws the markers at that size.
  - dash: '(note: dash: msize(1500) is not a recognised size; using the scheme's default.)' resolve_size refuses any size over 1,000 units (10,000 for lines).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:8314 (_SIZE_MAX_RS = 1000.0), :8421-8424; dash.sthlp:1177-1178 ('msize() ... any number'). Probe p1 case H.
  - fix: Correct the help (state the 1,000-unit bound) or raise the bound. The note's wording should say 'out of range', not 'not recognised'.
  - verifier: OptionTranslator.translate_msize('1500') raises 'size out of range' (_SIZE_MAX_RS = 1000, :8314, :8421-8424). The caller reports it as 'is not a recognised size' (p1 log; twoway rc 0). dash.sthlp:1177-1178 says 'any number'. msize(1000) already draws a 3840 px marker, so only absurd sizes are affected. The fix is to the wording and the help.
- **SD-12** fpfit/fpfitci with more than 20,000 distinct x is evaluated on a 20,000-point grid, silently — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway__fpfit_serset predicts at every distinct x of the sample.
  - dash: Above 20,000 distinct x, np.linspace(min, max, 20000) is used without a note. The curve is smooth, so the visible effect is nil apart from where points fall and cmissing breaks.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:429 (_FP_GRID_MAX), :27031-27034, :21747-21748. Not in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Name the cap in a note or in the help.
  - verifier: _FP_GRID_MAX = 20000 (:429). Both :21747-21748 and :27031-27034 return np.linspace(...) with no warning. The cap is not in dash.sthlp. The visible effect is negligible.
- **SD-13** text() whose globals expand past 4,000 bytes is refused — cosmetic, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway expands the globals in an added text and draws it.
  - dash: r(198) when the expansion exceeds 4,000 bytes.
  - evidence: dash_parse.mata:11461-11469; :11668. Not probed (edge case), and not in dash.sthlp.
  - fix: Document it, or raise the bound to c(macrolen).
  - verifier: The code is as cited (dash_parse.mata:11461-11469, :11668), but the finding is wrong that it is undocumented. dash.sthlp:784-785 documents it ('A line whose globals expand past 4,000 characters is refused, text(): expanded text is too long, r(198)'), as does :5232-5234 for every text option, and BUGS.md D13/D13b record it. So it is kind (b), a documented difference. It applies only when an unexpanded $ reaches dash (written \$name or through macval()). twoway itself draws only the last 252 characters of a line over 254 (dash.sthlp:5237-5239), so its picture of such a text is truncated anyway.
- **SD-14** The scheme's contour keys (yesno contours_outline, contours_colorlines, contours_reversekey, numstyle contours) are never read — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: zyx2style-default.style:7-10 and zyx2style-p#.style take levels, ownlines, reversekey and colorlines from the scheme, so a user scheme with 'yesno contours_outline yes' outlines the bands.
  - dash: No reader. Every shipped scheme sets them to no or 9 (only s2color sets them, the rest include it), so only user schemes differ.
  - evidence: grep: no 'contours_outline', 'contours_colorlines' or 'numstyle contours' in dash_render.py; scheme-s2color.scheme:56, :2239-2241.
  - fix: Read the four keys as the defaults of outlines, colorlines, reversekey and levels.
  - verifier: No contours_outline, contours_colorlines, contours_reversekey or 'numstyle contours' appears anywhere in dash_render.py, dash.ado, the mata files or the dash-*.scheme files. zyx2style-default.style:7-10 reads them from the scheme. s2color sets them to no / 9 (:56, :2239-2241), and no shipped scheme sets yes. dash reads user scheme files (dash.sthlp:6332ff), so only a user scheme that changes these keys would draw differently.

- **SD-16** The open natscale entry: after a filter move, 3 of 264 axes get ticks other than twoway's — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md "The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew"
  - twoway: Stata's compiled _natscale picks the ticks.
  - dash: Exact at build (Stata's ticks are embedded). After a filter move, dash's fitted model misses 3 of 264 measured axes.
