It applies only after a filter move, or to a spec built without Stata's embedded ticks. It is graded S3 in the entry.
- **SD-17** About fifty old-spelling and style options are named and not drawn, though twoway draws them — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp Unsupported Stata twoway options
  - twoway: Each of these changes the drawn graph (measured, graph export SVG against the plain graph): symbol(T) is identical to msymbol(T); mlabels(make) draws labels; msymangle(45); bcolor(red), bfintensity(30) and blwidth(vthick) on bar; bartype(spanning); afcolor(red) and afintensity(30) on area; xlog on lfit; numkeys(2); mlabjustification(right). By the help the same holds for xtransform(), dlpattern(), omit*, back/noback, onepanel, heatlabel, zvarlabel(), zvarformat(), subchar(), lowess weight and the bm* back-marker options.
  - dash: '(note: dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): symbol() bcolor())'; drawn as without them.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:6500-6560 (Unsupported section); test/fixtures/catchall_options_truth.json (rc and note only, not whether twoway draws); probe p4: every pair listed DIFFERs except omitanymiss, below and notitling alone, which are SAME; probe p5 N5 (dash's note).
  - fix: Fold the old spellings onto their current options (symbol into msymbol, mlabels into mlabel, msymangle into msangle, bsymbol into bmsymbol). Draw the bar and area style aliases (bcolor, bfintensity, blwidth, blpattern, afcolor, afintensity). Draw xlog/xtransform on the fits, numkeys, bartype(spanning) and mlabjustification. Keep naming only what Bokeh cannot draw.
  - verifier: These are documented in dash.sthlp:6497-6556, so kind (b). The p4 hashes confirm that twoway draws them: c2 (symbol(T)) equals c5 (msymbol(T)); c4 (mlabels) differs from c3; c8-c11 differ from c7; c13 and c14 differ from c12; c16 differs from c15; c20 differs from c19; c25 differs from c26; omitanymiss (c18 = c17) and below and notitling alone (= c1) are the same. twoway_yxview_parse.class:596 maps Symbol to marker(symbol()), and :141/:186 show MLabels is the full name of mlabel(), so mlabels() is a legitimate spelling. I lowered the severity from major: none of these options appears in Stata 19's twoway help (symbol() is documented only in gr7sym.sthlp for graph7), and each is named in a note.
- **SD-18** Sub-options of legend(), titles, axis titles, axis labels, scales and reflines are named and not drawn — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences 5 (sub-options 'named in a note')
  - twoway: twoway draws them all.
  - dash: '(note: dash: legend() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: ...)'. Set difference between twoway's tables and dash's honoured sets: legend: stacked/nostacked, textfirst/notextfirst, symplacement, showindex, width, height, orientation, angle, alignment, justification, linegap, margin, placement, xoffset, yoffset, tstyle, style, fcolor, lcolor, lwidth, lpattern, lstyle, lalign, bcolor, bfcolor, bl*, note, caption, t1/t2/b1/b2/l1/l2/r1/r2title, all, styleonly, draw/nodraw, drawview. title/subtitle/note/caption: orientation, angle, width, height, lstyle, lalign, bl*, astextbox, style. Axis titles: box, bcolor, fcolor, lcolor, margin, bmargin, orientation, angle, justification, alignment, placement, linegap, width, height, tstyle, bexpand. Axis labels: labstyle, tstyle, gstyle, tlstyle, glstyle, style, nogextend, pgmgrid, showlabels, labelminlen, labelkeepleadingzero. xline: extend/noextend. xscale: style, ring, logit/atanh/log1m (SD-5).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:53797-53803 (_DASH_LEGEND_SUBOPTS) against :53532 table; :53662-53675; :53826-53846; :56997; :45863-45869; :56685; :53470-53491 (note). Lists computed by importing the tables (probe subopts.py). Documented generically at dash.sthlp:1000-1006.
  - fix: Implement the commonly used ones (legend stack, textfirst and symplacement; axis-title box and orientation; title orientation and angle), and document the rest by name.
  - verifier: I recomputed the legend set from _DASH_LEGEND_SUBOPT_TABLE minus _DASH_LEGEND_SUBOPTS (:53797). It gives alignment, angle, the b/l/r/t titles, bcolor/bfcolor/bl*, caption, note, draw/nodraw, fcolor, height, width, justification, lalign/lcolor/lwidth/lpattern/lstyle, linegap, margin, orientation, placement, showindex, stacked/nostacked, style/styleonly, symplacement, textfirst/notextfirst, tstyle and xoffset/yoffset, which matches the finding. They are documented generically at dash.sthlp:1043-1049, and :4491-4500 names textfirst, stacked and symplacement explicitly. Kind (b). A few of them (all, styleonly, draw) are editing internals that twoway draws the same.
- **SD-19** Computed layers that are approximations: contour above 1,000 distinct points, and lpoly/lpolyci above degree 7 — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp contour section; Option values (degree())
  - twoway: twoway fits the exact surface on the unique(x) by unique(y) grid at any size, and lpoly at any degree (degree(1000) draws).
  - dash: contour: above 1,000 distinct points dash fits a seeded subsample of 1,000 on a 100 x 100 grid, with a note. lpoly: above degree 7 it notes 'can differ from Stata's' (measured: degree 8's bandwidth is 484.23 in Stata against 400.94 in dash); above 30 it fits at 30.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:13992-14003, :24376-24382; :543, :20950-20967; dash.sthlp:569-572 and :1166-1169.
  - fix: None required beyond the documentation unless exact parity is demanded. The lpoly gap comes from Stata's round-off in its pilot fit.
  - verifier: Documented: the contour subsample at dash.sthlp:570-577, and lpoly degree above 7 at :1168-1170 with the note in _lpoly_degree_note (:20951-20966; 484.23 against 400.94 at degree 8). Kind (b), approximations.
- **SD-20** A continuous colorvar() is drawn as a 256-step gradient with a colour bar; twoway draws its z-tick levels with marker keys — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp colorvar section (documented difference)
  - twoway: colorvar_options.sthlp: the range is cut at the z axis's major ticks. Measured K_tw.svg: scatter colorvar(price) draws four level colours, and its key is four marker circles beside an axis line with ticks.
  - dash: Markers, bars and spikes are mapped continuously through the rule in 256 steps, with a Bokeh ColorBar (title above, no axis line). Only lines and the markers of connected and rconnected take twoway's levels.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16723-16760 (LinearColorMapper, _rule_palette(256)), :16195-16201 (stepped only for lines), :18195; dash.sthlp:5525-5534 ('How the colours are drawn is dash's'). Probe p2 K_tw.svg.
  - fix: Route every continuous colorvar() layer through the stepped mapper and draw twoway's marker key.
  - verifier: map_scatter is stepped only when called from connected or rconnected (calls at :18563, :23560, :23568) or for lines (:17151). A plain scatter, bar or spike colorvar() goes through LinearColorMapper with _rule_palette(256) and a Bokeh ColorBar (:16736-16752). dash.sthlp:5523-5533 documents it ('How the colours are drawn is dash's'), so kind (b). I raised the severity: every continuous colorvar() scatter, which is the feature's main use, draws different marker colours (a 256-step gradient against twoway's 4 or so tick levels) and a different key. dash is also inconsistent with itself, since connected markers are stepped.
- **SD-21** Relative sizes are fixed at the 4-inch reference whatever xsize()/ysize(); the figure height is held between 160 and 560 px — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Figure proportions
  - twoway: A relative size is a share of the graph's smaller side, so text and markers scale with xsize()/ysize(). The graph is drawn at its declared size.
  - dash: 'dash ... draws it at 2.88 points whatever the graph's size'. The aspect ratio yields outside a 160-560 px height band, so a large graph has relatively smaller text than twoway's.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5700-5719; dash_render.py:518-519, :27696, :63428-63429. The band's values are not stated in the help.
  - fix: Document the band's values. Scaling relative sizes by the declared size would match twoway.
  - verifier: dash.sthlp:5705-5723 documents the fixed 2.88 pt unit and 'a height band' without its values. The values are _MAX_FIGURE_HEIGHT = 560 and _MIN_FIGURE_HEIGHT = 160 (dash_render.py:518-519). Kind (b). Part of this is a consequence of browser-sized layout, but it does change the proportions of the drawn graph.
- **SD-22** Resource limits refuse or truncate commands twoway draws — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Resource limits
  - twoway: twoway draws any number of observations, plots, by() groups, reference lines and added texts, and any length of function points.
  - dash: r(134) above 5,000,000 embedded cells, 100 plots, 10,000 reference lines, 1,000 added texts, or c(macrolen) characters of function points. by() draws the first 100 groups, with a note. Each limit can be raised with a global.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:2360-2375; dash.ado:5174-5361, :11366-11378; dash.sthlp:2933-3010, :3714.
  - fix: None (documented guards).
  - verifier: These are documented under Resource limits (dash.sthlp:2934-3000): max_cells 5,000,000, max_plots 100, max_reflines 10,000, max_added_text 1,000, max_by_groups 100 (the first groups are drawn, with a note), each raisable by a global. Kind (b). Only very large graphs reach them.
- **SD-23** Other documented drawing differences found in the code — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp Differences from twoway / Option values / contour, by(), dates sections
  - twoway: twoway draws contourline's level keys; partial markup such as {bf:} and {sup:} on part of a text; the graph region's inner box (ilcolor() and similar); by() date panel labels as numbers; number labels on the axis when the first layer is a fit or kdensity of a date; and it accepts an unknown scheme, drawing s2color with a note.
  - dash: contourline keys are named and not drawn; partial markup is drawn plain with a note; region inner-box options are named; by() date labels are written as dates; the date axis is kept; an unknown scheme is refused. The help also lists kernel(GAUSSIAN), xsize(()) and string colorvar() accepted where twoway refuses them; lwidth(-1) on scatter refused where twoway draws it; and colour intensities past the darkest computed by another rule.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:24402-24408; dash.sthlp:592-594, :5170-5182, :4298-4306, :3277-3281, :4815-4818, :1243-1260, :1307-1319, :1320-1330.
  - fix: Each is a gap against the claim. Per the help they are kept on purpose; closing them is separate work.
  - verifier: I checked each help citation. contourline keys named (:592-593); partial markup drawn plain with a note (:5171-5181); inner-box options named (:4298-4306); by() date panel labels written as dates (:3277-3281); date axis kept when the first layer is a fit (:4816-4817); kernel(GAUSSIAN), xsize(()) and string colorvar accepted, unknown scheme refused, and lwidth(-1) on scatter refused (:1243-1262); navy*52 (:1316-1318). All are documented, kind (b).

  Not covered: Served app and saved-page JavaScript twins, and parity after filter moves: dashboard-only and out of scope, except the natscale entry.; WebGL backend above 10,000 rows (dash_render.py:5567, :63397): whether Bokeh WebGL draws markers, dashes and outlines exactly as canvas does was not measured.; Statistical fidelity of each estimator (lowess, lpoly, kdensity, mspline, mband, fpfit): the existing truth fixtures were taken as given, not re-measured.; The scheme-key coverage of all 913 Stata style files and 26 schemes, beyond the contour keys and clegend_width: another auditor's area (dash.sthlp Stata scheme support).; Whether each sub-option in SD-18 is really unreachable along other code paths: the lists come from set differences of the renderer's tables, not from one probe per sub-option.; Marker-size compensation for outline alignment (SD-3): twoway's geometry was measured, but dash's rendered pixel sizes were not measured in a browser.; The string-length and macro caps in dash_parse.mata (4,000-character notes and text() expansion) were read, not probed.; dash_setup.ado and dash_setup_lib.py were not swept; they hold no drawing code.

### colorvar-family (16)

- **CV-1** colorfillonly strips the colour from every line part (line, connected's line, spike, dropline stems, rcapsym spines, pc shafts and arrowheads); twoway keeps those lines coloured — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: colorfillonly only keeps marker and bar OUTLINES in the series colour. Every line is still coloured by level: line (5 level paths plus a grey missing path), connected's line, spike, dropline stems, rcapsym spines, pcarrow shafts and heads. Measured in s2color: F1 line, F2 connected, F4 spike, F5 pcarrow, F7 rcapsym and F8 dropline all show line strokes in #0000ff..#ffcc00.
  - dash: The lines are drawn in the series colour #1a476f, with no note. On line/tsline the key also falls back to the 256-colour bar instead of twoway's levels (F1). Only fills are coloured: VBar/Scatter fill_color is mapped, while Segment, MultiLine and Line are left plain.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16983-16984 (_colorvar_paint sets only fill_color when fill_only; Segment/MultiLine/Line have no fill). dash_render.py:17145-17147 (_colorvar_line_parts returns None under colorfillonly). Stata colorvar_options.sthlp: colorfillonly affects 'dots, bars, and markers'. Probes are in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/colorvar/run1 (T_F*.svg vs D_F*.html). The only test is test_colorvar_options.py::TestFillonly (scatter only).
  - fix: Under colorfillonly, keep colouring line_color on line-only glyphs (Segment, MultiLine, arrow heads, and the level Lines from _colorvar_line_parts); keep the series colour only on the outlines of markers and bars. Add measured truth for the 8 types.
  - verifier: Confirmed. dash_render.py:16983-16984 sets only fill_color when fill_only=True, so Segment, MultiLine and Line keep the series colour. :17145-17147 returns None from _colorvar_line_parts under colorfillonly, so line and connected drop back to the 256-step ColorBar (D_F1 has a 256-colour LinearColorMapper; F2's connected markers are field-mapped through it, not twoway's levels). The twoway SVGs re-read in run1 show line strokes in #0000ff..#ffcc00 plus a #808080 missing path on F1, F2, F4, F5, F7 and F8. Each dash glyph is line_color #1a476f. Bars (F3, F6) match: fill mapped, outline #1a476f. Stata's colorvar_options.sthlp:178-185 limits colorfillonly to the outlines of 'dots, bars, and markers'. dash.sthlp:5452 says only 'outlines keep the series colour' and never says lines lose their colour. Untracked: BUGS C8b ('colorfillonly keeps the series outline') was measured on scatter only, and TestFillonly is scatter-only. Major stands: on 8+ types the colour encoding is lost altogether under a valid twoway option.
- **CV-2** colordiscrete with colorlevels(k) makes k-1 levels at range/k; twoway makes k levels at range/(k+1) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: yxview.class set_colorcuts_from_levels adds 1 to the count under colordiscrete, giving cuts min+i*range/(k+1) for i=1..k. rep78 colordiscrete colorlevels(4) gives cuts 1.8 2.6 3.4 4.2, so every marker is grey (L1). z100 (0..100 step 10) gives levels 20 40 60 80 (L2). With colorvarminmax the levels are 0 20 40 60 80 100 (L3). The existing truth disc_levels_price_4 has key 5,814 8,337 10,860 13,383.
  - dash: _dash_colorvar_cuts takes linspace(lo,hi,k+1)[1:-1] whether or not colordiscrete is set. L1 colours rep78 2/3/4. L2 gives levels 25 50 75. L3 gives 0 25 50 75 100. Partitions and colours differ from twoway.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:14227-14230. /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/y/yxview.class:2406-2427. Probes in run3 (L1-L3, L5 on line). test_colorvar_forms.py:128 test_colorlevels_under_colordiscrete_makes_the_cut_values_the_levels pins dash's rule. test_colorvar_truth.py passes disc_levels_price_4 only because both sides are all grey (n_groups 1).
  - fix: Under colordiscrete use k+1 equal steps (k cuts); with colorvarminmax add the min and max. Apply the same in the relevel path (_dash_cvar_relevel_bins). Flip the unit test and compare key_texts of disc_levels_price_4.
  - verifier: Confirmed in code and measurement. yxview.class:2406-2427 (set_colorcuts_from_levels) adds 1 to the count under colordiscrete, giving k cuts at min+i*range/(k+1). dash_render.py:14227-14230 always takes linspace(lo,hi,k+1)[1:-1] (k-1 cuts). The relevel path calls the same function (:52710). The run3 SVGs show: L1 key 1.8 2.6 3.4 4.2 with 74 grey markers, where dash colours rep78 2/3/4; L2 twoway 20 40 60 80, dash 25 50 75; L3 twoway 0 20 .. 100, dash 0 25 50 75 100. The level colours differ too (#00ffff/#00ff00/#ffff00 vs #00ffaa/#aaff00). test_colorvar_forms.py:128 pins dash's rule. disc_levels_price_4 passes vacuously: its fixture has n_groups 1, all #808080, and dash's 3 cuts also miss every price. dash.sthlp:5434 and :5494 call these options 'drawn as Stata draws them', so the help is wrong here. Severity lowered to minor: colordiscrete combined with colorlevels() is an unusual pairing, and twoway's own result is mostly grey.
- **CV-3** The discrete/binned colour key (clegend) differs from twoway's: order, label texts, value labels, an extra missing entry, swatch shape — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort L, tracked: untracked (partly documented as 'a discrete key entry per bin', dash.sthlp:5536)
  - twoway: twoway's clegend is a column of swatches with a z axis beside it. (1) The highest level is at the top: '5' and #ffcc00 at y=271, '1' at the bottom (K1). (2) Labels are z-axis ticks, not level names. colordiscrete on foreign keys 0 .2 .4 .6 .8 1 (K2); levels 3/4/5 key 3 3.5 4 4.5 5 (q_if_disc). Binned keys label the cut values between the swatches: 2.33333 3.66667 (K3), 2 4 (K4), 7,496 11,701 (T3). (3) There is no missing or 'Other' entry, even with colorotherlabel() (K1, K8/D05). (4) There are no value labels unless zlabel(, valuelabel) (K2 vs K7). (5) The swatch takes the plot type's key: a rect for bar and spike, a line for line types.
  - dash: dash draws a Legend with one entry per level from top to bottom in ascending order (1 at the top). Labels are value labels (Domestic/Foreign) or interval texts: '1–2.33333', '≤2', '2–4', '>4', '3,291–7,496'. It adds a '(missing)' entry, or the colorotherlabel text such as 'rest', whenever the domain has a missing value. L4 even shows an empty '>100' bin. Swatches are always circles (Quads under colorkeysrange).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:14319-14338 (interval labels), :16352-16366 and :16488 (value labels), :16591-16607 (entry order, missing entry), :16698 (Legend). Probes are in run1 (K1-K4, K8, K12, T1-T4), run3 L4 and run2 R1. The help documents only 'a discrete key entry is drawn per bin' (dash.sthlp:5523-5537). The value labels, order and missing entry are not documented. test_colorvar_options.py::TestOtherlabel asserts 'rest' in dash's clegend, where twoway's clegend draws none.
  - fix: Draw the discrete/binned clegend as twoway does: swatches stacked with the highest at the top, and a z axis beside them labelled at natscale ticks (discrete) or at the cuts (binned). No missing entry and no value labels unless zlabel(,valuelabel). Swatch in the plot type's key style. Add tests comparing key_texts and positions from the existing fixtures.
  - verifier: Confirmed against the run1/run3 SVGs and dash_render.py:16581-16698. _swatch is always a size-0 fig.scatter (default circle), or a Quad under colorkeysrange. The entries are ascending, so 1 is at the top of the vertical Legend. A '(missing)' or colorotherlabel entry is added whenever has_missing. Labels are value labels or interval texts. Twoway: K1 puts '5' and #ffcc00 at the top, has no grey swatch and draws an axis line with ticks. K2 is labelled 0 .2 .. 1, where dash shows Domestic/Foreign. K3/K4/T3 label the cuts between the swatches. K8 (fixture D05) has no 'rest'. L4 has 5 swatches, where dash has 6 including an empty '>100'. T1 (bar) uses rect swatches; T5 (rconnected) uses marker plus line. Two extras: dash's Legend has border_line_color #000000 where twoway's clegend has no frame (fixture D01 frame null), and I3 (one value, colordiscrete) twoway keys '2','4' where dash keys '3'. TestOtherlabel's 'rest' check compares dash's clegend with D05p, which is the plegend; D05, the clegend, has no 'rest'. dash.sthlp:5536 documents only 'a discrete key entry is drawn per bin'. Untracked; no open BUGS entry. Major stands: every colordiscrete/binned key differs visibly.
- **CV-4** coloruseplegend key: wrong order, '(missing)' for 'Other', an extra title, value labels; legend()/colorkeysrange applied — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: The plegend is a framed box (white fill, black frame). Levels run top to bottom as 5 4 3 2 1 Other, and there is no title (K5, D13, T2, T6, X1). Missing values are 'Other' (colorotherlabel overrides it). Texts are string(cut, variable format), not value labels: foreign gives '1','0' (K6). legend(rows(1)), legend(size(huge)) and legend(off) leave it unchanged (M4, Q3, M3). Under colorkeysrange it keeps marker swatches (C28).
  - dash: The same Legend as the clegend: ascending 1..5, then '(missing)'; titled with the variable label; value labels (Domestic/Foreign). legend(rows(1)) gives it nrows=1 (M4) and legend(size(huge)) 20pt text (Q3). colorkeysrange draws Quads (C28).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16606 (_other_label or '(missing)'), :16698 (title=var_display for both keys), :16715-16725. Probes are in run1 K5/K6 and run2 M3/M4/C28. test_colorvar_options.py:498 reads twoway's 'Other' but never checks dash's text.
  - fix: Under coloruseplegend draw descending levels plus 'Other' (or colorotherlabel), with no title, as numbers in the variable's display format unless zlabel(,valuelabel). Keep marker swatches. Leave legend() sub-options off it.
  - verifier: Confirmed. In the K5/X1/M3/M4 SVGs twoway draws a white box with a black frame (rect fill #FFFFFF, stroke #000000), with 5 4 3 2 1 Other top to bottom and no title. K6 (foreign) reads '1','0' with no value labels. C28 under colorkeysrange keeps circle swatches (12 circles, 2 frame rects). dash: the same Legend object as the clegend (dash_render.py:16698 title=var_display), ascending order, and '(missing)', because :16606 defaults to '(missing)', not 'Other'. Its labels are Domestic/Foreign, nrows=1 under legend(rows(1)) (D_M4), 20pt under legend(size(huge)) (D_Q3), and Quad swatches on C28. legend(off) (M3) leaves both visible, so that part matches. test_colorvar_options.py:498 checks only twoway's D13 'Other'. Untracked.
- **CV-5** legend(size()) and legend(rows()/cols()) restyle the colour key; twoway's colour key ignores them — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: legend(size(huge)) leaves the clegend and plegend text at 99.99 SVG units, the same as without it (Q0, Q1, Q3). legend(rows(1)) leaves the clegend vertical (P12) and the plegend vertical (M4).
  - dash: The colour-key Legend takes 20pt text under legend(size(huge)) (Q1, Q3) and nrows=1 under legend(rows(1)) (P12, M4).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:39702-39720 ('A colour key keeps the rule it had: the one the user gave'; 'size() on every legend, including a colour key'). Probes are in run7 Q0-Q3 and run5 P12.
  - fix: Exclude colorvar keys (_COLORVAR_LEGEND_NAME) from legend()'s grid and size steps.
  - verifier: Confirmed. dash_render.py:39703-39713 applies legend(rows()/cols()) to cv_legends, and :39715-39735 applies size() to every fig.legend, the colour key included. D_Q1/D_Q3 show label_text_font_size 20pt against 10pt in Q0, and D_P12 shows nrows 1. The twoway SVGs Q0-Q3 and P12 keep every key text at 99.99px, with the clegend vertical. dash.sthlp:5624 says only 'A colorvar() key is not the legend: it stays'; it does not document that legend() restyles the key. Untracked.
- **CV-6** Two colorvar layers (colorallowmultiple): dash draws one key per layer and no plot legend; twoway draws one key (the last layer's) and a plot legend — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `scatter mpg weight, colorvar(rep78) || scatter price weight, colorvar(foreign) colorallowmultiple` draws one colour key, the last layer's ('Car origin', 0 .2 .. 1), plus a plot legend keying 'Mileage (mpg)' and 'Price' in p1/p2 (M1, K11/R03b).
  - dash: dash draws two ColorBars, one per layer, and no plot legend at all (K11, M1).
  - evidence: _apply_colorvar adds a key per layer (dash_render.py:16859-16860). The strip loop at :16875-16886 removes each colorvar renderer's legend item. Probes are in run1 K11 and run2 M1.
  - fix: Draw one colour key per graph from the last colorvar layer. Keep the plot-legend items of colorvar layers when the graph has two or more keys.
  - verifier: Confirmed. D_M1.html holds two ColorBars ('Repair record 1978' and 'Car origin') and no Legend at all. D_K11 holds two rep78 ColorBars. T_M1.svg has one key (Car origin, 0 .2 .. 1) plus a framed plot legend with 'Mileage (mpg)' (#1A476F) and 'Price' (#90353B). _apply_colorvar adds a key per call (:16859-16860). The strip loop at :16875-16886 removes each colorvar renderer's items, and removes the Legend once it is empty. The help documents only that colorallowmultiple permits the second layer (dash.sthlp:5460). Untracked.
- **CV-7** In the plot legend, a colorvar layer's key swatch takes the first row's level colour, not twoway's colour — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: In a mixed graph the colorvar layer's plot-legend key is drawn in its pstyle colour: p1 #1a476f (K9), and #1a85ff under stcolor (q_if_cont). Under coloruseplegend it is level 1's colour, #0000ff (M2).
  - dash: The LegendItem's renderer is the field-mapped glyph, so BokehJS draws the swatch from row 0's level colour. With the data sorted by weight that is rep78=4's #66ff00.
  - evidence: run1 K9 D_K9.html: the plot Legend item renderer has fill_color field __dash_cvar1_rep78. T_K9.svg has a #1a476f key circle; run2 T_M2.svg has a #0000ff one. The BokehJS swatch colour is inferred from the model, not rendered.
  - fix: Give the colorvar layer's plot-legend item a proxy swatch in the pstyle colour (or level 1's under coloruseplegend).
  - verifier: Confirmed. In D_K9 the plot-legend item for the colorvar layer has renderers=[field-mapped Scatter] and no index. Row 0's __dash_cvar1_rep78 is '4', which maps to #66ff00. BokehJS 3.9's GlyphRendererView.get_reference_point (bokeh.js:32111-32112) falls back to index 0 when the label is a value, so the swatch is drawn in row 0's level colour. T_K9.svg's key circle is #1A476F, and T_M2.svg's is #0000FF under coloruseplegend. The colour is inferred from the model and BokehJS source, not from a rendered pixel. Untracked.
- **CV-8** DT-22/SD-20 (continuous default): 256-step gradient where twoway bins at the z-axis ticks; where it applies — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp 'How the colours are drawn is dash's' (DISCOVERY Q5; previously reported DT-22/SD-20)
  - twoway: An unbinned numeric colorvar is cut at the z-axis ticks at or above the minimum, giving n+1 levels in n+1 rule steps. The key is one swatch per level with tick labels; the 0 label shows for price. Price gives 4 colours #0000ff #00ffaa #aaff00 #ff0000 (K10, T7). Under s1mono: #bfbfbf #808080 #404040 #000000 (I7).
  - dash: A LinearColorMapper of 256 rule colours over min..max, with a gradient ColorBar. It applies on 17 types: scatter, dot, bar, rbar, spike, rspike, dropline, rcap, rcapsym, rscatter, rpcap, rpspike, pcspike, pccapsym, pcarrow, pcbarrow, pcscatter (probes K10, T7, C1, C2, F3-F8). Also under by() panels, on colorallowmultiple layers (K11, M1), and with zlabel()/zscale(log) on those types. It does not apply on line, tsline, rline, tsrline, connected or rconnected, which take twoway's levels (WP-32). Under the mono schemes the lowest value is #fefefe on a white plot region, which is nearly invisible (I7).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16730-16760, with stepped only from _colorvar_line_parts and the colorvar_stepped markers (:17150, :18563, :23560-23568). Documented at dash.sthlp:5523-5530 and in DISCOVERY Q5. test_colorvar_truth.py skips the default ('the continuous default is not compared').
  - fix: Use the stepped path (twoway's levels and stepped key) on all 23 coloured types, as WP-32 did for lines. Then remove the help sentence.
  - verifier: Confirmed and documented as deliberate (category b). dash.sthlp:5523-5527 says 'How the colours are drawn is dash's ... 256 steps ... where twoway bins it at its z axis's ticks'; :5402-5406 gives the WP-32 line exception. dash_render.py:16737-16745 builds a 256-colour LinearColorMapper, and only stepped=True (lines, connected/rconnected markers) goes through _colorvar_stepped_mapper (:16789-16800). K10/T7 twoway: 4 colours #0000ff #00ffaa #aaff00 #ff0000. dash: a 256 gradient over 3291..15906. Its ColorBar ticker includes 0, but that tick lies below the mapper's low, so twoway's 0 label is effectively lost. I7 s1mono: twoway #bfbfbf..#000000, dash's palette starts at #fefefe on a white region. test_colorvar_truth.py:91-92 skips the default ('the continuous default is not compared'). Major stands: this is the default case on every non-line type.
- **CV-9** colorvar value refusals differ in rc or words: colorcuts(abc), colorlevels(abc), colorvar(two vars), colorvar(nosuch) — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: colorcuts(abc): 'colorcuts() invalid -- invalid numlist', r(121). colorlevels(abc): 'option colorlevels() incorrectly specified', r(198). colorvar(rep78 foreign): 'foreign not allowed', r(101). colorvar(nosuch): 'variable nosuch not found', r(111).
  - dash: colorcuts(abc): 'dash: colorcuts() must be a list of at least 1 numbers (got: abc)', r(198). colorlevels(abc): 'dash: colorlevels() must be an integer (got: abc)', r(198). colorvar(rep78 foreign): 'dash: colorvar() must name one variable (got: rep78 foreign)', r(198). colorvar(nosuch): 'dash: colorvar(): variable nosuch not found', r(111).
  - evidence: dash.ado:3699-3701 (colorlevels=int, colorcuts=numlist,1, colorvar=var). Probes are in run1 A1/A2/A5 and run2 C5-C7.
  - fix: Refuse these in twoway's words and rc (r(121), r(198), r(101), r(111)).
  - verifier: Confirmed from run1/run2 probe.log and rc.txt. A1: twoway 'colorcuts() invalid -- invalid numlist' r(121), dash r(198) in its own words. A2: same rc 198, different words. A5: twoway 'foreign not allowed' r(101), dash r(198). C5-C7: same rc 111, but dash prefixes 'dash: colorvar(): '. Source: the generic validators at dash.ado:3699-3701 (colorlevels=int, colorcuts=numlist,1, colorvar=var) and :4329. Only A1 and A5 differ in rc; all four differ in words. Untracked.
- **CV-10** Malformed clegend()/plegend()/zlabel()/zscale() are drawn with a note where twoway refuses them — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: clegend(bogus) and plegend(bogus): 'option bogus not allowed', r(198). zlabel(bogus): 'invalid label specifier, :  bogus:', r(198), on continuous and discrete keys alike. zscale(bogus): 'option bogus not allowed', r(198).
  - dash: rc 0. The page is written and the note says '... is not a recognised sub-option; using not drawn' or 'zlabel() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: bogus'.
  - evidence: dash.ado:8776 (the label-refusal loop covers x/y labels and ticks, not zlabel; zscale has no check). dash_render.py:16917-16918 names unknown clegend sub-options. Probes are in run1 A8, run4/run6 P1, P5, P6, P9.
  - fix: Run zlabel() through _dash_axlabel_refusal and zscale() through the xscale/yscale check. Refuse words that are not clegend/plegend sub-options with r(198), as twoway does.
  - verifier: Confirmed. run6 rc.txt: P1 (plegend(bogus)), P5 (zlabel(bogus)), P6 (zscale(bogus)) and P9 (discrete zlabel(bogus)) are TW 198 / DA 0. run1 A8 (clegend(bogus)) is TW 198 / DA 0. probe.log shows twoway's 'option bogus not allowed' and 'invalid label specifier, :  bogus:', against dash's notes. dash.ado:8776-8782 runs _dash_axlabel_refusal only over x/y label and tick options; zlabel gets only _dash_subopt_check, and zscale has no word check. No help or BUGS entry documents it. Untracked.
- **CV-11** colorkeysreverse on a discrete clegend: twoway reverses only the swatch colours, dash reverses the entries and their labels — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (help dash.sthlp:5518 says it 'reverses a colordiscrete key')
  - twoway: The labels stay at 1 (bottom) to 5 (top) and the swatch colours flip, so level 1's #0000ff sits beside '5' (R1, D12).
  - dash: The entries are reversed with their labels: 5 (#ffcc00) at the top down to 1, then (missing) (R1).
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16596-16599. run2 T_R1.svg circle cy=236 is #0000ff and the text '5' is at y=271.
  - fix: Follow twoway once CV-3's axis-style key exists: reverse the colours, not the axis.
  - verifier: Confirmed. In T_R1.svg the labels keep 1 at the bottom (y=2388) and 5 at the top (y=271), while the swatches are flipped (#0000FF at cy=236 beside '5'); the plot markers are unchanged from K1. Fixture D12 records the same. dash_render.py:16598-16599 reverses the LegendItems with their labels (D_R1: 5 #ffcc00 first ... 1, then (missing)). test_colorvar_options.py::TestKeys::test_reverse pins label reversal and checks only that twoway's colours reverse. The help (dash.sthlp:5518) states dash's reading, so it is documented in dash's terms, but it differs from twoway. Untracked.
- **CV-12** colorlevels() over a single value draws k bins with a nonsense label — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `scatter mpg weight if rep78==3, colorvar(rep78) colorlevels(3)`: diff 0, so the cuts equal the value; the key reads '3','3' and every marker is #0000ff.
  - dash: edge_hi = lo+1, giving entries '3–3.33333', '3.33333–3.66667', '3.66667–3'. Markers are #0000ff, which matches.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:14228. run1 I5.
  - fix: Use diff 0 cuts as twoway does.
  - verifier: Confirmed. dash_render.py:14228 uses edge_hi = lo+1 when hi==lo, so I5 gives '3–3.33333', '3.33333–3.66667', '3.66667–3'. The last label's closed end uses the real maximum, 3. Twoway I5 keys '3','3' with every marker #0000ff, and dash's markers are #0000ff too. Only the key text differs. Untracked.
- **CV-13** Colour-key ticks, and so a line's colour levels, come from dash's natscale model at build, never from Stata's own answer — minor, tracked-open, confirmed, effort M, tracked: BUGS.md 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew' (open). Its grade 'only after a move or without Stata's embedded ticks' understates colorvar, which is affected at build.
  - twoway: `line mpg weight, colorvar(trunk) zlabel(#3) sort`: the key reads 0 20 and the line is drawn in 2 colours, #0000ff and #ff0000 (N1).
  - dash: The key reads 0 10 20 and the line is drawn in 3 colours, #0000ff, #00ff00 and #ff0000 (N1). The x/y axes take Stata's embedded tick table (WP-31 D15); the colour key never does.
  - evidence: dash_render.py:16773, :29216-29224 and :52420-52422 call _dash_axis_count_ticks with no table. Probe in run8 N1 (N2, the default #5, matches).
  - fix: Embed Stata's _natscale answers for the colour variable's range (as D15 does for x/y) and pass the table to the colour-key tick calls.
  - verifier: Confirmed. The colour-key tick calls at dash_render.py:16773, :29219-29224 and :52420-52422 pass no table to _dash_axis_count_ticks. _dash_stata_tick_plan has no colorvar or z request, so Stata's embedded answers (WP-31 D15) never reach the colour key. run8 N1 (trunk, zlabel(#3)): twoway keys 0 20 and draws 2 colours, dash draws 3 Lines (#0000ff, #00ff00, #ff0000). N2 (#5) matches. This is partly tracked: BUGS.md's open entry 'The natscale model misses three of the 264 axes twoway drew' names the model misses (trunk #2/#3, price #4). But it says 'At build the drawn axis is Stata's own', which is untrue for the colour key, and it does not mention colour levels. Kind: tracked-open, but its scope and grade are understated.
- **CV-14** Accepted where twoway refuses (documented): string colorvar, dash-only companions, an empty layer — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp Mapping a Variable to Colour (documented)
  - twoway: A string colorvar is r(7) ''make' found where numeric variable expected' on all 23 coloured types (C9). colormissing(), colpalette() and colornlevels() are r(198) 'option ... not allowed' (R04, HC2-14). An if that leaves the layer empty is r(3001). An all-missing domain draws the key title alone.
  - dash: All are accepted and drawn: one colour per string value; dash's own options; no key for the empty layer; a '(missing)' entry for the all-missing case. The aliases cv() and colv() are accepted too.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:1259-1260, :5499-5500, :5537-5540, :5573-5576. Probe in run2 C9.
  - fix: None, if kept as deliberate; otherwise refuse as twoway does.
  - verifier: Confirmed as documented deliberate differences (b). dash.sthlp:1258-1259 and :5537-5539 cover the string colorvar (run2 C9: TW r(7), DA 0). :5499-5500 says colpalette(), colornlevels() and colormissing() are dash's and twoway refuses them. :5573-5576 covers no key for an empty layer where twoway gives r(3001), and the '(missing)' entry for all-missing. :5422 lists the cv() and colv() synonyms. Each one accepts input twoway refuses, so no command that works in twoway breaks; it differs only from the 'same refusals' rule. Minor, or arguably cosmetic.
- **CV-15** z options on a discrete key and clegend()'s other sub-options are named, not drawn (documented) — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Mapping a Variable to Colour (documented)
  - twoway: On a discrete key, zlabel(1 3 5) relabels the key (C24), zscale(reverse) puts 1 at the top with colours to match (C26), and zlabel(, valuelabel) shows Domestic/Foreign at the ends (K7). clegend(region()), clegend(altaxis) and clegend(width(abc)) are drawn (P2-P4). The title runs up the key's right side, with an axis line.
  - dash: Each is named in the note and not drawn. The bar title sits above the bar, with no axis line.
  - evidence: dash.sthlp:5471-5478, :5531-5533, :5596-5597. Probes in run2 C24/C26, run1 K7, run4/run6 P2-P4.
  - fix: Draw them once the key is twoway's axis-style key (CV-3).
  - verifier: Confirmed and documented (b). dash.sthlp:5476-5478 says clegend's 'other sub-options are named in the note'. :5596-5597 says 'On a discrete key zlabel()'s ticks and zscale() are named in the note'. :5531-5533 covers the title above the bar with no axis line. Twoway draws them: C24 relabels 1 3 5, C26 puts 1 at the top with the colours following, and K7 shows Domestic/Foreign at the ends. dash's notes in run2 probe.log read 'zlabel() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: 1 3 5' and '... zscale(): reverse'. One irony: dash's default discrete key already has 1 at the top, which is the order twoway draws only under zscale(reverse).
- **CV-16** The colorvar truth fixtures leave the key, the non-scatter options and several interactions unmeasured or uncompared — minor, unverified, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Measured: colorvar_truth (18 scatter probes), colorvar_options_truth (67 scatter probes plus 26 schemes), colorvar_types_truth (44 types, default colorvar only), colorvar_qualifier_truth (24: if/in, by, missing x/y).
  - dash: The tests compare marker partitions and palettes only. They never compare dash's key texts, order, missing entry or value labels with twoway's (key_texts are recorded but unused). disc_levels_price_4 passes vacuously (all grey both sides). The continuous default is skipped. colorfillonly, coloruseplegend, colorkeysrange, colorlist and cuts/levels are measured on scatter only. The per-type truth checks only that a type is 'coloured', not its colours (except line_L01). Per-panel colordiscrete palettes under by(), key swatch shape per type, legend() interplay, colorallowmultiple keys, z/clegend refusals and colordiscrete+colorlevels are not measured.
  - evidence: test_colorvar_truth.py:70-104, test_colorvar_options.py:262-420, test_colorvar_coverage.py:245-295, scripts/extract_colorvar_*_truth.do probe lists.
  - fix: Extend the extract scripts with the run1-run8 probe set and compare key texts and positions and per-type colours in both modes.
  - verifier: Confirmed. test_colorvar_truth.py compares only marker partitions (:70-104), skips the continuous default (:91-92), and never reads key_texts in that test; key_texts is read only at :301-370, for numbers. test_colorvar_options.py compares palettes. Its key checks use twoway's own fixture fields (D11 swatch, D12 reversal, D05p 'rest', D13 'Other') without comparing dash's key text, order, frame or swatch with them, and TestOtherlabel pairs dash's clegend with D05p, the plegend probe. test_colorvar_coverage.py::TestEveryColouredType checks only that each type is 'coloured' (:247-262), and TestFillonly covers scatter only. disc_levels_price_4 passes vacuously (n_groups 1 on both sides). The colorfillonly-on-lines, colordiscrete+colorlevels, colorallowmultiple-key, legend()-on-key and z/clegend-refusal behaviours have no measured comparison.

  Not covered: The served app (bokeh serve) was not probed separately. I relied on the repo tests running the same GlyphMapper in both modes; all comparisons here are twoway SVG vs the saved page.; Behaviour after a filter move (dashboard-only) was not re-probed, beyond the natscale observation in CV-13.; Key geometry (swatch size and spacing, bar width and height, text sizes beyond Q0-Q3, frame styling) and BokehJS rendering: comparisons are at the model level. The legend swatch colour in CV-7 is inferred, not rendered.; colorkeysrange on a discrete clegend (P11): twoway's SVG showed no filled swatches in the key, which I could not interpret.; Only s2color and s1mono were probed; stcolor and other schemes rely on colorvar_options_truth.json.; Date-valued colorvar, zscale(log), zlabel(minmax) and zlabel(, format()) on non-line types were not re-probed (repo tests cover the line cases).; colorrule/colorstart/colorend variants, colorlist and colorkeysrange were not probed on non-scatter types beyond A3/A4/C28 (same code path assumed).; dot, pcbarrow, pccapsym, pcspike, rscatter and rpcap/rpspike were probed with the default colorvar only (C1/C2), not with colordiscrete or the cut/level options.; Hover tooltips with colorvar, and colorvar with mlabel or jitter.

### recast-horizontal (16)

- **RH-1** A range source recast to line, scatter or connected refuses horizontal and vertical, which twoway accepts and turns — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rarea lo hi x, recast(line) horizontal: rc 0. The plot type is line with bar_drop_to y: turned, the first bound along x, x axis 8..51 (both bounds) titled Lovar/Hivar. rcap lo hi x, recast(scatter) vertical: rc 0. tsrline lo hi, recast(line) horizontal: rc 0, turned.
  - dash: All three refused: "option horizontal not allowed" / "option vertical not allowed", r(198).
  - evidence: Probes P01, P02, P03 (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/recast-horizontal/records.txt and da_P0*.log). Stata: twoway_y2xview_parse.class:51-65 reads HORizontal VERTical for every y2x source after yxview has run. dash.ado:6659-6666 sets ptype to the yx target before _dash_hz_check (dash.ado:2939-2972), and line, scatter and connected are not in _hz_htypes or _hz_vtypes. connected is inferred from the same code path; it was not probed. Contradicts dash.sthlp:384-386 ("judged after recast()").
  - fix: In _dash_hz_check, accept h/v on kind range2yx (and on a ts range source) whatever the yx target is. The renderer then turns the line, scatter or connected target, keeping both bounds in the axis extent (see RH-3).
  - verifier: Code: dash.ado:6659 sets ptype to the yx target for kind range2yx before _dash_hz_check (dash.ado:2937-2972). line, scatter and connected are not in _hz_htypes/_hz_vtypes, and the derived branch does not apply, so the flag is refused. Twoway: yxview consumes horizontal only for BarOrBarDerived or check4horiz (twoway_yxview_parse.class:319-324, 456); for line/scatter/connected the flag reaches y2xview's own syntax (twoway_y2xview_parse.class:51-65), which sets drop_to y. Probes P01-P03 (twoway rc 0 turned, dash r(198)) re-read. My probe V2 (rarea lo hi x, recast(connected) horizontal) gives rc 0, b1=y, xs 8..51, so the untested connected case is now measured. The renderer would also need to turn these layers: _dash_is_horizontal (dash_render.py:45741) only turns HORIZONTAL types, and line/scatter/connected are not among them. Effort is S-M, not S. Contradicts dash.sthlp:384-386. Untracked; no horizontal_truth.json row covers a range source recast to a yx type.
- **RH-2** A range source recast to bar or area with horizontal is drawn upright by twoway, but dash turns it — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rspike lo hi x, recast(bar) horizontal and rarea lo hi x, recast(area) horizontal: rc 0, bar_drop_to x. The bars and the area stay upright at x, on a y axis of 8..51 titled Lovar/Hivar, with an x axis of 0..15 titled Xvar. horizontal has no effect: yxview consumes it, then y2xview's log_edits resets bar_drop_to to x.
  - dash: Turned: an HBar or HArea at y = x running to lo. The x axis is 8..41 titled Lovar, the y axis Xvar, and the key is Lovar.
  - evidence: Probes P10 and P11. In tw_P10.svg the rects are 308 px wide (1 x unit) and upright. Stata: twoway_yxview_parse.class:456-476 (overall_drop_to) is overridden by twoway_y2xview_parse.class:119 (`bar_drop_to.setstyle , style(`.drop_to.snm')`). dash: _dash_is_horizontal (dash_render.py:45741-45756) turns any bar or area target. spike, dropline and dot targets follow the same Stata path; they were not probed.
  - fix: For kind range2yx with a target in {bar, area, spike, dropline, dot}, take horizontal and vertical in silence and draw upright (_dash_is_horizontal false), keeping both bounds in the extent.
  - verifier: Twoway: for bar, area, spike, dropline and dot targets, BarOrBarDerived consumes horizontal in yxview (twoway_yxview_parse.class:456-476). y2xview then never sees the flag, and its log_edits re-pushes bar_drop_to = drop_to (default x) (twoway_y2xview_parse.class:119). tw_P10.svg's bar rects are the same as tw_P18.svg's (upright, 308.14 wide). My probes V3 (rspike recast(spike) horizontal) and V4 (rcap recast(dropline) horizontal) give b1=x and ys 8..51, upright, so the spike and dropline claims are now measured. Dash: the spec is type bar with horizontal set (s_P10.json), and _dash_is_horizontal turns it. Rendered: HBar, x 7.55..41.45 titled Lovar, y Xvar, key Lovar. Untracked.
- **RH-3** A range source drawn as a yx type loses its second bound from the axis extent, the default title and the legend key — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rline lo hi x, recast(line): the line runs through lo, but the y axis spans both bounds (8..51, ticks 10-50) and is titled Lovar/Hivar. In (rcap lo hi x, recast(scatter)) (scatter y x) the key is Lovar/Hivar. rbar lo hi x, recast(bar) and rline lo hi x, recast(sc) (drawn as rspike) also span 8..51.
  - dash: The y axis is 7.2..41.8 (ticks 10-40), titled Lovar, and the key is Lovar. dash.ado writes only yvar1.
  - evidence: Probes P17, P18, P19. Stata y2xview_g.class:60-75: ranges = min/max of yvar and y2var. dash.ado:6660 (`if "`_rc_kind'" == "range2yx" local yvar "`yvar1'"`) drops yvar2, and dash_render.py never reads recast_from (grep finds 0 hits). test/test_recast.py:186-188 pins the key as the first bound, and :206-210 pins the drawing to a plain line on the first bound. Both are dash-internal pins, with no twoway truth behind them. The help (dash.sthlp:3588-3589) says only "draws its first bound".
  - fix: Write yvar2 for range2yx layers. Include it in the axis hull and natscale rows in both modes and after a move, and title and key the layer label1/label2 as a range layer.
  - verifier: dash.ado:6660 keeps only yvar1. s_P17.json carries yvar lo only, and its stata_ticks y row is [8,41]. dash_render.py has 0 hits for recast_from. Rendered P17: y 7.24..41.76, ticks 10-40, ytitle Lovar. Twoway: ys 8..51, ytitle Lovar/Hivar (y2xview_g.class ranges = min/max of yvar and y2var). In P19 twoway keys 'Lovar/Hivar' and dash keys 'Lovar'. test_recast.py:186-188 pins the key as the bare first bound; it is a dash-internal pin with no truth behind it. recast_truth.json W-rl-line only counts SVG elements, so the axis is unverified there. Untracked.
- **RH-4** ciplot() drawn as a yx type leaves the upper confidence limit out of the axis — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: lfitci y x, ciplot(area), ciplot(bar) and ciplot(scatter): the band is drawn through the lower limit, but the y axis spans both limits (10..50, ticks 10-50), because the view is a y2xview_g.
  - dash: The y axis is 9.4..36.1 (ticks 10-35), so the upper limit is not in the extent.
  - evidence: Probes P27, P28, Q09. Stata y2xview_g.class:60-75. dash: GlyphMapper._dash_draw_range_as (dash_render.py, about 26599-26625) hands a yx target to _dash_draw_xy_as with locol only. U41-16 (test_wp31_truth.cscript:10812-10826) compares colours only, never the axis.
  - fix: When ciplot() names a yx type, add the hi column to the renderer's axis include or hull, in both modes and in the refit after a move.
  - verifier: GlyphMapper._dash_draw_range_as (dash_render.py:26601-26625) hands a yx target to _dash_draw_xy_as with locol only, and nothing else adds hicol to the hull. Rendered P27/P28: y range 9.41..36.09, ticks 10-35. Twoway: ys 10..50, because y2xview_g ranges span yvar and y2var. Untracked.
- **RH-5** histogram recast() is drawn in the target's scheme style, where twoway keeps the histogram style — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Every recast histogram view is restyled style(histogram). In stcolor: fill #3191ff, outline and line #5faaff at 4.86 SVG units. This was seen on recast(area), (spike), (dropline), (scatter) (marker outline #5faaff), (connected), (rline) and (bar).
  - dash: The target's p1<type> style: area fill #489dff with no outline, spike, dropline and line #1a85ff, bar #489dff/#1a85ff.
  - evidence: Probes P07, P09, P24, P38, Q23, Q24, R08 (colours.py output). Stata twoway_histogram_parse.class:188 (`.view.style.setstyle, style(histogram)`), applied for every viewtype. dash: the _dash_draw_xy_as docstring (dash_render.py, about 26546-26575) applies "the target's scheme chain". The other derived sources (lfit, qfit, lowess, function, kdensity) do take the new type's p1<suffix>, which matches twoway.
  - fix: When the source is histogram, draw the recast target (yx and range) in the histogram role's fill, line and width, in both modes.
  - verifier: twoway_histogram_parse.class:188 restyles every view style(histogram). In stcolor that is color histogram stc1 at inten90, histogram_line stc1*0.7 and linewidth vthin (scheme-stcolor.scheme:65-68). Re-ran colours.py: twoway P23/R08/Q22 fill #3191ff with stroke #5faaff, P09/Q23/Q24 stroke #5faaff at 4.86. Dash draws #489dff/#1a85ff at the target's widths. map_histogram's recast branches (dash_render.py:22223-22270) hand color_idx to the target mapper through _dash_draw_xy_as/_dash_draw_range_as, so the target's p1 chain applies to range targets too. Untracked.
- **RH-6** histogram recast(bar) bars are one x unit wide; twoway draws them the histogram's bin width — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: histogram mpg, recast(bar): bars as wide as the bins. The x scale is 12..41, the bin edges. The same bar_size (bin width x (100-gap)/100) is set whatever the target type is.
  - dash: VBar width = 1 (one mpg unit). The help says recast bars are "one x unit wide" for every derived source, histogram included. recast(rbar) already uses the bin width.
  - evidence: Probe R08: twoway xs = 12,41, dash VBar width =1. P06 and Q22 (horizontal) show twoway bars 11 and 3.625 units wide, which are the bin widths. Stata twoway_histogram_parse.class:193. dash.sthlp:3590-3592. dash_render.py: map_histogram's recast branch (about 22250-22258) goes through _dash_draw_xy_as, while rbar gets barwidth at about 22234-22238.
  - fix: Pass the histogram's bar width (and gap()) to the bar target, as is already done for rbar, and correct the help.
  - verifier: twoway_histogram_parse.class:193 sets bar_size = barwidth*(100-gap)/100 for every viewtype. Rendered R08: dash VBar width =1, while twoway xs 12..41 is the 8 bins of 3.625. map_histogram passes barwidth only for an rbar target (dash_render.py:22232-22236); the yx path goes through _dash_draw_xy_as. Kind correction: dash.sthlp:3590-3592 accurately describes what dash does ('bar (one x unit wide)'), so this is a wrong-output gap that is not listed under Differences, not a false sentence. Untracked.
- **RH-7** A histogram recast to a yx type leaves 0 out of the y axis, which twoway includes — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: The upright recast histogram's serset carries a zero series (height zero x), and y2xview_g.ranges spans it. histogram y, recast(area) / recast(dropline) on three equal bins: y scale 0..0.0303, ticks 0/.01/.02/.03.
  - dash: Only the heights reach the axis. On the same data the axis is degenerate: ticks -0.9697 and 1.03, range -1.02..1.08. On auto mpg the natscale rounding to 0..0.1 hides this; it shows whenever the smallest bin is far from 0 relative to the range.
  - evidence: Probes P23 and P24 vs Q20 and Q21. Stata twoway_histogram_parse.class:160-170 (the 3-variable serset) and y2xview_g.class:60-75. dash_render.py map_histogram's recast branch builds a source of (midpoint, top) with no zero row or axis include.
  - fix: Add 0 to the recast histogram's axis include on the height axis when it is not swapped, in both modes and after a move.
  - verifier: Twoway: the unswapped histogram serset is height, zero, x (twoway_histogram_parse.class:160-170), and y2xview_g.ranges takes min(yvar, y2var), so 0 is in the extent. Dash: _dash_tag_eval_extent (dash_render.py:61880-61897) stamps 0 for a histogram but returns early on any recast ('A recast()... leaves the layer unstamped'), and the recast source is (midpoint, top) only. Rendered P23/P24: y -1.02..1.08 with ticks -0.97/1.03, against twoway 0..0.0303 with ticks 0-.03. Confidence raised from medium to high. Untracked.
- **RH-8** histogram (and rbar) with recast() plus horizontal: twoway's swapped serset is not turned, but dash draws a clean turned layer — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: yxview's check4horiz/histSwap path. histogram, recast(bar|area|spike|rbar) horizontal: x = height, y = bin centre, bars still dropping to x, so the picture is vertical bars a bin width wide at x = height (x scale -1.81..1.91 on auto); rbar draws nothing. histogram, recast(rline) horizontal draws the height curve twice. rbar lo hi x, recast(rcap) horizontal draws x = lo against y = hi (axes titled Lovar and Hivar). Only recast(line), (scatter) and (connected) come out as a clean transpose.
  - dash: An exact transpose in every case. HBar bars are 1 unit high (rbar: the bin width), HArea fills to 0, and rline draws an extra bound at x = 0. rbar+rcap is a turned lo..hi cap.
  - evidence: Probes P06, P07, P08, P09, P12, Q22, Q23 (tw_*.svg rects and paths). Stata twoway_yxview_parse.class:319-324 and 456-468, :973-978 (the histSwap variable swap), and twoway_y2xview_parse.class:119.
  - fix: Either reproduce twoway's swapped-serset drawing for these targets or list it in dash.sthlp's Differences section as a deliberate difference. It is a Stata quirk, so documenting it is the cheap route.
  - verifier: Twoway: histogram's viewtype is rbar, so any recast sets check4horiz, and horizontal then sets histSwap (twoway_yxview_parse.class:319-324, 465-468). That builds a 2-variable swapped serset (twoway_histogram_parse.class:148-157; yxview:973-978), and y2xview resets drop_to to x. Measured: P06 xs -5.47..5.53 (bars a bin wide at x=height); P08 draws only the background rects; tw_Q23.svg has the same height path twice and no 0 line; P12 has x=lo, y=hi. My probe V6 (rbar lo hi x, recast(rbar) horizontal, a same-name recast) also gives xs 7.5..41.5, ys 18..51, while dash's _dash_recast_opt exits on a same-name recast and draws a clean turned rbar. This is a Stata quirk that draws a broken picture, so documenting it under Differences is the sensible fix. Untracked.
- **RH-9** dot with recast() to a type that is not bar-like takes horizontal and vertical, which twoway refuses — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: dot y x, recast(line) horizontal and dot y x, recast(scatter) vertical: "option horizontal not allowed" / "option vertical not allowed", r(198). yxview judges the flag on the recast viewtype before dot resets it. dot, recast(bar) horizontal: rc 0, turned dot.
  - dash: rc 0 in both cases, with a turned dot plot drawn.
  - evidence: Probes Q01, Q02 (tw_Q01.log, tw_Q02.log), Q03 (matches). Stata twoway_dot_parse.class:18-19 and twoway_yxview_parse.class:456. dash.ado:3258-3261 exits _dash_recast_opt for dot before the target is known, so _dash_hz_check sees ptype dot.
  - fix: For dot, keep the canonical recast target for the h/v judgement only: refuse h/v unless the target is in {bar, area, dot, dropline, spike}. Keep drawing the dot graph unchanged.
  - verifier: dash.ado:3258-3261 exits _dash_recast_opt for dot before the target is resolved, so _dash_hz_check sees ptype dot, which is in _hz_htypes. Twoway: twoway_dot_parse.class sets viewtype dot only after Super.parse, so yxview judges BarOrBarDerived on the recast viewtype. Re-ran Q01/Q02: twoway r(198) 'option horizontal/vertical not allowed', dash rc 0 with a turned dot drawn. Q03 (recast(bar)) matches. Note BarOrBarDerived compares the typed string, so a dot with recast(b) horizontal is refused by twoway as well. Untracked.
- **RH-10** scatteri with recast() to a bar-like type refuses horizontal and vertical, which twoway accepts and turns — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatteri 1 1 2 2 3 1, recast(bar) horizontal: rc 0, bar_drop_to y (turned). scatteri ..., recast(area) vertical: rc 0.
  - dash: Both refused: "option horizontal not allowed" / "option vertical not allowed", r(198).
  - evidence: Probes Q04, Q05. dash.ado:2942 `_hz_derived` omits scatteri, although scatteri is in _dash_recast_opt's _derived list (dash.ado, about 3270). dash_render.py:45710 _DASH_DERIVED_ORIENT_TYPES omits it too.
  - fix: Add scatteri to _hz_derived and to _DASH_DERIVED_ORIENT_TYPES, and pin the fixture row.
  - verifier: _hz_derived at dash.ado:2942 and _DASH_DERIVED_ORIENT_TYPES at dash_render.py:45710 omit scatteri, which _dash_recast_opt's _derived list (dash.ado:3270) includes. So ptype scatteri with kind derived is refused. Re-ran Q04/Q05: twoway rc 0 (b1=y, then area vertical), dash r(198). Plain scatteri horizontal is refused by both (horizontal_truth.json accept.scatteri), so only the recast path differs. Untracked.
- **RH-11** Abbreviated yx names on a y2xtype source (a range type, tsrline, histogram) are read as the full names; twoway does not unabbreviate them — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: y2xtype unabbreviates only rscatter, rline and rconnected. rline lo hi x, recast(sc) and histogram mpg, recast(li) print the two notes "named style sc|li not found in class y2xtype..." and draw rspike, y2xtype's fallback.
  - dash: No notes. They are drawn as scatter or line through the first bound or the bin heights. The same holds for co and b.
  - evidence: Probes R01, R07 (tw_R01.log and tw_R07.log show the notes; type rspike). Stata y2xtype.class (_unabbrev: RSCatter RLIne RCOnnected only) vs yxtype.class (SCatter LIne COnnected Bar). dash.ado:3288 `else if "`_rc_class'" == "y2xtype" local _sp "`_sp_yx' `_sp_rng'"` uses the yxtype minimums. dash.ado:10548 (the ciplot canon) already uses scatter:7 line:4 connected:9 bar:3 for y2xtype, and U11-04 measured ciplot(sc) as not found.
  - fix: Use the full-name minimums for the yx names in the y2xtype spelling table of _dash_recast_opt, and add recast_truth rows for them.
  - verifier: y2xtype.class _unabbrev knows only RSCatter, RLIne and RCOnnected; yxtype.class knows SCatter, LIne, COnnected and Bar. dash.ado:3288 gives y2xtype the yxtype minimums (scatter:2 line:2 connected:2 bar:1). The ciplot canon at dash.ado:10548 already uses scatter:7 line:4 connected:9 bar:3. tw_R01.log and tw_R07.log show the two notes and type rspike; dash prints no note and draws a Scatter or Line at the first bound or the heights. This also applies to tsrline, which is in _rng. Untracked.
- **RH-12** recast(b) on a yx source: twoway refuses horizontal and draws the p1 outline width; dash treats b exactly as bar — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter y x, recast(b) horizontal: "option horizontal not allowed", r(198). BarOrBarDerived and _type_suffix compare the typed string "b" with "bar". scatter y x, recast(b) draws bars with an outline 9.72 SVG units wide, where recast(bar) gives 6.48 (style p1, not p1bar).

Stata twoway_yxview_parse.class:324, :456, :1041-1045, :1326-1335. dash canonicalises the name before _dash_hz_check (dash.ado:3290-3297). Whether bar options such as barwidth() or base() are honoured under the abbreviation was not measured.
  - fix: Keep the typed spelling for the orientation and style judgement: refuse h/v and use p1 rather than p1bar when the typed name is not the full bar, line or area.
  - verifier: Severity raised from cosmetic to minor: the acceptance mismatch is the same class as RH-9. yxview assigns the typed string to .viewtype (twoway_yxview_parse.class:324), and BarOrBarDerived/_type_suffix compare it to 'bar' (:1040-1045, :1326-1335). R02: twoway r(198), dash rc 0. My probe V7 (scatter y x, recast(ba) horizontal) is also r(198) in twoway. tw_R03.svg's bar outline is 9.72 and tw_R04.svg's is 6.48. recast_truth.json W-sc-b has equal_to '' (not B-bar), where PR-4's recast(bar) equals B-bar, which corroborates it. recast(li) vs recast(line) gives identical SVGs (p1 = p1line in stcolor), so only bar, and in principle area, differ visibly. Untracked.
- **RH-13** A horizontal kdensity is keyed "kdensity var", but twoway keys it by its grid variable "x" — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: In (scatter mpg weight) (kdensity mpg, horizontal xaxis(2)) the legend reads "Mileage (mpg)" and "x": kdensity's yxswitch swaps the serset varlist, so the key is the grid's name. function, horizontal swaps the same way.

  - evidence: Probe Q25: tw_Q25.svg legend texts at x=4466 are 'Mileage (mpg)' and 'x'; the dash legend is ['Mileage (mpg)', 'kdensity mpg']. Stata twoway_kdensity_parse.class:177-181 and twoway_function_parse.class:153-157. function was not probed.

  - verifier: tw_Q25.svg: the legend texts at x=4465.63 are 'Mileage (mpg)' and 'x', and the x2 title is 'kdensity mpg'. Dash keys ['Mileage (mpg)', 'kdensity mpg']. twoway_kdensity_parse.class:177-181 swaps the varlist under yxswitch. _dash_legend_key (dash_render.py:58149) takes the last series' y label regardless of orientation, though dash already titles the y axis 'x' (horizontal_truth.json draw.kdensity ytitle 'x'). My probe V5 ((scatter y x) (function y=x^2, range(0 2) horizontal xaxis(2))) keys 'Yvar' and 'x' in twoway, so the function half is now measured on the twoway side; the help says dash keys a function by its name, y. Confidence raised to high. Untracked.
- **RH-14** An area drawn by recast(area) has no outline, where twoway draws its area line style (seen through recast; plain area uses the same mapper) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: qfit recast(area), function recast(area) horizontal and rarea recast(area) in stcolor: fill #489dff plus an outline stroke #1a85ff at 19.44 SVG units, clipped to the area (so about 9.72 visible inside).
  - dash: VArea or HArea fill only. map_area draws an outline only when lcolor(), lwidth(), lpattern() or an l*style() is given. A plain `area` spec rendered offline also draws only a VArea.
  - evidence: Probes P11, P14, P20 (tw_P11.svg:381-382, the <use> elements with stroke-width 19.44). dash_render.py:18702-18728 (the `if any(k in options for k in _LINE_KEYS) or _named_ol`). test_scheme_linewidth.py:279 gives area an lcolor(red) to get an outline at all. This belongs to the plot-type area and should be confirmed there.
  - fix: Draw the area's default outline in its scheme area line colour and width (linewidth parea / p#area), inner-aligned, in both modes. Then re-measure plain area.
  - verifier: Broader than recast. My probe V1, plain 'twoway area y x' in stcolor, draws fill #489DFF plus a clipped outline stroke #1A85FF at 19.44 (tw_V1.svg:381-382), as P11/P20 do. A dash plain area spec rendered offline gives one VArea with no outline. map_area (dash_render.py:18702-18728) strokes the outline only when lcolor, lwidth, lpattern or an l*style is given. GAPS3 E8 and its closed BUGS entry fixed only rarea and rbar, and dash.sthlp:6041 implies an area outline width is drawn. Every default area plot differs, not just recast ones. Confidence raised to high. Untracked.
- **RH-15** Help claims about recast and horizontal that the measurements contradict — minor, doc-claim-false, partly, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: See RH-1, RH-2, RH-3, RH-6, RH-8, RH-9, RH-10 and RH-12.

But dash.sthlp:3588 'a range type named as a yx type draws its first bound' is literally true of the drawn mark, since twoway's line also runs through lo; it only omits the axis and key differences of RH-3. And 'bar (one x unit wide)' accurately describes dash, so it is an undocumented difference rather than a false statement. None of these appears under 'Differences from twoway' (dash.sthlp:809) or 'Unsupported' (6456).
