The same 'Xvar...' appears in horizontal_truth.json draw.HZ-28 (bar). test_horizontal.py:378 _NO_XTITLE skips it. Tracking correction: this is not an open entry and not a listed difference. It sits only as a 'Residual, deferred (WP-30)' sentence in the closed BUGS.md P1.9a entry (line 3016). dash.sthlp:3630-3632 still says the first-equals-last rule titles 'as twoway titles it', so it is a known gap whose help text is false (kind closer to doc-claim-false than tracked-open).

  Not covered: Unmeasured cells of the type-by-target matrix before this audit (recast_truth.json mostly records glyph counts only): every recast drawing of qfit, fpfit, lowess, lpoly, mband and mspline; kdensity to anything but area; function to line, connected, area, bar, spike, dropline and dot; histogram to scatter, connected, area, bar, spike, dropline and dot; lfit to anything but scatter; area, bar, spike, dropline and connected as sources; rarea, rbar, rspike, rcap, rcapsym, rconnected, rscatter and tsrline as sources (only rline was measured); pccapsym, pcarrow, pcbarrow and pcscatter as sources; abbreviations on y2xtype sources; ciplot() geometry for every yx target and fitplot() beyond scatter; every recast+horizontal drawing (horizontal_truth measures rc only). This audit probed 53 commands in 3 slot runs (P01-P38, Q01-Q27, R01-R08). The scripts and outputs are in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/recast-horizontal/ (probe.do, analyse.py, colours.py, r2/, r3/).; Still unmeasured after this audit: the drawings of lpoly, fpfit, mband and mspline recast (same code path as lowess and qfit); the area, bar, spike, dropline and connected sources; rcapsym, rconnected, rscatter, rspike and rcap recast to other range types; tsrline to range types; pc sources other than pcspike; recast on a 2-y scatter to range names; recast combined with mlabel(), by(), colorvar(), yaxis(2) or weights; ciplot(spike), (dropline), (connected) and (dot) geometry; histogram recast to range types' colours (RH-5 predicts the histogram style); the function horizontal legend key (RH-13, inferred from code).; contour and contourline with recast(): dash.ado _dash_recast_opt classes them as yxtype base sources and would redraw them as the target. twoway's zyx2view behaviour was not measured.; Horizontal-by-type matrix: accept/refuse is measured for all 40 types x h/v/hv/vh, and the titles and labels for 24 types plus 9 combinations. A numeric geometry comparison against twoway (cap widths, rbar width, the dropline/dot base lines, marker label offsets in pixels) was not done; it relies on dash's internal transpose test. horizontal with yscale(log), with mlabposition() variants, and by() axis titles were not compared.; kdensity recast(dot): twoway's SVG holds 46,200 circles against dash's 22,800 markers; the dots per row were not reconciled.; Whether bar options (barwidth, base) are honoured under recast(b) in twoway (RH-12).; Served-app mode and the behaviour after a filter move: only the saved-page figures built by DashRenderer from each spec were inspected.; Whether the help's statement that recast() on dot is 'named in the note' (dash.sthlp:3612) holds at build time; no note appeared at registration (consistent with dash.sthlp:6598, 'taken in silence').

### by-interactions (6)

- **BY-26** by() panels' x axes are ticked at #5 where twoway's by() style ticks them at #4 — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (the open natscale entry is a different, model-level miss)
  - twoway: The default by() style sets xlabels(#4) and ylabels(#4) for every panel (the same in the compact, altleg and econ styles). Measured: e01 `lfit mpg weight, range(1000 6000) by(foreign)` draws 0(2000)6000 and the axis starts at 0. f15a/f15b (scatter + function with no range()) draw only 0 and 5000. g18 `histogram mpg, width(5) by(foreign)` draws 10(10)40, axis 10-44.
  - dash: _dash_by_numticks drops the count to 4 on the y axes only; x stays at the scheme's 5. So dash draws 1000(1000)6000 (axis from 943), 0(1000)5000, and 10(10)50 with the axis stretched to 50.5. The y axes match twoway in all 93 probes. Dash's own natscale model at #4 gives exactly twoway's ticks in all three cases.
  - evidence: /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/style/bygraphstyle-default.style:39 `set twoway_options "xlabels(#4) ylabels(#4)"` (compact/altleg/econ :39-40 too); dash_render.py:40785-40801 (_dash_by_numticks: `out["y"] = max(1, ny - 1)` only). Probes /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/byx/r2 (rec.txt K-lines e01, f15a, f15b, g18 vs out/dash_dump.json); ticks.py output: 12 mismatches, all on x1. _dash_natscale_ticks(1000,6000,4)=[0,2000,4000,6000], (0,4840,4)=[0,5000], (10,44,4)=[10..40].
  - fix: Reduce x to nx-1 as well, for axis 1 only (x2 and y2 keep the scheme's count, as the docstring's f02/c17 measurements show). Ask the WP-31 D15 Stata tick table for n=4 on by() axes (_dash_stata_tick_requests asks only {5} plus explicit #N), so rescaled panels get Stata's #4 answer. Add the three probes to by_scales_truth.
  - verifier: Confirmed in the style files, the code and dash's own fixture. bygraphstyle-default/compact/altleg/econ.style:39-40 set `xlabels(#4) ylabels(#4)` (rescale uses #3, combine sets nothing). dash_render.py:40785-40801 `_dash_by_numticks` sets only out["y"] = ny-1, so x stays at the scheme's 5. That count feeds the default ticker in both modes (:45318, served copy :70342). dash.sthlp:4679-4680 states the same y-only rule, so the help is wrong too. More in-repo evidence: test/fixtures/by_scales_truth.json d40 (`scatter mpg weight, by(foreign, yrescale) xscale(range(0))`) records twoway's x as 0/5000/5000, which is #4 (natscale #5 is 0(1000)5000). test_by_scales.py:486 checks only d40's range hull, never its ticks, so the miss passed. The probe results (e01 0(2000)6000, f15a/b 0 5000, g18 10(10)40) match `_dash_natscale_ticks(...,4)` exactly, and dash's dump draws the #5 ticks. x2 stays at #5, per f02/f03 in the same fixture. Untracked: BUGS.md:2962's residual covers only by(, style(rescale) noyrescale). This is a visible change of default tick labels on many by() graphs, so major stands.
- **BY-27** function range(varname) under by() spans the whole dataset in every panel; twoway spans each panel's own range of the variable — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (GAPS3 C12 / BUGS 'function under by() is evaluated once' closed only per-panel evaluation)
  - twoway: `twoway (scatter mpg weight) (function y=40-x/200, range(weight)), by(foreign)` draws the function over 1800-4840 in Domestic and 1760-3420 in Foreign. by(rep78, total) panel 1 draws 2730-3470, and so on; the Total panel draws 1760-4840 (serset x ranges).
  - dash: Every panel draws 1760-4840, the whole data's range (300 points, recomputed per panel but over one lo/hi). Same for f14b and e03; only the Total panel matches.
  - evidence: dash.ado:7183-7206 (range() resolved once to _fn_lo/_fn_hi from _fn_rnorm); dash.ado:8845-8873 (per-panel evaluation 'over the same range()' reuses _fn_lo_m/_fn_hi_m). Probe f14a/f14b/e03 in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/byx/r2 (fits.py: twx vs dx).
  - fix: When range() names a variable and the graph has by(), compute the min/max of that variable over each panel's rows (and the Total panel's) inside the per-panel _dash_fn_points loop. Pin it against twoway's per-panel sersets.
  - verifier: Confirmed in twoway's code and the measurements. twoway_function_parse.class log_create_serset calls `twoway__function_gen ... if `touse1'`, and twoway__function_gen.ado:101-102 runs `sum `range' `if'`, so range(varname) takes the panel's own min and max. bygraph_g.class RunGraph builds each panel on its own group. On the dash side, dash.ado:2401-2432 `_dash_fold_range` folds range(varname) to two numbers once, over the plot's whole sample. dash.ado:8845-8873 then reuses `_fn_lo_m`/`_fn_hi_m` for every panel. Probe sersets agree: f14a/f14b/e03 twoway 1800-4840 and 1760-3420 per panel, dash 1760-4840 everywhere. U46-04 and BUGS C12 pin only the default 0 1 range, which is why this was missed. Untracked. Likely the same root cause, read from code but not probed: kdensity range(varname) under by(). twoway__kdensity_gen.ado:100,117 also runs `sum `range' if `touse'` per panel, and _dash_fold_range folds it once. Severity lowered to minor: it is a rare combination, and the curve is right where it is drawn; it only spans the whole data's x in each panel. The shared x axis is unchanged unless xrescale is given.
- **BY-28** contour (and contourline) under by() cut each panel at its own z ticks; twoway cuts every panel at one shared z axis — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (BY-14 covers colorvar() only)
  - twoway: Contour cuts are the z axis's major ticks (zyx2view_g.class reset_dserset), and by() shares the z axis across panels. Measured from SVG fills: `contour price mpg weight, by(foreign)` draws the same 3 levels (#0000FF/#00FF00/#FF0000) in both panels. With by(rep78, total), all 6 panels draw the same 4 colours.
  - dash: Each panel has its own levels. by(foreign): Domestic 3 levels (cuts 5000, 10000), Foreign 5 levels (cuts 6000(2000)12000). by(rep78, total): 4, 5, 3, 3, 4, 3 levels. contourline draws different line levels per panel too (5000, 10000 against 6000-12000). No colour key in either tool.
  - evidence: /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/z/zyx2view_g.class:255-276; dash_render.py _dash_share_by_scales (47937) shares x and y only. Probe f29a/f29b/f30a: r2/out/dash_dump.json 'contours' levels vs SVG colour counts in r1/out/tw_f29a.svg and tw_f29b.svg.
  - fix: Under by(), compute the contour cut rule once, over the z range across all panels (the Total panel's included), and give every panel the same cuts and colours in both modes. Measure twoway's exact cuts with a drawn graph (lastcuts is empty under nodraw) and add them to a truth fixture.
  - verifier: Confirmed. zyx2view_g.class:255-276 reset_dserset takes the cuts from the z axis's major ticks. I re-counted the fills in r1/out/tw_f29a.svg by panel, splitting at the plot-region rects (x 553 and 2977). Both panels use the same 3 colours, #0000FF/#00FF00/#FF0000, and the whole SVG has only those 3. tw_f29b.svg has 4 colours across all panels. So twoway gives every panel one shared set of cuts. dash_render.py map_contour (around 24368-24371) computes zmin/zmax and `_dash_contour_cuts` from each panel's own uz, and `_dash_share_by_scales` shares only x and y. dash's dump confirms it: f29a Domestic levels cut at 5000/10000, Foreign at 6000(2000)12000. contourline f30a likewise draws 5000/10000 against 6000-12000. No contour+by() test exists in test_contour_layers.py, and BUGS.md has no entry. The same colour means a different z in each panel, so the drawing misleads; major stands.
- **BY-29** A scale() written outside by() enlarges dash's panels; twoway ignores it on a by() graph — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked in docs; may duplicate an earlier BY item from the prior by() audit's P5 probe (`scatter mpg weight, scale(1.5) by(foreign)`), whose findings list is not on disk
  - twoway: `twoway (scatter mpg weight) (lfit mpg weight), by(foreign) scale(1.5)` exports an SVG that is byte-identical, after id normalisation, to the same command without scale(1.5): same font sizes, marker radii and line widths.
  - dash: Multiplies everything by 1.5: tick labels 12.75pt against 8.5pt, marker size 5.10 against 3.40, line width 1.469 against 0.979.
  - evidence: diff of normalised r2/out/tw_h06.svg and tw_f01a.svg = 0 lines; r2/out/dash_dump.json h06 vs f01a axes label_size and glyph size/line_width. dash.sthlp:5686-5691 documents scale() with no by() caveat.
  - fix: When by() is present, ignore graph-level scale() (by(, scale()) or iscale() are the forms that act). Verify with one more measurement (e.g. scale(0.5), and with text options) before changing.
  - verifier: Re-measured in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/byx-verify/v.do, comparing SVGs with ids normalised. `scatter mpg weight, by(foreign)` against the same with scale(1.5) or scale(0.5): 0 lines differ. With title("Hi") added, scale(1.5) also changes 0 lines. by(foreign, scale(1.5)) changes 570 lines, and scale(1.5) without by() changes 482. twoway prints no note. bygraph_g.class:78,160-161 takes scale() only from by()'s own options. Panels are re-scaled by set_iscale, so a scale() passed through to them has no effect. dash_render.py:27487 `_dash_scale_factor` reads graph scale() with no by() exception, and it is applied at :64004 and in the served copy :70701. The auditor's dump shows h06 tick labels at 12.75pt against 8.5pt. dash.sthlp:5686-5691 has no by() caveat. Related and worth fixing together: by(, scale()), which does act in twoway, is not among dash's implemented by() sub-options (dash.sthlp:3307-3315). It falls to the named/unsupported branch at dash_render.py:36404-36405, so dash names it rather than drawing it. Valid syntax with visibly different output in a rare combination: minor.
- **BY-30** With a bare sort, tied x values are joined in data order; twoway uses Stata's unstable sort order, which differs again under by() — cosmetic, wrong-output, partly, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: `line mpg weight, sort by(foreign)` Domestic panel joins the tied weights 3370 (19,18), 3600 (16,18) and 4060 (21,14): all three pairs reversed from data order. Without by(), twoway reverses only 4060 (21,14).
  - dash: Always data order: 3370 (18,19), 3600 (18,16), 4060 (14,21), so the drawn path through each tie differs: 3 of 3 pairs under by(), 1 of 3 without.
  - evidence: r2/out/tw_f32a_s0.csv vs dash_dump f32a Line (6 swapped rows); r3/probe.log (no-by order); test/test_join_order.py:329 asserts data order by design, and join_order_truth.json is tie-free (test_join_order.py:71).
  - fix: Carry Stata's own post-sort order for a bare `sort` as a __dashs rank column (as sort(varlist) already does), computed per by() panel the way twoway sorts it. Or document this as a known difference.
  - verifier: The difference exists, but it is documented and decided, and twoway's order cannot be matched. Re-measured: `line mpg weight, sort [by(foreign)] nodraw` run 4 times in one session gave a different order of the tied weights 3370/3600/4060 on different runs, both without by() and under by(). For example, the no-by order of 3370 was 18,19 / 19,18 / 18,19 / 19,18. So there is no fixed 'twoway order'. The auditor's specific reversed pairs are one random draw, and the proposed fix (carry Stata's post-sort rank) would not reproduce twoway either. Already tracked: dash.sthlp:436-437 says ties keep the data order ('twoway's order among them changes from one run to the next'). BUGS.md:3755 (closed G6) says ties keep the stable rule because 'twoway's order among ties is not deterministic'. test_join_order.py:329 pins that rule. Category (b), a documented deliberate difference, not untracked; the only visible effect is the tiny vertical joins at tied x.
- **BY-31** Per-type by() drawing is not pinned by any truth test; the only per-type cert counts panels and glyphs — minor, unverified, partly, effort M, tracked: partly GAPS3 K2 ('per-type by() parity as a cscript', closed by U60-5 for five types)
  - twoway: Not applicable: this is test coverage. twoway draws fits, bands, bins, smooths and functions per panel and in the Total panel.
  - dash: This audit measured every family as matching except BY-26 to BY-30. But no fixture holds twoway's per-panel sersets for the fits, CI types, smooths, kdensity/histogram bins, function, or range/pc/immediate types, so a regression would pass. U60-5 compares saved against served for histogram, kdensity, scatter, line and bar only, on panel count and glyph counts, not against twoway. The served mode was not compared to twoway for any of these families.
  - evidence: test/test_wp31_truth.cscript:14395-14409 (U60-5). The by_scales/by_chrome/by_grid truths measure axes and chrome only; help_claims_6 has one lpoly by() serset.
  - fix: Turn /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/byx/r2 (rec.txt plus out/tw_*_s*.csv, 93 commands) into a scripts/extract_by_types_truth.do fixture. Add a pytest that compares both modes' per-panel glyph data against it.
  - verifier: Mostly right, but the coverage is not as empty as stated. Two families are pinned per panel against Stata. Function: test/test_wp31_truth.cscript U46-04 (12968-12996) compares dash's per-panel function points with twoway's live per-panel sersets under by(foreign), default range only. lpoly: test/test_lpoly_rot.py::test_by_panels_use_their_own_bandwidth checks per-panel lpoly against Stata's per-foreign-subset curves in both saved and served modes, plus the help_claims_6 serset. The P1A-83/84 certs in test/test_parser.cscript:2362-2560 compare by() rc and refusals for fits, lpolyci, kdensity, mband and fpfit, not the drawn data. parser_twoway_truth.json holds rc and log only, and by_scales/by_chrome/by_grid hold axes and chrome only. That leaves no per-panel drawn-data truth for lfit/qfit/fpfit, the CI bands, lowess/mband/mspline/median, kdensity/histogram bins, range/pc/immediate types, or function range(varname) (which is how BY-27 escaped). U60-5 compares saved against served only. The gap is real; tracked in part as GAPS3 K2 / U60-5.

  Not covered: Served mode was not compared with twoway; only saved-mode models were rendered.; twoway's exact contour and contourline cut values: lastcuts is empty under nodraw. Contour levels were inferred from SVG fill colours; contourline lines are all black, so its cut values are inferred from the code, not measured.; by(, clegend(on)) with contour, and colorvar() under by() (BY-14), were not probed.; mlabel(), text() and multi-y style lists under by() were not re-measured; existing truths cover them (by_grid, added_text, stylelist, mlabvpos).; xsize()/ysize() geometry under by() (by_grid covers it), and numeric font, marker and line sizes per scheme beyond the scale() check, were not compared.; Multi-variable by(foreign rep78) with stat layers, kdensity range(), lfitci stdf, and yaxis(2) fits under by() were not probed.; The prior by() audit's BY-1 to BY-25 list is not on disk (only its probe files are), so BY-29 may duplicate an earlier item.; Behaviour after a filter move is excluded by the brief.

### visual-corpus-schemes (18)

- **PIC-1** Default area plot is drawn with no outline in every scheme — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'rarea and rbar outlines are not stroked...' fixed rarea/rbar only)
  - twoway: twoway area strokes the polygon (fill, baseline and closing edges) in the p#arealine colour at twice the parea width, clipped to the inside. Measured: stcolor #1a85ff over fill #489dff (0.3 rs visible); s2color #1a476f over #486c8c; s1mono #606060 over #808080; economist #3e647d over #658397 at 0.2 rs. Same in all 10 schemes run, and the legend key rect is outlined too.
  - dash: Draws only a VArea fill. map_area adds the border Line only when lcolor()/lwidth()/lpattern() or a named line style is given. The area key has no outline either.
  - evidence: gr_example #31 (twoway area d.gnp96 date): tw31.svg has a usepath stroke #1a85ff 0.6 rs clipped; d31.html has a VArea only (screenshot shots/p31.png). Corpus c2 (area le_wm year) gives the same in s1mono, s1color, s2mono, sj, economist, meta, stmono1, stgcolor, stcolor and s2color (picture/corpus_all.txt). Code: dash_render.py:18711 `if any(k in options for k in _LINE_KEYS) or _named_ol`. The help claims at dash.sthlp:6041 ('an area's outline width (linewidth parea)') and BUGS.md:2535 (resolution 'area outlines are drawn') are not true of the default.
  - fix: Always draw area's border Line in the scheme's p#area line style (colour p#arealine, width p#area, pattern p#area), in both renderers and on the legend key, keeping cmissing() breaks and filter redraws.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. dash_render.py:18711-18733 adds the border Line only when lcolor/lwidth/lpattern or lstyle/alstyle/blstyle is given; d_c2_stcolor.html holds one VArea (#489dff) and nothing else. tw_c2_stcolor.svg strokes the area path: <use ... stroke:#1A85FF;stroke-width:19.44 clip-path>. rk_economist shows the same (#6E8E84 at 6.34, clipped). The key zoom shows twoway's area key with an outline and dash's without. TestBarAndAreaOutlines passes only because _layers('area') injects lcolor(red) (test/test_scheme_linewidth.py:279). GAPS3 E8 fixed rarea and rbar only. dash.sthlp:6041 ('an area's outline width (linewidth parea)') holds only once an outline is asked for. Major because it hits every default area plot in every scheme. It is a styling difference, not a data one.
- **PIC-2** Diamonds (D, d, Dh, dh, diamond, smdiamond) are drawn 2/3 as wide as twoway's — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: A diamond is a square on its point, as wide as it is tall. D at msize(vlarge) is 4.84 x 4.84 rs outer, and s1mono's default p2 diamond is 2.59 x 2.59 rs.
  - dash: Maps the symbol to Bokeh's 'diamond' marker, which is drawn 1.5 times taller than wide (BokehJS: moveTo(0,r) lineTo(r/1.5,0)). Height matches (factor sqrt 2), but width is about 1.8 rs against 2.59 rs under s1mono. This hits the default p2 symbol of s1mono, s2mono, sj, stmono1/2 and stsj, and their legend keys.
  - evidence: Symbol zoo picture/run1/tw_sym_stcolor.svg against d_sym_stcolor.html (shots/z_sym_stcolor.png, row 1). Corpus c11 under s1mono, s2mono, sj and stmono1: twoway polygon 2.59x2.59 rs, dash diamond size 2.161 rs. dash_render.py:8220 `"diamond": "diamond"` and _SMALL_SYMBOL_FACTOR sqrt(2); the bokeh.min.js diamond path T(l,n). BUGS.md:3645 (WP-31 E1) matched heights only.
  - fix: Draw diamonds as Bokeh 'square' at angle pi/4 with size = height/sqrt(2), adding the angle to msangle(), or use a custom marker. Re-pin test_every_symbol_is_twoways_height to check width too.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. dash_render.py:8220-8225 and 8256-8257 map D/d/Dh/dh/diamond/smdiamond to Bokeh 'diamond'. bokeh.min.js 3.9 draws it as moveTo(0,n) lineTo(n/1.5,0) lineTo(0,-n) lineTo(-n/1.5,0), so it is 2/3 as wide as it is tall. symbol_pattern_truth.json sym.D has bbox_w == bbox_h (62.37), and dash's own fixture shows this. test_every_symbol_is_twoways_height (test/test_symbol_pattern.py:231) checks size against height only. scheme-s1mono/s2mono set symbol p2 diamond (and p8 diamond_hollow, p12 smdiamond), so their default cycles are hit. Downgraded to minor: it is the same symbol, colour and height, only narrower. It changes no data and hits only mono schemes or an explicit D.
- **PIC-3** A ci layer with nofit still uses two plot numbers, so every later layer is drawn in the wrong p# style — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: With nofit, lfitci/qfitci/lpolyci/fpfitci draw one plot (the band). Measured: in (lfitci mpg weight, nofit) (scatter mpg weight) (lfit mpg weight) the scatter is p2 (#d41159 stcolor, #90353b s2color) and the lfit is p3 (#00bf7f, #55752f). In gr_example #78 the scatter is p3 #00bf7f.
  - dash: Counts every ci layer as two plots whatever nofit says (the fit line is kept as a hidden renderer). The scatter is drawn in p3 and the lfit in p4 (#00bf7f/#ffd400 stcolor, #55752f/#e37e00 s2color). In #78 the scatter is #4f2c99 (p5). Colour, symbol, pattern and width all shift.
  - evidence: picture/run2 nf_stcolor and nf_s2color (corpus.py output); gr_example #78 tw78.svg circles #00bf7f against d78.html Scatter #4f2c99 (shots/p78.png). Code: dash_render.py:57783-57787 (`if is_ci: ... n += 2`). ci_plot_index_truth.json has no nofit case.
  - fix: In _dash_plot_style_indices, count a ci layer as one plot when nofit is set (and check fitplot()/ciplot() numbering against twoway). Add nofit rows to ci_plot_index_truth.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. In twoway_lfitci_parse.class, log_create_serset sets .n = 2 only when .fit is on, else .n = 1 ('only call for ci'), so nofit makes one view and uses one plot number. _dash_plot_style_indices (dash_render.py:57783-57787) adds 2 for every ci type and never reads nofit. Probe nf_stcolor: twoway scatter #D41159 (p2) and lfit #00BF7F (p3); dash scatter #00bf7f and lfit #ffd400, with the fit Line kept as a hidden renderer. ci_plot_index_truth.json has no nofit case. Likely knock-on, not verified: legend numbering (dash.sthlp:4441, 'a confidence-interval fit such as lfitci is two') probably also counts two under nofit, so order()/label() numbers would be off by one too.
- **PIC-4** economist: a graph's note() is not drawn at all — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (the help claims it is drawn)
  - twoway: twoway draws note() in the reverse_big box (white medlarge text on #7b92a8) at the top right of the title band.
  - dash: The page model holds a Label 'The note' (tags dash_textbox/note, background #7b92a8) in fig.center, with x/y Nodes targeting the Title's right/top. BokehJS paints nothing: no note appears at 384, 424 or 1000 px. The by() note 'Graphs by Car origin' is drawn under economist, but a plain note() is not.
  - evidence: picture/run2 tt_economist: tw_tt_economist.svg shows the boxed note; shots/y_tt_economist.png and shots/full_tt_econ*.png show no note. bk.py finds the Label in d_tt_economist.html. The help claim at dash.sthlp:6204 ('the note sits in its box in the title's band, at the top right') is false in the drawn page. test_scheme_rendered_output.py and test_mode_parity.py check only the model.
  - fix: Place the note where BokehJS paints it (an 'above' panel Title/Label row aligned right, or frame-anchored screen units), and add a headless-Chrome test that the note is drawn.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked, and the help claim is false. I re-rendered d_tt_economist.html myself in headless Chrome (test/range_probe.py, 1000 px wide, no JS errors). Title, subtitle, caption and axes are painted; the note is not, neither its #7b92a8 box nor its text. The model holds Label 'The note' with x/y = Node(target=<Title>, symbol right/top) (dash_render.py:54518-54527, the in_band branch). BokehJS evidently does not resolve a Node that targets a Title for a Label in fig.center. tw_tt_economist.svg draws the boxed note. dash.sthlp:6202-6205 says the note 'sits in its box in the title's band, at the top right'. Only economist among the shipped schemes takes this branch (note clock 1/11 beside an aligned title).
- **PIC-5** Hollow V and v (and smv) are drawn as closed triangles, not twoway's open chevron — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked; help claims parity
  - twoway: V/v are an open chevron: a 3-vertex path, not closed, stroked only.
  - dash: Bokeh 'inverted_triangle' at fill_alpha 0: a closed triangle outline with a top edge.
  - evidence: shots/z_sym_stcolor.png (row 2 x=17-19 is V, row 3 x=1-3 is v). symbol_pattern_truth.json sym.V: path, vertices 3, closed false. dash_render.py:8262 `"V": "inverted_triangle"`. The help claim at dash.sthlp:6069 ('V and v an open chevron') is false. test_V_is_the_open_chevron checks only the marker name and alpha.
  - fix: Draw the chevron as a custom path or two segments per point (both renderers and filter paths), or document it as a difference.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), and the help claim is false. dash_render.py:8261-8263 map V/v/smv to 'inverted_triangle'. BokehJS draws rotate(PI) then s(), a closed path (closePath), so with fill_alpha 0 the top edge is stroked. symbol_pattern_truth.json sym.V/sym.v: one path, 3 vertices, closed false. dash.sthlp:6069 ('V and v an open chevron') and the comment at dash_render.py:8147 both claim the chevron. test_V_is_the_open_chevron checks only the marker name, fill_alpha and size ratio. The proportions match (both are equilateral); only the closing edge differs.
- **PIC-6** + / plus / smplus are drawn as a fat filled cross — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Two stroked lines (0.3 rs wide), no fill. + at vlarge is 3.12 x 3.12 rs.
  - dash: Bokeh 'plus' is a filled 12-vertex polygon whose arms are 0.375 x size thick (about 1.2 rs at vlarge), filled in the marker colour. This also hits p6 of the mono scheme cycles.
  - evidence: shots/z_sym_stcolor.png (row 2 x=5-7, row 4 x=17-19). dash_render.py:8232/8233/8260 map to 'plus'. bokeh.min.js plus: const a=3*o/8 ... fill.apply.
  - fix: Map + / plus / smplus to Bokeh 'cross' (two lines), with fill off.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. dash_render.py:8232, 8233 and 8260 map +, plus and smplus to Bokeh 'plus'. BokehJS plus is a 12-vertex polygon with arm half-width 3*r/8, filled (fill.apply). d_sym_stcolor.html sets fill_color with no fill_alpha 0, so the cross is drawn filled. symbol_pattern_truth.json sym.+ is tags [line, line], filled false. The extent matches O; the arms are about 5 times too thick. scheme-s1mono and s2mono set symbol p6 plus. Bokeh's 'cross' marker (lines only) is the right fix.
- **PIC-7** economist: range, paired, spike and dropline lines are drawn at linewidth p (1.2) where twoway uses pother (0.8) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'A custom scheme's linewidth p#...' S3 resolution claims otherwise)
  - twoway: rspike, rcap, rcapsym, rline, rconnected, spike, dropline, pcspike, pccapsym and the pcarrow/pcbarrow shafts take linestyle p#other: 0.8 rs (pother thick) under economist. The arrowheads are 1.2 and markers 3.63 rs.
  - dash: Draws all of these lines at 1.2 rs (linewidth p), so economist range plots are 50% too thick.
  - evidence: picture/run2 rk_economist (corpus.py): TW lines 0.8 rs for #3e647d, #7b92a8, #82c0e9, #2d6d66, #bfa19c, #97b6b0, #d7d29e, #1a476f, #90353b and the shafts of #9c8847/#938dd2; dash Segment/Line 1.2. Also c4_economist (rcap): TW 0.8, dash 1.2 (shots/s_econ_a.png). Style chain: ado/base/style/seriesstyle-p#.style (area p#) -> areastyle-p#.style (linestyle p#other) -> linestyle-p#other.style (width p#other). BUGS.md:2467 resolution ('plain p# for ... spikes and caps, dropline, ... pcspike, pccapsym'; 'under economist 6.0 px (1.2 units), as Stata draws them') is contradicted. BUGS.md:1921 had recorded pother.
  - fix: Route the line width of the range, spike, dropline and pc types through p#other -> pother -> p# -> p (keeping p# for heads and markers), with economist rows in linewidth_chain_truth.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c); the BUGS S3 resolution is wrong. The style chain is seriesstyle-p#: area p# -> areastyle-p#: linestyle p#other -> linestyle-p#other: width p#other. Economist sets linewidth p 1.2 and pother thick (.8). tw_rk_economist.svg (rs 15.84 units): rspike/rcap/spike/dropline/pc shafts and rline/rconnected paths at 12.67 (0.8 rs); pcarrow heads and markers at 19.01 (1.2). The dash page draws every Segment, Line and MultiLine at 4.608 px (1.2 rs). dash's own linewidth_chain_truth.json lwt7 (#include economist) records area_line 0.8 for rcap, spike, dropline and pcarrow, against 'line' 1.2. dash follows 'line', twoway draws 'area_line'. test_economist_draws_its_pc_lines_at_linewidth_p therefore pins the wrong key. BUGS.md:1921 had noted pother .8 and 'left it'.
- **PIC-8** economist: the rarea outline is 0.8 rs centred where twoway draws parea 0.2 rs inside — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'rarea and rbar outlines...' resolution assumes the chains agree)
  - twoway: rarea and area outlines take linestyle p#area (parea thin): stroke 0.4 rs clipped to the polygon, so 0.2 rs is visible.
  - dash: The rarea bound Lines use p_other_band_styles (pother thick, 0.8 rs), centred on the bound, so the outline is about 4 times as heavy.
  - evidence: c3_economist: TW usepath stroke #3e647d 0.4 clipped against dash Line 0.8; rk_economist: TW #c10534 0.4 against dash 0.8 (shots/s_econ_a.png, row 2). dash_render.py:12429-12446 (p_other_band_styles reads linewidth p#other/pother). DECISIONS A34 ('the p#other chain ends at the same width as p#area') does not hold for economist. meta, s2color and stcolor match.
  - fix: Style the rarea (and the new area) outline from the p#area chain (linewidth p#area -> parea).
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. map_rarea (dash_render.py:22978-23006) styles both bound Lines from p_other_band_styles, whose line_width follows linewidth p#other -> pother -> p# (dash_render.py:12429-12446). Economist's pother is thick, so dash draws 3.072 px (0.8 rs) centred on each bound. tw_rk_economist.svg strokes rarea #C10534 at 6.34 clipped (0.2 rs shows inside), as linestyle p#area -> parea thin says. linewidth_chain_truth lwt7 rarea area_line is 0.2. The comment's premise that 'the p#other chain ends at the same values' (DECISIONS A34) fails under economist. s2color and stcolor agree.
- **PIC-9** Histogram empty bins are drawn as an outline line along the base — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: A bin with zero count draws nothing. #59 has 7 rects; #60 (discrete) has 18 rects for 26 bins; #61 has 52 rects in total.
  - dash: Emits a Quad for every bin, including top==bottom ones (1 of 8 in #59, 8 of 26 in #60, 29 in #61). These draw a light-blue outline segment along y=0 across the empty range.
  - evidence: tw59/60/61.svg rect counts against d59/60/61.html Quad rows with top==bottom; shots/p60.png and shots/sheet1.png (#59) show the line from 54.5 to 61.5.
  - fix: Omit zero-height bins (or give them line_alpha 0) in both renderers and in the saved page's refit after a filter move.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. tw59.svg has 7 fill rects and tw60.svg 18. Decoding dash's Quad 'top' columns gives 8 bins with 1 zero (#59), 26 with 8 zeros (#60) and 81 with 29 zeros (#61: 81-29 = 52, matching twoway). The Quad has line_width 0.576 and line_color #5faaff, so a zero-height bin strokes a line along y=0. shots/p60.png shows it from 54.5 to 61.5. No test or doc covers empty bins (test_statistical_truth compares non-empty bins only).
- **PIC-10** dot: no row of dots for an observation whose y is missing (dotextend yes) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: yxview_dot_draw.ado draw_full_dots loops over every serset observation using x only, so an observation with missing y still gets its full row of dots. sp500 in 1/45 gives 45 rows x 75 = 3375 dots and 44 markers.
  - dash: Drops the row ('A missing value draws no row'), giving 44 rows x 75 = 3300 dots.
  - evidence: gr_example #48/#49: tw 3375 circles in 45 distinct columns against dash Scatter n=3300 in 44 (shots/sheet1.png, leftmost column). The same in all 10 schemes (corpus c7). Code: dash_render.py:23751 and :23767 (`ok = np.isfinite(vals)`). test/test_dot_rows.py:227 pins dash's rule with no truth. dotextend(no) matches (#50: 44 rows both).
  - fix: Under dotextend yes, draw the full row for every observation with a non-missing x, in _dot_positions and its JS twin dash_dot_positions.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. yxview_dot_draw.ado draw_full_dots loops j = 1/serset N and draws from base to min and max using x only. tw48.svg: 3375 dots (r 4.93 fill + r 1.69 stroke) in 45 distinct columns and 44 markers. dash _dot_positions (dash_render.py:23766-23770) masks ends with np.isfinite(vals) under extend, so the row is dropped. test_a_missing_value_draws_no_row (test/test_dot_rows.py:226) pins the dotextend(no) case only. That case matches twoway: draw_dots exits when abs(. - base)/delta > 10000.
- **PIC-11** Legend keys of rcap, rcapsym, pccapsym, dropline, pcarrow, pcbarrow and rarea have the wrong shape — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rcap's key is a line with end caps; rcapsym and pccapsym have a marker at each end; dropline a marker at the right end; pcarrow a line with an arrowhead; pcbarrow heads at both ends; rarea a filled, outlined rect.
  - dash: Bokeh draws each referenced renderer's own legend glyph. rcap is a line with a small horizontal dash in the middle; rcapsym, pccapsym and dropline are a line with one marker in the centre; pcarrow and pcbarrow are a plain line; rarea is a rect with its two outline Lines drawn as a stroke through the middle.
  - evidence: Key zoo picture/run1 key_stcolor (shots/z_key_zoom.png), run2 rk_stcolor (shots/y_rk_stcolor.png), gr_example #101 (shots/p101_key.png).
  - fix: Give these types dedicated legend-only renderers in twoway's key shape (caps or end markers at the key ends, an arrowhead, the band rect only), in both modes.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. In shots/z_key_zoom.png, rcap has a line with end caps in twoway and a mid dash in dash. rcapsym/pccapsym have end markers in twoway and a centre marker in dash; dropline an end marker against a centre one. pcarrow has an arrowhead in twoway and a plain line in dash. rarea is an outlined rect in twoway and a rect with a stroke through it in dash. dash_render.py has no legend-only key renderer, and dash.sthlp:4453 says 'The legend is laid out as twoway lays it out' with no note on key shapes.
- **PIC-12** A right-hand y axis title reads top-to-bottom; twoway's reads bottom-to-top — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Every y axis title is rotate(-90) (reads upward), including yaxis(2) and economist's right-hand y axis.
  - dash: Bokeh's right-side axis label is turned +90 (reads downward); no axis_label_orientation is set on right axes.
  - evidence: tracking/y2_tw.svg 'Price' rotate(-90 ...) against y2_dash.html (shots/y2.png). Every economist page (c1/c3/c4, shots/s_econ_a.png).
  - fix: Set the right axes' axis_label_orientation so the text reads upward (both renderers, by() edge titles too).
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. tw_tt_economist.svg writes the 'Y title' text with transform rotate(-90 ...), so it reads upward. My headless render of d_tt_economist.html shows the right-hand 'Y title' reading top to bottom. dash_render.py never sets axis_label_orientation (it only appears in the _DASH_ALT_TITLE_PROPS list, line 45402), so Bokeh's 'parallel' default on a right axis applies. It hits every economist graph and any yaxis(2).
- **PIC-13** A legend placed in a by() at() hole keeps full-size text — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked (BUGS.md 'by()'s legend in an at() hole sat at its cell's top left...' fixed placement only)
  - twoway: In by(region, legend(at(4) pos(0))) the legend in the hole takes the panel scaling: key labels 2.953 rs (0.85 x).
  - dash: Labels stay 10pt (3.474 rs) with the full 31x13 px key box.
  - evidence: gr_example #65: tw65.svg legend text 2.953 rs against #64's 3.474; d65.html Legend label_text_font_size 10pt (shots/sheet2.png).
  - fix: Scale an at()-hole legend's text and keys by by()'s size multiplier.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. tw64.svg legend text is 112.56/32.4 = 3.474 rs; tw65.svg (legend in at(4) pos(0)) is 95.68/32.4 = 2.953 rs (0.85 x). Every Legend in d64.html and d65.html has label_text_font_size 10pt and glyph box 31x13, so dash does not scale the hole legend. Confidence raised to high.
- **PIC-14** Legend marker keys are drawn at 0.4 x the key box, not at the marker's size, and the boxes are wider — minor, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Legend section (D-31.a)
  - twoway: A key marker is the plot's own marker size, and a marker-only key is only as wide as the marker. Key markers measured: s2color 1.83 rs, economist 3.63, meta 1.73, stcolor 1.34.
  - dash: Bokeh draws legend markers at 0.4 x min(glyph_width, glyph_height), at angle 0. Outer sizes: s2color 1.65 rs, economist 2.76 (-24%), meta 1.03 (-40%), stcolor 1.65 (+23%). msize()/msangle() never reach the key. Marker-only keys are a full 13 (s2color) or 8 (stcolor) rs wide. Under economist at its 384 px reference the 3-column legend overflows the figure and its labels are clipped at both sides.
  - evidence: bokeh.min.js MarkerView._get_legend_args (size=.4*Math.min(...)); corpus c11 key markers (tw.py); shots/s_lg.png and shots/s_misc.png (economist clipping). Documented at dash.sthlp:4456-4461 (maintainer ruling D39 D-31.a, BUGS.md:4442-4444).
  - fix: None under the current ruling. To match, draw legend-only renderers sized in the key or a custom legend.
  - verifier: Confirmed as a documented deliberate difference (b). dash.sthlp:4456-4461 says the browser draws a key's marker at 0.4 of the box's smaller side, where twoway uses the marker's own size and width. shots/y_lg_economist.png shows the smaller keys. One part goes beyond the documented ruling: shots/s_misc.png (economist, 384 px, 3 columns) shows dash clipping 'Mileage (mpg)' and 'Turn circl...' at the figure edges. twoway overlaps the labels but does not clip them. That clipping is not documented.
- **PIC-15** Outlines are centred on bars, histogram bins, CI bands and rarea, where twoway strokes them inside — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Bar and bin fill rects are exactly barwidth wide (54.34 SVG units = 1.0015 day in #32; 0.597 day for barw(.6)), with the outline inset by half its width. Area and CI outlines are drawn at 2x width clipped to the inside. rcap caps use stroke-linecap round.
  - dash: Bokeh centres strokes, so bars look 0.2 rs (about 0.8 px) wider and band outlines spill 0.15 rs outside. rcap caps are butt-ended (0.15 rs shorter at each end). Markers compensate for the stroke; bars do not.
  - evidence: tw32/33/98.svg rect widths against VBar width 86400000/51840000 ms with line_width 0.768 px; tw100.svg 74 caps with stroke-linecap=round. Help documents centred stroking for markers only (dash.sthlp:4770).
  - fix: Optionally shrink the bar/bin width by line_width in data units (or draw the outline as an inset rect), and round the cap ends.
  - verifier: Confirmed (c), untracked. In tw32.svg the fill rect is 54.34 wide and the outline rect is inset by 3.24 (half of stroke 6.48), so twoway strokes inside. Bokeh canvas strokes are centred, so dash's bars look 0.2 rs wider. tw100.svg has 74 stroke-linecap='round' cap lines (37 obs x 2); dash's Segments use the default butt cap. The help documents centred stroking for markers only (dash.sthlp:4770).
- **PIC-16** The 1 px floor makes dot-plot dots 0.46 rs against twoway's 0.30 — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:3811
  - twoway: A dot is a 0.1 rs circle with a 0.2 rs outline: 0.30 rs (1.15 px at reference) overall.
  - dash: The fill is floored to 1 px before the 0.768 px outline is added: 1.77 px (0.46 rs).
  - evidence: #48 and corpus c7 in all 10 schemes; dash_render.py:23733-23735 (`g.size = 1.0`). Documented at dash.sthlp:3811 ('a dot is drawn at least one pixel across').
  - fix: Apply the floor to fill plus outline, not to the fill alone.
  - verifier: Confirmed (b), documented. In tw48.svg a dot is a fill circle r 4.93 plus a stroke circle r 1.69 at width 6.48: 9.86 units = 0.30 rs (about 1.17 px) overall. dash_render.py:23733-23735 floors the fill size to 1 px before adding the 0.768 px outline, giving 1.77 px (0.46 rs). Documented at dash.sthlp:3811 ('a dot is drawn at least one pixel across'). twoway's dot is already wider than 1 px overall, so moving the floor to fill plus outline would close the gap.
- **PIC-17** Dot-type line patterns are coarser after whole-pixel rounding — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort S, tracked: dash.sthlp:1287-1289
  - twoway: dot is 0.1 on / 0.7 off rs (0.8 rs period); tight_dot is 0.1 / 0.4 (0.5 rs).
  - dash: Bokeh line_dash takes integers, so dot is [1,3] px (0.26/0.78 rs: dots 2.6x longer, period +30%) and tight_dot is [1,2] (period +56%). The dash/longdash families round within 4%.
  - evidence: run1 pat_stcolor: SVG segment runs against the Line line_dash values (shots/z_pat_zoom.png). Documented at dash.sthlp:1287-1289 ('to the whole pixel').
  - fix: None required. Could scale the dash array by a DPR factor or use line_dash_offset tricks.
  - verifier: Confirmed (b), documented at dash.sthlp:1287-1289 ('to the whole pixel'). d_pat_stcolor.html draws dot as [1,3] and tight_dot as [1,2]. symbol_pattern_truth pat.dot runs are about 0.1/0.7 rs and tight_dot about 0.1/0.4. Bokeh's DashPattern rejects floats (checked: Line(line_dash=[0.4, 2.8]) fails validation), so the rounding is forced by the library.
- **PIC-18** Graph text with markup on part of it is drawn plain — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp 'Text in graphs'
  - twoway: #13/#14/#15 draw only the tagged run in bold, italic or another font ({bf:Source}: {it:Consumer Reports}).
  - dash: Draws the whole text in one plain style, with a note.
  - evidence: shots/p14.png; documented at dash.sthlp:5172-5182.
  - fix: None under the current design (Bokeh text has one style).
  - verifier: Confirmed (b), documented at dash.sthlp:5172-5182: one style per text, partial markup drawn plain, with one note per graph and kind of text. Bokeh text glyphs carry one style per string, so effort L is fair.

  Not covered: Schemes outside the requested subset were not re-run: s1rcolor, s1manual, s2color8, s2gcolor, s2manual, s2gmanual, stcolor_alt, stcolor_meta, stcolor_mv, stcoloralias, stgcolor_alt, stgcolor_meta, stgcolor_mv, stmono2, stsj and sttrans. PIC-7 and PIC-8 will recur in any scheme whose pother or parea differs from p.; by() layout at the 384 px reference: dash stacks panels below n x 240 px (documented reflow). by() pages were compared for strips, colours and glyphs only, not panel geometry (the by auditor's area).; Served app (dash serve / python app.py): only saved pages were rendered. PIC-4's served behaviour is unverified.; Pixel-exact picture comparison. Geometry was compared through the SVG and Bokeh models in rs, and shapes by side-by-side headless-Chrome screenshots (picture/shots). There is no automated image diff.; Contour heatmap/outlines/alpattern parts, contourline colorlines, and the colour-key title placement (the economist and s1mono key titles sit differently by eye; left to the colorvar/contour auditors).; Histogram bin edges and kdensity curves were checked by eye only (kde_hist_fn_truth covers them). Axis ranges and ticks were seen to differ in several pairs but belong to the axes/natscale auditors.; msangle() on non-circle symbols, mlabel text geometry and legend key marker angle were not measured.; My pcarrow corpus row (nlswide1 in 1/12) was refused by both commands (r(198): nlswide1 has only 9 observations), so pcarrow was measured through the key zoo and the arrow probe instead.

### series-lists-merges (14)

- **MS-1** Six label options given twice (yvarlabel, xvarlabel, mlabprefix, mlabsuffix, lalign, mlabstyle) are refused; twoway uses the last copy — major, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: advanced_options.sthlp says these options are 'rightmost'. Measured: scatter mpg weight, yvarlabel("Q") yvarlabel("R") draws the title R with rc 0. xvarlabel twice draws R. mlabprefix("[") mlabprefix("<") draws '<AMC Concord'. mlabsuffix twice draws 'AMC Concord>'. lalign twice, mlabstyle twice and yvarlabel("Q") yvarl("R") are all rc 0.
  - dash: Each is refused with r(198): 'option yvarlabel() not allowed' (and the same for xvarlabel(), mlabprefix(), mlabsuffix(), lalign(), mlabstyle() and yvarl()). This contradicts dash.sthlp:6461-6463, which says a copy-pasted command 'always runs'.
  - evidence: Probes run1 D11, run2 E06, run3 G01-G04 and G06 (twoway rc 0 / dash rc 198; SVGs t_G01.svg and t_G02.svg). Root cause: dash.ado:2517-2560 _dash_label_split joins every token it accepted and parses them again with a single -syntax- (line 2551), which refuses a repeated option. _dash_opts_repeats never sees these options because they are taken out first.
  - fix: In _dash_label_split, keep only the last copy of each option (resolve each by full name, as _dash_rep_reduce does) before the final syntax call. Add TP-14a rows for all six options.
  - verifier: Code: _dash_parse_plot_opts calls _dash_label_split (dash.ado:1240) before _dash_opts_repeats runs (dash.ado:6640). The final non-capture -syntax- at dash.ado:2551 refuses a second copy. Measured outputs (multiseries run1 D11, run2 E06, run3 G01-G04/G06; repo clean at 7dced3a, probes run after the last code commit): twoway rc 0 every time, dash rc 198 'option yvarlabel()/xvarlabel()/mlabprefix()/mlabsuffix()/lalign()/mlabstyle()/yvarl() not allowed'. The twoway SVGs draw the last copy: title R, labels '<AMC Concord' and 'AMC Concord>'. This contradicts dash.sthlp:6459-6463, which says only an option twoway takes once is refused when given twice. Not in BUGS.md (the natscale entry is the only open one) and not in TP-14a. Kept at major because the whole command is refused, not drawn differently, although it is rare to type these options twice.
- **MS-2** yvarlabel()/xvarlabel() are ignored on scatteri, pci, pcarrowi, function, kdensity and histogram — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: TR-6 (prior note, function only); the rest untracked
  - twoway: twoway uses the strings on these types too. scatteri ..., yvarlabel("Q") xvarlabel("R") is titled Q/R, and function is titled Q/R. kdensity mpg, xvarlabel("M") yvarlabel("D") is titled D/M. histogram is titled 'D/Density' on y and M on x. pci ..., yvarlabel("Q" "R") xvarlabel("S") is titled 'Q/R' on y and 'S' + '/xb' (two lines) on x.
  - dash: dash draws the defaults: y/x, kdensity mpg/x, Density/Mileage (mpg), y/yb and x/xb. This contradicts dash.sthlp:4761-4763 ('the axis titles and the legend keys take them').
  - evidence: Probes run1 D03, D04, D05, D06 and D07. In dash_render.py:58096-58117, _dash_layer_series returns before reading _yvl/_xvl in the univariate, scatteri, pci/pcarrowi and function branches. pcarrowi is inferred from the same branch.
  - fix: Apply Ly/Lx in those branches as twoway does. The histogram joins the string with its scale word, and pc x titles keep '/xb'. Measure pcarrowi and add truth rows.
  - verifier: In dash_render.py:58096-58117, the univariate, scatteri, pci/pcarrowi and function branches return fixed text without calling Ly/Lx. Twoway SVGs: D03 Q/R, D04 Q/R, D05 D/M, D06 'D/Density'/M, D07 'Q/R' / 'S' + '/xb'. The dash pages draw y/x, y/x, 'kdensity mpg'/x, Density/'Mileage (mpg)' and y/yb, x/xb. varlabel_mlabel_truth.json has no rows for these types. This contradicts dash.sthlp:4760-4762. pcarrowi was not measured but goes through the same branch.
- **MS-3** yvarlabel("") gives the legend key 'scatter' and no y title; twoway uses the variable name — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: An empty string means 'no label', so the name is used (advanced_options.sthlp). scatter mpg weight, yvarlabel("") is titled 'mpg'. scatter mpg price weight, yvarlabel("" "B") keys 'mpg' and 'B'. scatter mpg price weight, yvarlabel("A" "") keys 'A' and 'price'.
  - dash: No y title is drawn, and the legend key text is the plot type, 'scatter' (keys 'scatter','B' and 'A','scatter').
  - evidence: Probes run2 E04 and E05, run1 D21. dash.ado:2512-2514 and dash_render.py:58038-58040 generalise varlabel.x_empty (xvarlabel("") draws no x title, which was measured) to y. The legend falls back to the type name when the text is empty.
  - fix: For yvarlabel, treat "" as 'use the variable name' in both the key and the y title. Keep the measured x_empty rule for xvarlabel only. Never fall back to the plot-type word for a key.
  - verifier: Twoway SVGs: E04 titles the y axis 'mpg', E05 keys 'mpg','B', D21 keys 'A','price'. This matches advanced_options.sthlp:211-215 ("" is treated as no label, so the name is used). Dash pages: E04 has no y axis_label; E05 keys 'scatter','B'; D21 keys 'A','scatter'. Cause: dash_render.py:58038-58040 treats "" as an empty label for y too. The x rule comes from the measured varlabel.x_empty row (scripts/extract_varlabel_mlabel_truth.do:103), and twoway's own docs disagree with that measurement for x. So keep the x rule, and fix y only.
- **MS-4** yvarlabel() relabels the variable in every plot of the graph in twoway; dash applies it per layer — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: (scatter mpg weight) (scatter mpg weight, yvarlabel("B")) keys B,B with y title B. (scatter mpg weight, yvarlabel("A")) (scatter mpg length, yvarlabel("B")) keys B,B with y title B: the last yvarlabel given for mpg wins in every plot. A different variable is unaffected (E01: A, Price).
  - dash: Keys 'Mileage (mpg)','B' and 'A','B', with no y title.
  - evidence: Probes run2 E02 and E03, run1 D16. dash.ado:5453 writes yvarlabel per record; dash_render.py _dash_layer_series reads it per plot. The help (dash.sthlp:4761: 'that layer's variables') states the per-layer reading.
  - fix: Resolve yvarlabel/xvarlabel per variable name across the graph, last copy wins, before keys and titles are built. Measure x and mixed cases first.
  - verifier: Twoway: E02 keys B,B with y title B; E03 keys B,B with y title B; E01 keys A,Price (a different variable is not affected). Dash: E02 keys 'Mileage (mpg)','B' with no y title; E03 keys A,B. dash.sthlp:4760 documents the per-layer reading ('that layer's variables'), but 'Differences from twoway' does not list it, so it is untracked. The twoway behaviour probably comes from shared sersets, so plots with different if/in or samples may behave differently. Measure those cases first, as the finding says.
- **MS-5** xvarformat()/yvarformat() do not reach marker labels of the plotted variable — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight in 1/5, mlabel(mpg) yvarformat(%9.2f) labels 22.00, 17.00, ... . mlabel(weight) xvarformat(%9.1f) labels 2930.0, ... . mlabel(price) with yvarformat is unaffected.
  - dash: Labels use the variable's own format: 22, 17 and 2,930.
  - evidence: Probes run1 D10 and run2 E20 (labels compared); E19 matches. dash_render.py _dash_layer_tick_format (around line 1849) applies x/yvarformat to ticks only.
  - fix: When mlabel() names the layer's x or y variable, write its labels in that layer's xvarformat/yvarformat.
  - verifier: Twoway SVG D10 labels 22.00, 17.00, ... and E20 labels 2930.0, ...; E19 (mlabel(price)) keeps 4,099. In the dash pages the __dash_mlabel_mpg column is ['22','17',...] and __dash_mlabel_weight is ['2,930',...]. dash.sthlp:4746-4748 and BUGS.md:2986 limit x/yvarformat to axis ticks. Nothing tracks the marker-label case.
- **MS-6** pcarrowi headlabel is not drawn: labels stay at the tail — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: pcarrowi 20 3000 30 4000 "A" 25 3000 35 4000 (9) "B", headlabel draws A and B at the heads (4000,30) and (4000,35). The same holds with recast(pcbarrow) headlabel.
  - dash: The labels are drawn at the tails (3000,20) and (3000,25). dash.sthlp:3472-3473 says headlabel moves the labels on pcarrowi.
  - evidence: Probes run1 C08 and run2 E24 (SVG text x=3837.6 is the head; dash LabelSet x=3000). dash_render.py:25834-25886 map_pcarrowi always passes x1/y1 to _immediate_labels (line 25885). test_mode_parity 'pcarrowi headlabel with labels' compares only the two modes, not twoway.
  - fix: Read headlabel in map_pcarrowi and use x2/y2. Add a truth row.
  - verifier: map_pcarrowi (dash_render.py:25834-25886) always passes x1/y1 to _immediate_labels and never reads headlabel; map_pcarrow and map_pcbarrow do read it (25461, 25565). Twoway SVG C08: A at x=3837 (4000 is at 3795) and y=946 (30 is at 918), i.e. at the head; E24 (recast(pcbarrow)) is also at the head. The dash LabelSet source is x1=[3000], y1=[20]. dash.sthlp:3471-3473 says headlabel moves the labels on pcarrowi, so the help is wrong. test_mode_parity only compares the two modes, and TP-14f only checks headlabel given twice.
- **MS-7** pci labels are dropped under recast(pccapsym/pcarrow/pcscatter), with a false note — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: pci ... "A" ... (9) "B", recast(pccapsym) draws A at 3 o'clock and B at 9 at the first points. recast(pcarrow) and recast(pcscatter) also label the tails (twoway_pci.sthlp points to pccapsym's marker options under recast).
  - dash: No labels are drawn. The note says 'pci draws no labels, as twoway pci draws none', which is false once recast is given.
  - evidence: Probes run1 C07 and C13, run2 E15. dash_render.py:24916-24939 map_pci_literal drops _labels; the note is at dash.ado:6230.
  - fix: When recast() names a type that has marker labels, pass the labels and clocks to _immediate_labels (as map_pcarrowi does), and show the note only for plain pci.
  - verifier: map_pci_literal (dash_render.py:24913-24929) filters _labels and never draws them, including under _pc_recast. Twoway SVGs C07 (pccapsym) and C13 (pcarrow) draw A and B; E15 (pcscatter) draws A. The dash pages contain no LabelSet model, and their logs carry the note from dash.ado:6230-6232, which is false once recast() is given.
- **MS-8** scatteri labels vanish under recast() — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatteri 20 3000 "A" 25 4000 "B", recast(connected) and recast(dropline) draw labels A and B. recast(line) draws none.
  - dash: No labels are drawn for any recast() target.
  - evidence: Probes run1 C05 (C06 matches) and run2 E18. dash_render.py:24764 in map_scatteri returns _dash_draw_xy_as before the _immediate_labels call at 24797.
  - fix: Draw the immediate labels after the recast draw whenever the target type has marker labels.
  - verifier: map_scatteri returns _dash_draw_xy_as at dash_render.py:24764-24767, before the _immediate_labels call at 24796. Twoway SVGs C05 (connected) and E18 (dropline) draw A and B; C06 (line) draws none, and dash matches C06. The dash pages for C05 and E18 contain no LabelSet model.
- **MS-9** mlabel(nosuch) on scatteri is refused, r(111); twoway draws — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatteri 20 3000, mlabel(nosuch) and scatteri 20 3000 "A" 25 4000 "B", mlabel(nosuch) give rc 0 (the immediate labels are drawn).
  - dash: r(111) 'dash: mlabel(): variable nosuch not found'. With an existing variable, mlabel(make), the result matches (G05).
