  - verifier: Probe records: C15 twoway 0 / dash 111, E16 twoway 0 / dash 111. The dash log says 'dash: mlabel(): variable nosuch not found'. The twoway SVG for C15 draws the immediate labels A and B; E16 draws none. G05 (mlabel(make)) matches. The command is refused, but the input is odd, so minor.
- **MS-10** Legend keys are merged when two different pstyles look the same (s1mono, 14 or more line series) — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked (refines closed BUGS 'C13' merge rule)
  - twoway: line v1-v14 weight, sort scheme(s1mono) shows 14 keys, and v1-v16 shows 15 keys (only v1/v16, the same pstyle after wraparound). p6 and p14 (both #C0C0C0 solid, 8.64 wide) stay separate keys, as do p12 and p15.
  - dash: Keys 'v6/v14' and 'v12/v15' are merged: 13 keys instead of 14 or 15, with 7 rows instead of 8.
  - evidence: Probes run1 A08 and run2 E21 (SVG paths 6 and 14 are identical). dash_render.py:39385-39431 merges by _dash_key_signature (the drawn look), not by pstyle. Scatter under s1mono (A07) matches.
  - fix: Merge only keys whose resolved p# (after pcycle/wrap) is the same and that have no style options; the existing merge_* cases should still pass. s1manual and stmono1 share the colour duplicates, but this is unmeasured.
  - verifier: dash_render.py:39403-39431 merges keys by _dash_key_signature, the drawn look, unless a style option is given. Twoway SVGs: E21 has 14 keys v1..v14; A08 has 15 keys, only v1/v16 merged. Dash legends: E21 has 13 keys with v6/v14 merged; A08 has 13 keys with v6/v14 and v12/v15 merged. E17 (explicit pstyle(p6)/pstyle(p14)) matches in both. This is consistent with twoway merging by pstyle identity: the legend_geometry fixtures merge_pstyle and hist_only are cases where the pstyle is shared.
- **MS-11** Default x title when plots on different y axes have different x variables: twoway writes '<first>...' — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: partly: deferred residual in closed BUGS entry 'horizontal is not drawn on any plot type...' (the horizontal yaxis(2) case only)
  - twoway: (scatter mpg weight) (scatter price length, yaxis(2)) is titled 'Weight (lbs.)...'. (scatter mpg weight) (scatteri 20 3000 25 4000, yaxis(2)) is titled 'Weight (lbs.)...'. (scatter mpg weight) (scatter mpg length, yaxis(2)) (scatter price weight, yaxis(2)) is titled 'Weight (lbs.)...'.
  - dash: No title, no title, and 'Weight (lbs.)'. dash uses the first-equals-last rule over all layers.
  - evidence: Probes run2 E12 and E13, run1 C04. dash_render.py:39809-39841 _dash_axis_default_titles. series_label_truth.json has no probe with differing x across y-axis groups.
  - fix: Measure the rule (it looks like: axis-group 1's title, plus '...' when another y-axis group's title differs) and apply it in both modes.
  - verifier: Twoway SVG x titles: E12, E13 and C04 are all 'Weight (lbs.)...'. Dash: E12 and C04 have no x title; E13 has 'Weight (lbs.)'. The y titles match, including E13's empty axis-2 title. The only tracking is the horizontal yaxis(2) 'Xvar...' residual in closed BUGS.md:3012/3016, and no fixture outside horizontal_truth.json has a '...' title, so 'partly tracked' is right.
- **MS-12** A graph whose only plot is on yaxis(2) is drawn with y gridlines; twoway draws none — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, yaxis(2) and pci 20 3000 30 4000, yaxis(2) draw one left axis and no #EAF2F3 gridlines (axis 2's grid is off by default).
  - dash: One left axis with the axis-1 grid visible.
  - evidence: Probes run2 E14 and run1 C10 (SVG has no grid lines; the dash page has a visible Grid on dimension 1). dash_render.py:5003-5017 _dash_lone_second_axes renumbers the lone axis to 1, so it takes axis 1's grid default.
  - fix: When a lone axis k>=2 is renumbered, keep axis k's grid default (and its other default looks).
  - verifier: Twoway SVGs E14 and C10 each have a single #EAF2F3 stroke, the plot-region border; E01 (axis 1) has 5, four of them grid lines. So twoway draws no y grid on a lone yaxis(2). The dash pages for E14 and C10 carry a dimension-1 Grid with grid_line_color #eaf2f3, the same as E01. _dash_lone_second_axes (dash_render.py:5003) renumbers the lone axis to 1. No fixture or test checks the grid for this case.
- **MS-13** ytitle(A) ytitle(B, suffix) is merged onto one line; twoway draws two lines — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: ytitle(A) ytitle(B, suffix) draws 'A' and 'B' as two stacked lines; the same with a third copy, ytitle(, color(red)). title(A) title(B, suffix) and note(A) note(B, suffix) draw one line 'A B', which dash matches.
  - dash: The axis label is 'A B' on one line.
  - evidence: Probes run2 E08 and run1 B17 (SVG texts at x=191 and x=291); E09 and B11 match. dash_parse.mata:11170 _dash_rep_merge (category T) joins the texts with a blank for every title key.
  - fix: For axis titles (x/y/t titles and their axis(2) forms) join suffix/prefix copies as separate lines. Measure xtitle, ttitle, l1title etc. first.
  - verifier: Twoway SVGs E08 and B17 draw the y title as two rotated texts, A at x=190.7 and B at x=290.7. E09 and B11 (title/note suffix) draw one line 'A B'. Dash: E08 and B17 have axis_label 'A B'; E09 has title 'A B', which matches. The existing truth row t_xtitle_two_suffix uses quoted strings, which give two lines in dash too, so only the unquoted axis-title form is wrong.
- **MS-14** xscale(range()) on a log axis adds a tick at the range end — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight, xscale(range(1000)) xscale(log) widens the axis to 1000 but labels only 2,000 3,000 4,000 5,000. axis_truth.json b2_log_range (yscale(log range(1000 20000))) has drawn ticks 5000 10000 15000.
  - dash: FixedTicker [1000, 2000, 3000, 4000, 5000]: an extra '1,000'. A render of b2_log_range in the test frame adds 20000 the same way.
  - evidence: Probe run1 B04. test/test_axis_extents.py checks only the hull for b2_log_range, not the ticks. Found in passing while checking repeated options; this is axis territory.
  - fix: On log axes, do not add range() endpoints to the default ticks. Compare b2_log_range's drawn_ticks in a test.
  - verifier: Probe B04 (real Stata): the twoway SVG labels the x axis 2,000 3,000 4,000 5,000; the dash page has LogAxis with FixedTicker [1000,2000,3000,4000,5000] tagged dash_default. I rendered b2_log_range (yscale(log range(1000 20000))) with test_axis_extents._one in both modes and got FixedTicker [5000,10000,15000,20000], where axis_truth.json draws only 5,000 10,000 15,000. Plain yscale(log) gives [5000,10000,15000], which matches. Not covered by the open natscale entry and not tracked elsewhere. Confidence is now high, because the difference shows in both the real build and the test frame.

  Not covered: The served app and the page after a filter move were not compared (saved page at build only). The mode-parity suite covers the agreement between the two modes.; Only s2color was used, plus s1mono for the 16-series cycles. Other schemes' symbol and pattern cycles past 15, and whether s1manual/stmono1 merge keys as in MS-10, are unmeasured.; pcycle() combined with stylelists, multi-series styles under by() beyond stylelist_truth's BY case, and legend key pixel geometry for 16 keys were not checked.; Repeated text(), ttext(), xline(), by() and region merges were not re-probed for drawing. TP-14a/TP-14d and added_text truth already cover them through spec equality.; Not measured: yvarlabel on pcarrowi (inferred from code), xvarlabel("") on the immediate types, and what twoway draws for mlabel(existingvar) on pci or pcarrowi.; _dash_contour_split (dash.ado) uses the same parse-together pattern as _dash_label_split. Repeated contour options were not probed; the same refusal is possible but unverified.; The rarea outline (twoway strokes one outline path; dash strokes two bound lines) was seen but not compared. It is documented at dash.sthlp:449-451 and belongs to another area.

### data-values-edges (17)

- **DG-1** contour over zero observations crashes the renderer with a Python traceback — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway contour price mpg weight if price < 0: rc 0. It draws an empty plot with axis titles 'Mileage (mpg)' and 'Weight (lbs.)' and a colour key titled 'Price'.
  - dash: r(7102). A full Python traceback is printed, ending 'RuntimeError: dash_render: failed to build figure '': lower_level and upper_level cannot be NaN'. No page is written.
  - evidence: run1 case D07 (/private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/degenerate/run1/probe1.log). dash_render.py:24424 map_contour calls fig.contour with NaN levels when there are no rows. No truth test or cscript covers an emptied contour.
  - fix: In map_contour, when the layer has no rows, skip fig.contour and draw the empty layer plus the titled key the way twoway does. Add an emptied-contour probe to a truth script.
  - verifier: I re-read map_contour (dash_render.py:24350-24425). With no rows zmin and zmax are NaN, so _dash_contour_cuts (29227-29261) returns cuts=[zmax]=[nan]. The not-drawable branch then builds lv=[0.0]+[nan]+[1.0] and passes it to fig.contour at 24424. Bokeh raises 'lower_level and upper_level cannot be NaN'. The probe1.log D07 traceback matches the current line numbers, so it ran on this HEAD. twoway gives rc 0 and its SVG draws the axis titles plus the key title 'Price'. No contour test covers an empty sample, so this is untracked. The fix needs finite placeholder levels, or skipping the band renderer, when zmin is not finite.
- **DG-2** Default histogram x axis is ticked over the bin edges; twoway ticks it over the bin midpoints — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: twoway histogram mpg (auto): x ticks 15(5)40 over an axis running 12-41 (natscale over midpoints 13.8-39.2). On a constant 5 the ticks are 4.5 and 6.5 (natscale of the single midpoint 5.5).
  - dash: dash histogram mpg: x ticks 10(10)40, axis 9.6-41.4. On the constant: 5(.2)6. The same happens with if/in, '.' or '.a/.z' in the variable (H01-H04, K04, EM08). The bars themselves are identical.
  - evidence: run2 cases H01-H04 and K04; run1 case EM08. The truth exists: test/fixtures/default_axes_truth.json P08 has x major [15,5,40]. But test/test_default_axes.py:230-233 excludes it (_NOT_DEFAULT = {("P08","x")}, 'not this section's rule'). dash.sthlp:4528 claims every default axis takes Stata's _natscale over the rows each layer draws.
  - fix: For histogram layers, request the Stata tick table over the bin midpoints, not the bin edges. Keep the axis extent at the edges (twoway cur 12-41). Then remove P08 x from _NOT_DEFAULT.
  - verifier: Truth fixture default_axes_truth.json P08: x min/max 13.8125/39.1875 (the bin midpoints), major 15(5)40, cur 12-41. The run2 H01 digest shows dash ticks at 10,20,30,40 over 9.58-41.42. The cause is in dash_render.py:41810: _DASH_STATA_TICK_SKIP leaves histogram out of the Stata tick table, so the axis takes dash's model (dash.sthlp:4529, 'takes the model at once') over the bar edges 12-41, and natscale(12,41) is 10(10)40. On the constant variable (K04), twoway's 4.5/6.5 is exactly natscale's hi==lo rule (lo±1) applied to the midpoint 5.5, which confirms the midpoint rule. The only acknowledgement is the comment on test_default_axes.py:230-233 _NOT_DEFAULT. No BUGS/GAPS/help entry exists, so it is untracked. It hits every default histogram.
- **DG-3** a(b)c tick rules write floating-point residue as the zero label — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: ylabel(-.3(.1).3) labels -.3 -.2 -.1 0 .1 .2 .3. ylabel(-.7(.1).7) labels ... -.1 0 .1 ... (Stata's numlist yields an exact 0).
  - dash: In the browser the middle tick reads '5.551e-17' for -.3(.1).3 (T17, T27) and '1.110e-16' for -.7(.1).7 (T26). The tick also sits at that value. format(%9.2f) hides it ('0.00', T18).
  - evidence: run1 T17 and run2 T26/T27 (dash labels via CustomJSTickFormatter in headless Chrome; twoway text from the SVG). Cause: dash_render.py:39963-39967 _dash_expand_numlist computes a + i*b in plain floating point, without numlist's rounding.
  - fix: Reproduce Stata numlist's arithmetic for a(b)c, a[b]c and 'to': round each value at the step's precision, e.g. snap |v| < 1e-12*|b| to 0 and round to about 15 significant digits relative to b. Pin it with a truth probe of several rules that cross zero.
  - verifier: _dash_expand_numlist (dash_render.py:40061-40082) builds the values as a + b*k in plain floats with no snapping. Python gives -.3+.1*3 = 5.551e-17 and -.7+.1*7 = 1.110e-16, and the run2 digest for T26/T27 shows those exact tick values and the labels '5.551e-17' and '1.110e-16'. twoway's SVG labels '0'. By contrast, tline() positions go through Stata's own numlist (dash.ado:9555-9560), so only the label/tick rules are affected. The only zero-crossing grammar test is -1(.5)2.5, whose values are exact in binary. Untracked. A visible garbage label in a common construct such as ylabel(-.6(.2).6).
- **DG-4** Parenthesised dates that contain blanks are refused in tlabel(), tline() and tscale(range()) — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rc 0, drawn: tlabel((1 jan 2001) (1 jul 2001)), tlabel(01jan2001 (1 jul 2001)), tline((15 jun 2001)), tscale(range((1 dec 2000) (31 jan 2002))). The datelist help says dates with spaces, slashes or commas must be bound in parentheses.
  - dash: Refused. tlabel gives r(198) 'invalid label specifier, :  (1 jan 2001) (1 jul 2001):'. tline gives r(198) 'invalid line argument, (15 jun 2001)'. tscale gives r(121) 'range() invalid -- invalid numlist'. Parentheses without blanks ((1jan2001)) work (DT18, DT19).
  - evidence: run1 DT04, DT05, DT09; run2 DT20. dash_parse.mata:9310: _dash_tdate_value's allowed characters exclude the blank. dash_parse.mata:9512: _dash_tdate_lines uses tokens(seg), which splits inside parentheses. The taxis_truth.json probes c55 and lit_* never put a blank inside parentheses.
  - fix: Tokenise datelists binding parentheses. Inside parentheses, convert the text as twoway does (td()/date with blanks allowed). Then add blank-holding probes to extract_taxis_truth.do.
  - verifier: Stata's datelist.sthlp documents the form: dates with spaces, slashes or commas must be bound in parentheses, e.g. (15 apr 1973). probe1.log DT04/DT05/DT09: twoway rc 0, and t_DT04.svg labels 01jan2001 and 01jul2001. dash refuses with exactly the messages the finding quotes. _dash_tdate_value's whitelist (dash_parse.mata:9308) has no blank, and _dash_tdate_lines splits with tokens(seg) (:9512), which does not bind parentheses. twoway itself refuses the comma form (july 1, 2001), and dash matches that (DT06). No help or BUGS entry, so untracked.
- **DG-5** rarea with cmissing(n) draws its outline across the gaps — major, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: rarea y y2 x, cmissing(n) with y, x or y2 missing in some rows: one closed polygon per run, nothing drawn in a gap, and nothing for an isolated observation (3 polygons in M05).
  - dash: The fill splits correctly. But the two bound Lines run on the rebuilt source through the zero-height gap vertices, so visible diagonal outline segments join the polygons across each gap, plus a vertical spike at the lone observation x=9.
  - evidence: run1 M05 screenshots /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/degenerate/run1/shots/t_M05.png vs d_M05.png. The bound Line data hold no NaN. dash_render.py:22928 (_dash_fill_rebuild) and :22984 ('Lines on the same source'). test/test_area_cmissing.py TestRareaConnect.test_split checks only the VArea runs; area has test_the_outline_breaks, rarea has no equivalent.
  - fix: Give rarea's bound Lines their own columns, broken with NaN at each gap. Drop the synthetic gap vertices from them, as area's outline already does. Add a rarea outline-run test against area_cmissing_truth R02.
  - verifier: In map_rarea (dash_render.py:22918-22932, 22986-22998), cmissing(n) rebuilds the source with zero-height gap vertices pinned to y1 (_dash_fill_vertices 59843, _dash_fill_rebuild 59916). Both outline Lines are fig.line on that same source, which holds no NaN, so they run through each gap. The screenshots confirm it: d_M05.png has diagonal outline segments joining the polygons and a spike at x=9, while t_M05.png has three clean polygons. map_area routes its outline through _dash_join_glyph (18718), and rarea does not. test_area_cmissing.py has test_the_outline_breaks for area only, and TestRareaConnect.test_split checks only the VArea runs. Untracked. rarea CI bands with gaps under cmissing(n) are a realistic use.
- **DG-6** %tC axis ticks are placed and labelled in leap-second-corrected time, not twoway's raw tC units — minor, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg tC (%tC, January 2001): ticks at round raw tC values, labelled '01jan2001 20:26:18', '07jan2001 15:19:38', ..., '24jan2001 23:59:38'.
  - dash: The same tick numbers are applied on the converted (UTC) axis and labelled '01jan2001 20:26:40', ..., '25jan2001 00:00:00'. Every label differs from twoway's by the accumulated leap seconds (22 s here). %tc matches exactly (T04).
  - evidence: run1 T05 vs T04. dash.sthlp:4706-4707 says %tC values are converted with Stata's leap-second table. stata_ticks_truth.json has a TC probe (%tc) but none for %tC.
  - fix: Shift Stata's tick values from raw tC to the converted axis (subtract the leap seconds at each tick) before placing them. Label the ticks with the %tC writer. Add a %tC probe to extract_stata_ticks_truth.do.
  - verifier: probe1 T04/T05: dash puts identical tick ms and labels on the tc and tC axes (20:26:40 ... 25jan2001 00:00:00). twoway's tC SVG reads 20:26:18 ... 24jan2001 23:59:38, 22 s earlier, the leap seconds accumulated by 2001. The BUGS.md 'set dp comma ... %tC ... leap seconds' fix (closed, WP-31 G2) converts the values with _dash_tc_from_tC, but its resolution says nothing about where ticks go. stata_ticks_truth.json has no %tC probe. Untracked. Every label is off by up to 27 s, on a rarely used format.
- **DG-7** Default titles and keys of emptied or unfit layers differ from twoway's — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: lfit mpg weight if price<0: y title 'mpg', because the fit variable is left unlabelled when regress cannot fit. lfit y x on a dataset with no observations: y title 'y'. histogram mpg if price<0: x title 'x'.
  - dash: y title (and legend key) 'Fitted values' in both lfit cases. x title 'Mileage (mpg)' on the empty histogram. The kdensity and scatter titles on empty samples match.
  - evidence: run1 D03, E04, D05 (twoway SVG texts vs dash axis_label in Chrome). dash.sthlp:3618-3627 states the default-title rule without these cases.
  - fix: When a fit layer is noted 'regress could not fit model', title and key it with the y variable's name, as twoway does. Title an empty histogram's x axis 'x'.
  - verifier: probe1: D03 twoway y title 'mpg' vs dash 'Fitted values'. E04 twoway 'y' vs dash 'Fitted values'. D05 twoway x title 'x' vs dash 'Mileage (mpg)'. All are rc 0 on both sides. The legend is hidden in both, so the key difference shows only under legend(on). dash.sthlp:3618-3627's rule does not cover these cases. Untracked. I downgraded it to cosmetic because it appears only on empty or unfittable samples, where nothing is drawn anyway.
- **DG-8** contour that has no surface (two observations) draws no colour key — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: contour price mpg weight in 1/2: an empty plot plus a four-level colour key 4,000-4,800 titled 'Price'.
  - dash: Empty polygons, no ColorBar and no key title (C01). With 3 or more observations, or a constant z, the key is drawn (C02, C03).
  - evidence: run2 C01 screenshot /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/degenerate/run2/shots/sbs_C01.png. The digest shows no colour-bar axis for C01, while C02 and C03 have one.
  - fix: Build the colour key from the levels even when the triangulation yields no polygons.
  - verifier: In map_contour, the key is built only under 'if not lines_only and drawable: self._contour_key(...)'. A two-observation surface has note 'degenerate', so it gets no key. run2 C01: the dash digest has no third (colour-bar) axis, while C02/C03 have one. twoway's SVG for C01 draws key ticks 4,000-4,800 and the title 'Price'. The not-drawable branch's comment, 'twoway draws nothing', is wrong for the key. Untracked. I downgraded it to cosmetic: it only happens on a contour with no surface.
- **DG-9** A missing code in a label list is accepted with a misleading note; twoway refuses it — minor, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg rep78, xlabel(1/5 .a, valuelabel): r(198) 'invalid label specifier, :  1/5 .a:'.
  - dash: rc 0, with '(note:  dash: xlabel() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: .a)'. It treats the position as a sub-option.
  - evidence: run1 T11.
  - fix: In the axis-label list reader, refuse an extended-missing (or other non-number) position in twoway's words. Never route it to the sub-option note.
  - verifier: probe1 T11: twoway gives r(198) 'invalid label specifier, :  1/5 .a:'. dash gives rc 0 with the Python note from _dash_report_suboptions (dash_render.py:53486). dash.ado's _dash_axlabel_refusal (dash_parse.mata:2326ff, called at dash.ado:8776-8781) refuses unpaired labels and second rules, but not a missing-code position. Untracked.
- **DG-10** A date tline() on a %tb axis is refused with different words — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg tb (%tbsimple), tline(10nov2011): r(198) 'invalid number, (10nov2011)'.
  - dash: r(198) 'invalid line argument, 10nov2011'. tlabel(07nov2011 ...) on the same axis does match twoway (DT14).
  - evidence: run1 DT16.
  - fix: Route tline() on business-date axes through _dash_tdate_lines' numeric-axis branch, which already writes 'invalid number, (<tok>)'.
  - verifier: _dash_taxis_unit (dash_parse.mata:9264-9279) maps %tb... to 'c' under family 2. So in _dash_tdate_lines a date token gets rating 2 with unit 'c' and falls to the else branch, 'invalid line argument, <seg>', which is the %tc wording. probe1 DT16: twoway says 'invalid number, (10nov2011)'. The rc (198) matches and only the words differ. Untracked.
- **DG-11** Data spanning about ±8e307 end in a Python OverflowError traceback — cosmetic, wrong-refusal-or-acceptance, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter y x with y = ±8e307: r(199) 'unrecognized command' (twoway itself fails).
  - dash: r(7102) with a full traceback: OverflowError (34, 'Result too large') at dash_render.py:40322 in _dash_natscale_ticks (d = m * 10.0 ** z). One-signed ranges up to 8e307 draw and match twoway (X08-X10).
  - evidence: run1 X06; run2 X08, X09, X10.
  - fix: Guard 10.0 ** z against overflow in _dash_natscale_ticks. Copy twoway's r(199) refusal, or at least stop with a one-line message and no traceback.
  - verifier: _dash_natscale_ticks (dash_render.py:40316-40322): for a range of 1.6e308, z_hi = ceil(log10(rng))+1 = 310, and 10.0**310 raises OverflowError before the isfinite guard runs. probe1 X06 shows the traceback, r(7102). twoway itself fails (r(199) 'unrecognized command'), so the user loses no graph twoway would draw. Only the rc and message differ, plus an unguarded traceback. I downgraded it to cosmetic. Untracked.
- **DG-12** Markers with a missing or zero weight are invisible; twoway still draws a dot — minor, doc-claim-false, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: scatter mpg weight [aw=we] with we missing (.c) in 20 rows: those 20 markers are drawn at 'size zero', which in the SVG is a fill r=5.2 plus a 9.72-wide stroke. They show as visible small dots.

  - evidence: run1 EM03 screenshots /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/degenerate/run1/shots/t_EM03.png vs d_EM03.png; the circle radii are in t_EM03.svg. dash.sthlp:4192-4194 says a zero or missing weight's marker 'is drawn at size zero ... as in twoway'.
  - fix: Draw zero or missing-weight markers at twoway's minimal dot (the outline stroke at size 0), or document the difference.
  - verifier: t_EM03.svg: each of the 20 missing-weight rows is drawn as a filled circle of r=5.20 plus a stroked circle of r=0.34 and stroke-width 9.72 (viewBox 5400 wide). t_EM03.png shows them as visible tiny dots, and d_EM03.png shows nothing at those points. dash.sthlp:4192-4194 says such a marker 'is drawn at size zero ... as in twoway', which is true of the size but not of what is drawn. test_weighted_markers.py treats twoway's size-zero marker (_ZERO_R) as 0 and never checks visibility. Untracked. The observations twoway still shows are hidden, so I kept minor rather than cosmetic.
- **DG-13** ylabel(minmax) on an empty scale draws generic ticks where twoway labels ±1e300 — cosmetic, wrong-output, confirmed, effort S, tracked: untracked

Otherwise Bokeh's default ticker stays on the _DASH_EMPTY_SCALE 0-1 Range1d (40235-40242, 'with no tick'). run2 S07: twoway labels the y axis '-1.e+300' and '1.e+300', and the dash digest shows default ticks 0-1. Untracked, and only on an empty sample.
- **DG-14** WebGL backend above 10,000 rows draws thick dashed lines speckled; previously never measured — minor, unverified, partly, effort M, tracked: untracked
  - twoway: Not applicable (twoway has one renderer). The reference is dash's own canvas drawing.
  - dash: Seven 20,000-row pages were rendered in headless Chrome (SwiftShader WebGL active: output_backend webgl, canvas_view.webgl set). Scatter, Line and VBar use WebGL; VArea and Segment stay on canvas. Markers O/D/T/S/X/Oh with outlines and %40 alpha, the area outline, bars and rcap match the canvas render within antialiasing. line lpattern(dash) lwidth(thick) is the exception: dashes are fragmented and lighter (1,970 vs 3,985 solid-red pixels). Also, the switch counts the dataset's rows (len(self.df)), not the graph's.
  - evidence: run1 L01-L07, side-by-sides in /private/tmp/claude-501/dropin-audit/degenerate/run1/shots/ (sbs_*, zoom_*, px_L02.png); canvas copies made by rewriting "output_backend":"webgl". Threshold at dash_render.py:5567 and :63396; served app at :70285. No test renders WebGL (test_html_output.py checks only the backend name).
  - fix: Either keep dashed or patterned lines on the canvas backend (set the Line renderer's output_backend, or skip WebGL when any dashed line is present), or accept and document it. Base the threshold on the rows the figure draws. Add a headless-Chrome pixel check of WebGL against canvas.
  - verifier: Confirmed in code: _WEBGL_THRESHOLD=10_000 (dash_render.py:5567). The backend is chosen from len(self.df), the dataset's rows (63396-63397; served twin at 70284-70285), not the rows a graph draws. No test pixel-checks WebGL; test_html_output.py checks only the backend name. px_L02.png does show a fragmented, lighter thick dashed line under WebGL versus solid dashes on canvas. But that was rendered with headless Chrome's SwiftShader software WebGL, so whether real GPU WebGL renders it the same way is unverified. twoway has a single renderer, so this is internal fidelity, not a twoway comparison.
- **DG-15** The embedded-cell cap counts every observation regardless of if/in, so graphs twoway draws are refused — major, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort M, tracked: dash.sthlp Resource limits ($dash_max_cells)
  - twoway: twoway scatter y x in 1/10 draws 10 points on any dataset size. twoway has no cap.
  - dash: _dash_cell_cap uses _N times the variables. Over $dash_max_cells (default 5,000,000; ceiling 20,000,000) it refuses with r(134) before export, even for in 1/10. A two-variable graph of more than 10M observations cannot be drawn at any setting.
  - evidence: dash.ado:5219-5250 (comment: 'the renderer applies if/in itself, so if/in reduce nothing here'; nobs = _N). dash.sthlp:2945-2957 (Resource limits).
  - fix: Count only the rows the qualifiers select (they are already evaluated in Stata by _dash_qual_gen) when no filter, link() or csv needs every row. Export only those rows. Keep the documented cap for the rest.
  - verifier: _dash_cell_cap (dash.ado:5222-5250): nobs defaults to _N, and the comment says if/in 'reduce nothing here'. The callers at 11726/13171 pass nvars plus the qualifier columns (_dq_n), so an if/in actually adds a column. The cells ceiling is 20,000,000 (dash.ado:5011), so a two-variable graph of more than 10M observations is refused at any setting, and above 2.5M by default, even for 'in 1/10'. This is documented in dash.sthlp:2945-2957 (Resource limits), so it is a deliberate but documented difference. Kept major: it is a hard r(134) on graphs twoway draws, for a whole class of large datasets.
- **DG-16** With tied sort keys, cmissing(n) can break the line at a different place than twoway — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp Join order (ties)
  - twoway: line ye weight, sort cmissing(n) (ye = mpg with .a/.z in rows 1-15): 14 sub-paths. Among the tied weight 3690, the present row comes before the missing one.
  - dash: 13 runs: ties keep data order, so the missing row comes first and two runs merge.
  - evidence: run1 EM05 (twoway path sub-path lengths vs dash NaN runs). dash.sthlp:436-437: 'Points whose keys tie keep the order of the data (twoway's order among them changes from one run to the next).'
  - fix: None required, since it is documented. To match a given twoway run would need twoway's unstable sort order.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:436-437: ties keep data order, and twoway's order among ties changes from run to run. Because twoway's own result is not deterministic, dash's output is not wrong against any fixed twoway output. No fix is needed; downgraded to cosmetic.
- **DG-17** Partial SMCL styles in tick labels are drawn plain — cosmetic, deliberate-difference, confirmed, effort L, tracked: dash.sthlp Text in graphs
  - twoway: xlabel(3000 "{it:italic} text" 4000 "{bf:bold}" 5000 "a{sup:2}"): 'italic' in italics, 'bold' in bold, 2 raised.
  - dash: 'italic text' and 'bold' drawn plain, 'a²' via Unicode, with a note. The quoted text 'say "hi"' matches.
  - evidence: run1 T12. dash.sthlp:5171-5183: 'Tick labels and legend keys take a style only when every one of them has it.'
  - fix: None (documented). Would need rich-text tick labels in BokehJS.
  - verifier: Documented at dash.sthlp:5171-5183: markup on part of a text is drawn plain with a note, and tick labels and legend keys take a style only when every one of them has it. probe1 T12 behaves as documented. This is a deliberate difference, and cosmetic is right.

  Not covered: Real-GPU WebGL: only Chrome's SwiftShader software WebGL was available headless, so DG-14's dashed-line artefact needs confirming on a GPU.; Served mode (dash serve / bokeh server): every probe used the saved one-line page. Behaviour after filter moves was not probed, including the open BUGS.md:4139 natscale entry (ticks after a move), which stays the one tracked open gap in this area.; cmissing(n) on rconnected, tsline/tsrline, scatter with connect() and function was not re-probed. The fits and smooths are covered by area_cmissing_truth.json E-cases.; Marker size scale at 20,000 rows was not compared between twoway's PNG and dash: the canvas sizes differ, and the weighted_markers truth covers it.; %tC with tline()/tlabel() date literals, %tc/%tC under set dp comma, and value labels on a second axis were not probed (two-slot budget).; Degenerate data under by() (empty or one-observation panels) was not re-probed: the P1A-80/P1A-83/P1A-84 cscripts already compare it with twoway.; The CustomJSTickFormatter fallback that has labels but no format (dash_render.py:42418-42421, which writes String(tick)) was read but not reached by any probe.
